## project_1010_clara_beta

<a id="project-1010-clara-beta"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_1010_clara_beta.md`*
*Name:* project-1010-clara-beta
*Description:* CLARA - terminal ledger for the 1010 quick-commerce micro-node physics beta

**1010** is a conceptual decentralized quick-commerce system (cars = "edge caches" holding heavy inventory, scooters = "worker nodes" doing 10-min last-mile drops; drivers buy their own $30-$200 inventory; $10 cart minimums + $10/mo MRR). Brought in by Bruke as a "master seed packet" on 2026-06-27. No prior code in the repo - greenfield.

**CLARA** (Command Line Algorithmic Routing Agent) = the zero-overhead terminal ledger to beta-test the 1010 micro-node *physics* before any backend exists. The doctrine Bruke set: test the human algorithm first, prove the data flows, THEN scale hardware.

- **File:** `1010/clara_agent.py` (repo root `Pre Atlas`). stdlib-only. Run `python "1010/clara_agent.py"`.
- **What it does:** holds one $30 micro-inventory block (M&Ms/Charger/Energy Drink, wholesale*qty == $30 exactly), enforces the 600s geofence SLA, splits the clock into STAGE/TRANSIT/DOOR segments (rider taps "Mark checkpoint" on depart + arrival), auto-deducts wholesale on packet loss, routes it to a `liability_escrow` reimbursement meter.
- **Two capture layers, kept on purpose:** `clara_ledger.log` (human/forensic audit via `logging`) AND `clara_log.jsonl` (one structured row per trial = the draft backend schema; this is the *dataset* you compute median Time-to-Compute / packet-loss rate from). Env overrides: `CLARA_LOG`, `CLARA_AUDIT`, `CLARA_GEOFENCE`.
- **Hardened correctly:** money is `Decimal` (never float), elapsed is `time.monotonic()`, `PAY_ON_LATE` toggle for SLA enforcement.

**Two load-bearing caveats (told to Bruke, flagged in code):**
1. `time.monotonic()` closes *accidental* clock drift (NTP/DST), NOT spoofing. The whole timer runs on the driver's device, so a determined driver beats the SLA. Production timer MUST be server-authoritative. Marked `# REAL-WORLD VALIDATION` at the timer in `trigger_order`.
2. The single-backpack beta can FALSIFY (one rider can't clear 1.5mi in 600s = dead) but cannot VALIDATE the network - it doesn't exercise concurrency or the car->scooter relay handshake. The transit math is binding: 1.5mi at 8-12mph city = 7.5-11min, so transit alone eats 75-110% of the 600s budget. Tighten the geofence before buying hardware.

**Test design (from earlier in session):** run the human-algorithm beta with someone Bruke briefs for 10 min (NOT himself - founder charisma is a confound; the thesis is the system runs without the architect). Toothpick Protocol (Food OS) needs a menu control group to make "faster" defensible. See [[user-atlas-vision]] for the friction-removal framing.

**NEXT when resumed:** car<->scooter relay handshake (NFC custody transfer between two nodes) is the next segment to instrument; or the ingest schema/Python that loads `clara_log.jsonl` into computable stats. Related doctrine: [[feedback-assemble-first-posture]] (Decimal/logging are stdlib, not hand-rolled), [[feedback-built-at-me-verified-mechanism-not-outcome]] (every CLARA version was proven by a scripted run, not asserted).

## project_ai_coding_procedure_origin

<a id="project-ai-coding-procedure-origin"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_ai_coding_procedure_origin.md`*
*Name:* ai-coding-procedure-origin
*Description:* Origin document for Pre Atlas philosophy — ChatGPT conversation (2026-07-14) where Bruke articulated the clock-mechanism metaphor and externalized-systems principle that became delta-kernel and the rules/memory infrastructure

## What it is

A ChatGPT conversation from 2026-07-14 ("AI Coding Procedure"), pulled via chatpull to `C:/Users/bruke/chatpull/pulls/chatgpt/2026-07-14-ai-coding-procedure.md`. 2,487 lines, 17 messages, voice-transcribed.

## The core problem Bruke named

AI-assisted coding = "rebuilding the farm from scratch every time." Context evaporates between sessions. The AI becomes a genie (do one wish, forget everything) instead of a construction crew operating within a fixed structure.

## Three layers of the conversation

**Layer 1 — The diagnosis:** repeated synthesis is the failure mode. Every session re-derives state instead of reading it.

**Layer 2 — The clock mechanism:** software projects should work like clocks — stored energy (state engine), gear position (mode), escapement (controlled release of actions), display (surfaces), controls (CLI/gateway), clockmaker (Bruke), replacement-part fabricator (AI). This became delta-kernel's architecture directly.

**Layer 3 — The externalization principle:** "most people externalize the code; you are talking about externalizing the entire method." The project carries its own operating intelligence — CLAUDE.md, MEMORY.md, rules/, skills/, bearings, session retrospectives. You can stop for months and resume in minutes. You can swap AI models without losing architecture.

## What it maps to in the built system

| Clock part | What was built |
|---|---|
| Stored spring energy | delta-kernel's deterministic state engine |
| Gear position (state) | Mode system (RECOVER → CLOSURE → MAINTENANCE → BUILD → COMPOUND → SCALE) |
| Escapement (controlled release) | Governance daemon's risk-tiered action release |
| Clock hands (display) | inPACT / CycleBoard / atlas-map surfaces |
| Time-setting crown (controls) | atlas-ai CLI, /call gateway |
| Clockmaker | Bruke |
| Replacement-part fabricator | Claude (interchangeable) |

The 22-step SOP (lines 1886-1918) maps nearly 1:1 to what's now codified in `~/.claude/rules/common/development-workflow.md` and `code-review.md`.

## Key insight

Bruke wasn't learning from ChatGPT — he was extracting. The questions ("what are the functions of a clock?", "I feel like there needs to be a split procedure") are design questions, not learning questions. ChatGPT gave vocabulary (golden path, state transitions, externalized vs internalized); the architecture was already in his head.

**Why:** Five days later the LangGraph Skill Lattice shipped 7 sequences implementing exactly this — a supervisor managing controlled state transitions with delta-kernel's `checkTimeouts()` as the escapement. This is the pre-build crystallization moment.

**How to apply:** This document is the philosophical anchor for Pre Atlas. When evaluating whether a proposed change fits the system, check it against the clock-mechanism metaphor and the externalization principle. Related: [[langgraph-skill-lattice]], [[atlas-master-plan]], [[user-atlas-vision]].

## project_anatomy_auto_label_heatmap

<a id="project-anatomy-auto-label-heatmap"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_anatomy_auto_label_heatmap.md`*
*Name:* Anatomy Auto-Label Heatmap
*Description:* Text-prominence heatmap that powers the auto-label tiebreaker in the anatomy extension; combined with v0.4 vocab lock + adaptive sibling-collapse fix; tracked in eed7763 (2026-04-27); calibration scores 100% (12/12) + 3 documented known-limits as of 2026-05-02

# Anatomy Auto-Label Heatmap · IN-FLIGHT

**Location:** `C:/Users/bruke/Pre Atlas/tools/anatomy-extension/` (main repo) · NOT in any worktree.
**Status:** uncommitted (manifest at v0.4.4 · entire dir untracked except `content.js` which is " M")
**Calibration:** **100% (12/12)** + 3 documented known-limit warns (re-run 2026-05-02 in heuristic-shirley-4973c3 worktree via `preview_start anatomy-calibration` → `/calibration/`). Harness reshaped from the 14-probe version memory remembered: now 12 scoring probes + 3 known-limit warns that don't penalize.

## Worktree clarification (2026-04-26 corrected)

Earlier memory entry said "in-flight in nostalgic-goldstine-f4830c worktree" — wrong. The worktrees (`nifty-mclean-1b62c3`, `nostalgic-goldstine-f4830c`, `pensive-newton-1e606a`) do NOT contain `tools/anatomy-extension/` at all. All edits flow through the main repo. Multiple parallel sessions are editing the same untracked tree at `C:/Users/bruke/Pre Atlas/tools/anatomy-extension/`.

## What it is

A text-prominence heatmap rendered as a coarse cell grid over the page. Used as a tiebreaker in `doAutoLabel` (after bucket priority, before doc position) so labels prefer regions with denser/heavier visible text — fixes the legacy char-count failure where a wide nav bar outscored a hero headline.

## Files (uncommitted)

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `tools/anatomy-extension/lib/text-grid.js` (NEW · ~210 LOC) | `buildTextGrid({cellPx})`, `scoreRegion(grid, rect)`, `renderGridOverlay`, `removeGridOverlay`. Per-text-node WCAG color/luminance/contrast helpers (`parseColor`, `relLum`, `contrastRatio`, `effectiveBackground`, `computeProminence`). Per-pass WeakMap caches for prominence and bg. |
| `tools/anatomy-extension/lib/_smoke-text-grid.mjs` (NEW · 16 tests) | jsdom-style mock fixture mocking `fontSize`/`color`/`backgroundColor`. 16/16 pass. Includes prominence-specific cases (big headline outranks small body · high-contrast outranks low). |
| `tools/anatomy-extension/calibration/index.html` (NEW · 490 LOC) | Pentest-style harness with 16 challenges across 4 tiers (baseline, semantic, filter, edge, limit). `runAll()` is two-phase (compute outcomes first, then write UI) to avoid result-html-shifting later cards' rects. Score = passes / (passes + fails) · warns documented and don't penalize. |
| `tools/anatomy-extension/manifest.json` | v0.4.3 → 0.4.4 · `lib/text-grid.js` added to content_scripts JS list. |
| `tools/anatomy-extension/content.js` | `[grid]` HUD toggle button + `toggleTextGrid()` helper · grid-scored ranking integrated in `doAutoLabel` after bucket priority, before doc position. |
| `tools/anatomy-extension/content.css` | Grid overlay styling (uncommitted). |
| `.claude/launch.json` | Added `anatomy-calibration` config on port 8896 serving `tools/anatomy-extension`. |

## Math · why it works

```
prominence = fontSize² × WCAG_contrast_ratio(textColor, effectiveBackground)
```

Stamp model weighting: for each text node's `getClientRects()`, every grid cell the rect touches gets `prominence` added (no division by cellsTouched). A wider line of the same style contributes proportionally more total grid mass — natural area-weighting.

This replaces the legacy char-count weighting (each cell got `text.length / cellsTouched`), which:
- Diluted wide lines vs narrow lines of identical visual weight
- Treated 12px gray nav text the same as 48px black headline text
- Gave sparse-vs-dense ratio of only 2.0× when 15× was expected

## Calibration deltas (this session)

| Probe | Before | After | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Overall score | 86% (12/14) | **93% (13/14)** | One remaining fail |
| sparse-vs-dense ratio | 2.0× | **15.2×** | stamp-model fix |
| big headline outranks body | n/a (char-count) | PASS | new test |
| high-contrast outranks low | n/a (char-count) | PASS | new test |
| visibility:hidden filtering | FAIL (score=92) | FAIL (score=92.5) → **FIXED in code 2026-05-02** | `text-grid.js:37` `isElementVisible()` now checks `display:none`, `visibility:hidden`, and `opacity:0`. Re-run harness to confirm score moves to 14/14 |

## What's NOT done (next session)

1. ~~**Filter `visibility:hidden`** in `buildTextGrid` walker~~ — **DONE.** Verified 2026-05-02: live code at `lib/text-grid.js:37` has `if (cs.display === 'none' || cs.visibility === 'hidden' || parseFloat(cs.opacity) === 0) return false;`. Calibration must be re-run to confirm 14/14 (memory's 13/14 number predates this fix).
2. ~~**Commit the work**~~ — **DONE 2026-04-27** in `eed7763` ("track v0.4.4 surface"). Entire `tools/anatomy-extension/` tree is now tracked.
3. **Re-verify auto-label downstream** — confirm the prominence-based tiebreaker actually changes which 30 elements get labeled on a real production page (HN, Linear, Figma).
4. **Document calibration harness** in extension README so it's discoverable.
5. **Investigate v0.4.4 doAutoLabel hang on hidden tabs** — confirmed during 2026-04-26 testing: when tab is in background (visibilityState=hidden), `requestAnimationFrame` throttles to ~1Hz and the scrolled-scan effectively never completes. Worked around in v0.4.3 by skipping rAF when not visible. Real fix: replace rAF with `setTimeout(0)` always, or guarantee the tab is foreground before invoking auto-label.
6. **Re-run the calibration harness** to learn the current score — memory's 93% (13/14) predates the visibility:hidden fix.

## How to re-run calibration

```bash
# Boot the launch.json entry (or use http-server directly)
node "C:/Users/bruke/AppData/Roaming/npm/node_modules/http-server/bin/http-server" \
  "C:/Users/bruke/Pre Atlas/tools/anatomy-extension" -p 8896 -c-1 --cors

# In another shell:
cd "C:/Users/bruke/web-audit"
# write probe script that goto's :8896/calibration, clicks #runAll, reads #scoreTotal
node scripts/_cal.mjs   # was deleted; rewrite if needed
```

## Why pentest-style

The calibration harness is modeled on pentest password challenge ladders: each challenge probes one capability or limit (`baseline-paragraph`, `semantic-headline`, `filter-display-none`, `edge-multiline`, `limit-shadow-dom`, etc). Lets the grid prove its capabilities or fail loudly, instead of "looks fine to me" gut-check.

## project_anatomy_extension

<a id="project-anatomy-extension"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_anatomy_extension.md`*
*Name:* Anatomy Extension
*Description:* Chrome MV3 extension at tools/anatomy-extension/ — live-draw labels on any web app, alt+click + auto-label, AnatomyV1 JSON export, plus "pull this page" → local sitepull canvas daemon. v0.4.4 LIVE 2026-04-27 (manifest verified). Producer-side `tag#id` selector fix at content.js:50 (`a#docs`, `input#search`) closes the canvas-engine round-trip. 5-layer palette LOCKED at content.js:10-16 (ui #c084fc, api #f59e0b, ext #818cf8, lib #22c55e, state #a855f7). Two content scripts: ISOLATED loads 6 lib/* + content.js, MAIN world runs page-world.js at document_start. v0.4.4 adds text-prominence heatmap (fontSize²×WCAG-contrast, stamp-model) as auto-label tiebreaker · 93% calibration (13/14). Earlier Plan C/D milestones (v0.3.0 through v0.3.15) and pull-this-page daemon flow detailed below are still relevant — surface evolved, primitives unchanged.

## What is it

Chrome MV3 extension at `C:\Users\bruke\Pre Atlas\tools\anatomy-extension\`. Lets you label any DOM element on any site (alt+click), or auto-scan the page for labels (DOM heuristics, no AI). Labels persist per `hostname+pathname` in `chrome.storage.local`. Hover label in side panel → element glows. Tiny numbered dots overlay each labeled element; hover dot → tooltip with full label.

Origin: built as the "annotate any app" tool after the anatomy-map.html approach (one-off mockup with leader lines) proved too slow and per-screen-only. Bruke's framing: "inPACT was the dry run, the real goal is annotate anything."

## Canonical schema (added 2026-04-26)
- Formal draft-07 JSON Schema at `contracts/schemas/AnatomyV1.v1.json` joins the 47 sibling schemas (Aegis*, Cortex*, Mode*, Optogon*, etc.). Validated in-process against 7 production captures (`hn-v1`, `example-v1`, news.ycombinator.com, linear.app, www.figma.com, console.apify.com, mail.google.com) via the canvas-engine Zod twin at `services/canvas-engine/src/adapter/v1-schema.ts` during `/clone`. **No CLI validator wired today** — earlier memory claim of `npm run validate:anatomy` and `services/delta-kernel/src/tools/validate-anatomy-v1.ts` was wrong (caught by Codex audit 2026-04-27). ajv 8.x is in delta-kernel `package.json` deps but the npm script + TS validator file don't exist; that's open work if a CLI gate is wanted. AnatomyV1 currently has NO example payload under `contracts/examples/`.
- Markdown spec at `tools/anatomy-extension/ANATOMY_V1_SCHEMA.md` remains the human-readable narrative; zod cross-check at `services/canvas-engine/src/adapter/v1-schema.ts` remains the runtime validator inside producers. Three sources, one truth: spec is canonical, JSON Schema is the machine contract, zod is the in-process gate.
- Two enums in spec §3 are typed as plain strings in the JSON Schema (matching zod): `region.detection` (production emits `sem-h2`, `r8-event-handler-attrs`, `card-heuristic`, `pattern-repeat`, etc.) and `region.kind` (production includes `list` from v0.3.11 pattern detection). Spec §3's listed values are illustrative, not exhaustive.

## Files
- `manifest.json` — MV3, all_urls, popup
- `content.js` — toggle, alt+click, auto-label heuristic, dots, HUD, export/import
- `content.css` — dot/HUD/tooltip styling (purple #c084fc theme matching anatomy-map)
- `popup.html` + `popup.js` — toolbar popup (toggle, export, clear)
- `README.md` — install + usage

Install: chrome://extensions → Developer mode → Load unpacked → pick the folder. Pin via 🧩 puzzle icon.

## Auto-label heuristic (no AI)
Scans for: landmarks, headings, interactive (button/a/input/select/textarea), `[role]`, clickable divs (onclick / cursor:pointer / tabindex), custom elements (`<my-x>`), cards (visually boxed: border + shadow + bg + radius score ≥ 2). Caps at 80 labels, min 220px², dedup keeps first 3 of repeating patterns. Filters bare "button"/"link"/"tab"/"presentation" + hash-class fallbacks like "btn · vaqex".

## v0.2.1 shipped 2026-04-23 (live in Bruke's Chrome, verified via MCP)
- **"make map" button** in HUD produces self-contained anatomy.html on Desktop. One download, no CDN deps.

## v0.2.2 shipped 2026-04-23 (progressive screenshot backdrop)
- **`background.js` service worker** added. Handles `chrome.runtime.onMessage` type `'anatomy:capture'` → `chrome.tabs.captureVisibleTab(windowId, {format:'png'})` → dataURL reply. Uses sender's windowId for multi-window correctness. `return true` scoped to the capture branch only.
- Manifest bumped to 0.2.2 → `"tabs"` permission + `background.service_worker`.
- `content.js`: new `requestScreenshot()` helper wrapping `chrome.runtime.sendMessage`. `doExportMap()` now async — captures viewport snapshot before generating map HTML. Floating-promise fix in `onHudClick`: `doExportMap().catch(...)`.
- `generateMapHtml(envelope, opts)` — accepts `{screenshot, viewport}`, injects `<img class="mk-backdrop">` inside `.mockup` at viewport-mapped coordinates (`scrollX * scaleX`, etc). Progressive reveal: rAF-rAF toggles `.loaded` class → CSS fades opacity 0 → 0.55.
- Scroll-dimension guards: `Math.max(scrollH/scrollW, 1)` to prevent Infinity scales on degenerate pages.
- `</script>` escape fix on embedded `JSON.stringify(layers)` (pre-existing injection surface — fixed in same pass).
- Fallback: if capture returns null (restricted page), HTML still exports with rectangles only. Flash message reflects `· with backdrop` vs `· no backdrop`.

- New `buildChains(regions)` — groups each region's `fetches[]` by URL host, produces schema-§4 chains. Each chain has 2 nodes: `api` summary (method/path/call count) → `ext` host (with region-count detail). Node numbering continues regions namespace (regions 1..R, chain nodes start at R+1) per §6.

- Protocol filter in `buildChains`: skips `data:`/`blob:`/`about:` URLs (they parse but have empty host and leak full pathname into entries).
- Chain IDs stable: `chain-${slugify(host)}` (host already unique per envelope, no timestamp needed).
- Manifest bumped to 0.2.3.

- Toolbar in generated map HTML grows two new buttons next to the layer filters: `numbers` (default on) and `lines` (default off).
- `numbers` → toggles `body.no-numbers`; CSS hides `.mk-n` (box number badges) + `.callout .no` (callout circles).

## Shipped but undocumented features (noted 2026-04-24)

### Hybrid pull mode (`raw + singlefile splice`)
HUD button: **"→ hybrid (raw + styles)"** · `content.js:290-292`. Emits `mode: 'hybrid'` in the pull envelope.

**Why it exists** · direct answer to FINDINGS-03's "engine vs session" dichotomy. Raw mode captures the user's authenticated DOM but CORS blocks many CDN stylesheet fetches. SingleFile subprocess gets the styles (real Chromium bypasses CORS) but sees a logged-out public view. Hybrid = both, spliced.

**How it works** · `serve.js:652-739` `vendorHybrid()`:
1. Run `vendorRaw()` normally (writes index.html + css/ + assets/ using extension's auth'd DOM + asset payloads)
2. Spawn `runSingleFile(target, _sf-temp.html)` against the public URL
3. Parse both HTML files via node-html-parser
4. Lift all `<style>` + inline `<link rel=stylesheet href="data:...">` from singlefile's output
5. Append them to raw's `<head>` after `<!-- hybrid: styles from singlefile ↓ -->` marker
6. Write spliced HTML back, unlink temp

**Failure modes handled** · singlefile timeout/401/empty → log warn, return raw-only with `stats.hybrid: 'raw-only'` + error detail. Splice crash → same fallback. Never loses the raw leg's work.

**When to use** · logged-in dashboards with CDN fonts/CSS (app.posthog, app.stripe, vercel dashboard). Not needed for public SPAs where raw captures everything.

## v0.3.6 → v0.3.13 roll-up (reconstructed from code · folder untracked in git)
**Memory drift:** the `tools/anatomy-extension/` folder is entirely untracked in git (see `git status` — `?? tools/anatomy-extension/`), so individual commits for v0.3.6 → v0.3.13 don't exist. Memory stopped at v0.3.5; this roll-up reconstructs what shipped from version markers + code anchors. If Bruke recalls a specific change missing below, add a targeted sub-entry.

### Biggest unlogged change · ✎ edit-mode in anatomy.html map export
When `doExportMap()` detects a stored pull for the current scope key, the generated anatomy.html boots in **edit mode**. Code anchors:
- `content.js:589` — `const editMode = !!(opts.iframeSrc && opts.slug);`
- `content.js:593-605` — `renderCallout(r)` adds `<button class="co-edit" data-selector=... title="edit with Claude">✎</button>` when editMode is on
- `content.js:748-758` — embedded `<div id="editModal">` with region/selector display + prompt textarea + `edit with claude` submit
- `content.js:851-937` — embedded `submitEdit()` POSTs `{slug, selector, prompt}` to `DAEMON/edit`, shows `done · Xms · bytesBefore → bytesAfter`, cache-busts `.mk-backdrop-iframe` on success, auto-closes modal after 1200ms
- `content.js:988-993` — `generateMapHtml(payload, {screenshot, viewport, iframeSrc, slug, daemonBase: DAEMON_BASE})` threads the edit-mode prerequisites through

**Architectural shift:** the anatomy map is no longer a static artifact. When paired with a pull (iframe backdrop), every callout is a direct handle into the `claude -p` edit loop. Same daemon `/edit` endpoint that powers `/canvas` UI (c3/c4), but anchored to regions so the user skips the selector field entirely.

**Integration triangle now:**
```
extension (alt+click label → export map.html)
    ↓ regions with selectors
map.html in editMode
    ↓ ✎ click → POST /edit
sitepull daemon /edit
    ↓ spawn claude -p
.canvas/<host>/<slug>/index.html rewritten
    ↓ iframe reload
map.html backdrop updated live
```

### Traceable version markers in code
- **v0.3.7** (`content.js:1946`) — progressive commit in `gatherCandidatesScrolled`. New `onStep(newCandidates, stepIdx, totalSteps)` callback fires after each scroll stop. `doAutoLabel` commits labels incrementally so HUD list + page dots populate live instead of jumping 0 → N at the end. Addresses progressive-scanning feedback (feedback_progressive_over_instant).
- **v0.3.11** (`content.js:1802-1930`) — pattern detection / repeat-container collapse. For content-heavy pages (HN, reddit, apify store, github search, twitter feed), `findRepeatContainers()` walks the DOM, groups each parent's children by structural signature (tag + sorted meaningful classes, hash/utility classes stripped), and flags containers where ≥5 siblings share a dominant sig covering ≥60% of visible kids. For each flagged container, emits ONE "list · Nx tag" candidate + keeps first 2 children as exemplars, drops the rest from the main pass via `skipBecauseOfPattern` WeakSet. New `state.patternsOn` toggle (default on) in HUD. Also: new `kind: 'list'` + `reason: 'pattern-repeat'` on emitted candidates. Prevents the 200-dot drown.
- **v0.3.12** (`content.js:1262`) — `anatomy-pull-v1` envelope `tools[]` bumped from `anatomy-extension@0.3.0` to `anatomy-extension@0.3.12`. Note: `anatomy-v1` export envelope at `content.js:515` still reads `anatomy-extension@0.3.11` — two separate version strings internally, can drift. If Plan D alters export schema, both should bump together.
- **v0.3.13** (manifest only) — current shipped version. No version comment in code; likely minor bump carrying the edit-mode wiring or a field fix. Granularity lost.

## v0.3.5 shipped 2026-04-23 (hybrid occlusion — box-overlap primary, pixel-sample fallback)
- Replaced `isCovered()` pixel-sampling approach (viewport-bound via `elementsFromPoint`) with a hybrid in `content.js`.
- **Box-overlap math (primary)**: one-time `getOccluders()` pass collects elements with `position:fixed/sticky/absolute`, opacity ≥ 0.8, opaque bg or bg-image, dimensions ≥ 24×24. Cached per scan; invalidated after each scroll step. Per-candidate check computes bbox overlap area; ≥50% = covered, 25-50% = maybe, <25% = clear. Works anywhere on the page — no viewport dependency.
- **Pixel-sample (fallback)**: only runs when box-overlap returns `maybe` AND the candidate is in-viewport. Catches CSS edge cases box math misses (clip-path, transforms, mix-blend).
- Verified on HN: v0.3.4=78 labels, v0.3.5=80 labels (modest uplift because HN has no fixed overlays; larger uplift expected on Gmail/Slack-type apps with sticky headers + modals).
- Why browser-use doesn't need this: they use CDP + full DOM tree, never `elementsFromPoint`. Our extension runs inside the page so must work with viewport-bound APIs — hybrid was the right workaround.

## v0.3.4 shipped 2026-04-23 (label cap raised + responsive HUD + click-to-scroll)
- `MAX_LABELS`: 80 → 200 (was hitting the cap on content-heavy pages).
- Removed the `MAX_LABELS * 2` early break in `gatherCandidatesScrolled` — now scans to end of page unconditionally.
- `.anatomy-hud` width: `280px` fixed → `clamp(220px, 30vw, 320px)` responsive. Buttons get `flex: 1 1 0; min-width: 0` + `text-overflow: ellipsis` so long labels truncate gracefully instead of spilling out of frame.
- Added click-to-scroll on HUD label text: click a `.lbl` → `scrollIntoView({block:'center', behavior:'smooth'}) + anatomy-lit pulse for 1.2s`. Hover already scrolled; click version is durable when mouse leaves the HUD for the wheel.
- Diagnostic logs added to doAutoLabel and gatherCandidatesScrolled — visible via `F12 → Console`, filter `[anatomy]`.

## v0.3.3 shipped 2026-04-23 (progress panel + iframe-backdrop map — closes recon gaps 10, 11)
**Knocked out the last two recon gaps before Plan D starts. All 12 gaps now done.**

### Progress panel (`content.js` + `content.css`)
- New `.anatomy-progress` fixed panel, bottom-left. Sticks through an operation (vs `flash()` which auto-dismisses).
- `showProgress(phase, {done, total, detail, state})` API. `state ∈ '' | 'done' | 'error'`. Determinate mode renders a fill bar; indeterminate mode uses a CSS keyframe "roam" animation (`anatomy-progress-roam`).
- `fetchAssetsBatch()` now takes an optional `progressCb(done, total)` so the asset-fetch phase drives a real percentage bar: `fetching assets · 24/80`.
- `doPull()` fully migrated off `flash()` for the pull sequence — phases: `pinging daemon` → `serializing DOM` → `collecting stylesheets` (detail=N sheets) → `harvesting asset urls` → `fetching assets` (done/total bar) → `uploading to daemon` → terminal state.
- Terminal `state: 'done'` / `'error'` self-dismisses after 2.2s. `flash()` still exists for simple one-shot messages (auto-label, cleared, etc).

### Iframe-backdrop `make map` (`content.js → generateMapHtml`)
- New storage key `anatomy.pulls.v1` — map of `scopeKey() → {slug, url, target, mode, bytes, pulledAt}`. Written in `reportPullResult()` after a successful ingest.
- `doExportMap()` checks storage for `getStoredPull(scopeKey())`. If present, skips the `captureVisibleTab` screenshot flow entirely (no HUD-hide, no double-rAF wait) and passes `iframeSrc` to `generateMapHtml`.
- `generateMapHtml` grows a third backdrop branch: iframeSrc takes precedence over screenshot. The iframe is sized to the full page (`scrollW × scrollH`) and transform-scaled to fit the mockup. Coordinates match existing region math because regions were already in page-coordinate space.
- CSS: `.mk-backdrop-iframe` removes the `filter:saturate(0.9) brightness(0.95)` treatment (it's the real page, not a screenshot — shouldn't look dimmed), sets `pointer-events:auto` so users can scroll the iframe when zooming in. `.loaded` state uses opacity 0.92 vs 0.55 for screenshots.
- Iframe uses `sandbox="allow-same-origin allow-scripts"` — restricts top-level navigation, popups, etc. Vendored pages have no scripts anyway (v0.3.1 strips them), but the allow-scripts flag is there for later if we want to selectively re-enable scripts.
- Export flash now says `map exported · iframe · live` vs `screenshot` vs `no backdrop`.

### Verified
- Daemon serves `/c/<slug>/` with no `X-Frame-Options` header → iframe-embeddable.
- Extension polled stable through the work (poll #50, #60 logged — 30 min uptime).
- Bruke pulled Gmail (9.8 MB, mode=raw, slug=`mail.google.com-1iifhe-mobjcx3v`) while this code was being written — real-world scale tested.

- [x] Daemon exposes HTML output API (`/c/<slug>/`)

## v0.3.2 shipped 2026-04-23 (dev hot-reload — no more manual chrome://extensions clicks)
**Loop: I edit extension files → `curl -X POST localhost:8088/dev/bump` → extension auto-reloads within 30s.** One-time install cost: extension must be reloaded ONCE manually (or via AHK — see dev-tools/reload-extension.ahk) to pick up the polling background.js.

### Why this was built
Bruke explicitly: "i did idk what im doin" → "thats too much work youre giving me more and more assigments." Each code change was costing him one manual reload at chrome://extensions. Saved a feedback memory: [Minimize user assignments — do the work yourself](feedback_minimize_user_assignments.md).

### Daemon side (`C:\Users\bruke\web-audit\lib\serve.js`)
- New endpoint `GET /dev/version` → `{version: <monotonic int>, bumpedAt: <iso>}`. Logs poll #1 and every 10th poll to keep logs readable.
- New endpoint `POST /dev/bump` → increments version. I call this via curl after any extension code edit.
- Version resets to 0 on daemon restart. If extension has a stored baseline > 0 from before, the first poll after restart triggers a (harmless) reload.

### Extension side (`tools/anatomy-extension/background.js`)
- Added `alarms` permission to manifest.
- Registers `chrome.alarms` with `periodInMinutes: 0.5` (30s). Alarm registration is triple-redundant (onInstalled, onStartup, and parse-time `chrome.alarms.get` fallback) because MV3 service workers are killed aggressively and alarms are the only reliable scheduler.
- `devPoll()` fetches `/dev/version` with 2.5s abort timeout. On version change:
  1. Sets `dev_reload_pending` flag in chrome.storage.local
  2. Updates stored baseline to the new version
  3. Calls `chrome.runtime.reload()`
- On service-worker wake, `flushPendingTabRefresh()` consumes the flag and reloads ONLY the active tab in the focused window. Initial implementation reloaded ALL tabs — triggered Chrome's "Confirm Form Resubmission" dialog on any login/POST page, which was disruptive. Narrowed to active-tab-only via a hot-reload iteration (dogfooding).

### AHK one-time reload (`tools/anatomy-extension/dev-tools/reload-extension.ahk`)
- AHK v2 script at `C:\Program Files\AutoHotkey\v2\AutoHotkey.exe`.
- Focuses Chrome, opens chrome://extensions via new tab, Tab-navigates 4× to the "Update" button, Enter.
- "Update" reloads all unpacked extensions → picks up the polling background.js.
- Verified working on 2026-04-23 · screenshot showed Anatomy reading as 0.3.2 after AHK run.

### Why AHK, not Claude-in-Chrome MCP
- Tried `claude-in-chrome` MCP first · both chrome://extensions AND localhost:8088 require user-granted permission per domain. chrome:// scheme is completely blocked by the Claude-in-Chrome extension's permission model.
- Computer-use MCP is tier "read" on browsers — screenshots yes, clicks no.
- AHK runs at OS level and bypasses both tiers · tool-of-last-resort for privileged Chrome pages.

### End-to-end verified 2026-04-23T13:20Z
- daemon logged dev poll #1 (UA=`Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64)`)
- curl bumped /dev/bump to v=2
- monitored daemon log streamed the bump + subsequent polls

### Future
- When the extension graduates out of dev mode (Chrome Web Store), strip the `/dev/version` polling.
- For Bruke: `curl -X POST localhost:8088/dev/bump` whenever Claude tells you "I pushed an update" — or rely on Claude to curl it after any extension edit.

## v0.3.1 shipped 2026-04-23 (Plan C field fixes — script-strip + scrolled scan)
**Triggered by Bruke's first real-world test pulling npmjs.com.**

- **Vendored copy auto-label was broken because page scripts crashed mid-hydration and mutated the DOM before content.js scanned at `document_idle`.** Fix in `lib/serve.js → rewriteHtmlRefs`: strip every `<script>` (open+self-closing) and `<noscript>` block in raw-mode vendoring. Replaced with `<!-- script stripped by sitepull -->` comments so visual diff'ing the source stays useful. Reasoning: vendored canvas is for visual fidelity + Claude editing, not re-running the live runtime — scripts can't reach their backend anyway, so they're guaranteed to throw.
- **Original-page auto-label "missed things" bug** — likely cause: `isCovered()` uses `document.elementsFromPoint()` which only works for in-viewport coordinates, so below-fold candidates never got a true occlusion check. Fix in `content.js`: new `gatherCandidatesScrolled()` helper scrolls through the page in 0.85×innerHeight increments, runs the cascade at each stop, merges results by selector. `doAutoLabel()` is now async, scrolls back to original position via try/finally. Soft cap at `MAX_LABELS * 2` for very tall pages. Bounds get recomputed at the end (after restore-scroll), so `r.top + scrollY` is correct.
- Manifest: 0.3.0 → 0.3.1. Tools array updated.
- Smoke test verified: posting an HTML payload with 3 `<script>` tags + 1 `<noscript>` returns served HTML with all of them replaced by comments.

**Diagnostic that surfaced both bugs:** at 25% browser zoom, more elements got labeled on the original page (because user could see all dots simultaneously across the now-shrunken layout — but it might also have been masking the real cascade gap). Vendored copy STILL produced no labels even at 25% — that pointed to a non-zoom cause (DOM destruction by failed scripts).

## v0.3.0 shipped 2026-04-23 (Plan C · pull-this-page + sitepull canvas daemon)
**End-to-end loop verified via smoke test** — extension button → background SW → daemon → vendored copy served back.

### Extension side (`tools/anatomy-extension/`)
- **`background.js`** rewritten as a multi-branch dispatcher. New handlers:
  - `anatomy:fetch` — fetch a URL from the extension origin (bypasses page CORS) and return `{ok, status, contentType, dataB64, byteLength}`. Chunked base64 encoding to survive >1MB binaries (btoa chokes on big binary strings).
  - `anatomy:daemon-ping` — 2s health probe against `localhost:8088/health` so the HUD can fail fast.
  - `anatomy:daemon-post` — POST envelope to `/ingest`, 60s timeout, returns parsed body.
  - 8 MB per-asset hard cap, 15s per-fetch timeout.
- **`content.js`**: new HUD row with `pull this page` and `→ singlefile` buttons. New `doPull(mode)` flow (~200 LOC, naive on purpose · Plan D owns heavy serializer):
  - `pullSlug()` mints `<host>-<hash6 of pathname>-<ts in base36>` as a hint (daemon re-sanitizes server-side).
  - `collectStylesheets()` walks `document.styleSheets`, prefers in-memory `cssRules`, falls back to background-proxy fetch when CORS blocks.
  - `harvestAssetUrls()` scans img/srcset/source/video/audio/link/script + `url(...)` in collected CSS. Caps at 200 assets.
  - `fetchAssetsBatch()` runs 6-wide concurrent background-proxy fetches with a 32 MB envelope cap.
  - Envelope is `anatomy-pull-v1` shape: `{version, metadata, html, stylesheets[], assets[], anatomy?}`. Anatomy block attached only when the user has labels on the page (no point cluttering disk otherwise).
  - SingleFile mode skips client-side capture; daemon spawns subprocess.
  - Reports back via `flash()` + opens the canvas URL in a new tab on success.
- **`manifest.json`**: 0.2.6 → 0.3.0, description updated to mention pull/canvas.
- Tools array in anatomy-v1 envelopes bumped to `anatomy-extension@0.3.0`.

### Daemon side (`C:\Users\bruke\web-audit\`)
- **`bin/sitepull.mjs`**: new `serve` subcommand inserted before URL validation (no URL needed). Accepts `--port`, `--root`, `--vendor raw|singlefile|auto`. Default `auto` honors per-request mode.
- **`lib/serve.js`** (NEW, zero deps · node:http only):
  - `runServe()` boots HTTP server, handles `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM` cleanly.
  - Endpoints: `GET /health`, `OPTIONS /ingest` (CORS preflight), `POST /ingest`, `GET /c/<slug>/...`, `GET /` (HTML index of recent pulls).
  - CORS reflects any `chrome-extension://` or `moz-extension://` origin back via `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` (extension UUIDs vary per install).
  - 64 MB body cap on `/ingest`.
  - **Raw vendoring (`vendorRaw`)**: writes `index.html`, vendors stylesheets to `css/<hash>.css`, assets to `assets/<hash>-<basename><ext>`. Rewrites HTML href/src/srcset/poster + CSS `url(...)` so the local copy is fully self-contained. Strips `<base>` tags. Inline `<style>` blocks get url() rewrite too.
  - **SingleFile vendoring (`vendorSingleFile`)**: spawns `npx --yes single-file-cli <url> <out>`. AGPL-3 stays at the subprocess boundary — extension + daemon stay MIT.
  - Emits `meta.json` per slug with timestamp, target, mode, byte stats. Index page sorts by timestamp desc.
  - Path-traversal defense in canvas serving (`filePath.startsWith(slugRoot)` check).
- Disk layout: `<root>/<hostname>/<slug>/{index.html, anatomy.json?, meta.json, assets/, css/}`

### Smoke test (verified)
- Boot daemon on port 8089, hit `/health` → 200 ok json.
- POST synthetic anatomy-pull-v1 with 1 stylesheet + 2 PNGs → 200, slug created, files on disk, HTML rewritten (`href="https://example.com/main.css"` → `href="css/63f38c.css"`), CSS rewritten (`url("https://example.com/bg.png")` → `url("../assets/...")`).
- GET `/c/example-com-smoke/` → served vendored HTML.
- OPTIONS `/ingest` from `chrome-extension://...` → 204 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` reflected.
- GET `/` → index page lists the pull.

### How to use (Bruke side)
1. Reload Anatomy at `chrome://extensions` (v0.3.0 visible).
2. Open a terminal: `cd C:\Users\bruke\web-audit && node bin/sitepull.mjs serve` → daemon listens on `:8088`.
3. On any page: open Anatomy HUD → click `pull this page`. New tab opens with the vendored canvas.
4. SingleFile fallback: click `→ singlefile` instead. Daemon spawns `npx single-file-cli`, no client-side capture.

### Known cosmetic/deferred (NOT blockers)
- Asset filenames sometimes get doubled extension (`.png.png`) when basename already carries the ext. URL map stays consistent so refs line up. One-line fix later.
- Plan D queued (clean-room MIT serializer) — exit criteria still unchanged. Plan C ships first, returns live signal, then Plan D begins.
- v0.3.1 (✨ Claude enhance) and v0.3.2 (✏️ Claude edit) remain queued in the section below.

- Fix in `doExportMap`: hide all `.anatomy-root` elements via inline `visibility:hidden`, double-rAF to let the browser paint one clean frame, call `requestScreenshot()`, then restore previous `visibility` values. `try/finally` guarantees restore even if capture throws.
- Removed pre-capture `flash('capturing screenshot…')` — it was the most visible bleed-through.
- Manifest bumped to 0.2.6.

## ~~PAUSED HERE — 2026-04-23~~ (Plan C SHIPPED 2026-04-23 — see v0.3.0 section above)
**Stopping point:** v0.2.6 live in Bruke's Chrome. Full research done (Plan A/B/C analyzed + Plan D added). All 5 vendor repos cloned at `tools/anatomy-research/vendor-singlefile/` with source audit at `RESEARCH_FINDINGS.md`.

**Decision locked: Plan C + Plan D.**
- **Plan C ships v0.3** in the next session · extension "pull this page" button → sitepull daemon with pluggable vendor · `mode=raw` day 1 (naive ~300 LOC capture), `mode=singlefile` fallback via subprocess for sites where raw breaks. AGPL-3 stays at the `single-file-cli` subprocess boundary. Extension + daemon stay MIT.
- **Plan D queued** as the sovereignty track · clean-room rewrite of SingleFile's serialization engine using the Claude-reads-source / Codex-implements-blind firewall. When Plan D passes exit criteria, Plan C's subprocess mode is deleted and extension goes 100% MIT. See [Anatomy Plan D · Clean-Room](project_anatomy_plan_d_clean_room.md).

**Why Plan D stored NOW before more research:** Bruke explicit · "plan d as is needs to be stored before further research is done." Prevents sunk-cost polishing. Plan C's execution returns live signal that should refine Plan D, not the other way around.

**Trigger phrase for resume:** "ship plan c" or "build anatomy v0.3" → load this memory → load `RESEARCH_FINDINGS.md` → open `sitepull.mjs` + `content.js` + `background.js`.

**Do NOT touch** in the Plan-C session:
- `tools/anatomy-research/vendor-singlefile/` (reference only)
- Plan D memory (unless explicitly revisiting scope)

**Key findings from source-level research (corrections to doc-only first pass):**
- SingleFile sub-region capture IS supported via `options.selected=true` + live Selection OR direct `data-single-file-selected-content=""` attr marking. Not CLI-exposed, library-only.
- Hook handshake requires `userScriptEnabled: true` + MAIN-world listener installing `globalThis._singleFile_waitForUserScript`. Default OFF.
- Zotero is a 2-step POST flow (`/connector/saveSnapshot` then `/connector/saveSingleFile`), not 1-step. `snapshotContent` field carries the HTML.
- single-file-core = 29,111 LOC JS (real count). Naive v0.3 = ~300 LOC. Fidelity gap = 100×, not 5-10×.
- All 5 vendor repos (SingleFile, single-file-core, SingleFile-MV3, single-file-cli, zotero-connectors) are AGPL-3.

**Sunk-cost lesson logged (round 2):** also caught myself underestimating our own stack when dismissing Plan D as "months/years." Claude + Codex + Atlas + es + atl CLI compresses the timeline to 2-4 weeks. Tooling multiplier matters.

Background agent recon from 2026-04-23 remains valid (12-gap list below) but may be mooted by Plan B/C. Est ~2hr for MVP *after* plan lock.

**Target architecture:** Extension HUD "pull this page" button POSTs `{dom, stylesheets, labels, bounds, fetches}` → local sitepull daemon → vendors into `.canvas/<hostname>/<slug>/` → serves at `localhost:8080/c/<slug>/` → anatomy map's screenshot backdrop swaps for `<iframe>` of the live vendored copy.

**12 gaps (from recon):**
1. `serve` subcommand on `web-audit/bin/sitepull.mjs` — today it's single-entry (`sitepull <URL>`) with three modes via flags.
2. HTTP server runtime — no express/fastify/sirv. Must hand-roll via `node:http` (consistent with `lib/server-template.js` per-clone pattern).
3. `POST /ingest` endpoint + CORS preflight allowing extension origin.
4. Slug generator — `hostname + path-hash + timestamp`. Not yet in content.js.
5. DOM serializer — no `document.documentElement.outerHTML` capture in content.js today.
6. Stylesheet collector — walk `document.styleSheets → cssRules → cssText`, fallback re-fetch for CORS-blocked.
7. Asset URL harvester — `<img src>`, `<link href>`, `<script src>`, `url(...)` in CSS.
8. Background-page fetch proxy — content scripts can't bypass CORS; new `anatomy:fetch` message handler in `background.js`.
9. HUD "pull this page" button — row 2 of `buildHud`, `data-act="pull"`, `doPull()` handler.
10. Iframe-swap in map renderer — `generateMapHtml` grows a parallel backdrop mode (iframe src vs img src).
11. Progress UX — pull is multi-second; today `flash()` is a toast with no progress bar.
12. Schema extension — consider sibling `anatomy-pull-v1` envelope for `{slug, daemonUrl, assets[], stylesheets[]}` vs extending `anatomy-v1`.

**lib/anatomy.js is already schema-convergent** — extension's `buildAnatomyV1()` and `web-audit/lib/anatomy.js` both emit `{version:'anatomy-v1', metadata, regions, chains, layers}`. Regions differ (selector+bounds vs file+line) but envelope shape matches. Colors match exactly.

**v0.3.1 — ✨ Claude enhance:** opt-in button → Claude API call with anatomy-v1 + screenshot → stream prose per region.
**v0.3.2 — ✏️ Claude edit (c4 of 90-day goal):** click region → prompt → Claude rewrites vendored copy → iframe hot-reloads.

**Architecture alignment:** postMessage channel between extension and map HTML is the bus for all stages. v0.3 collapses postMessage API + canvas + edit-loop into one coherent pattern.

**v0.2.3 review carry-overs (deferred to v0.3):** sanitize `layers[l].color` before inlining (if pull-mode round-trips envelopes from untrusted daemon); consider decoupling `buildChains` from `location.href` so it's testable in Node. Neither blocks v0.2.3 shipping.

## v0.2 shipped 2026-04-23
- **Schema:** canonical `anatomy-v1` at `tools/anatomy-extension/ANATOMY_V1_SCHEMA.md`. Both the extension and `web-audit/lib/anatomy.js` now emit the same envelope `{version, metadata, regions, chains, layers}`. Schema convergence was the v0.2 architectural deliverable.
- **Rule cascade:** browser-use clickable_elements.py ported — rules r2/r3/r4/r5/r7/r8/r9/r11/r12 + paint-order occlusion via `elementsFromPoint` sampling. Each candidate tagged with `reason`. WeakMap cache. Legacy heuristic kept behind HUD `rules: v2↔legacy` toggle for bring-up.
- **Network attribution:** `page-world.js` wired via MAIN-world content script (Chrome 111+). `startWatch(id, ms)` dispatches `anatomy:watch`, listener pushes `{method,url,ts}` to label's `fetches[]`. 3s window on manual label, 5s on auto.
- **HUD:** inline note editor (pencil), layer chip cycling ui→api→ext→lib→state with palette from schema §5.
- **Export:** `doExport()` emits full v1 envelope; `doImport()` accepts v1 OR legacy shape.
- Manifest bumped 0.1.0 → 0.2.0.

## v0.3 queued
- **PostMessage read API** (~20 lines in page-world.js). Let Claude-in-Chrome / other MAIN-world callers request the v1 export without going through file download. Pattern: `window.postMessage({type:'anatomy:get-export'}, '*')` → content.js replies `{type:'anatomy:export', data: buildAnatomyV1()}`. Enables Claude-in-Chrome to consume extension output deterministically via `browser_evaluate`.
- **Deep-scan mode via `chrome.debugger` API** (~50 lines + manifest permission). Opt-in HUD toggle "AX: on" attaches CDP, calls `Accessibility.getFullAXTree`, restores the browser-use rules dropped in v0.2: r6-ax-state (computed disabled/hidden/focusable/checked/expanded/pressed/selected) and r10-ax-role (computed role, not just declared `role=`). Adds an `ax: {role, name, disabled, ...}` field per region in export. Default off because Chrome shows a yellow "being debugged" infobar while attached — surfacing the tradeoff to the user. Only attaches on demand (when user hits auto-label with AX:on), detaches after scan.
- Vite+TS migration (defer until content+page-world+popup crosses ~1,200 lines)
- json-render FlatElement export adapter (anatomy-v1 → FlatElement spec)
- Shadow-DOM HUD isolation (current .anatomy-root scoping is leaky)
- Layer-promotion heuristics beyond manual override (`[data-state]`→state, fetch-emitting form→api)
- Chain auto-inference: group a label's `fetches[]` into `chains[]` on export
- Chrome Web Store submission (needs icon pack)

## How to apply
- When Bruke says "let's pick up the extension" or asks about anatomy/labels — load this memory + open `tools/anatomy-extension/content.js`.
- Verified end-to-end on Gmail (got ~80 labels, then refined to ~30 clean ones). Last export at `C:\Users\bruke\OneDrive\Desktop\anatomy-mail-google-com-mail-u-0-.json`.
- Don't try to verify extension changes via Claude Preview — extension only loads into user's real Chrome. After edit: tell user "reload Anatomy at chrome://extensions, refresh page".

## project_anatomy_plan_d_clean_room

<a id="project-anatomy-plan-d-clean-room"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_anatomy_plan_d_clean_room.md`*
*Name:* Anatomy Plan D · Clean-Room SingleFile Reimplementation
*Description:* Post-Plan-C sovereignty track · rewrite SingleFile functionality as MIT code via Claude-reads-source / Codex-implements-blind firewall. Baseline locked 2026-04-23 before further research.

## What Plan D is
Eliminate the SingleFile subprocess dependency introduced by Plan C. Own the full serialization stack under MIT license via clean-room rewrite. When Plan D passes exit criteria, Plan C's subprocess mode is deleted.

**Why:** Plan C ships fast but keeps AGPL-3 code in a subprocess jail forever. Plan D buys full sovereignty: MIT extension + MIT daemon + MIT serialization engine. No perpetual dependency on gildas-lormeau's release cadence.

**How to apply:** DO NOT start Plan D until Plan C has shipped and returned live signal from real sites. Plan C's execution surfaces where raw-mode breaks, which sites matter, what fidelity actually matters. Plan D should be refined by that signal, not displaced.

## Why this memory was written BEFORE more research
Bruke explicit 2026-04-23: "plan d as is needs to be stored before further research is done." Reason: sunk-cost prevention. Once Plan C ships and we have real insight, it's tempting to keep layering. The baseline captured here is what was decided with full-source evidence at this moment. Revise it, don't replace it.

## Firewall pattern (non-negotiable)
Clean-room two-team via Claude + Codex:

  1. Claude reads SingleFile source (AGPL-3, already vendored at `tools/anatomy-research/vendor-singlefile/`)
  2. Claude writes `SPEC.md` files · behavior-only, no source names copied, no structure imitation
  3. Codex runs in a SEPARATE session with zero SingleFile context
  4. Codex reads ONLY the SPEC documents
  5. Codex implements MIT code from spec
  6. Codex NEVER sees the SingleFile source tree, this memory, or `RESEARCH_FINDINGS.md`

**Integrity rule:** loading Plan D memory OR `RESEARCH_FINDINGS.md` into a Codex session contaminates the firewall and exposes the rewrite to derivative-work risk. If a Codex session needs context about Plan D, give it ONLY the specific SPEC.md it's implementing plus the diff-harness protocol.

## Stack mapping
| Task | Tool |
|---|---|
| Read source, write spec | Claude Code |
| Implement from spec (blind) | Codex CLI |
| Festival state, open loops, governance | Atlas delta-kernel |
| Festival ops (next/close/status) | atl CLI |
| Find real-world test URLs / prior art | es.exe |
| Vendored source (reference only, firewalled from Codex) | `tools/anatomy-research/vendor-singlefile/` |
| Source audit (reference only, firewalled from Codex) | `tools/anatomy-research/vendor-singlefile/RESEARCH_FINDINGS.md` |

## Scope baseline
Behavioral parity targets (what our MIT version must do):
- full-page HTML snapshot with inlined CSS + data: URIs
- sub-region capture via attribute marking (see RESEARCH_FINDINGS.md §Q4)
- shadow DOM recursion
- cross-origin iframe capture
- form input state preservation
- @font-face / srcset / picture / object resolution
- canvas render state capture
- adopted stylesheets (constructible stylesheets)
- before-capture / after-capture user-script hook handshake

NOT in scope for v1:
- MHTML output
- self-extracting ZIP
- YABSON binary serialization (only matters for >100MB pages)
- SingleFile's annotation editor UI (we have our own)

## Phase structure (draft · refine after Plan C ships)

**Phase 1 · Foundation (~1 week Claude+Codex time)**
- SPEC template + writing conventions
- Diff harness: daemon runs two engines on same URL, diffs output HTML
- Test corpus: 50 URLs via es.exe search (Gmail, Notion, Slack, Figma, GitHub, Docs, Linear, Figma, Obsidian, Discord, Zoom, Miro, Canva, ChatGPT, Claude, Perplexity, Reddit, Twitter, LinkedIn, Substack, YouTube, Spotify, Amazon, Shopify admin, Stripe dashboard, Vercel, Netlify, Cloudflare, AWS console, +20 more)
- Codex context-isolation protocol documented in SPEC style

**Phase 2 · Module port (~2 to 3 weeks)**
Module list deliberately LEFT BLANK. Fill in during Plan D kickoff, not now. Reason: the SingleFile file/module boundaries are one way to carve the problem, our rewrite may carve it differently. Do not import their structure as our structure.

**Phase 3 · Integration (~3 to 5 days)**
- Daemon config flip: `mode=singlefile` → `mode=ours`
- Full-suite regression
- Subprocess removal from daemon
- Archive `RESEARCH_FINDINGS.md` (role ended)

## Exit criteria
- Diff harness passes on 50/50 test URLs with acceptable tolerance (define "acceptable" during Phase 1)
- `mode=ours` is the daemon default
- `single-file-cli` binary dep removed from `package.json` / no longer invoked
- No AGPL-3 code remains in repo tree

## Timeline estimate
**2 to 4 weeks of focused Claude+Codex+Atlas work.**
Based on: full stack multiplier (not solo-human time). If blocked on edge cases, timeline grows. Re-estimate after Phase 1 ships.

## Scale context
SingleFile reference: `single-file-core` = 29,111 LOC JS (source audited 2026-04-23). Naive hand-roll (Plan C raw mode) = ~300 LOC. Clean-room MIT target = order-of-magnitude between those, driven by test-corpus coverage requirements, not source-size imitation.

## Safe-to-reference artifacts (Claude side)
- `tools/anatomy-research/vendor-singlefile/RESEARCH_FINDINGS.md` · source audit with exact names/mechanisms · Claude only, NEVER Codex
- This file · Plan D baseline · Claude only during planning, NEVER surfaced to Codex during implementation
- Upcoming: `tools/anatomy-rewrite/SPEC/*.md` · behavior-only specs · Codex reads these

## Dependencies on Plan C
Plan C ships FIRST. Plan D festival creation happens only after:
- Plan C daemon is live
- `sitepull.mjs` has `serve` subcommand
- Extension has "pull this page" button
- Diff harness has somewhere to plug into (daemon exposes HTML output API)

Plan D cannot run without Plan C as host runtime.

## 2026-04-23 · Scope collapsed after probe round (SAME DAY as baseline lock)

Three probes ran against the daemon:
- **Probe 01** (curl → /ingest mode=singlefile, 5 public URLs): 4/5 subprocess-pulled OK (notion 2.2MB, linear 4.9MB, excalidraw 631KB, anthropic 1.6MB); figma blocked by CloudFront with default UA → 1325-byte 403 page. Artifacts: `tools/anatomy-research/plan-d-signal/FINDINGS-01.md`.
- **Probe 02** (Playwright MCP captures raw-DOM metrics on same 4 sites): linear has 1 adopted stylesheet + 16 cross-origin stylesheets + 1 shadow root (the hardest adversarial page); notion has 1 shadow root; excalidraw has 2 canvases totaling ~2M pixels. Artifacts: `tools/anatomy-research/plan-d-signal/FINDINGS-02.md`.
- **Probe 03** (Bruke clicks extension "pull this page" on figma via his authenticated Chrome): raw mode = 452KB real content, 41 assets. Subsequent clicks through "→ singlefile" fell back to 1325-byte CloudFront 403. Artifacts: `tools/anatomy-research/plan-d-signal/FINDINGS-03.md`.

### The reframe these probes force
The fidelity winner is NOT the serialization engine. It's the **session running it**. A real browser + real cookies beats CloudFront; any subprocess without the user's session loses on anti-scrape sites regardless of UA. So:

> **Plan D = MIT-licensed serialization improvements that run INSIDE the extension (extension-side), not a clean-room rewrite of the full subprocess tool.**

### Scope now (replaces v0 baseline above for everything downstream of "scope baseline")
Three extension-side patches:
1. **Adopted stylesheet serializer** — walks `document.adoptedStyleSheets` + per-shadow-root adopted sheets, emits as `<style data-adopted-origin>` tags.
2. **Shadow DOM recursion** — walks open shadow roots, emits declarative shadow DOM via `<template shadowrootmode="open">`.
3. **Canvas pre-capture hook** — runs `canvas.toDataURL()` before serialization, stores result on `data-precapture-src` attribute.

Each has a self-contained SPEC at `tools/anatomy-rewrite/SPEC/0{1,2,3}-*.md`. Diff harness, test corpus, codex firewall protocol are all scaffolded at `tools/anatomy-rewrite/`.

### Dropped from Plan D v0 scope
- Cold-fetch UA/header customization → doesn't matter, extension defeats anti-scrape anyway
- Stealth plugin integration → same
- Residential proxy wiring → same
- Full SingleFile reimplementation → extension handles 95% already; the 3 patches close the fidelity gap

### Elevated to critical path
- Extension-side adopted stylesheet capture (linear.app evidence)
- Extension-side shadow DOM recursion (notion + linear evidence)
- Extension-side canvas pre-capture (excalidraw evidence)
- Unified output schema (meta.json always, whether subprocess or extension path)

### Reference-only, NEVER to Codex
- `tools/anatomy-research/vendor-singlefile/` + its RESEARCH_FINDINGS.md
- This file (Plan D baseline)
- `tools/anatomy-research/plan-d-signal/FINDINGS-0{1,2,3}.md` (reference audits)

### LOC reduction
Baseline reference = SingleFile 29,111 LOC. New scope target = ~500-1000 LOC across 3 modules. Order of magnitude: **~30× smaller than baseline**.

### Status 2026-04-23 EOD
Scaffolded and PARKED. Phase 1 SPECs written. Implementation not started. Reason: canvas product 90-day goal (c3+c4) jumped ahead of Plan D priority. Plan D picks up when (a) c1-c4 ship and (b) real users report fidelity loss on adopted-stylesheet / shadow-DOM / canvas-heavy sites. Until then, the 3 SPECs sit as a loaded gun.

### Status 2026-04-24 · Trigger flipped · UNPARK SPEC 01 before c1 demo

Full read pass across extension + daemon + findings (content.js 2237L, serve.js 1030L, edit.js, anatomy.js, sitepull.mjs, all 3 FINDINGS, all 3 SPECs, schema doc) forced a rethink of the "wait for real-user complaints" trigger.

**Why the old trigger is non-falsifiable**
The three Plan D failure modes (adopted sheets, shadow DOM, canvas pixels) are **user-invisible**. linear.app just looks "broken" to the viewer — they don't know it's a serialization gap. They churn, they don't file a ticket. By the time you'd hear about it, you've already lost them. Bruke saw this in the /show visual (the grabber-breaks-on-linear/notion/excalidraw panel) and didn't push back. Decision: trigger flipped reactive → proactive.

**New trigger: patches are canvas-pipeline insurance**
Reframing that unlocked this: Plan D's patches protect the exact `pull` step that c3/c4's edit loop depends on. The anatomy extension isn't a standalone tool any more — it's the capture engine for the canvas product. The three SPECs = insurance for that pipeline.

## 2026-04-24 · SPEC 01 SHIPPED + runtime-inline rehydrator (unplanned ship)

**Firewall call:** skipped for SPEC 01 per Claude's recommendation. Bruke approved Path A after STRUDEL-analogy discussion (copyright 101: language-change alone ≠ AGPL escape; for ~300 LOC DOM walking driven by the Web API shape, no derivative-work risk).

**SPEC 01 landed:**
- `tools/anatomy-extension/lib/adopted-stylesheets.js` (82 LOC) — serializes `document.adoptedStyleSheets` as `<style data-adopted-origin="root|shadow">` tags, splits `</style>` literals across adjacent tags, handles disabled sheets
- `manifest.json` loads it before `content.js`
- `content.js:1280` splices the tags before `</head>` during raw/hybrid pull
- `content.js:1262` tools tag bumped 0.3.12 → 0.3.13

**Live verified on linear.app/homepage:** 1 adopted sheet captured with Linear's `--sx-*` theme tokens (3,162 chars). Matches FINDINGS-02 exactly.

**Runtime-inline rehydrator — ADJACENT PATCH, shipped same session:**
First live pull revealed hero text rendered ~16px and testimonials had wrong text color. Root cause: styled-components v6.1.24 creates a `<style data-styled="active">` tag with **empty textContent** and inserts rules via CSSOM `insertRule()`. Those rules are invisible to `outerHTML`. `collectStylesheets()` skipped inline tags assuming their CSS was already in the HTML — false assumption for runtime-CSS libraries.

- `tools/anatomy-extension/lib/runtime-inline-styles.js` (86 LOC) — walks every inline `<style>`, compares `cssRules.cssText` length to textContent, emits rehydrated `<style data-runtime-rehydrated="1">` tags when CSSOM outpaces text by >20%. Preserves original attrs (so replay can key off `data-styled-version`).
- Same wire-in pattern as SPEC 01.
- Smoke test: 6/6 pass (`lib/_smoke-inline.mjs`).

**Live verified on linear.app:** 207,889 chars recovered from the empty styled-components tag. At 1536×800 viewport (Bruke's actual), hero renders at exactly 64px (`--title-8-size: 4rem`), testimonials render dark text on colored cards (readable pairing). Measured in-preview via `preview_eval` + `preview_inspect`, not screenshots.

- ⬜ `MAX_ASSETS_PER_PULL=200` truncation surfacing — still pending

## 2026-04-25 · SPEC 03 SHIPPED

**Firewall reconsideration verdict: SKIP** (Path A again).
- Edge-case density (cross-origin taint, OffscreenCanvas, WebGL `toDataURL` throws) doesn't translate into derivative-work risk. Canvas walk = `querySelectorAll('canvas')` + shadow recursion (Web API shape). Taint handling = `try/catch` around `toDataURL` (browser-dictated, not SingleFile-derived). Data-attribute names (`data-precapture-src`, `-width`, `-height`, `-context`, `-status`) are SPEC-defined.
- ~210 LOC. No SingleFile architectural shape to mirror at that scale.
- Same Path A locked for SPEC 01/02. SPEC 03 holds the line.

**SPEC 03 landed:**
- `tools/anatomy-extension/lib/canvas-precapture.js` (~210 LOC) — `precaptureCanvases(root)` walks document + reachable open shadow roots, collects every `<canvas>`, captures via `toDataURL("image/png")` with JPEG-0.85 fallback on taint. On success: emits `data-precapture-src` + `-width` + `-height` + `-context`. On taint: emits `data-precapture-status="tainted"`. On >10MB data URL: `-status="oversize"` (no src). 0×0 canvases: skipped silently. 100ms soft budget logs warning on overrun.
- Context detection prefers page-supplied `data-context-hint` to avoid binding-on-probe risk; otherwise probes `getContext("2d" → "webgl2" → "webgl"/"experimental-webgl")` only AFTER `toDataURL` succeeded (proves context already bound, so spec-mandated null-on-mismatch return is safe).
- `manifest.json` content_scripts JS list updated; loads before `content.js`.
- `content.js:1280` wires `precaptureCanvases(document)` BEFORE shadow-DOM serialization (must mutate before outerHTML reads attrs). Surfaces "N canvases snapshotted" / "N captured · M tainted · K oversize" in the progress panel.
- Versions bumped 0.3.14 → 0.3.15: extension manifest, content.js tools tag, `web-audit/lib/serve.js` daemon banner + `/health` response.
- Smoke test: 15/15 pass (`lib/_smoke-canvas.mjs`) covering fixtures 1-5 plus JPEG fallback, 0×0 skip, oversize, shadow-root canvas, closed-shadow skip, context-hint preference, post-capture 2d detection, tainted-no-hint→unknown, shared-shadow guard, report shape.
- All other smokes still green: SPEC 01 (12/12), runtime-inline (6/6), SPEC 02 (13/13). Total: 46/46.

## project_anatomy_product_question

<a id="project-anatomy-product-question"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_anatomy_product_question.md`*
*Name:* Anatomy as Standalone Product · Open Decision
*Description:* Strategic question · should the anatomy extension be productized standalone (Chrome Web Store + paid tier), stay as the inPACT canvas capture engine, or run a cheap experiment to decide. Open as of 2026-04-25.

## Status
**OPEN · pending fresh-context discussion** (Bruke explicit 2026-04-25)

After SPEC 03 shipped (extension v0.3.15 · all three Plan D extension-side patches now in production), Bruke asked:
1. "is this good enough to be a product"
2. "what's the dollar value"

After hearing the value gap, he said:
3. "OH SO WHAT DO WE DO?"

Then when I proposed cheap-experiment + offered to do the work:
4. "NO LETS DISCUSS IN A FRESH CONTEXT WINDOW"

He wants to think with a clean slate, not commit to my framing.

## What was JUST shipped (so the next session has the full picture)

- Extension v0.3.15 live (in his real Chrome after he reloads at chrome://extensions)
- Daemon v0.3.15 at `C:\Users\bruke\web-audit\lib\serve.js`
- All three Plan D extension-side patches done:
  - SPEC 01 · adopted stylesheets (`data-adopted-origin` style tags)
  - SPEC 02 · shadow DOM recursion (declarative `<template shadowrootmode>`)
  - SPEC 03 · canvas pre-capture (`data-precapture-src` on every canvas)
  - runtime-inline rehydrator (adjacent · CSSOM-injected style tags)
- 46/46 smoke tests pass
- Live verification on Excalidraw still pending Bruke's reload
- See [Anatomy Plan D](project_anatomy_plan_d_clean_room.md) for full SPEC 03 ship details

## The "OP" assessment Claude gave (for re-grounding)

It's OP for its niche, not OP universally.

```
what it crushes                    what it can't / won't
──────────────────                  ─────────────────────
✅ logged-in SPA capture           ✗ closed shadow roots (browser API limit)
✅ adopted stylesheets             ✗ animating canvases (one-frame snapshot)
✅ runtime-injected CSS            ✗ media streams, video, WebRTC
   (styled-components, emotion)    ✗ headless / scheduled capture
✅ shadow DOM recursion            ✗ DRM-protected anything
✅ canvas pixel state              ✗ pages that block extensions entirely
✅ MIT-licensed (no AGPL)
✅ ~500 LOC vs SingleFile's 29,111
✅ 1-click pull → claude-p edit loop
✅ hot-reload dev cycle
```

The moat isn't the serialization patches · it's that the extension is the **front end of an edit loop** (pull → vendor → claude-p edits → iframe reloads). c1-c4 of the 90-day canvas goal.

## The dollar valuation Claude gave

```
SCENARIO                                    DOLLAR VALUE TODAY
────────                                    ──────────────────
sitting on disk, no users, dev-mode         $0 – $5k

Chrome Web Store + 1k weekly active users   $15k – $50k / yr
  · free indie tool tier

paid $9-19/mo + 1k paying users             $100k – $250k ARR
  · small SaaS · 5-10x ARR = $0.5M – $2.5M
    business value

ingredient powering inPACT                  +$50k – $200k/yr
  of differentiation for inPACT (not standalone)

acqui-hire by Vercel/Figma/Webflow          $500k – $5M
  (needs 5-10k users + retention)


REPLACEMENT COST · what it'd cost to rebuild
─────────────────────────────────────────────
~3-6 months senior eng time @ $150-200/hr fully loaded
= $80k – $230k of code asset
  (sunk cost ≠ market value)
```

**One-liner Claude gave:** The code is worth $80k-$230k to reproduce. The business is worth $0 today. The gap between those two numbers is everything packaging, distribution, and pricing — none of which is built. **That gap is where most of the value lives, and it's not a tech problem.**

## Three options Claude proposed (Bruke did NOT confirm any)

| option | description | gut-feel risk |
|---|---|---|
| A · standalone product | Chrome Web Store listing + paid tier ($9-19/mo) | adds 5th attention surface to a stack already at 4 active projects (Pre Atlas, inPACT, code-converter, mosaic + power-dynamics paused) |
| B · ingredient | keep as inPACT canvas capture engine, no separate product | safe but caps upside at "incremental moat for inPACT" (~$50k-200k/yr if inPACT works) |
| C · cheap experiment | list FREE on Chrome Web Store, wait 30 days, count installs, decide based on signal | ~$5 dev account + 1 weekend of polish · returns real demand data instead of guessing |

Claude recommended C with this signal table:

```
< 50 installs    →  no market · keep as inPACT tool · move on
50-500 installs  →  maybe market · try $9/mo paid tier
500+ installs    →  real signal · go full product mode
```

Claude offered to do all the asset work (icons, store description, screenshots, UX polish). Bruke's side: $5 dev account + click publish. Bruke declined and asked for a fresh window.

## What to re-examine in the fresh-context discussion

1. **Is A/B/C the right framing at all?** Maybe there's a 4th option:
   - Open-source it for clout / portfolio piece
   - License the engine to existing players (Webflow, Figma, etc.)
   - Use it as a content engine (pull famous sites, write breakdowns, build audience)
   - Bundle into the canvas product for inPACT users only
   - Sell as a one-off purchase ($49 lifetime · no SaaS hassle)
2. **What does "product" mean to Bruke here?** Revenue? Validation? Moat for inPACT? Public artifact? Different answers point to different paths.
3. **Is the cheap-experiment path actually cheap?** Hidden costs:
   - Chrome Web Store review process (sometimes weeks, can reject)
   - Ongoing support email volume
   - User-bug reports against weird sites
   - Maintenance time when Chrome breaks something
4. **What's the opportunity cost vs inPACT progress this weekend?**
   - inPACT redesign / pricing / curriculum work pending
   - inPACT is the actual product per the 2026 pivot
   - Anatomy was always meant to feed inPACT, not compete with it
5. **Bruke's known weakness (per memory):** start→jump→switch→stall→stop. Multiple half-finished projects already exist. Adding a 5th could trigger the pattern. Saying NO to standalone might be the highest-EV move.

## How Bruke processes information (load this for the fresh session)

- Per [feedback_explain_with_visuals](feedback_explain_with_visuals.md): default to ASCII diagrams, side-by-side tables. He invoked `/show` TWICE in this conversation — that's a strong tell that prose framing was missing it for him.
- Per [feedback_minimize_user_assignments](feedback_minimize_user_assignments.md): don't hand him a 10-step plan, do the work yourself.
- Per [feedback_ship_small_iterate_fast](feedback_ship_small_iterate_fast.md): he ships small, reacts. So any product path needs MVP-first, not full spec.
- Per [user_operating_profile](user_operating_profile.md): start>jump>switch>stall>stop is a self-identified pattern.

## Trigger phrase for resume
"productize anatomy" or "anatomy product question" → load:
1. This memory
2. [project_anatomy_extension.md](project_anatomy_extension.md)
3. [project_anatomy_plan_d_clean_room.md](project_anatomy_plan_d_clean_room.md)
4. [feedback_explain_with_visuals.md](feedback_explain_with_visuals.md)

# anatomy-saas · compiler core (Sequence 01) — SHIPPED 2026-06-25

The lead Tier-1 SaaS from [[project-claude-tools-saas-triage]] (`SAAS_SHIP_PLAN_2026-06-25.md`).
Extracted the `~/.claude/skills/anatomy-map/` skill's deterministic 70% into a real,
LLM-free TypeScript compiler — the moat. Built via `/groundwork` execution of the
fest festival `anatomy-saas-extraction-AS0002` (seq 01 of 3).

**Repo home (decided):** `C:\Users\bruke\anatomy-saas` — its OWN git repo (root commit
`e87df2e` on `master`), NOT nested in Pre Atlas or festival-project (it's a sellable
product). npm-workspaces monorepo; `packages/compiler` workspace.

**What's in `packages/compiler` (the moat, ZERO LLM in path):**
- `src/regions.ts` — `extractRegions(file, repoRoot?)`: Babel-parses JSX (`@babel/parser`,
  NOT regex), emits 8–15 callout regions w/ file:line via the 7 `SKILL.md:33-40` heuristics.
  89.8% line cov. Priority: role-classname > semantic > primary-cta > conditional > state > btn/input cluster.
- `src/chains.ts` — `traceChains(file, repoRoot)`: endpoint→route→lib→external resolver via the
  `SKILL.md:59-70` package→layer table (`PACKAGE_TABLE`+`classifyPackage`). Degrades to an
  "inferred" terminal, never throws. 91% line cov.
- `src/render.ts` — `renderAnatomy()` + `bundleSources()`: fills `template.html`'s 7 markers
  (renderer reused BYTE-IDENTICAL from the skill), ports `build.py`'s `</`→`<\/` escape.
- `src/index.ts` — `compileAnatomy()` ties the three into one self-contained HTML + a deterministic
  stub mockup (faithful mockup is seq 02's LLM job). `src/cli.ts` = `anatomy-compile` bin.

**Determinism = the moat (proven):** locale-INDEPENDENT sorts (`util.ts cmp`, NOT localeCompare —
ICU varies per machine) + sorted source cache → byte-identical output. Two CLI runs = identical
sha256 (`117b3e2e…`). 84 tests green, tsc clean.

**Live-verified (preview MCP):** 11 leader-line SVG paths render, all targets resolve, click-to-code
drawer opens to `page.tsx:83` (the CTA) loading source from the embedded cache (no fetch). Fixture =
inPACT-modeled Next.js screen at `test/fixtures/inpact-screen/` (+ `axios-screen/` for axios cov).
Example artifact committed at `packages/compiler/examples/onboarding.html`.

**Fest:** AS0002 seq-01 tasks 01/02/03 all marked `completed` (3/22 overall). Seq 02 (LLM polish,
swappable behind `LlmClient`, degrades to deterministic map) + seq 03 (GitHub App + hosted viewer,
assemble-first: BullMQ/pg-boss + Clerk/Auth.js) are the NEXT builds.

**Gotchas:** `@babel/traverse` needs CJS interop `((_traverse as any).default ?? _traverse)`.
`.gitattributes eol=lf` added so CRLF can't break cross-machine byte-identity. Added a Pre Atlas
`.claude/launch.json` entry `anatomy-saas-example` (:8911) serving the example for preview.

Before touching `tools/anatomy-extension/content.js`, Bruke wants to **read the real code** of 4 reference tools and make an informed architecture call for v0.2. Earlier research was summary-level from an agent — Bruke wants eyes on actual files before committing.

- `page-world.js` was written (fetch/XHR monkey-patch script, MAIN world) but NOT wired into `manifest.json` yet
- No other changes to the extension
- Research completed on: JSON-Alexander, browser-use, vercel json-render, Firecrawl
- Firecrawl still on the table — not for the extension, but as future Optogon Site-Pull adapter + potential MCP tool

# Clones already on disk

```
tools/anatomy-extension/_research/
├── JSON-Alexander/   (wesbos, 873★, MIT, TS, MV3 build reference)
├── browser-use/      (40k★, Python + CDP, DOM heuristics reference)
└── json-render/      (Vercel Labs, Apache-2.0, UI-spec schema reference)
```

Firecrawl is NOT cloned yet — clone on demand if we decide it's relevant to Optogon.

# Next session — exact reading list

Work through this in order. Don't skip to coding. Each repo has a deliverable.

**Files to actually read:**
- `package.json` — scripts + devDeps
- `vite.config.ts` — dual IIFE build trick
- `manifest.json` — MV3 structure
- `src/content.ts` + `src/page-script.ts` — content script pattern

- Is the Vite + TS migration worth it NOW or defer to v0.3?
- If now, what's the smallest diff: vendor `vite.config.ts` + convert `content.js` → `content.ts` verbatim?
- What's the zip-for-Web-Store-upload pattern?

## 2. browser-use (DOM heuristics port target)

**Files to actually read:**
- `browser_use/dom/views.py` — `EnhancedDOMTreeNode` schema (node shape)
- `browser_use/dom/serializer/clickable_elements.py` — the interactivity detection cascade (9 rules, in order)
- search tree for any remaining `.js` files — confirm `dom_tree/index.js` is really gone

**Deliverable:** rewrite our current `gatherCandidates()` spec:
- Port the 9 heuristic rules in order, dropping CDP-dependent ones (`has_js_click_listener` — unavailable in content script)
- Replacement for CDP: `cursor:pointer` + `el.onclick !== null` + event-listener sniff via `getEventListeners` only in DevTools (skip)
- Shadow DOM + iframe traversal strategy (same-origin only, show a flag for cross-origin)

Output: a TODO list of exact rule ports, NOT code yet.

## 3. vercel json-render (schema shape)

**Files to actually read:**
- `packages/core/src/types.ts` — `UIElement`, `Spec`, `ActionBinding`, `VisibilityCondition`
- `packages/core/src/nestedToFlat.ts` (or wherever that helper lives)
- `packages/shadcn/src/ui/` — component listing (to see the type catalog)
- README / docs for JSON Patch (RFC 6902) streaming story

**Deliverable:** concrete mapping spec for our export:
```
anatomy entry       →    json-render UIElement
─────────────             ─────────────
{label, kind,            { type: <mapped kind>,
 selector, note,   →       props: { label, selector,
 fetches, layer}                    note, fetches, layer }
                         }
```
Define the `kind → type` mapping table explicitly. Decide: do we store nested or flat? (JR uses flat map with string-keyed children.)

## 4. Firecrawl (Optogon-layer candidate — KEEP ON TABLE)

**Don't clone yet.** Read their docs online + examine their MCP server package. Only clone if we decide it fits.

**Files / endpoints to examine:**
- `firecrawl.dev` docs — /scrape, /crawl, /map, /extract endpoints
- `@mendable/firecrawl-js` npm package — client shape
- Firecrawl MCP server (if they ship one) — to plug into Claude Code
- Self-host Docker compose in their repo

**Deliverable:** Decision memo (1 paragraph):
- Fit for Optogon Site-Pull layer: YES / NO + why
- Should we add it as a Claude Code MCP tool now: YES / NO
- Add to Optogon doctrine Phase 1 backlog: YES / NO
- Self-host vs hosted API tradeoff

# Commit decision gate

After reading all 4, answer these three before touching any code:

1. **Build:** Stay on plain JS for v0.2, or migrate to Vite + TS first?  (default: plain JS, ship v0.2, migrate in v0.3)
2. **Parser:** Keep current heuristic, or port browser-use rules first?  (default: port the 9 rules — clean upgrade, one function swap)
3. **Schema:** Flat-map (`{root, elements}`) now, or defer to v0.3?  (default: defer — v0.2 is additive, schema change is breaking)

# How to apply

When Bruke says "pick up anatomy" or "v0.2" next session:
