## project_anatomy_v04_vocab_lock

<a id="project-anatomy-v04-vocab-lock"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_anatomy_v04_vocab_lock.md`*
*Name:* Anatomy v0.4 Vocab Lock + Adaptive Collapse
*Description:* Closed detection vocabulary, validateRegion gate at envelope-build, MAX_LABELS bump, adaptive sibling-collapse, dropout-by-stage instrumentation, Python verification harness; uncommitted 2026-04-26 in main repo

# Anatomy v0.4 · Vocab Lock + Adaptive Collapse

**Status:** content.js committed 2026-04-27 in `28caebf` (which bundled vocab gate + adaptive collapse + drop counters + buildSelector tag#id fix + Bruke's prominence integration). `services/canvas-engine/src/pattern-library/normalize.ts` + tests committed 2026-04-27 in `2abba2b`. **Still uncommitted:** `lib/detection-vocab.js`, `ANATOMY_V1_SCHEMA.md`, `dev-tools/verify_vocab.py`, `dev-tools/analyze_collapse.py`, manifest version bump. Folder-wide bulk-tracking decision for the rest of `tools/anatomy-extension/` deferred.
**Bundles together:** schema lock (mine) + adaptive collapse fix C (mine) + Bruke's parallel text-prominence heatmap.
**Verified by:** Python harness · 1426 regions across 16 captures · 0 default-bucket · 1 designed-failure (the `novel-thing` typo).

## Why each change exists

| Symptom | Fix |
|---|---|
| Auto-label emits free-form detection strings · canvas-engine pattern picker silently buckets unknowns to "default" (no real pattern, generic stub render) | Closed vocab in `lib/detection-vocab.js` · `validateRegion()` runs at envelope build · drops + warns unknowns instead of leaking them |
| Producers (extension + web-audit) emit different vocabularies; canvas-engine only recognized some | Updated `services/canvas-engine/src/pattern-library/normalize.ts` to recognize the union: r2/r3/r4 prefixes, auto-label, alt-click, manual, legacy, custom-element, cursor-dwell, button-cluster, hero, sem-form, landmark, heading. + 3 vitest cases. |
| MAX_LABELS=200 truncated dense pages · low-value won the cap | Bumped to 1000. Bruke's parallel text-prominence ranking (v0.4.4) breaks ties so the 1000 picked are the ones around real text content. |
| "Identical-shape siblings, one labeled, others missing" — Bruke's complaint that triggered this work | `findRepeatContainers()` adaptive cap (fix C): <10 sibs label all · 10-30 keep 5 + summary · >30 keep 2 + summary (legacy). HN dropout went 33 → 0 after fix. |
| Couldn't see WHERE candidates die in the cascade | `state._dropCounters` per-stage counter in `gatherCandidatesV2` · logged by `gatherCandidatesScrolled` as `[anatomy] dropout-by-stage: {...}`. Stages: total, anatomyRoot, patternRepeat, noReason, notVisible, isCovered, noSelector, dupSelector, junkLabel, kept. |
| Hidden tabs throttle rAF to ~1Hz so cascade never completes during automated tests | Skip the double-rAF wait when `document.visibilityState !== 'visible'`. Settle still happens via `setTimeout(40)` which is clamped but doesn't deadlock. |

## Files (all untracked except content.js)

| File | Author | LOC | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tools/anatomy-extension/lib/detection-vocab.js` | mine | ~140 | DETECTION_LITERALS · KIND_VALUES · `isDetectionValid()` · `normalizeDetection()` mirror of canvas-engine · `validateRegion()` · `migrateDetection()` |
| `tools/anatomy-extension/content.js` | mine + Bruke (mixed) | ~edits | buildAnatomyV1 validates+drops · `findRepeatContainers` adaptive cap · gatherCandidatesV2 drop counters · gatherCandidatesScrolled reset+log+rAF guard · plus Bruke's grid HUD button + toggleTextGrid + prominence integration in doAutoLabel |
| `tools/anatomy-extension/manifest.json` | Bruke | n/a | v0.3.15 → 0.4.4 (multiple bumps · Bruke's last bump at 0.4.4 for prominence stamp-model with 93% calibration) |
| `tools/anatomy-extension/ANATOMY_V1_SCHEMA.md` | mine | edits | Added §3.2 closed vocabulary table · 30 detection values mapped to 7 canvas-engine groups |
| `tools/anatomy-extension/dev-tools/verify_vocab.py` | mine | ~150 | Python port of normalize logic · walks every anatomy.json on disk · prints group dist + dropouts in <1s |
| `tools/anatomy-extension/dev-tools/analyze_collapse.py` | mine | ~150 | Counts pattern-repeat collapse impact + selector-fanout per capture · proved figma drops 168 siblings/capture |
| `tools/anatomy-extension/dev-tools/reload-extension.ahk` | mine (existed earlier) | 50 | AutoHotkey v2 script · clicks chrome://extensions Update button (4 tabs from default focus) |
| `services/canvas-engine/src/pattern-library/normalize.ts` | mine | ~edits | Recognizes 11 new strings · routes to clickable/heading/landmark/form |
| `services/canvas-engine/test/pattern-library.test.ts` | mine | +3 cases | Vocab tests pass 36/36 · suite 72/72 |

## Verification commands

python "C:/Users/bruke/Pre Atlas/tools/anatomy-extension/dev-tools/verify_vocab.py"
# Expected: TOTAL ~1426 · FAILURES 1 (novel-thing) · DEFAULT-BUCKET 0

cd "C:/Users/bruke/Pre Atlas/services/canvas-engine" && npm test
# Expected: 72 tests pass

# Live: reload extension via AHK

  "C:/Users/bruke/Pre Atlas/tools/anatomy-extension/dev-tools/reload-extension.ahk"

# Live: trigger reload via daemon poll (faster, no UI clicks)

## Console signal · how to read dropout-by-stage

```
[anatomy] dropout-by-stage: {
  total: 4895,           // DOM elements iterated × scroll passes
  anatomyRoot: 2408,     // side panel itself · expected, ignore
  patternRepeat: 0,      // siblings collapsed · should be 0 after fix C on small lists
  noReason: 1599,        // didn't match any cascade rule · expected
  notVisible: 392,       // display:none, opacity:0 · expected
  isCovered: 2,          // paint-order occlusion · should stay tiny
  noSelector: 0,         // buildSelector returned null · should be 0
  dupSelector: 230,      // already labeled across passes · expected
  junkLabel: 11,         // whitespace/empty text · expected
  kept: 231              // committed labels
}
```

If `patternRepeat` jumps on a real production page, that's a regression in fix C. If `isCovered` jumps, paint-order needs attention. Everything else is normal cascade hygiene.

## Empirical proof

Live HN before/after fix C (cascade-only, prominence bypassed during test):

```
                    BEFORE   AFTER   delta
  patternRepeat:    33       0       -33
  kept labels:      220      231     +11
  scrolled-scan:    222      231     +9
```

The 33 siblings recovered are HN story rows (under various tbody parents) and footer link clusters that previously got collapsed. Match the symptom Bruke described: "identical shape, one labeled, the other wrong/missing."

# Atlas Agenda-Book Convergence

## Session Header

- **Date:** 2026-04-29
- **Worktree:** `C:\Users\bruke\Pre Atlas\.claude\worktrees\stoic-noether-96bea8` · branch `claude/stoic-noether-96bea8` · synced with `main` at `82bb613`

## Origin of this analysis

Bruke pasted a long "MAPE-K Finish Plan" (10 sections, 5 schemas, 25 source files, 8 CLI commands, OCR pipeline, build prompt for Claude Code). It was likely ChatGPT-authored and the author had not seen the actual repo.

Bruke's correction (verbatim): *"i think the plan was made without seeing the actual code so instead of shutting it down bc it's more code we should look at the code we have and se where the idea already converges. I don't think the system has to change i just think it's the shape in which we need to interact with the system just needs a little tweaking."*

## Bruke's stated need (across this session)

1. *"I need work to get done. The tools are supposed to help me work. Code feels like work and is not progress when I need things getting done. Paper would work better if it allowed me to keep moving."*
2. *"I would need to plan my week out. I want my atlas to look like a schedule for the week, like a virtual agenda book."*

The interaction shape Bruke wants: weekly agenda book, planned with Claude, surfaces today's leads, approvals, appointments, and what got closed.

## Convergence finding: ~75% already built

Of 8 plan elements, 6 exist as data + storage today. The remaining gap is a user-facing view, not architecture.

| # | Plan Element | Already Exists At | Gap |

*Name:* Atlas AI CLI

## Atlas AI CLI — Status

**Drop 1: COMPLETE (2026-04-10)**
**Drop 2: COMPLETE (2026-04-11)**

**File:** `services/delta-kernel/src/cli/atlas-ai.ts` — 1056 lines, single file, zero new deps
**Built:** `dist/cli/atlas-ai.js` — 49KB, `node` runtime, sub-100ms startup
**Alias:** `alias a="node C:/Users/bruke/Pre\ Atlas/dist/cli/atlas-ai.js"`

### Three Layers
- **Layer 1 — Primitives:** 28 commands (state, next, close, energy, day, task, win, loops, cognitive, directive, health, osint, capabilities, etc.)
- **Layer 2 — Compounds:** 9 total — morning, close-stale, done, checkpoint, wrap, do + **process-inbox, research-queue, draft-responses** (LLM)
- **Layer 3 — Agent loop:** --once / --daemon / --dry-run, config at ~/.atlas/agent_config.json, state dedup at ~/.atlas/agent_state.json, NDJSON log at ~/.atlas/agent.log

### Architecture Decisions (Codex-reviewed)
- `next` does NOT recompute mode — reads from `/api/state/unified` (avoids split-brain with routing.ts + server.ts)
- `state` uses `/api/state/unified` as primary source (server does the cascade)
- `do` and compounds go through work-controller admission (`POST /api/work/request` → execute → `POST /api/work/complete`)
- Energy < 30 is global override with `allowed_actions: ['rest', 'health_actions']`
- Aegis-indirect: atlas-ai only talks to delta-kernel. Aegis wired into work-controller as follow-up.
- Brain file fallback marked `meta.degraded: true`

### Drop 2: LLM Layer (COMPLETE)
- Gated behind `ATLAS_LLM_ENDPOINT` env var (base URL, e.g., `http://localhost:11434`)
- `ATLAS_LLM_MODEL` env var (default: `llama3.1:8b`)
- Uses `/v1/chat/completions` (OpenAI-compatible, same as crucix/ollama.mjs) with 90s timeout
- `llmAvailable()` health check via `GET /api/tags` with 3s timeout
- Three LLM compounds (189 lines added):
  1. `process-inbox` — reads ~/Desktop/inbox.md, LLM categorizes → LOOP/TASK/RESEARCH/REFERENCE, creates entities via API, archives inbox
  2. `research-queue` — processes ~/atlas/research_queue.jsonl pending entries, LLM summarizes, appends to ~/atlas/research_digest.md
  3. `draft-responses` — reads ~/atlas/awaiting_response.json (manual), LLM drafts replies to ~/atlas/drafts/
- If `ATLAS_LLM_ENDPOINT` not set, all three return `{ skipped: true, reason: 'no llm' }`. Deterministic engine never breaks.
- Agent daemon schedules: process-inbox (7-11am), research-queue (11am-3pm), draft-responses (3-8pm)
- All emit timeline events (INBOX_PROCESSED, RESEARCH_COMPLETED, DRAFTS_GENERATED)

### User-Facing Files (~/atlas/, no dot)
- `~/atlas/research_queue.jsonl` — JSONL with {id, query, context, status, created}
- `~/atlas/research_digest.md` — Date-stamped research summaries
- `~/atlas/reference_log.md` — Filed reference items
- `~/atlas/awaiting_response.json` — Manual: [{convo_id, context, tone?}]
- `~/atlas/drafts/<convo_id>_<date>.md` — LLM-generated response drafts

**How to apply:** Drop 2 is shipped. Next potential work: Aegis integration into work-controller, or `atlas-ai await <id> --context "..."` primitive for managing awaiting_response.json.

## project_atlas_atm_uasc_synthesis

<a id="project-atlas-atm-uasc-synthesis"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_atm_uasc_synthesis.md`*
*Name:* project-atlas-atm-uasc-synthesis
*Description:* The three-way Atlas × ATM × UASC analysis — one engine, three operations; self-expansion is the always-stripped element.

The three-way analysis ran 2026-06-28 → artifact `ATLAS_ATM_UASC_ANALYSIS.md` (repo root), staged by `ATLAS_ATM_UASC_PREP.md`. Grounded first-hand: `types-core.ts:79-86` (Delta = prev_hash→new_hash), `atlas-manifest.yaml:22` (hub: delta-kernel), plus the verified ATM map + UASC report.

**Thesis (proven):** Atlas, ATM, UASC are not three projects — they are ONE engine (delta-kernel: deterministic, event-sourced, signed append-only deltas) under three *operations*, NOT three symmetric "faces":
- **Atlas ⟷ delta-kernel = IDENTITY** — delta-kernel IS Atlas's core (engine pointed IN at one life). BUILT.
- **ATM ⟷ delta-kernel = ANCESTRY** — delta-kernel is ATM scaled OUT, scoped to one node; city layer unbuilt (hardware/human wall, not software). DREAM + engine.
- **UASC ⟷ delta-kernel = COMPOSITION** — uasc-executor (:3008) bolts on as the HANDS via `executor-bridge.ts` (ActionType→token, HMAC). BUILT + wired.

**The sharpest finding (the reason to remember this):** the prep doc's 5-element shared signature — when tested against every *shipped* artifact — shows **4 survive universally, exactly 1 is always stripped: self-expansion** (emergent/generative intelligence). The shipped systems are "the dream with the AI removed and the audit log kept." Same surgery every time: cut emergent intelligence, keep deterministic replay of signed deltas. UASC is the **Rosetta Stone** — the only system with all 3 dream→build→ship tiers preserved as separate diffable artifacts on disk (glyphs/base-9/道 → 16-bit opcodes+PKI → 7 flat tokens).

**Shared unit shape:** Delta→state ≡ Seed→content ≡ Glyph→ExecutionGraph / Token→Profile. "Pass the key, not the blob." One idea, three notations.

Extends [[project_atm_vision_delta_kernel_lineage]]; confirms [[user_systems_are_me_shaped_signature]]; UASC detail in [[project_atm_vision_delta_kernel_lineage]] sibling report `UASC_LANGUAGE_REPORT.md`.

**Follow-up resolved (2026-07-06): why self-expansion can't safely come back.** `ActionType` is a closed literal union in `types-core.ts:263-270` (7 fixed strings), enforced at execution time by `isActionAllowedInMode` (`cockpit.ts:128`, wired into both `POST /api/actions/confirm/:id` and `workController.claimNextExecutable` per commit `348788b`). Bruke's correction to an initial "staged proposal + review gate" idea: that framing only protects determinism/audit, not injection-resistance — any runtime path where the system can register a *new* capability from AI output becomes exactly what sophisticated prompt injection targets (get a new capability approved, not just misuse an existing one), and the reviewer/gate itself becomes the new attack surface. **The actual property: the action-type list can only be changed by a source diff + redeploy, never by a runtime request/proposal/payload.** Compile-time and runtime are different trust domains; capability changes may only cross from the compile-time side. This is why every shipped system strips self-expansion — not caution, but the only architecture that stays injection-proof. "Self-expansion" as a live system property is not achievable without reopening that surface; the only safe version of "expansion" is ordinary software development (human decides, writes it, ships it) — which isn't the system expanding itself at all.

**Does UASC have the same guarantee? Checked first-hand, 2026-07-06 — tier-dependent, and corrects a count error above.** The shipped service (`services/uasc-executor`, :3008) has **10 tokens, not 7** (`schema.sql:55-65`: `@WORK @WRAP @CLEAN @BUILD @DEPLOY @CLOSE_LOOP @SEND_DRAFT @BRIEF @EXECUTE @SNAPSHOT`) — the "7 flat tokens" line above was wrong, correct it on read. `server.py` exposes only 4 routes (`POST /exec`, `GET /commands`, `GET /health`, `GET /runs`); no route ever does `INSERT INTO commands` — the only insert is the one-time seed in `schema.sql`. `execute_command` (`server.py:138`) 404s on an unknown `cmd`. **Same closed-by-source guarantee as delta-kernel.** But the **reference implementation** (`research/uasc-m2m/reference-implementation/core/registry.py`) is structurally different: `Registry.register_graph()` (`:99`) and `.bind_glyph()` (`:113`) are live, callable Python methods with no compile/runtime separation — call them with a new graph + glyph code and the registry accepts it, no source change needed. Not wired to anything live today (lab artifact only), but if it ever were exposed, it would need the exact same closed-by-source retrofit or it inherits the injection-surface problem the delta-kernel thread resolved. Three tiers, three answers: shipped service = closed/safe, lab reference impl = open/would-need-fixing, dream/LANGUAGE corpus = the thing being protected against by design.

**Does ATM have the same guarantee? Checked first-hand, 2026-07-06 — different shape of problem, not yet solved because nothing is built.** What's actually running in ATM is delta-kernel itself (same closed-by-source code, confirmed above) — trivially "same story" at the code level. But ATM's transport/learning layer (the part that would make it ATM) has zero code (`find` for `data-mule`/`sundial`/`federated` returned nothing outside the brainstorm doc) and its design isn't a capability-registration problem like UASC's — it's a **model-weight-adaptation** problem: `GEMINI_ATM_MAP.md:93` names Federated Distillation, KL divergence, gradient-based optimization as core to the plan — nodes are *designed* to update their own behavior from runtime data (`:224`, converge to Homeostasis). "Closed by source" doesn't apply to a system whose whole point is that predictions change from observed data. **If ATM's transport layer is ever built, the discipline required is narrower but still undesigned: let the federated model adapt predictions/routing/compression — never let that adaptation touch the action surface** (no path from a Federated Distillation update to a new `ActionType`, a new UASC glyph binding, or anything `isActionAllowedInMode` gates). This is the one open design decision left in the whole thread — not resolved, because there's no code yet to resolve it against.

**Is the "hardware/human-coordination wall" (line 14/459 in the earlier note) still real? Checked first-hand, 2026-07-06 — real, but narrower than stated.** The undifferentiated "hardware wall" phrase bundles three separate things, and only one still blocks: (1) **security-hardware worry — FALSE ALARM, already resolved inside the source transcript itself.** `GEMINI_ATM_MAP.md:425-430` is Bruke directly asking "do I need to fabricate a custom chip?" — answer: no, Secure Enclave/TEE, hardware monotonic clocks, and hardware TRNGs already ship in stock iPhone 13/14 Pro and Snapdragon Android chips. (2) **Neuromorphic camera — soft blocker only.** `:428` says the event-based vision logic can be software-simulated on a normal phone camera for v1 (battery cost, not a hard stop). (3) **LoRa radio hardware — the actual remaining wall.** The whole transport design assumes LoRa (`delta-sync.ts:48` `DEFAULT_MAX_PACKET_BYTES = 220 // LoRa-safe`), but stock smartphones don't ship a LoRa radio (separate chip class, e.g. Semtech SX127x) — the Q&A that resolved (1) never addresses this gap. Reaching city-scale would require physical LoRa modules distributed to enough households to hit mesh-convergence density: real manufacturing/distribution cost, unsolved by the design. **Plus the human-adoption piece was never addressed at all** — Gemini names this honestly at `:112`: *"VCs would be fascinated but flag hardware/human roadblocks."* Getting a town's worth of people to install, charge, and physically carry devices as "Data Mules" is a social-coordination problem no crypto design touches. **Bottom line: drop the general "hardware" worry (resolved); the real wall is LoRa distribution + human adoption, not silicon.**

**The unifying rule underneath all four follow-ups (2026-07-06) — write this once, stop re-deriving it per-instance.** Every question in this thread (self-expansion, UASC's two tiers, ATM's Federated Distillation, the delta-scp bridge idea) was the same question at a different scale: *where is trust allowed to change, and who's allowed to change it?* One rule answers all of them: **capability/trust changes only by source-diff + redeploy; anything derived from live/untrusted content must never reach the action surface, no matter how many review steps sit in between.** Paste this at the top of `ATLAS_ATM_UASC_ANALYSIS.md` (or a new `TRUST_BOUNDARY.md`) so the next design question — a new UASC lab feature, the ATM build-out, the delta-scp bridge, or whatever comes after — gets checked against one sentence instead of re-litigated turn-by-turn like this thread was. Costed Bruke three corrections to get here iteratively; a single higher-effort synthesis pass at the start would have found it in one shot — worth noting as the shape of question best front-loaded, not discovered through back-and-forth.

## project_atlas_audit_findings

<a id="project-atlas-audit-findings"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_audit_findings.md`*
*Name:* project-atlas-audit-findings
*Description:* Open Atlas-substrate audit findings produced by tools/atlas-audit/audit.sh; both items will re-surface every run until closed

Two open findings from `tools/atlas-audit/audit.sh` first run 2026-06-08. Both will re-surface in every audit run (and would feed Atlas directives once Atlas is online) until closed.

## 1. HIGH · no_atlas_tests

`services/delta-kernel/src/atlas/` is 906 LOC across 5 files (close-signal, directive, lattice-projection, preferences-store, signals-store) with **zero test files**.

**Why:** The directive emitter at `directive.ts` is the policy-decision surface for the whole Atlas loop. Today's by-hand dogfood confirmed the emitter shape matches `Directive.v1.json` schema (id, issued_at, priority_tier, leverage_score, task, context_bundle, execution, interrupt_policy, schema_version) and that the `task.type` literal union matches the schema enum exactly. With no test net, that alignment can rot silently — a schema bump or an emitter rename ships undetected.

**How to apply:** First test should be a contract test that calls `emitDirective(...)` with a fixed snapshot input and validates the output against `Directive.v1.json` via AJV or a Zod twin (the canvas-engine pattern at `services/canvas-engine/src/adapter/v1-schema.ts` is the local exemplar). Coverage target: 80% per global testing rule, but ANY contract test closes the open loop. Second-priority tests: leverage_score clamping at boundaries (0, 1, negatives, NaN), priority-tier derivation per `leverageToPriority()`.

## 2. MED · missing_mode_since

`services/cognitive-sensor/governance_state.json` has no `mode_since` (or `mode_changed_at`) field. Confirmed keys: `[active_lanes, build_allowed, date, generated_at, guardrails, lane_status, lane_violations, life_phase, mode, north_star, risk, targets]`.

**Why:** [`cockpit.ts:472`](../../../Pre Atlas/services/delta-kernel/src/core/cockpit.ts) has the TODO `mode_since: ctx.systemState.entity.created_at, // TODO: track mode changes`. It returns the system creation timestamp because there's no mode-change time anywhere to source from. Anything in the UI (CycleBoard, lattice, inPACT) showing "in CLOSURE mode for X days" is **lying** — it's actually showing "system has existed for X days." This is the kind of silent drift the cockpit can't self-detect.

**How to apply:** Three coordinated edits:
1. Add `mode_since: string` (ISO timestamp) to `governance_state.json` schema (`GovernanceState.v1.json` if it exists; otherwise the writer code).
2. On every mode transition in cognitive-sensor (find via `rg "mode = " services/cognitive-sensor` — likely `directive_engine.py` or `governance_daemon.ts`), set `mode_since` to the transition timestamp.
3. Update `cockpit.ts:472` to use `ctx.systemState.entity.mode_since ?? ctx.systemState.entity.created_at` so the fallback is explicit and the field becomes load-bearing.

## How the audit will treat these

Both findings re-emit on every `bash tools/atlas-audit/audit.sh` run until closed. The exit code reflects "NEW findings since prior run" — so once these are addressed, exit code stays 0 unless something new appears.

Related: [[feedback_search_protocol]] (the toolbelt these were found with) · [[project_atlas_executor_wiring]] (broader Atlas wire context).

## project_atlas_autostart_broken

<a id="project-atlas-autostart-broken"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_autostart_broken.md`*
*Name:* project-atlas-autostart-broken
*Description:* CLOSED 2026-06-15 — Atlas autostart bundle committed (92c686a), 4 Python-service quoting bugs fixed, 15-min self-heal trigger added so delta-kernel re-spawns within 15min of any crash

The "delta-kernel always has to be initialized" pain point — **CLOSED 2026-06-15 in commit `92c686a`**.

Resolution arc (multiple sessions on 2026-06-15):
1. Earlier session wrote `scripts/start_atlas.ps1` (115 LOC, 13-service orchestrator with inline `Start-Process powershell.exe -Command` spawn) and committed it in `699be90`. Skipped the `_atlas_runner.ps1` helper — orchestrator inlines its own per-service spawn instead. Did NOT also commit the 7 supporting files.
2. This session adversarially reviewed the committed script, surfaced 4 quoting bugs + the need for self-heal, fixed them, and committed the support bundle as `92c686a`.

**What `92c686a` did:**
- Added 7 untracked support files (`_atlas_runner.ps1`, `install_atlas_autostart.ps1`, `status_atlas.ps1`, `stop_atlas.ps1`, `uninstall_atlas_autostart.ps1`, `start_atlas.bat`, `stop_atlas.bat`).
- Fixed `start_atlas.ps1` quoting: 4 Python services (openclaw, mosaic-orch, cortex, optogon) were silently failing because single-quoted `'$env:PYTHONPATH="src"'` had its inner `"src"` stripped by `CommandLineToArgvW` during `Start-Process` marshaling. Switched to backtick-escaped `$env:` + single-quoted value (delta-kernel pattern — single quotes survive `CommandLineToArgvW`).
- Fixed log seed quoting same way (was polluting first lines of every service log with a `Set-Content` error).
- Added 15-min `TimeTrigger` repetition to `Atlas-Autostart` scheduled task alongside the existing logon trigger. Orchestrator is idempotent (`Get-NetTCPConnection` guard skips live services), so periodic refire only respawns dead services. **Worst-case downtime after a crash now ≤15 min.**
- Added `:8887` (atlas-substrate) to `status_atlas.ps1` and `stop_atlas.ps1` — they were silently ignoring the service that `start_atlas.ps1` spawns.
- Fixed misleading comment in `_atlas_runner.ps1` ("Called by start_atlas.ps1" — it isn't; orchestrator inlines its own spawn).

**Why this layout (not a watchdog daemon):** Bruke's hard constraint was no PM2 / nssm / Docker / userland supervisor. The 15-min periodic re-trigger uses Task Scheduler — Windows' built-in supervisor — instead of building a new one. See [[user-engineering-fingerprint]] for the "use the platform's primitive, not a third-party one" pattern.

**Verified gates during this session:**
- Gate 1 manual launch — `:3001 LISTEN`, HTTP 401 from `/api/state` (server alive, auth required = correct).
- Gate 2 stop_atlas — 13 procs killed including `:8887`.
- Gate 3 `start_atlas.bat` — 13/13 services up.
- Gate 4 `Start-ScheduledTask Atlas-Autostart` — `LastTaskResult=0`, `:3001` bound.

**Sharp edge for next session:** `start_atlas.ps1`'s 60s poll deadline is too short when all 13 services cold-start simultaneously (npx tsx + next dev contesting CPU). The status table reports `[FAIL]` for delta-kernel/blueprint-gen during the run even though both bind ~30-60s later. Functional → services bind; UX → status table lies on first run. Workflow review (this session) called this out as HIGH but the fix (longer deadline or health-probe per service) was deferred. Bumping to 120s would close it.

**Stale-handoff trap:** `HANDOFF_AUTOSTART.md` at repo root is still untracked and now stale (describes `start_atlas.ps1` as missing). Delete or update next time you're in the repo. Same for the matching session note that fed this memory — both predate the `699be90` commit.

Related: [[project-droplist-packet-engine]] (PKT-008 shipped earlier 2026-06-15; unrelated work track but adjacent in session history); [[feedback-agent-report-distrust]] (workflow review caught 4 real bugs the handoff didn't surface — adversarial verification earned its keep here).

## project_atlas_completeness_assessment

<a id="project-atlas-completeness-assessment"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_completeness_assessment.md`*
*Name:* project-atlas-completeness-assessment
*Description:* Fable 5's evidence-grounded assessment of what Atlas needs to be 'complete' — two unsoldered joints and one inverted flow, not missing parts. 6-change plan, ranked.

Ran 2026-07-06 in response to Bruke's frustration: doesn't use Atlas at all, inPACT "harasses me daily, starts blank, does nothing," Optogon "isn't living up to its purpose." Fable 5 agent verified every claim against live code + the actual SQLite databases (`.delta-fabric/state.db`, UASC `registry.db`, Optogon `sessions.db`) rather than trusting prior memory summaries — and corrected two of them.

**Corrections to prior assumptions:**
- inPACT/today.html **is** wired to delta-kernel (`apps/inpact/js/api.js`, `today.html:16-19` now redirects into the app, `cycle_board` entity at v16,119 synced same-day). The earlier "pure localStorage, never wired" claim was stale.
- Optogon's leaf actions are **more real than assumed**: `SEND_DRAFT_v1.json` already does a real ntfy.sh phone push (the smallest-win fix from [[project_atlas_physical_leaves]] already landed), `CLOSE_LOOP_v1.json` does a real PUT to delta-kernel tasks.

**Root causes (the actual gaps):**
1. **inPACT is wired but inverted** — it asks Bruke to author a blank plan every day instead of projecting what Atlas already knows (tasks/goals/next-best-move) into it. Zero consequence either way: what you type never becomes a Task entity, never touches the work queue. Data proof: only 8 Today entries ever, none in the last 17 days, while the app itself was opened today. This is why it reads as harassment — a memoryless, consequence-free daily nag.
2. **Optogon's brain→hands nerve carries zero traffic.** Sensor feed is unscheduled/dormant (feeds nothing in). `OPTOGON_SIGNAL_EMIT` defaults off (nothing out). `POST /api/actions/pending` / `createPendingAction` (`cockpit.ts:487`) has **no caller anywhere in the repo** — grep-verified. Only 2 `pending_action` entities ever created (both 2026-07-05, this week's testing). `RiskTier` (`'auto'|'notify'|'confirm'`, `types-core.ts:273`) is defined but used nowhere. UASC ledger: 42 runs total, `@CLOSE_LOOP` run exactly once ever.
3. **"Doesn't use Atlas at all" = the sum of 1+2** plus every joint being designed human-in-the-loop when the human is the scarcest resource. The good news: the machine-facing control plane already exists and is solid (`atlas-ai.ts` 1,582 lines, `atlas-map` MCP, `/api/work/*`) — it's just never invoked on a cadence and no doctrine tells an agent session it's the front door.

**Progress:** Changes #3 and #1 SHIPPED 2026-07-06, commit `221a888` on `feat/atlas-setup-ui`.
- **#3** — repo-root `CLAUDE.md`: documents the `atlas-map` MCP describe/call gateway (10 surfaces/74 capabilities, role/clearance model per `services/atlas-map-api/SELF_DESCRIBE.md`) as the preferred path, `atlas-ai` CLI (`services/delta-kernel`, `npm run atlas-ai -- <cmd>`, self-documenting via `capabilities`/`help`) as the second, direct REST (`/api/work/*`, `/api/auth/token`) as the fallback. Documents the Optogon→pending-action gap so agents don't assume that loop is live.
- **#1** — `governance_daemon.ts`'s existing 06:00 `day_start` cron now calls a new `seedTodayPlan()`: pre-fills inPACT's `Today.daily[date]` p1-p3 from the top 3 open `task` entities by priority (never invents winTarget/why/lever — those stay blank for Bruke), pushes a phone summary via the existing `notifyPhone`, skips if a plan for that date already exists (idempotent, verified by re-running the job).
- **Real bug found+fixed in the same pass** (code-as-furniture): `GET /api/cycleboard` (`server.ts:2695`) was returning `entities[0].state` (`{data: blob}`) instead of unwrapping to the raw blob PUT accepts. This meant `remote._localUpdatedAt` was always `undefined` in `apps/inpact/js/state.js`'s `syncFromApi()`, so remote never looked newer than local — **pull-down sync silently never fired**, only push-up did. Same bug also broke `atlas-ai.ts`'s `day`/`task`/`journal` commands reading `cb.DayPlans` etc. Fixed by returning `entities[0].state.data`. Verified live: restarted delta-api (port 3001, with the user's explicit go-ahead since another session had it running), confirmed unwrapped shape, triggered `day_start` via `POST /api/daemon/run`, confirmed today's plan seeded with real task titles and re-running is a no-op.
- Browser-side (inPACT :3006) verification was skipped — an unmanaged process already held that port and wasn't disrupted; frontend correctness was confirmed instead by direct source read of `today.js`'s `populateInputs()`/`blank()`, which match the seeded field names (`p1`/`p2`/`p3`/`winTarget`/etc.) exactly.

**Correction (2026-07-06, found while building #2): `apps/inpact/js/today.js` and `today.html` are DEAD CODE, not the live render path.** `today.html` is just a redirect stub into `index.html`'s SPA, and `today.js` is not in `index.html`'s script list at all (confirmed by reading `index.html`'s `<script src>` tags: api/state/validator/ui/helpers/ai-context/ai-actions/screens/functions/app/signals/backbone — no today.js). The REAL live Today/Daily fields are rendered by `screens.js`'s `Home()` and `Daily()` functions, backed by `functions.js`'s `getTodayFields()`/`saveTodayField()`/`buildLinkSelect()`. Same `state.Today.daily[date]` shape and field names as `today.js` assumed (so change #1's seeding was accidentally still correct), but `today.js` itself never executes. Worth a cleanup pass later (delete `today.js` + the `today.html`/`method.html`/`followup.html` redirect-stub trio if truly unused, or confirm intentional legacy bookmarks).

**Also corrected:** p1/p2/p3 link to `state.AZTask` (inPACT's own client-side A-Z task list) via `buildLinkSelect`, which is a SEPARATE concept from delta-kernel `task` entities. These are two distinct task systems that happen to share a screen. Change #2 threads real task entity ids through a new `p{n}TaskId` field (not reusing the AZTask link mechanism) so seeded backend-derived priorities can be marked done against real Atlas state without conflating the two systems.

**Progress update:** Change #5 (wire prepared work → pending_action) SHIPPED 2026-07-06 — but shipped by **Bruke himself**, commit `f6a0c61` "wire prepared-work into pending_action for notify/confirm tiers", landed mid-session while this conversation was in progress. `notifyPreparedActions()` in `governance_daemon.ts` runs every minute alongside the existing `autoExecutePreparedActions()`: for each of `buildCockpit()`'s prepared actions (only 3 derivations exist — OPEN task→`complete_task`, unread thread→`reply_message`, READY draft→`send_draft`, all hardcoded in `cockpit.ts`) whose effective risk tier is `notify`/`confirm` (never `auto`, that stays on the existing path), it creates a `pending_action` entity + phone push, with a dedup guard against existing PENDING entries. `createPendingAction` — previously an unused function — got its first caller. Given this directly touches the trust boundary, ran a 3-lens adversarial verification workflow (trust-boundary/injection, concurrency, code-quality, each cross-checked by an independent refuter) rather than taking it on faith:
- **Trust-boundary lens: clean, zero findings.** Confirmed `POST /api/actions/confirm/:id` (`server.ts:2810`) is the only place a pending_action ever becomes CONFIRMED (grepped the whole repo for callers — one production call site), it re-checks the mode gate at confirm time not just creation time, and `PendingActionData.payload` only ever stores `{label, priority}` — never an executable command. A pending_action sitting in PENDING never auto-executes.
- **Concurrency + code-quality lenses: 2 real bugs confirmed** (all 3 raw findings collapsed to 2 root causes, each independently re-verified by a refuter agent who could not overturn them): (1) none of the 10 `cron.schedule()` calls passed `noOverlap`, and node-cron 4.2.1 defaults it to `false` — verified directly in `node_modules/node-cron/dist/cjs/scheduler/runner.js` that an overlapping tick only logs a warning and proceeds anyway, meaning a slow `work_queue` tick (now doing up to 7 awaited hash operations in the pending-action loop) could race the next tick's dedup read against not-yet-persisted writes, producing duplicate pending actions + duplicate phone pushes for the same target; (2) `notifyPreparedActions()`'s for-loop had no per-iteration try/catch, so one action's failure silently aborted every remaining prepared action for that tick with no visibility beyond the job_history log.
- **Both fixed same session**, commit `450d8f0`: added `{ noOverlap: true, name: '<job>' }` to all 10 cron jobs (verified against node-cron's actual runner source that `noOverlap:true` genuinely skips the overlapping tick before `checkAndRun`, not just logs it — every job here re-derives state from current data each run, so a skipped tick is always safe), and wrapped each pending-action-creation iteration in its own try/catch so one failure no longer kills the batch. tsc clean. Could not do a full live restart-and-test pass because Bruke's own delta-api dev server was running on :3001 at the time (his own active session, unlike earlier unmanaged/orphaned processes on 3006/3001 — did not touch it); verified instead by direct inspection of node-cron's actual source plus the adversarial workflow's own line-by-line trace.

**Progress update:** Change #4 SHIPPED 2026-07-06, commit `09d64fb`. New `sensor_daily` cron (05:45 daily, ahead of Day Start) spawns `cognitive-sensor/run_daily.py` via the same spawn/timeout pattern as `refresh`/`agent_pipeline`, non-critical by design. `OPTOGON_SIGNAL_EMIT=1` set in `.claude/launch.json` + `services/optogon/start.bat` — confirmed safe against TRUST_BOUNDARY.md (`/api/signals` POST only validates+stores via `ingestSignal`; resolving still needs an explicit `action_id` from the caller, no capability reopening). **Real finding while verifying live:** a direct foreground run of `run_daily.py` showed it gets through ingest/analyze + machine-wide `es_scan` (23 leaked .env, 43 stalled projects, 20 large artifacts) fine, then `auto_triage.py` (phase 1.7) throws trying to reach Optogon's `/session/start` — **because Optogon (:3010) wasn't actually running**, just env-flagged. That phase is non-critical so the pipeline correctly continues (FEST cards + cognitive wiring both completed after), but each HIGH-severity finding's failed HTTP call still burns real time (10s socket timeout each). This is why the original 5-minute job timeout was too short; bumped to 15 minutes (matching the `agent_pipeline` precedent) rather than guessing. **Open note for later:** flipping `OPTOGON_SIGNAL_EMIT` only turns on visibility — the auto_triage phase's actual reasoning only fires once Optogon is a running service, not just env-flagged. Starting Optogon as a scheduled companion service was out of scope for this change (the assessment only asked to schedule the sensor + flip the signal flag) but is the natural next lift if the auto_triage phase itself matters, separate from #5/#6's pending-action wiring.

**Progress update:** Change #2 SHIPPED 2026-07-06, commit `47c11dd`. `seedTodayPlan` now stores `p1TaskId`/`p2TaskId`/`p3TaskId`; `screens.js`'s Daily screen shows a "Done" button on any priority carrying a task id, wired to `completeSeededPriority()` (`functions.js`) → `AtlasAPI.completeBackendTask` → `PUT /api/tasks/:id` (same path already used by the Ask-Atlas modal's "Handle next" list, confirmed via `server.ts:500` to set `status:'DONE'` and fire the closure feedback pipeline). Also promoted that same "Handle next" prepared-task list (`ai-actions.js`'s `_renderHandleNext`, previously reachable only inside the "Ask Atlas" modal) directly onto the Home screen via a new `_prepCache`/`handle-next-card` block, mirroring the existing Strategic HUD refresh pattern. Verified live: re-seeded today's plan, confirmed `p1TaskId` populated, called the exact PUT the Done button fires and got `{"success":true}`. Frontend syntax-checked via `node --check`; full in-browser pass skipped (port 3006 held by an unmanaged process, user didn't respond to the restart prompt) — verified instead by tracing `index.html`'s script load order confirming `_renderHandleNext` is defined before `screens.js` runs.

**The 6-change plan (no rewrite, all wiring, ranked by leverage/effort):**
1. **(S)** Daemon seeds the day into inPACT each morning from what Atlas already knows + phone push — kills the blank-form mechanic directly.
2. **(S-M)** Today's p1/p2/p3 link to real Task entities; checking one calls the existing `completeBackendTask`; surface the "Ask Atlas" next-best-move panel by default instead of behind a modal.
3. **(S) — do this same week.** Write the agent front-door contract as doctrine (skill or CLAUDE.md section): `atlas-ai` commands + MCP + `/api/work/*` + token bootstrap, so agents stop rediscovering the control plane every session.
4. **(S)** Schedule `cognitive-sensor/run_daily.py`, flip `OPTOGON_SIGNAL_EMIT=1`. Leave `AUTO_TRIAGE_APPLY` off (dry-run first per the existing whitepaper rollout plan).
5. **(M)** Wire prepared work → `pending_action` entities using the existing unused `RiskTier`, through the existing confirm path (mode gate, template lock, HMAC bridge) to UASC. Start with `@SEND_DRAFT`/`@CLOSE_LOOP` only. **Passes TRUST_BOUNDARY.md test explicitly**: the 7 ActionTypes/10 UASC tokens stay frozen in source; this changes traffic *through* the closed set, never the set itself.
6. **(M, after 5)** Join Optogon's `action_required` signals to the same producer — fs-triage verdicts become notify-tier phone pushes with one-tap confirm.

**Dead weight, named plainly:** cortex's execute arm and the 502 batch `triage_fs_loop` sessions are lab residue until 4-6 give them a live feed. UI surfaces already marked DROP in [[project_atlas_headless_map]] (setup grid, dashboards, viewers) — stop maintaining, the plan never touches them. `research/uasc-m2m/reference-implementation` stays quarantined per [[project_trust_boundary]] — never expose `register_graph`/`bind_glyph`.

**Verdict in one line:** not missing parts, two unsoldered joints (sensor→scheduler, brain→pending-actions) and one inverted flow (inPACT asks instead of projects). Changes 1-4 ≈ a week, and alone would turn Atlas from a museum of working parts into something that phones Bruke in the morning.

Extends [[project_atlas_headless_map]], [[project_atlas_physical_leaves]], [[project_inpact_product_pivot]], [[project_atlas_inpact_role_split]], [[project_optogon_stack]]. Uses [[reference_fable5_leverage]] as the intended use case (ambiguity + high-consequence synthesis across a large repo, first real application of the doctrine).

## project_atlas_consolidation_presearch

<a id="project-atlas-consolidation-presearch"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_consolidation_presearch.md`*
*Name:* project-atlas-consolidation-presearch
*Description:* Groundwork presearch mapping Atlas's real shape + a 4-wave consolidation plan for Fable to fan out on

2026-07-20 Groundwork brownfield pass on Atlas itself (Bruke: "find the shape, it's a loose sloppy mudball, presearch before I offload to Fable to fan out and consolidate/complete the agent harness"). Deliverable: **[ATLAS_CONSOLIDATION_PRESEARCH.md](../../../../Pre Atlas/ATLAS_CONSOLIDATION_PRESEARCH.md)** at repo root — file:line-cited, fan-out-ready.

**The reframe (verified firsthand):** Atlas is NOT a monster — ~218k LOC of TS/JS/Python, ~18 live services + 2 apps. The spine already exists and is ~70% the "harness": one state store (`~/.delta-fabric/state.db`, delta-kernel), three live front doors (atlas-map MCP · `atlas-ai` CLI · delta-kernel REST ~15 route families), and `atlas-map-api:3072` self-describes **40 surfaces**. The "loose/sloppy" feeling = the backbone is undocumented and the UIs bypass it, not a missing backbone.

**Three pains → mechanisms:**
- Invisible processes = `\Atlas-Autostart` **logon-triggered** task boots 12 services in HIDDEN windows (`start_atlas.ps1:58`); delta-kernel governance daemon auto-starts with NO env gate (`server.ts:81`), 11 cron jobs, 60s work_queue tick fires phone notifications; **7 scheduled tasks all Ready**; morning pipeline triple-fires (05:45/06:30/06:45).
- Tab spam = per-service `start.bat` visible terminals + 42-entry `launch.json` (has a DANGLING `mosaic-orch` cwd → deleted dir, `launch.json:266`) + `start_atlas.ps1` opens 2 tabs.
- Static-info mudball = 3 disagreeing inventories (20/74/~18 services; freshest inflated 62% by vendored `tools/anatomy-research` 572k LOC), 5 map artifacts frozen 2026-04-14, `system-map.html` forked 4×, 147-file root dump-bucket (~90 stray PNGs + garbage `nul`/`10mb`/`err.log`), ~55MB untracked blobs, 12 dead stub apps + 181-file `_retired` dirs.
- **TWO atlas repos:** `C:\Users\bruke\atlas` is a SEPARATE git repo running as `:8887 atlas-substrate`.

**Plan = 4 waves** (leverage÷risk): W0 litter/orphan sweep (zero code risk) · W1 visibility (daemon switch + status surface + dedup pipeline) · W2 collapse map/UI dup to single source of truth · W3 complete harness (atlas-map-api drives UIs not just describes — `ui` invocation currently 422; docs 10→40; resolve two-repo split).

**Delegation harness (2026-07-20):** Bruke wants Fable to LOOK+REASON via his recon tools (bearings/delta-scp/code-recon/groundwork/seam) and DELEGATE work to Haiku (mechanical) + Sonnet (real code) — Fable writes zero code, to save tokens. NOT Dask (wrong category: parallel-compute vs LLM-call orchestration). Two artifacts at Pre Atlas root: `ATLAS_CONSOLIDATION_RUNBOOK.md` (operating loop LOOK→REASON→DELEGATE→VERIFY + per-task itinerary + routing.json) and `atlas-consolidation.workflow.js` (launch-ready Workflow script: recon+reason agents inherit Fable, executor agents `model:haiku|sonnet`, wave-ordered via pipeline, budget-guarded, GATE tasks self-park). Script is BUILT, deliberately NOT run (launch via `Workflow({scriptPath})` in a Fable session). Substrate chosen = native Claude Code subagents (option A), not standalone py.

**Fest scaffolded (2026-07-20):** `atlas-consolidation-AC0002` in `festivals/planning/` — **validates 100/100**. 1 impl phase `001_IMPLEMENT`, 4 sequences = 4 waves (`01_wave0_litter`→`04_wave3_harness`), 16 file:line-anchored tasks + per-wave quality gates (testing/review/iterate/commit). `fest_working_dir: projects/pre-atlas`. Each task's DoD is tool-provable. Promote planning→ready when Fable starts. Fill scripts in scratchpad (`fill_tasks.py`, `fill_sequences.py`).

**Open decisions (Bruke deferred both 2026-07-20):** (1) sibling atlas repo — merge/declare/retire (gates task 3.3); (2) autostart posture — explicit+switchable/visibility-only/manual (gates task 1.1 default only). Fable does W0-W2 first; these gate W3. Method: 3 parallel recon agents + firsthand verification of load-bearing claims. ## project_atlas_executor_wiring

<a id="project-atlas-executor-wiring"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_executor_wiring.md`*
*Name:* atlas-executor-wiring
*Description:* Atlas autonomous executor is fully wired in code, kept in dry-run by 3 safety switches; bearer-header bug across cognitive-sensor closure POSTs all patched 2026-05-27 — only remaining gate is whether to flip AUTO_TRIAGE_APPLY=1

The autonomous executor chain (auto_triage → thread_decisions → auto_actor → delta-kernel → mode engine) is fully wired in code and verified live 2026-05-27. Closure POST roundtrip confirmed working (HTTP 200, mode flipped CLOSURE in real time on a smoke test).

**Why the system felt like a "glorified todo list":** all three safety switches are off:
- `AUTO_TRIAGE_APPLY=1` — lets Optogon write real verdicts to `thread_decisions.json`
- `CORTEX_BRIDGE_APPLY=1` — directives flow to Cortex for queueing
- `CORTEX_BRIDGE_RUN_PROPOSAL=1` — Cortex runs proposals

Why: early executor version deleted things without preserving content. Bruke kept building safety layers. The fix already shipped — `auto_actor.extract_value()` (lines 274-340) mines topics, classification, related ideas, strategic relevance from each loop BEFORE archiving. Past self solved the destructive-delete problem and current self forgot.

**Bearer-header bug pattern (cognitive-sensor → delta-kernel) — RESOLVED 2026-05-27:**
- ✅ `auto_actor.py:184-203` — patched session 1
- ✅ `close_loop.py:230-251` — patched session 2 (key loader + Bearer header)
- ✅ `decide.py:69-83` — patched session 2 (refresh endpoint)
- ✅ `fs_actor.py:24-37, 44-56` — patched session 2 (`_notify_delta` helper)
- ✅ `atlas_triage_cli.py:_post_close_loop` (line 384-401) — already correct; memory line 28 was just the URL constant
- ✅ `cycleboard_push.py:_http` (line 76-101) — already correct; uses `_load_api_key()`
- ✅ `fest_actor.py:_tenant_key` + POST at line 202-220 — already correct
- Delta-kernel expects `Authorization: Bearer <contents of .aegis-tenant-key>` (NOT X-API-Key)
- Without the header → silent 401 (live-verified 2026-05-27: WITH bearer → 200, WITHOUT → 401 on `/api/cycleboard`)
- All cognitive-sensor closure POSTs now carry the header.

**Why:** The closure-avoidance pattern that applies to 50 in-progress projects also applies to Bruke's own protective work — he kept adding safety after he'd already shipped the safety.

Rate-limited to 5 closures/run, default confidence 0.8, 7-day reopen feedback auto-tunes threshold. Safe to test in isolation. Related: [[project_optogon_stack]], [[project_atlas_substrate_repo]].

## project_atlas_gps_substrate

<a id="project-atlas-gps-substrate"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_gps_substrate.md`*

# Atlas GPS Substrate

Three-layer read surface over the Pre Atlas system map. Same data, three consumers:

## Layer 1 — HTTP API (`services/atlas-map-api/` · port 3072)
- FastAPI + uvicorn + rapidfuzz; reads `<repo>/audit/system-index.json` + `<repo>/atlas-map.json` on startup, caches in process.
- 9 endpoints under `/map/*` plus `POST /admin/reload`.
- Console script: `atlas-map-api`. Registered in `.claude/launch.json` as `atlas-map-api`.
- 13 HTTP tests + 10 MCP tests = **23/23 passing** as of 2026-06-20.

## Layer 2 — CLI (`tools/atlas-cli/`)
- Python `typer` + `httpx` + `rich`; thin shell over the HTTP API.
- 10 subcommands: `where`, `locate`, `neighbors`, `path`, `search`, `list`, `show`, `status`, `reload`, `open`.
- `--json` flag for pipe-friendly machine output; `--api` to override base URL.
- Console script: `atlas`. **11/11 tests passing**.
- `where` auto-detects repo root by walking up from cwd looking for `audit/system-index.json`.
- **WHY:** Bruke asked for "GPS for code and data" — needed a verb-style CLI you can run from any cwd to answer "where am I, what depends on what, where does this file live."
- **HOW TO APPLY:** When working inside any service dir, run `atlas where` to confirm subsystem identity; `atlas neighbors <name> -n 2` for dependency context; `atlas locate <file>` to attribute any path.

## Layer 3 — MCP server (same package, `atlas_map_api.mcp_server`)
- FastMCP 3.x; **direct-imports loader + graph** — does NOT require the HTTP server to be running.
- Same 9 tools exposed: `atlas_where`, `atlas_locate`, `atlas_neighbors`, `atlas_path`, `atlas_search`, `atlas_list`, `atlas_show`, `atlas_status`, `atlas_reload`.
- Console script: `atlas-map-mcp`. Registered in `~/.claude.json` as `atlas-map` (stdio).
- **WHY:** so Claude can query the system map natively as MCP tools instead of shelling out to curl or atlas-cli.
- **HOW TO APPLY:** invokable inside any Claude Code session after reconnect; tools start with `atlas_` prefix.

## Shared snapshot semantics
All three layers read the **same** files: `audit/system-index.json` + `atlas-map.json`. Edits go through `audit/imports/_build_map.py`. After it reruns:
- HTTP API: `POST /admin/reload` (or `atlas reload` via CLI).
- MCP server: call the `atlas_reload` tool.
- The two reloads are independent — each layer has its own in-process cache.

## Path-form smarts
The CLI's `to_repo_relative()` and MCP server's `_to_repo_relative()` both accept three input forms equally:
1. **Absolute path** → strip repo prefix
2. **Repo-relative** (e.g. `services/foo/bar.py`) → pass through if it exists under repo root OR if cwd-joined version doesn't exist
3. **cwd-relative** → join to cwd, resolve, strip repo prefix

This lets you type `atlas locate services/delta-kernel/src/api/server.ts` from any cwd (the venv, your home dir, the repo root) and it just works.

## Bugs caught + fixed during build
- **Decorator broke typer introspection** — `@_handle_http` wrapping commands with `*args/**kwargs` destroyed typer's signature parsing → "No such option: --group". Fix: context manager around the client instead of a decorator on each command.
- **Windows cp1252 stdout** crashes on Unicode arrows (`→` U+2192). Fix: reconfigure `sys.stdout`/`sys.stderr` to UTF-8 with `errors="replace"` fallback at import time. `rich.Console(legacy_windows=False)` was a red herring — the encoding lives one layer lower.
- **CSS Grid auto-fullscreen glitch** in the viewer was rooted in `min-width: auto` defaults on grid items preventing #cy from shrinking. Fix: `min-width: 0; min-height: 0` on grid children + `minmax(0, var(...))` wrappers.
- **`where` returned "no subsystem owns ."** because `to_repo_relative(".", repo)` returned `"."` (no prefix match). Fix: bypass `to_repo_relative` in the `where` command; compute `cwd.relative_to(repo_root)` directly + append `"/."` so prefix-match works even when cwd is exactly a service root.

## Composition diagram
```
  audit/_build_map.py  ──writes──>  audit/system-index.json + atlas-map.json
                                          │
                              read-only ↓
              ┌──────── atlas-map-api (:3072) ────────┐
              │            (HTTP, FastAPI)            │
              │                                       │
              │ direct-imports loader/graph           │
              │  (no HTTP needed)                     │
              ↓                                       ↓
     atlas-map MCP server                    atlas-cli (`atlas …`)
     (FastMCP, in Claude Code)              (typer, shells over HTTP)
              │                                       │
              ↓                                       ↓
       Claude tool calls                    Your shell, any cwd
                          \                  /
                           ↘                ↙
                       audit/system-map.html (browser viewer)
```

## `GET /map/viewer` — the wire foundation (SHIPPED 2026-06-20)
Added the single endpoint both wires stand on. Returns the **exact `window.SERVICES` shape** the viewer consumes (name/group/port/`running`/`state`/lang/framework/files/loc/deps_count/in_autostart/purpose/entry_points) + `edges` as `{from,to}`, field-adapted from the snapshot (API's `language/file_count/total_loc` → viewer's `lang/files/loc`).
- **Live `running`:** concurrently TCP-probes every ported subsystem via `asyncio.gather` (~0.2s total). Live-verified it caught 7 services up (delta-kernel, aegis-fabric, code-converter, mosaic-dashboard, triangulation, uasc-executor, ws-gateway) — more accurate than the stale startup banner.
- **`state` slot = null** — reserved for Wire 2 (dormant/gated/void facts from [[project-triage-loop-trace]]).
- `?probe=false` returns `in_autostart` as cheap proxy (used in tests). server.py tests now **15/15** (added `test_viewer_shape_matches_window_services` + `test_viewer_live_probe_returns_bool_running`).

## The two wires — BOTH SHIPPED + VERIFIED 2026-06-20
**Wire 1 (viewer → live API):** `apps/lattice/system-map.html` boot is now `async` — `hydrateLiveState()` fetches `:3072/map/viewer?probe=true` (1.5s abort), overlays `running`/`health`/`gov`/`state` onto baked `window.SERVICES` by name, **keeps baked file previews** (the 1.19MB `system-map-data.js` holds per-file source the API doesn't serve), falls back silently to baked data if API down. Green/gray live badge on `#meta-line`. Verified: 33/33 matched, nodes recolor to live `running` (caught delta-kernel down accurately).

**Wire 2 (state into the map):** added `governance` block to `atlas-map.json` (per [[project-triage-loop-trace]]: cognitive-sensor=dormant, cortex/optogon=gated, delta-kernel=active, droplist=on_demand). `loader.py` reads it → `Subsystem.gov`; `/map/viewer` surfaces `gov` + derived `health` (`ok`/`down`(autostart but not answering)/`idle`). Viewer hover shows `⚙ automation — note`; `#meta-line` shows summary `⚠ N down (expected up) · ⚙ M dormant: …`. Verified live: "⚠ 7 down · ⚙ 1 dormant: cognitive-sensor".

**Durability:** all viewer JS ported into the `_build_map.py` HTML template (not just the generated file). Ran the builder → exit 0, regenerated all 3 copies (audit/, apps/lattice/, atlas/pages/), each carries the wires (grep ✓). `atlas-map.json` governance preserved (builder only writes config if absent; `load_config` does `cfg.update`). **Tests: 26/26** (server +3: viewer shape, live-probe, governance/health). API restarted on :3072 with new code (old autostart instance killed; editable install → autostart respawns new code).

**COMMITTED `e0dea1c`** (2026-06-20) on `experiment/droplist-remediation-2026-06-15` — full atlas-map-api package (was never committed) + atlas-map.json + _build_map.py. Review-gated before commit: security-reviewer = nothing blocking (loopback-only bind, ports from on-disk index not user input, no secrets; CORS `*` is tighten-later). code-reviewer found 2 HIGH, both FIXED pre-commit: (1) XSS — `escapeHtml` on gov.note + dormant names; (2) `_probe_port` socket close moved to `finally`. Plus MEDIUM gov-shape normalization (`_normalize_gov`). **Generated `system-map.html`/`.js` left UNcommitted (regenerable via builder)** — decide whether to track them. **Deferred MEDIUM:** `hydrateLiveState` mutates `window.SERVICES` in place (violates immutability doctrine but render-order-safe today; reviewer's `.map()` fix noted for next pass).

## Wire 3 — the map DRIVES (start/stop/restart) — SHIPPED + REVIEWED `1435bf4` (2026-06-20)
The map could see a service down but not act (read-only). Added an action layer:
- `launcher.py` (new, only write/exec surface): resolve subsystem→port→launch.json config, spawn the config's argv **verbatim, shell=False** (allowlist firewall — nothing from the HTTP request reaches the command). Idempotent start, detached spawn, psutil stop-on-port, refuses SELF_PORT (3072). `psutil>=6.0` added to deps.
- `server.py`: `POST /map/{start,stop,restart}/{name}`, each requiring the subsystem to exist AND have a launch.json config before acting.
- viewer (`_build_map.py` template): service-node context menu → `▶ start` when down, `■ stop`/`⟳ restart` when up; `runServiceAction` POSTs then re-probes + recolors; badge made idempotent.
- **Verified LIVE end-to-end**: drove memory-hub down→up→down via API and via the browser context-menu path; details panel showed "now running". Self-protection + allowlist gates hold. **Tests 34/34.**
- security-reviewer (nothing blocked): folded in CORS `*`→explicit viewer origins (3011/8897/8888), `stop` now requires a launch config (no killing unmanaged procs), log filename sanitized, and fixed `optogon` launch bind `0.0.0.0`→`127.0.0.1` in launch.json.

**KNOWN LIMITATION (data bug, not code):** `droplist` and `memory-hub` BOTH carry port 3071 in `system-index.json` (only one can bind it). Action resolution is by port, so start/stop on `droplist` actually targets the 3071 owner (memory-hub config). Fix = correct droplist's port in the index/atlas-map.json. Until then, port-based resolution mis-targets these two.

## Write-token guard on all POSTs — SHIPPED + LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-06-20
HIGH security finding (state-changing POSTs had NO auth: `/items/{id}/status` mutates droplist packets, `/map/{start,stop,restart}` spawns/kills procs, `/admin/reload`). Bind-127.0.0.1 + restricted CORS covered casual use but not a same-machine rogue process or DNS-rebind tab.
- **`auth.py`** (new): `X-Atlas-Token` shared secret. Resolution order: `ATLAS_WRITE_TOKEN` env → `<repo_root>/.atlas-write-token` (gitignored) → generated (`secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`) + written at startup. Single FastAPI dependency `require_write_token` (uses `secrets.compare_digest`); wired as `dependencies=[Depends(...)]` — one line per POST route. Fails closed.
- **`GET /admin/write-token`** (open, CORS-gated): browser bootstrap. Static lattice can't read the gitignored file off disk; a DNS-rebind foreign origin can't read this response (CORS). Local procs with FS access could read the file anyway, so it exposes nothing new.
- **Consumers wired:** `atlas-cli` client `_post` reads token (env/file) → header; `apps/lattice/index.html` `setBackboneStatus` + `apps/lattice/system-map.html` `runServiceAction` fetch token once (cached promise) → header. **MCP server needs NO change** — it's fully in-process (direct loader import, zero HTTP).
- **Tests 56/56** (was 36): parametrized 401 (no-token + bad-token) across all 5 POSTs, valid-token-passes-guard, open handout matches, token idempotency + env-override. CLI 11/11.
- **Live-verified** on a fresh :3099 instance: handout→token; POST no/bad token→401; POST valid token→200 write-through (needs_human→in_progress on disk, restored); `.bak` is gitignored, packet shows no git change.
- **ACTION:** the live autostart :3072 is still OLD (no-auth) code until restarted — restart to apply the guard.

## Still open (small)
- `/map/viewer` doesn't serve file previews — viewer keeps baked previews; fine unless previews need to go live.
- `state` lifecycle slot (retired/new) still snapshot-driven, not in `gov`.
- Manually-started uvicorn dies with the shell; autostart will respawn updated code on next cycle.

(project = THE UI)`. Only the last step is UI. Steps 1-4 are backend. Killing the UI loses **visualization,
not capability** (every surface is a thin client over delta-kernel/spoke APIs). Control surface already

cycleboard CLI parity (`atlas.ts` vs `cycleboard/js/*`, memory-claimed, stale); lattice `POST /api/lattice/correct`

## project_atlas_inpact_role_split

<a id="project-atlas-inpact-role-split"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_inpact_role_split.md`*
*Name:* Atlas / inPACT role split (canonical framing)
*Description:* Atlas is the manager/operator backend view; today.html inPACT is the worker/client frontend view. Execution flows worker→manager.

Atlas Core (with boot + CycleBoard) and inPACT today.html are **two audiences of one system**, not two competing products.

- **Atlas = manager view** — operator/coach console. Full governance instrumentation: modes, routing, CycleBoard screens, drift, closures, cohort state. Raw, operator-grade, no styling compromises.
- **today.html inPACT = worker/client/student view** — thin execution surface. One day, one directive at a time. Light theme, no em dashes, shared `ls-*` lesson template. No governance machinery visible.
- **Same backend, same state, two front-ends.** Public Integrity Toggle is the bridge — the worker opts into letting the manager view see their signal.

**Execution flow (worker → manager):**
- The user works the day inside today.html (sees the day laid out, completes items as they go).
- Completions stream back into Atlas state — "as you complete the day it fills up the Atlas."
- Atlas is fed by today.html actions, not the other way around. today.html is the input device; Atlas is the aggregate readout.

**Why:** Bruke framed this himself on 2026-04-21. It matches the locked pricing model (free lessons / paid execution / premium Public Integrity) and the product pivot (today.html IS the product, CycleBoard dashboard rejected as user-facing).

**How to apply:**
- Never suggest merging Atlas visuals into today.html or vice versa. They serve different audiences.
- Anything user-facing → today.html's visual language. Anything operator-facing → Atlas, unstyled OK.
- When designing new features, ask: is this a worker action or a manager readout? That decides which surface it lives on.
- Execution events (task done, lesson complete, directive acknowledged) originate in today.html and must write to the shared state Atlas reads. Do not build manager-only write paths for execution data.

## project_atlas_lattice_seam

<a id="project-atlas-lattice-seam"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_lattice_seam.md`*
*Name:* project-atlas-lattice-seam
*Description:* NEXT BUILD. The viewmodel + write-back layer between Atlas substrate and the lattice frontend so cognitive-sensor / perception / triangulation output renders into items[] + events[] that the user can correct via lattice's right-click and inline-add interactions.

**Frame (named by Bruke 2026-05-28):**
- Atlas = substrate. delta-kernel + aegis-fabric + ws-gateway + cognitive-sensor + perception + triangulation + cortex + optogon + 47 contracts + 6-mode state machine.
- Lattice = frontend. Tree / graph / timeline lenses over items + events. See [[project-lattice]].
- The thing that doesn't exist yet = the seam between them. That's the build.

**What the seam needs to do:**

1. **Read path (Atlas → lattice).** Project delta-kernel state + cognitive-sensor clusters + leverage scores + perception/triangulation signals into the lattice viewmodel shape. Probably a `GET /lattice/viewmodel` endpoint returning `{ items, events, projects }`.

2. **Write path (lattice → Atlas).** When user mutates in lattice (right-click status change, inline add, link edit, delete) → route as authoritative correction back into the substrate. These corrections become training signal for the substrate's classifiers / clusterers. The right-click context menu IS a labeling interface disguised as ergonomic UX.

3. **(Eventually) Subscription.** WebSocket or short-poll so ghost-executor / optogon / cortex writes appear in lattice without manual refresh.

**Viewmodel contract (locked in `lattice.html`, see `items` and `events` arrays):**
```
item:    { id: number, title: string, project: string, status: Status, time: string, links: Link[] }
event:   { date: 'YYYY-MM-DD', day: string, time: 'HH:MM', kind: EventKind, project: string, itemId: number, desc: string }
project: { id: string, name: string }
Status:    'open' | 'active' | 'blocked' | 'done' | 'dropped'
EventKind: 'add' | 'status' | 'link' | 'done'
Link:    { kind: 'blocks' | 'spawned' | 'relates', to: number }
```

**Why this is the load-bearing build, not Atlas or lattice individually:**
- Atlas alone is overbuilt. Power without legibility. Cognitive-sensor already clusters topics, scores leverage, detects temporal boundaries, but produces JSON files nobody opens.
- Lattice alone is undersourced. Legibility without leverage. A clean surface fed by hand-typed data is just a nicer task tracker.
- Combined via the seam: substrate proposes → lattice surfaces → user corrects via right-click → correction trains substrate. Compounds over months.

**The conversation that named this (key quotes for context):**
- "so atlas in the background this on the frontend"
- "ingesting thousands of conversations will look like magic"
- "atlas plus lattice is OP"
- Earlier in same convo: lattice's "feels simple" reaction was correct because the surface is supposed to be small — the magic happens in what the substrate does silently.

**Starting questions for the new chat:**

1. **Where does the read endpoint live?** Extend delta-kernel (already port 3001, owns state), spin up a new projection service, or have cognitive-sensor expose it directly? Probably delta-kernel since it already owns the canonical state and would need to know about cross-source signals anyway.

2. **What's the minimum-viable projection?** Which `item` and `event` fields can come from existing cognitive-sensor outputs (cluster_leverage_map, idea_registry, temporal pipelines) versus need new derivation logic? Status is the tricky one — that's a fairly opinionated inference.

3. **Write-back routing.** When the user flips an item to `blocked` in lattice, which contracts get touched? Does it go through Atlas Directive emitter? Through ghost executor? Direct to delta-kernel state with a provenance tag (user-correction vs system-inferred)?

4. **Does lattice migrate into the monorepo?** Currently at `C:\Users\bruke\Downloads\lattice.html`. Options: stay standalone and HTTP-call Atlas; migrate into `apps/lattice/` as a sibling of inpact; or absorb into the existing cycleboard / atlas-shell entrypoints.

5. **Provenance tagging on items/events.** Every projected item should probably carry a `source: 'cognitive-sensor' | 'optogon' | 'user' | 'ghost-executor'` field so the user can see what proposed it before correcting.

**Things already exist that this build wires up (don't rebuild these):**
- cognitive-sensor's clusters and leverage scoring (see `services/cognitive-sensor/cluster_leverage_map.py`, `idea_registry.json`)
- perception (`services/perception/`) and triangulation (`services/triangulation/`) — have BUILD_LOG.md files, check those first
- delta-kernel state at port 3001 with the 6-mode machine
- The 5 Rosetta Stone contracts wired in [[project-optogon-stack]] (Atlas Directive emitter, Ghost Executor consume/emit, InPACT Signal store)
- [[project-optogon-signal-ingestion]] (PR #15) and [[project-optogon-delegate-to-codex]] (PR #12) might be adjacent — open PRs at session end

**What this build is NOT:**
- Not a new tracker. Lattice is the tracker. This is the projection + write-back layer.
- Not a redesign of cognitive-sensor. Use what's already producing JSON output; map it.
- Not a UI build. The UI is done in `C:\Users\bruke\Downloads\lattice.html`.

*Description:* Groundwork plan (fest AL0002) to integrate 6 external repos into Pre Atlas; recon overturned 3 plan assumptions before any clone.

Groundwork run (2026-06-25) to integrate six external repos as a leverage layer. Plan of record is **fest festival AL0002** at `festival-project/festivals/planning/atlas-leverage-integration-AL0002/` (FESTIVAL_GOAL.md + 001_IMPLEMENT/PHASE_GOAL.md hold the full 6-sequence map; only `01_libsql_spine` is fully task-specified). One transient `test:api` red was a **harness port-race** (back-to-back driver-matrix runs), NOT a libSQL defect — 3 isolated libSQL runs 9/9 green; logged as a test-isolation follow-up. Festival 1/9 tasks (11%), still in `planning` — `fest promote` to drive the rest. Default driver stays better-sqlite3; `^0.5.29` range retained (reversible).

**Live status (gh, 2026-06-25):** all six unarchived, pushed same day, MIT/Apache-2.0 — turso 22k★ Rust, headroom 51k★ Py, codebase-memory-mcp 14.8k★ C, open-code-review 9.3k★ Go, superpowers 238k★ Shell, withastro/flue 6.7k★ TS.

3. **UGG has zero code** — vision-only (one workflow-transcript fragment: "UGG but shipped and benchmarked / the modern spine"). Decision: build UGG net-new, headroom = its CCR engine, design spike first.

**Located seams:** codebase-memory-mcp → Lattice (`apps/lattice`, cytoscape) + Ledger (`~/.claude/scripts/ledger`); open-code-review → code-recon ladder (`~/.claude/skills/code-recon/SKILL.md:24-42`, after semgrep). flue + agentfs PARKED.

**Follow-up landed (2026-06-26, `51c52cc`):** the `test:api` auth-gap surfaced under the libSQL driver-matrix run is now fixed (commit `51c52cc`), closing the test-isolation follow-up noted above.

**Next ship:** fill the other 6 sequence bodies (02_codebase_memory, 02_turso_graph_eval, 03_ugg_design_spike, 04_ugg_build, 01_superpowers_gate, 01_codereview_gate) — fresh-context, parallelizable. ## project_atlas_manifest_yaml

<a id="project-atlas-manifest-yaml"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_manifest_yaml.md`*
*Description:* atlas-manifest.yaml — single interface-first YAML map of all of Atlas, sized for LLM upload

`atlas-manifest.yaml` (repo root · ~449 lines / **~6.9k tokens**) — one compact, **interface-first** map of the entire Atlas system so it can be uploaded to an LLM (or power Atlas-as-agent) to reason about real structure without cherry-picking. Captures, per system: `provides` (route surface) · `consumes` · `requires` (auth) · `speaks` (contracts) · `stores`; plus the directed **seam graph** (21 edges), **50 contracts**, data-artifact shapes, and lifecycle flags. **Interface, not implementation** — NOT a file tree. Reads the filesystem not git, so gitignored stores (SQLite DBs, cognitive-sensor JSON dumps) are included.

**NOW GENERATOR-BACKED (2026-06-25):** regenerate deterministically with **`python audit/build_atlas_manifest.py`**. Mechanical facts (ports/routes/loc/deps/seams/contracts) re-derived every run from system-index.json + atlas-map.json + launch.json + a pure-Python route sweep; curated knowledge (what each UI asks, store shapes, security flags, contract ownership, roles, cross-repo nodes) lives in **`audit/manifest-overlay.yaml`** (edit there, not the output). Overlay model = drift only touches the derived layer. `verification.port_drift_resolved` + `contract_strays_unassigned`/`missing` auto-surface staleness. The generator already caught same-day `launch.json` drift (ws-gateway gained a `tsx index.ts` config on :3011 — collides with lattice:3011; c110-trace:8897 collides with audit-map:8897).

Provenance (all first-hand, re-runnable — see the file's PROVENANCE header): identity/loc/deps ← `audit/system-index.json`; purposes/lifecycle ← `atlas-map.json` + `REPO_RUNDOWN.md`; **runtime ports ← `.claude/launch.json` (boot truth — overrides system-index port drift: canvas-engine is :3050 not :3060, cortex :3009, optogon :3010, triangulation :3075)**; `provides` ← `rg` route sweep (paths starting `/`); seams ← `service_edges` + launch env (e.g. droplist→delta-kernel via `DROPLIST_ATLAS_SIGNALS_URL`=:3001/api/signals/ingest).

The earlier "7 inferred services" gap is **CLOSED**: the generator's no-cap sweep route-verified **18/22** systems (optogon shows real `/session/*` path-runtime endpoints, search-stack `/search|/extract|/budget`, etc.). Only `ws-gateway` is genuinely REST-less (socket.io, in `no_rest_surface`); pure stubs have none. `provides` is auto-grouped by path segment. Per-system metadata sits in `class: {role,lifecycle}` + `props: {lang,framework,port,loc,files}`. Built via [[project_groundwork_skill]]; reconciles the trusted REPO_RUNDOWN map. **Runner-up not yet built:** serve it live from atlas-map-api `GET /map/manifest` so the agent fetches current truth instead of re-uploading a file.

## project_atlas_master_plan
