*Name:* project-atlas-master-plan
*Description:* 2026-07-07 master pivot plan: Atlas to peak as human prosthetic + machine execution substrate. Live doc ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN.md at repo root + proof-gated festival atlas-pivot-AP0001 (6 phases, 17 tasks, validated 100/100).

# Atlas Master Plan + atlas-pivot festival (2026-07-07)

Bruke asked for no-code recon + a full start-to-finish plan (Greene-style grand strategy, Atomic Habits ritualization): Atlas to its peak as HIS execution prosthetic AND pivoted to an execution substrate for machines/AI. Built on [[project-atlas-completeness-assessment]] + [[project-atlas-potential-assessment]] + two fresh recon passes (machine-substrate readiness; live/dead census), load-bearing claims spot-verified at HEAD `8baf31f`.

**Artifacts:**
- `Pre Atlas\ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN.md` — the live strategy doc (diagnosis, convergence thesis, 8 Laws, 6 campaigns, guardrails, sequencing).
- Festival `C:\Users\bruke\festival-project\festivals\planning\atlas-pivot-AP0001` — 6 phases / 12 sequences / 17 evidence-cited tasks + quality gates, `fest validate` = 100/100. Phases: 001_UNLOCK (metric bugs A/B + loop drain + doc archive) → 002_RITUAL (habit loop on the push spine) → 003_LIGHTS_ON (autostart memory-hub :3071 + atlas-map-api :3072, atlas_reload cadence, drop blueprint-generator from start_atlas.ps1:25) → 004_SUBSTRATE (AGENT_SUBSTRATE.md contract, agent_id, aegis tenant keys, event-driven claims, retry doctrine) → 005_TRIAGE (census verdicts, metadata reconcile) → 006_COMPOUND (idea→queue→agent→receipt flywheel; steering metric = human-minutes per shipped item).

**Convergence thesis (the pivot):** machine substrate IS the human prosthetic — agents execute, human shrinks to 3 touchpoints (morning push, one-tap decision, REQUIRE_HUMAN approval, which is permanent by design).

**New recon findings beyond prior audits:**
- Work queue carried **53 completed jobs** (real `services/cognitive-sensor/work_ledger.json`, verified by direct read — a subagent undercounted at 4 from a stale worktree copy). Claim leases 15-min TTL + heartbeat (`work-controller.ts:246,878`); expired claims die as failed, **no requeue** (`:1004`).
- Fleet verdict: "instrumented for ONE agent, not fleets." Gateway + receipts READY; tenancy DARK but aegis tenant routes **already built** (`tenant-routes.ts:14-24` returns tenant_id+api_key); `ATLAS_USER_ID` env-default `'bruke'` (`server.ts:53`); NATS emitter real w/ graceful no-op (`event-emitter.ts`), no broker; no agent onboarding doc.
- Census: 13 LIVE services, dormant-load-bearing = cognitive-sensor front-of-spine / triangulation / ws-gateway (:3011 collision w/ lattice); DEAD = blueprint-generator (**still in start_atlas.ps1**), ai-exec-pipeline, _retired/×3; lava-layers.json stale on openclaw (says non-load-bearing, was re-wired 07-06).

**Phase 001_UNLOCK: EXECUTED 2026-07-07 (same day).** Bruke had already shipped Bugs A+B (`4408478`) and recorded CLOSE for loops 5272+3411 (00:48) before the execution session started. Execution session found+fixed a **third bug in the family**: `data/projections/today.json` (dead projection pipeline, last written **2026-01-12**) sat FIRST in `buildUnifiedState`'s mode cascade, so the served mode stayed CLOSURE for six months regardless of governor output. Fix (`3485186`): 48h staleness guard on daily directives + `cycleboard/brain/daily_payload.json` (contract-validated) as the fresh mode source + `export_daily_payload.py` now passes closure_quality into compute_mode (gate silently never applied on that path) + refresh_pipeline regenerates the payload per closure. Plus 5 regression tests (`tests/test_closure_quality.py`). **Verified live: mode BUILD / risk LOW / build_allowed TRUE, quality 100, governor alerts {}.** Doc archive done: 59 snapshots → `docs/archive/2026-07/` + INDEX.md, root at 9 .md (was 68), all reference-checked (`94852ac`, `98439a9`). Festival now in `festivals/active/`, phase 001 tasks+gates complete with seam.v1 receipts in `.audit/recon-ledger.jsonl` (cited via `.repo` junction → Pre Atlas). **10 loops deliberately left open** = Campaign I one-tap fodder (bulk-archiving would re-poison quality to 20%). Governor confrontation now targets Trinity OPS (375d) — working as designed.

**Next action:** phase 002_RITUAL (seed idea+loop into 06:00 push; evening wins push; RECOVER-aware seeding) + 003_LIGHTS_ON can run parallel.

**Concurrent-writer caveat:** during execution, a parallel session committed my staged code as `3485186` under its own message minutes before my commit — on this repo, always `git log` immediately before committing and never assume the index is yours alone.

**Fest CLI gotchas learned:** `fest create task` hangs headless without `--skip-markers`; `fest create sequence` hangs without `--json`; `fest task completed` needs `Write-Output "Y" |` piped (no --yes flag); its recon quality-gate demands `.audit/recon-ledger.jsonl` with seam.v1 receipts whose `file_line` resolves from the FESTIVAL dir (use a `.repo` junction to the real repo) and whose cited files are older than `produced_at`; `fest promote` ALWAYS fails on native Windows (self-blocks: own cwd locks the rename — task chip filed; workaround = Move-Item the festival dir + append status_history in fest.yaml).

## project_atlas_physical_leaves

<a id="project-atlas-physical-leaves"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_physical_leaves.md`*
*Name:* project-atlas-physical-leaves
*Description:* Atlas isn't 'done' all at once — it's done one verb at a time. Only @WORK and @BUILD are physical today; @SEND_DRAFT and @CLOSE_LOOP lie. Smallest next: ntfy.sh swap.

**The shift (2026-06-30):** Bruke's question — *"what makes Atlas done bc I want to see it actually moving like, what is physically and tangibly getting done in the real world."* That broke the abstraction. Atlas isn't "done" as one big bang. It's done **verb by verb**: each `@TOKEN` in `executor-bridge.ts:18-26` is its own "does this leaf actually bite the world?" question. The conversation that led here started from the wrong frame: we kept naming/analogizing (Gemini's ATM doc → trading bot metaphor → "shape of a car") instead of inspecting leaves. Once we did, the gap surfaced in 4 file reads.

**The 5 ActionType → @TOKEN paths and what they ACTUALLY do today** (read 2026-06-30 at HEAD):

| Token | Profile | Reality | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `@SEND_DRAFT` | [SEND_DRAFT_v1.json](services/uasc-executor/profiles/SEND_DRAFT_v1.json) | 3 `type: "log"` steps. Logs the text. **Sends nothing.** | ❌ journal only |
| `@CLOSE_LOOP` | [CLOSE_LOOP_v1.json](services/uasc-executor/profiles/CLOSE_LOOP_v1.json) | One HTTP PUT to `localhost:3001/api/tasks/{id}` marking DONE. **In-system only** — no GitHub close, no calendar, no external mirror. | ❌ in-Atlas only |
| `@WORK` | [WORK_v1.json](services/uasc-executor/profiles/WORK_v1.json) | `code .` opens VS Code · `start chrome --new-window` opens Chrome · fake DnD echo. | ✅ **opens apps on screen** |
| `@BUILD` | [BUILD_v1.json](services/uasc-executor/profiles/BUILD_v1.json) | `npm install` · `npm run build` · `npm test` real shell. | ✅ **builds on disk** |
| `@DEPLOY` | DEPLOY_v1.json | not read this session; per pattern likely shell. | ⚠️ unverified |

**The honest "done" criterion:** Atlas is a journal that names what should happen. It becomes a **doer** verb by verb, at the leaf. Pipeline (gate → token → executor → step) is correct; **the leaves lie about being physical.** The whole rest of the engine (modes, gates, deltas, sync, log, FSM, 22 systems) pays for itself the moment ONE leaf bites the world.

**THE SMALLEST NEXT MOVE (do this first in fresh chat — 15 min):** Replace SEND_DRAFT_v1.json's 3 `type: "log"` steps with ONE `type: "http"` step that POSTs to **ntfy.sh** (free, no API key, push notifications to phone). Test: fire from delta-kernel via cockpit confirm → phone buzzes → Atlas has physically moved something for the first time. Bigger swaps after (Resend for email, Twilio for SMS, Slack webhook). Same shape unlocks `@CLOSE_LOOP` external mirror (GitHub close, calendar event).

**Context this came from / what NOT to redo:**
- We did NOT lose the plot to build a trading bot — that was the user's *recognition of the engine's shape*, mistakenly built literally by me. Trading-probe fest TP0001 is PARKED, not the move. See [[project_trading_probe]].
- Re-read of Gemini ATM map (479 lines) confirmed: ATM is a fully-spec'd dream; delta-kernel is "ATM-minus-the-hardware, running solo." The trading-architecture analogy maps cleanly to ATM's *engine* layer, NOT the hardware/mesh layer. But that's still abstraction — the physical-leaves question cuts past it entirely.
- The 13-box parts list of delta-kernel was the moment we stopped analogizing. Source-grounded in `services/delta-kernel/src/core/` exports — atoms (types-core.ts) · engine (delta.ts) · brain (routing.ts) · gate (cockpit.ts) · speculate (preparation.ts) · compress (dictionary.ts) · generate (ai-design.ts) · sync (delta-sync.ts) · log (timeline-logger.ts) · act (executor-bridge.ts) · project (daily-screen.ts) · surfaces (api/server.ts) · enforce (governance_daemon.ts).
- 23,200 LOC total across `services/delta-kernel/src`; 22 core .ts modules; biggest are api/server.ts (3157) + cli/atlas-ai.ts (1582) + cli/atlas.ts (1557).

**The deeper feedback this names:** Bruke pushed back that we'd been "stringing concepts and what makes sense but didn't turn it into an actual plan" — like describing a car by analogy to a buggy when cars already exist. Rule reinforced: assemble-first + skip the analogy layer when there's a real bill of materials to make. See [[feedback_assemble_first_posture]].

Extends [[project_atm_vision_delta_kernel_lineage]], [[project_atlas_atm_uasc_synthesis]], [[project_atlas_headless_map]].

## project_atlas_potential_assessment

<a id="project-atlas-potential-assessment"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_potential_assessment.md`*
*Name:* project-atlas-potential-assessment
*Description:* Fable 5 strategic assessment 2026-07-07: what would move Atlas toward its potential. Verdict: the mode-lock is a solvable arithmetic bug, the goldmine already exists unmined, and the proactive spine went live this week — three ordered moves, no new architecture.

# Atlas Potential Assessment — 2026-07-07

Read-only investigation. Every claim below was verified first-hand against live state (running `atlas-ai state/next/directive`, querying `results.db` directly, reading handler source at HEAD `8baf31f`), not taken from docs. Companion to [[project_atlas_completeness_assessment]] (2026-07-06) — this extends it, does not repeat it.

Bruke's own definition of potential, verbatim, gathered mid-investigation:
- "use cognitive sensor as a goldmine"
- "a digital version of me based on my conversation histories"
- "rehash all my old business ideas"
- "sort through all my loops"
- "my Dexter's Lab, my Tony Stark's JARVIS, my Batman's Alfred"
- "my executional executive-functioning prosthetic for my neurodivergence. I want it to have my day ready for me"
- "all my projects/files/activity tracked; capture my highs and sustain me if not uplift my lows"

---

## 1. Verified structure snapshot (2026-07-07, ~00:30)

**Live now:** delta-kernel :3001 (auth-gated, responding — governance daemon crons active inside it), aegis-fabric :3002, openclaw :3004, inpact :3006, code-to-numeric :3007, uasc-executor :3008, cortex :3009, optogon :3010, canvas-engine :3050.

**Down:** atlas-map-api :3072 and delta-scp :3012.
- delta-scp is a **red herring** — it lives in the `pre-atlas` (hyphen) repo and serves repo-compression jobs, not the Atlas control loop.
- atlas-map-api being down is **half a red herring**: the `atlas-map` MCP still answered `atlas_status` in this session (the MCP path works without :3072), so the CLAUDE.md front door holds for agents. But the map data it served is **stale and self-contradictory**: `generated_at: 2026-06-24`, `openclaw` listed in both `autostart` and `retired`, aegis-fabric ported as :3010 (that's optogon's port; aegis is :3002). The #1-preferred front door serves a 13-day-old map of a repo that changed 38+ commits since. `atlas_reload` exists; nothing calls it on a cadence.

**The "10 live surfaces" framing in CLAUDE.md is directionally right** but the map underneath reports 49 subsystems and disagrees with itself about which are retired. Same-day doc drift confirmed elsewhere too: CLAUDE.md still says `OPTOGON_SIGNAL_EMIT` is off by default, while `09d64fb` (07-06) set it =1 in `.claude/launch.json` + `services/optogon/start.bat` — already flagged as risk 5 in `BRANCH_CONSOLIDATION_PLAN_2026-07-06.md`.

## 2. The mode-lock forensics — this is the load-bearing finding

Atlas's live self-assessment (rerun and confirmed this session): mode **CLOSURE**, risk HIGH, `build_allowed: false`, 15 open loops, `closure_quality: 0`, drift 10, three drift alerts (closure drought 15d, archive gaming 15d, mode stagnation 14d). `next` says `close 3411`. Governor confrontation: "'AI Workflow Orchestration' has been open 488 days. Ship it this week or kill it permanently."

I traced every number to its source. What I found is not a discipline problem — it is **three ledger bugs stacked**, and the confrontation is literally unsatisfiable as built:

**Bug A — the metric is mathematically unrecoverable.**
`closure_quality` = CLOSE / (CLOSE + ARCHIVE), all-time, from `loop_decisions` in `services/cognitive-sensor/results.db` (`cognitive_api.py:36-45`). That table contains exactly **145 rows, all ARCHIVE, all recorded in one night (2026-06-21 23:09 + 23:22)** — a bulk triage session. Zero CLOSE rows have ever existed. `atlas_config.py:340`: quality < 30 forces CLOSURE/HIGH/build-locked. To reach 30% against a 145-archive denominator requires **63 consecutive CLOSE decisions**. Closing every currently-open loop (15) yields 15/160 = **9.4% — still locked**. The one bulk-archive night permanently poisoned the all-time denominator; no achievable behavior escapes. The "15 days" in the drought alerts is exactly the time since that night — the drought clock started the moment the archives were recorded.

**Bug B — the governor's own recommended command doesn't move the governor's own metric.**
`atlas-ai close 3411` POSTs `/api/law/close_loop` (`atlas-ai.ts:340`). That handler (`server.ts:1253-1443`) writes `closures.json`, removes from `loops_latest.json`, appends `loops_closed.json`, updates streaks — and **never inserts into `loop_decisions`**. The Python governor computes quality *only* from `loop_decisions`. So the TS closure ledger and the Python decision ledger are disconnected: obeying `next` verbatim would leave `closure_quality` at 0 forever. Only the Python path (`close_loop.py:229`, wrapped by the new `loop_clearer.py`) writes the table the mode gate reads.

**Bug C — the loop lists themselves are fragmented across generations.**
Three artifacts disagree: governor says 15 open loops (old-index `semantic_loops.json`: ids 266, 1356, 143...), `cognitive_state.json` carries 5 (new-index ids: 3411, 5272, 3598, 1614, 2344), `closure.open` says 12. Two id-generations coexist: "AI Workflow Orchestration" is id **143** in the old index and id **5272** in the new one. All sources agree on the two facts that matter (quality = 0, mode = CLOSURE), but any per-loop operation must know which index it's talking to.

**The punchline:** 12 of the 15 governor-counted loops (1075, 1136, 1207, 1209, 1222, 1247, 1356, 143, 266, 430, 546, 889) **already have completed deep-analysis extraction docs in `services/cognitive-sensor/extractions/`** — the intellectual work of closing them was already done; the decision was simply never written back. The closure drought is, in miniature, the same disease the completeness audit named at system level: work happens, state doesn't update, the governor keeps punishing.

### Loop verdicts (deliverable, with evidence)

**Loop 5272 / old-143 — "AI Workflow Orchestration", 488 days.** Conversation dated 2025-03-05 in `convo_time` (488 days before 07-06 — governor math checks out). 708 messages / 364 user turns. `extractions/143_ai_workflow_orchestration.md` already analyzed it: *"This IS the Atlas vision in conversational form — the entire cognitive-sensor → governance → execution pipeline described before it was built."* Status line in that doc: **CLOSED — proto-Atlas derivative, frameworks extracted.** The thing this loop asked for **is the repo itself**. Verdict: **CLOSE as shipped** — record decision CLOSE via loop_clearer, artifact = the extraction doc + the repo. Do not archive it; it is the single most legitimately closable loop in the system, and the governor has been demanding a decision on an already-decided item for 15 days because nobody wrote the row.

**Loop 3411 — "CUBECORE64 Project Initialization", top-scored (43,422), conversation dated 2025-07-13 (~359 days).** No extraction doc exists. Repo-wide grep for CUBECORE artifacts: the only hits are four ChatGPT conversation *titles* in exported conversation data ("Project Initialization", "CMake Setup", "AI Builder Protocol", "Project Overview") — he talked about it across four sessions, and it never became code, a file, or a project anywhere in Pre Atlas. IDEA_REGISTRY carries three CUBECORE64 rows (statuses "started"/"idea"/"completed" — internally contradictory, low priority 0.17-0.27). Verdict: **kill permanently — but record it as CLOSE, not ARCHIVE.** A deliberate, recorded kill is a resolution ("ship it OR kill it" — the governor itself offers kill as valid closure); routing kills through ARCHIVE is what created the archive-gaming trap. Bruke should make this call in loop_clearer with 30 seconds of looking at the conversation; the evidence says nothing was ever built.

**Remaining:** 3598 (Trinity OPS), 1614 (System Reconstruction), 2344 (JSON patch) in the new index; 81, 715, 717 lack extractions in the old. One loop_clearer session covers them.

**loop_clearer.py is the right tool and it shipped last night** (`54206ef`, 2026-07-06 23:36, 433 lines + working screenshot): steerable UI wrapping the existing `get_open_loops`/`record_decision`/`refresh_pipeline` path — the exact path that writes the metric the gate reads. Note its default recommendation is "No signal. → ARCHIVE" (`loop_clearer.py:95`); given Bug A, every archive digs the hole deeper. Use its CLOSE button deliberately.

## 3. Vision vs. reality: the 6-change plan is essentially SHIPPED

Cross-referenced the completeness audit's plan against git. All six changes have landed, five of them within 07-06 alone:

| # | Change | Status | Evidence |
|---|--------|--------|----------|
| 1 | Daemon seeds day into inPACT + phone push | **SHIPPED** | `221a888`; verified live in source: `governance_daemon.ts:149` (06:00 cron), `:464` `seedTodayPlan()`, `:528` `notifyPhone('Today seeded', ...)`, idempotent (`if (daily[date]) return`) |
| 2 | p1-p3 → real Task entities + Done buttons + handle-next on Home | **SHIPPED** | `47c11dd` |
| 3 | Front-door doctrine (CLAUDE.md) | **SHIPPED** | `221a888` |
| 4 | Schedule sensor daily + `OPTOGON_SIGNAL_EMIT=1` | **SHIPPED** | `09d64fb` (sensor_daily cron 05:45, 15-min timeout) |
| 5 | Prepared work → pending_action (notify/confirm tiers) | **SHIPPED** | `f6a0c61` (Bruke himself), hardened `450d8f0` (noOverlap on all 10 crons + per-action isolation), timeout fix `0633505`, tests `7880517` |
| 6 | Optogon signals ↔ confirm loop | **SUBSTANTIALLY SHIPPED** | `42487e6` (resolved approval_required forwarded back into Optogon session turns), `c1d8981` (openclaw `/pending`), `4e12bfe` (openclaw `/approve` → confirmation gate), `ffbb050` (inPACT confirm banner), `4b82016` (auto-tier completions → closure feedback + UASC audit). Remaining: Optogon must actually be running for auto_triage volume (noted in `09d64fb`'s open note) — it is running right now (:3010) |

Plus unplanned wins: `de4dd81` (task completion → real phone push), `ddad44a` (aegis-fabric approve actually executes), `10643bd` (UASC execution daemon actually runs). **The wiring week the audit estimated at ~1 week happened in ~3 days.** The vision/reality gap the audit named — "museum of working parts" — is closing fast on the *plumbing* side. What has NOT moved: the closure-metric arithmetic (§2), and the goldmine (§4).

## 4. The five vision threads, audited

**The archetype test first.** What Dexter's Lab / JARVIS / Alfred share: private, always-on, **proactive** (it speaks first), knows you from your history, surfaces the right thing unprompted, handles logistics quietly. Against that bar, Atlas today: private ✓, always-on ✓ (9 services), knows-your-history ◐ (the data exists; almost nothing reads it back), proactive ◐ (**genuinely new this week** — 06:00 seeded-day push, pending-action pushes, task-completion pushes all went live 07-04→07-06), handles-logistics-quietly ✗ (execution paths exist but carry near-zero traffic). The honest one-line gap: **Atlas has a JARVIS-grade memory sitting in SQLite and a JARVIS-grade push spine that is 72 hours old, and the two have never been introduced.**

**The prosthetic test** ("does using it require the executive function it's supposed to replace?"): until this week, almost everything was pull — `morning`, `wrap`, `day create`, dashboards, `next` all require *remembering to ask*, which is precisely the failure mode for an executive-function prosthetic. As of `221a888` the single most important flow flipped: **the day arrives seeded, by cron, with a phone push, without initiation.** That is the correct shape. It is currently the *only* flow with that shape. Everything below should ride the same spine.

**Thread 1 — "cognitive-sensor as a goldmine": the goldmine exists and is unmined.**
`results.db`: **6,534 conversations, 533,286 messages, 258,777 message embeddings already computed.** `IDEA_REGISTRY.md`: **1,335 ideas**, tiered (7 Execute Now / 116 Next Up / 907 Backlog / 305 Archive), priority-scored with dependency edges and "gateway ideas that unlock 72 others" — **regenerated daily by the daemon** (`e7b3926` is daemon output, not a one-off). The governor already reads its #1 ("Venture Portfolio Architecture" appears in `top_decision`). But nothing pushes ideas to Bruke, no API serves the registry, and no session can query it without knowing the file path. It is a goldmine with the ore stacked at the entrance.

**Thread 2 — "digital version of me": substrate present, retrieval BUILT but dark.**
The corpus above + 999 Claude Code session JSONLs indexed in `cc-session-index.md` (853 MB — index is 5+ weeks stale, and nothing mines the transcripts) + pre-Atlas parser outputs per [[reference_chatgpt_conversations]]. And the retrieval layer already exists: **memory-hub (:3071) per its own README exposes `POST /search` that routes across cognitive-sensor's embedding cluster search (`atlas_query.py`), the idea registry, and DropList packets** — "ask my history" as a merged REST query. It is simply **not running** (:3071 dark right now, not in the live-service set) and nothing calls it on a cadence. The digital twin's v1 is a service start + autostart entry, not a build.

**Thread 3 — "rehash old business ideas": already built, sitting inert.**
`BUSINESS_IDEAS_CLUSTERED.md` (79 conversations clustered), the idea registry's tiers, `CAPSULES.md` (86 branches → ~17 capsules of unmerged build-intent, explicitly framed as "open loops with a record"). Rehashing needs zero extraction work — it needs a **delivery mechanism** (one idea/day into the morning push) and a **decision loop** (promote / park / kill, like loop_clearer but for ideas). Note the registry's own moratorium: `auto_park_threshold: 0.0` — every new idea auto-parks until a lane opens, which loops back to §2: lanes open when things close.

**Thread 4 — "sort through all my loops": §2 IS this thread.** The tool shipped last night; the metric bug is the blocker; the verdicts for the top two loops are above.

**Thread 5 — "projects/files/activity tracked": mostly exists, split across three systems nobody reads together.**
- Item backbone: `GET /items`, `POST /items/{id}/status`, `GET /items/{id}/workflow` are **live code in atlas-map-api** (`server.py:507,520,546`) unifying droplist/cycleboard/inpact/festival — but atlas-map-api is the one core service **not running**, so the unified view is dark right now. No push, no adjusted seeding, no supportive content. The 6-mode ladder treats lows as a *gate* (what you're not allowed to do) — never as a *trigger* (what the system does FOR you). The notification spine to do better shipped this week and is unused for this.

## 5. THE RECOMMENDATION — three moves, strictly ordered, all assembly

**Move 1 (today, ~2-3 hours total): make closure arithmetically possible, then run the drain.**
This is the answer to "is clearing the drought actually the top move?" — **yes, and it is not in competition with the bigger vision; it is its gate.** `build_allowed: false` is the governor's veto on every other ambition, the idea-reservoir moratorium only lifts when lanes open, and "sort through all my loops" is Bruke's own stated want. But do it in this order or it fails:
- **1a. Fix Bug A** — window `closure_quality` (last-30-days or last-N-decisions) in `cognitive_api.py:36-45`, or reclassify the 2026-06-21 bulk-archive rows as a distinct `TRIAGE` decision type excluded from the quality denominator. It was one triage night, not 145 considered decisions; as built, it locks BUILD mode forever (63 CLOSEs needed to counterweigh it). Without 1a, running loop_clearer perfectly still leaves quality ≤9.4% and the mode locked — Bruke would do the right thing and the system would tell him it didn't count, which is the exact "harassment" dynamic that made him stop using inPACT.
- **1b. Fix Bug B** — one INSERT: `/api/law/close_loop` (server.ts:1253) also writes a `loop_decisions` row, so the CLI/API closure path and the Python metric agree. Two ledgers, one truth.
- **1c. Run loop_clearer** (it shipped last night) over the open loops. CLOSE 5272/"AI Workflow Orchestration" as shipped (artifact: the repo + `extractions/143_...md`). Kill 3411/CUBECORE64 permanently, recorded as CLOSE-with-kill-outcome. Use the 12 existing extraction docs as ready-made closure artifacts for the old-index loops. Expected end state: mode exits CLOSURE, `build_allowed: true`, all three drift alerts clear, and the governor's 488-day confrontation is finally answered.

**Move 2 (this week, ~1 day): point the 72-hour-old proactive spine at the goldmine.**
Extend `seedTodayPlan`/the morning push (governance_daemon.ts:464-528) to carry, alongside the top-3 tasks: **one Execute-Now idea from IDEA_REGISTRY** (rotating; 1,335 ranked ideas currently have zero delivery mechanism) and, while any remain, **one open loop with its extraction summary and a one-tap CLOSE/ARCHIVE** (the pending-action confirm path from `f6a0c61`/`4e12bfe` already does one-tap decisions through openclaw and inPACT — reuse it, don't build). This single edit converts three of Bruke's five wishes (goldmine, rehash ideas, day-ready) from "data exists" to "arrives at 06:00 without me asking." It is the prosthetic shape applied to what already exists. Also in this move, two 15-minute patches: add atlas-map-api :3072 to autostart (the items backbone and the freshest map shouldn't be dark), and schedule `atlas_reload` after the nightly manifest regen so the front door stops serving a June map.

**Move 3 (next, ~1-2 days, only after 1-2): light up "ask my history" — the digital-twin seam.**
memory-hub (:3071) **already implements this**: its README documents `POST /search` merging cognitive-sensor embedding search (`atlas_query.py`), the idea registry, and DropList packets. It is dark. Move 3 = start it, add to autostart, confirm it's reachable via `atlas_call(memory-hub, search, ...)`, and let the morning brief + any agent session query 6,534 conversations of Bruke-history. Then wire the cheapest highs/lows response on the same spine: `win` triggers an evening "3 wins today" push; RECOVER/low-energy mornings seed a lighter day + one past win from MomentumWins instead of a full task load. This is the JARVIS voice — same push spine, now speaking from his own history. Almost pure assembly: the embeddings are computed, the search service is written; what's missing is the on-switch and two small daemon hooks.

**What NOT to do:** no new services, no new UI surfaces, no schema rewrites, no starting Move 2/3 while build_allowed is false — that would be building the 21st capability of a system whose governor is begging for closure. The completeness audit's plumbing plan is done; this is the second story on that foundation.

## project_atlas_rag_context_engine

<a id="project-atlas-rag-context-engine"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_rag_context_engine.md`*
*Name:* project-atlas-rag-context-engine
*Description:* Docker-free local RAG over the Pre Atlas corpus (atlas-rag), built 2026-07-22 to escape a Docker/WSL crash loop; plus verified OpenMetadata research and a PENDING context-engine direction decision.

## What got built (DONE, verified 2026-07-22)
`C:\Users\bruke\atlas-rag\atlas_rag.py` — single-file local RAG, **no Docker**. One dep (numpy) + native Ollama (stdlib urllib, no ollama client dep).
- Corpus: memory/ (247) + `~/.claude/rules/` (31, recursive) + Pre Atlas CLAUDE.md = 279 files -> 1375 chunks, ingest in ~18s, **zero failures**.
- Embeddings: `nomic-embed-text` (native Ollama). Answer model: `CHAT_MODEL` = `llama3.2` (fast on CPU); swap to `qwen2.5:7b` for richer/slower. LLM layer is one-line swappable = "lose Claude, keep going" insurance.
- Commands: `python atlas_rag.py ingest` | `"question"` (cited answer) | `--search "q"` (instant, no LLM, raw chunks).
- Store: `index.npz` (~4MB) + `index_meta.json`. Citation label = `parentdir/filename` (disambiguates the 4 `coding-style.md`).
- Fixed inline (code-as-furniture): Windows cp1252 crash on `→` -> `sys.stdout.reconfigure(utf-8)`; oversized/dense chunks -> conservative 1200-char packing + `truncate:true`.
- Proof: retrieved `common/code-as-furniture.md` (0.742) and answered origin+triggers correctly; RAGFlow gave same on Docker, this gives it Docker-free.

## Root cause of the Docker crashes (the whole reason for the pivot)
Running **OpenMetadata + RAGFlow simultaneously on 16GB** = TWO Elasticsearch instances + Airflow + 2 DBs + MinIO/Redis. RAGFlow alone already used 8.7GB of a 10GB WSL cap. Overcommit -> WSL2 VM wedged -> `no route to host` / `docker ps` hangs. NOT a Docker bug or user error — a hard resource ceiling. A reboot clears the wedged WSL. `.wslconfig` now sets `memory=10GB` (was unset = 50% default).

## OpenMetadata — VERIFIED research (Bruke was right, I under-researched it first)
- Installed at `C:\Users\bruke\openmetadata-install\` (has `docker\docker-compose.yml`).
- **Has a first-party MCP server** (SearchMetadataTool, GetLineageTool, GlossaryTool, DatabaseTool). Rebranded "The Open Context Layer for Data and AI ... for humans, AI assistants, and agents" — literally Bruke's context-engine phrasing. NOT the dusty DB catalog I first framed it as.
- BUT: **no out-of-box markdown/folder connector.** Cataloging the Pre Atlas corpus needs a CUSTOM connector (JSON schema + YAML + code). 130+ connectors exist (incl. "documents") but none point-and-go for a note folder.
- Docker-heavy: own ES + Postgres + Airflow + server; 4GB RAM min, ES 8GB prod. Can't coexist with RAGFlow on 16GB.

## RAGFlow (the other stack, still installed)
`C:\Users\bruke\ragflow`, v0.26.4, full Docker stack. A dataset `pre-atlas-knowledge` (274 docs, 794 chunks) + chat assistant `pre-atlas-knowledge-chat` were built + retrieval-verified there too — but it's the Docker path the Atlas RAG replaces. API key + curl workflow used (create key in UI -> `Authorization: Bearer ragflow-...`). Ollama must bind `OLLAMA_HOST=0.0.0.0:11434` for containers to reach it.

## DECIDED SEQUENCE (2026-07-22): Sourcegraph FIRST -> OpenMetadata SECOND -> "everything else is fine"
Bruke's plan: the context-engine layers get added in order — code index (Sourcegraph) first, then data/metadata catalog (OpenMetadata). The Atlas RAG + RAGFlow are considered settled ("everything else is fine"); no more work wanted there for now.

### STATUS 2026-07-22 PM: BOTH context-engine halves now LIVE as Docker-free MCP tools
- **Notes half:** `atlas-rag` MCP (`atlas_search`/`atlas_ask`) — corpus (memory+rules+CLAUDE.md). DONE (this file).
- **Code half:** `zoekt` MCP (`zoekt_search`/`zoekt_status`) — Bruke ported zoekt WSL->Windows-native himself; indexes 3 shards (Pre Atlas 200MB + pre-atlas/delta-scp 47MB + STRUDEL 111MB = 349MB), ctags symbol ranking (`sym:` verified: `sym:WorkController` -> exact `export class` defs in both Pre Atlas repos). See [[project_zoekt_windows_native]]. This IS the "Sourcegraph first" step, done via the Docker-free zoekt path below (NOT the Enterprise server).
Together = the context engine Bruke described: deterministic code+notes indexes, LLM (Claude/Ollama) reasons over them, zero Docker.

### Sourcegraph — VERIFIED (do it WITHOUT repeating the Docker crash)
- Sourcegraph **has an MCP server** (cross-repo search/navigation/history/Deep Search for agents) — BUT **Enterprise-plan only** + needs a running instance (Docker server or their cloud). = paid + Docker/cloud. The attractive MCP piece is the expensive piece.
- **zoekt** (Sourcegraph's engine) = standalone Go binary, Docker-free CLI index+search, free — but NO MCP on its own.
- **Cody** = IDE extension, consumes MCP tools (client, not server), free tier needs sourcegraph.com cloud account.
- **Docker-free path that fits the vision:** zoekt standalone (or existing ripgrep/ast-grep/ctags from code-recon) wrapped in a small custom MCP server -> "Claude queries my code as a tool", no Docker, no Enterprise license. Same pattern as the pending RAG-as-MCP idea. Full Sourcegraph server only worth its Docker weight for the web UI + Deep Search + hosted Cody.

### OpenMetadata — (second) verified findings unchanged (see above section): has first-party MCP, "Open Context Layer", but no out-of-box markdown connector (custom build) + Docker-heavy (own ES+Postgres+Airflow). Sequenced after Sourcegraph.

### RAG-as-MCP — DONE (2026-07-22)
`C:\Users\bruke\atlas-rag\atlas_mcp.py` — FastMCP (official `mcp` SDK) stdio server wrapping `atlas_rag.retrieve()`. Two tools: `atlas_search(query,k)` (raw scored chunks; Claude reasons over them) + `atlas_ask(query)` (local-Ollama cited answer). Reuses the CLI's numpy index — no Docker. Smoke-tested end-to-end via `_smoke_mcp.py` (stdio handshake -> list tools -> call; returned code-as-furniture.md @0.717). **Registered at USER scope** (`claude mcp add atlas-rag -s user -- C:\Python313\python.exe ...\atlas_mcp.py`; written to `~/.claude.json`); `claude mcp list` = ✔ Connected. Note: new servers load at session START, so the tools `mcp__atlas-rag__atlas_search|atlas_ask` appear in the NEXT session, not the one that registered it. This is the headline OpenMetadata/Sourcegraph-MCP benefit delivered Docker-free over the corpus.

**NEXT when resumed:** ~~start Sourcegraph via the Docker-free zoekt path~~ **DONE 2026-07-22** — zoekt is now a native Windows binary (no WSL/Docker), 3-file port, index+search proven, on PATH. See [[project_zoekt_windows_native]]. Remaining: wrap zoekt as an MCP server (Python) like `atlas_mcp.py`. (reboot to clear wedged WSL still applies only if you go back to the OpenMetadata/Docker second step.) The Atlas RAG is the banked, working win. Aligns with [[project_ai_coding_procedure_origin]] externalization principle + [[feedback_narrative_is_the_moat_not_the_determinism]]. Verify-before-verdict fired correctly twice: [[feedback_argued_from_priors_not_evidence]] — mischaracterized OpenMetadata from priors (Bruke's "they have MCP" push was right), then verified Sourcegraph's MCP is Enterprise-gated before recommending.

## project_atlas_setup_profile

<a id="project-atlas-setup-profile"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atlas_setup_profile.md`*
*Name:* project-atlas-setup-profile
*Description:* Headless one-profile setup system that fans routines + A/B/C day types out to all behavioral surfaces via the delta-kernel cycleboard hub

**Atlas Setup — single behavioral profile → all surfaces (SHIPPED 2026-06-25, uncommitted)**

Built to kill the manual per-surface setup and the "bs placeholder data that doesn't apply to my actual life." Lives in `setup/` (new top-level folder):
- `atlas_profile.json` — the active single source of truth (seeded from old `apps/inpact/js/state.js` defaults so nothing regressed; mission/motto left blank for Bruke to fill).
- `atlas_profile.template.json` — blank canvas for a new user / shipping the product.
- `atlas_profile.schema.json` — draft-07 contract.
- `apply.py` — stdlib-only headless fan-out engine (no pip install). Validates → maps snake_case profile to the app's camelCase keys → GET/merge/PUT.
- `test_apply.py` — 18 pytest, all green.
- `setup.ps1` — turnkey: `start_atlas.ps1` then `apply.py`.

**The load-bearing insight:** every behavioral surface reads ONE blob — the delta-kernel cycleboard state at `PUT /api/cycleboard` (:3001). cortex reads day-type templates straight from it ([orchestrator.py:67-74](services/cortex/src/cortex/inpact/orchestrator.py)); inPACT app+CLI and the atlas/atlas-ai CLIs read the same. So one write propagates everywhere — NOT 35 integrations.

**Gotchas discovered (verify, don't trust recon):**
- delta-kernel `/api/*` requires `Authorization: Bearer <key>` from `.aegis-tenant-key` (recon wrongly said GET was open). `apply.py` fetches the token via the exempt `GET /api/auth/token`, same as the browser. Dev mode (no key file) = no auth.
- The cycleboard blob is NESTED: PUT body becomes `entity.state.data`, so GET returns `{ok, data:{data:<blob>}}`. Canonical blob = `response.data.data`. `extract_blob()` unwraps it and strips stray `data` keys (an early bug double-nested it; the unwrap self-heals on re-apply).

**Behavior:** normal apply overwrites only template keys (Routine, DayTypeTemplates, FocusArea, Settings, AZTask, Today.mission/motto) and PRESERVES runtime (DayPlans/Journal/History/MomentumWins/etc). `--reset` wipes runtime too (blank canvas). Idempotent — verified stable across 4 applies. Sets `onboardingDone=true` so the inPACT wizard never re-prompts.

Relates to [[project_inpact_onboarding_ai_assistant]] (the wizard Bruke had experimented with — now superseded for setup) and [[project_self_describing_surfaces]] (the 35-surface registry).

**Original want (Bruke, verbatim 2026-06-25):** *"a ui i can click on and whatever llm i choose or api i put or whatever can set me up... or i can manually type in whatever and get my shit started frfr."*

**The want (Bruke, verbatim 2026-06-25):** *"a ui i can click on and whatever llm i choose or api i put or whatever can set me up... or i can manually type in whatever and get my shit started frfr."*

So: a **clickable setup/boot page** (single-file, lattice/inpact style) where you
(1) pick an LLM provider + paste an API key, OR (2) type freeform what you want,
hit a button, and **Atlas services start**. "Setup" = get Atlas running your way,
no CLI. NOT an onboarding-config-knobs page and NOT an item control surface — it's
**boot/launch**.

**Assemble-first reality — the backend is ~80% there (verified in atlas-map-api this session):**
- `POST /map/launch/{name}` ([server.py:418](services/atlas-map-api/src/atlas_map_api/server.py)) — start any `launch.json` entry by NAME (lattice, cycleboard, droplist-ui, hydra, …), idempotent.
- `POST /map/start|stop|restart` — drive the ported subsystems; token-guarded (`X-Atlas-Token`).
- `GET /map` + `.claude/launch.json` — the list of everything startable.
- So this is mostly a **frontend wire**, not net-new backend.

## project_atm_vision_delta_kernel_lineage

<a id="project-atm-vision-delta-kernel-lineage"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_atm_vision_delta_kernel_lineage.md`*
*Name:* atm-vision-delta-kernel-lineage
*Description:* delta-kernel is the scoped-down build of a larger decentralized-network vision (\"ATM\"); the ATM was the original target Bruke couldn't build solo

A long Gemini conversation (exported as `Canceled Conversation export.txt`, reverse-organized this session into repo-root `GEMINI_ATM_TRANSCRIPT.md` + `GEMINI_ATM_MAP.md`, both uncommitted) designs a decentralized, time-based content network nicknamed the **ATM (Asynchronous Temporal Mesh)**: a super-server ("The Sun") does all heavy compute/forecasting; commuters ("Data Mules") sneakernet compressed "seeds" into dead zones over LoRa/BLE mesh; phones are stateless "clocks" replaying an append-only ledger ("The Sundial") onto a headless runtime ("The Ghost Canvas"); system trends to homeostasis so tiny pings carry large meaning. Spec is a 5-phase / 21-part adversarial review (event sourcing, speculative orchestration, CRDT reconciliation, INR/DyNCA rendering, neuromorphic vision, aBFT/TOTP/TRNG security).

**KEY FACT (Bruke, 2026-06-28):** delta-kernel is **not a coincidental cousin** of the ATM — it **IS the ATM, scoped down**. Bruke wanted to build the full ATM, couldn't do it solo (the hardware/coordination wall: the Sun, city-scale LoRa mesh, neuromorphic cameras), so he built its *engine* — the event-sourced, hash-chained, LoRa-safe, deterministic-replay substrate — and aimed it at a single-node problem he could ship (personal behavioral governance) instead of a town's content network. "i actually couldnt build this system before so i just built that instead."

**Why:** delta-kernel's design choices are inherited from the ATM target, not arbitrary — [delta.ts](services/delta-kernel/src/core/delta.ts) append-only hash-chained deltas = the Sundial/Part 2.1; [delta-sync.ts:48](services/delta-kernel/src/core/delta-sync.ts) `DEFAULT_MAX_PACKET_BYTES = 220 // LoRa-safe` = Phase 1 transport; "No blobs / deterministic conflict resolution" = pass-keys-not-blobs + Part 2.3. The software was never the wall; the hardware/human-coordination layer is.

**How to apply:** treat the ATM map as the parent design and delta-kernel as ATM-minus-the-hardware running solo. If Bruke wants to extend delta-kernel, the ATM's 21 parts are the latent roadmap (which parts are shipped on one node vs. still need the multi-person/hardware layer). Don't reintroduce the "coincidental overlap" framing — it's deliberate lineage. Relates to [[project_libsql_spine_decision]] (delta-kernel DB spine) and the assemble-first posture [[feedback_assemble_first_posture]].

## project_bearings_external_package

<a id="project-bearings-external-package"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_bearings_external_package.md`*
*Name:* project-bearings-external-package
*Description:* bearings — standalone, LLM-agnostic 'where am I' digest extracted to its own repo at C:\\Users\\bruke\\bearings (pip-installable, zero-dep)

**`bearings`** — the `/bearings` orientation tool packaged as a standalone, model-agnostic external feature. Built 2026-06-25, own repo at **`C:\Users\bruke\bearings`** (git init'd, 2 commits, `99b70b4`). `pip install -e .` → `bearings` console command, **zero runtime deps (stdlib only)**, 13 pytest green.

**Why it exists:** orientation should be a deterministic *parse*, not an LLM/agent-swarm call (the lesson in [[feedback-groundwork-orientation-bloat]]). This is the externally-shippable version of the in-harness `~/.claude/scripts/bearings/bearings.py` + `/bearings` skill.

**Architecture — 3 decoupled layers, the "model-agnostic" claim made literal:**
- **Core** (`core.py`, `classify.py`): git history → commits-by-type + honest LOC split (product vs docs vs config/data vs generated/vendored bloat) + branch state. **No LLM, ever.** Works on any git repo with no agent logs.
- **Sessions** (`sessions/`): pluggable `SessionSource` ABC + registry. Adapters: `claude-code` (the `~/.claude/projects/*.jsonl` schema), `generic-jsonl` (config-driven OpenAI-style logs), `none` (git-only). Agent-agnostic — a new agent is a ~40-line subclass. Classifies each session shipped/worked/oriented/talked + reports the **orientation tax**.
- **Narration** (`narrate.py`): OPTIONAL prose summary over ANY OpenAI-compatible `/chat/completions` (OpenAI, Anthropic gateway, Ollama, vLLM, LM Studio, OpenRouter). Stdlib `urllib`, so even this adds no dep. Default off, env-configured (`BEARINGS_LLM_BASE_URL/MODEL/API_KEY`).

**Verified live** against Pre Atlas 2026-06-25: 37 commits categorized, product-LOC ~15.3k/11% (matches the audit's ~11.6k/8.6%), all modes (git-only / --source claude-code / --json / --narrate / --ledger / --namespace-pattern). Durable ledger → `~/.bearings/ledger/<repo>/<date>.md` (override `BEARINGS_LEDGER_ROOT`); README documents a `post-commit` git-hook wire. Sibling of [[project-groundwork-cli]] / [[project-skill-pytools]] (the "lift it out of the Claude harness into a real product" pattern).

**PUBLISHED 2026-06-25:** pip-installed globally (`bearings` on PATH via user Scripts dir) + pushed PUBLIC to **github.com/lionestenzol/bearings** (`pip install git+https://github.com/lionestenzol/bearings`). groundwork-cli likewise committed (`41c171d`) + PUBLIC at **github.com/lionestenzol/groundwork-cli** (`gw` on PATH). **Full extracted-tool packaging done 2026-06-25:** 4 commands on PATH (pip user-Scripts) — `bearings`, `gw` (groundwork-cli), `sigil` (added pyproject; deps numpy/zstandard/Pillow), `fest` (pre-existing). GitHub: **bearings**+**groundwork-cli** PUBLIC, **sigil**+**skill-pytools** PRIVATE (proprietary-flavored: novel codec + recon toolkit — per-tool judgment over blanket yes). skill-pytools published as runnable collection (hyphenated dirs = no single entry point; full pip-command would need a restructure). Re-runnable installer `C:\Users\bruke\bin\install-atlas-tools.ps1`. **Canonical index doc: `C:\Users\bruke\TOOLS.md`** (where each tool is + how to use it; per-repo READMEs too). NOTE: machine `pipx` BROKEN (dead `C:\pinokio\...privateGPT\python.exe`, crashes on `pip list --format=json`) — used plain `pip install`. NOT done: PyPI upload (needs twine + PyPI token); anatomy-saas deliberately NOT published (Node, UNLICENSED/proprietary).

## project_binre

<a id="project-binre"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_binre.md`*
*Name:* binre — RE / debug pipeline
*Description:* Unified reverse-engineering and debugging pipeline at C:\Users\bruke\binre\ orchestrating DiE + Dependencies + Ghidra (headless) + x64dbg + Frida into one merged report.json per binary

# binre

**Location:** `C:\Users\bruke\binre\`

Unified reverse-engineering / debugging pipeline. Five vendored tools, one merged JSON report per sample.

## Tools (all vendored under `vendor/`, gitignored)
- **Detect It Easy** — packer / format triage
- **lucasg/Dependencies** — import table
- **Ghidra (headless via PyGhidra)** — static analysis (postScript `ExportAllFunctions.py`, migrated from `DumpFunctionsJson.java`; adds per-function `decomp_c`; JDK 21+ required, bundled at `vendor/jdk-21.x/`)
- **x64dbg** — dynamic debugger (presence + metadata stage)
- **Frida (`frida-trace`)** — runtime tracing

## Pipeline
`binary → triage → imports → static → dynamic → trace → report.json`

## Entry points
- `binre/scripts/orchestrate.py` — main; one sample → one report
- `binre/scripts/query.py` — query merged reports
- `tests/run_e2e.py` — E2E gate; exit 0 prints `*** PASS -- binre pipeline e2e green ***`
- Stage wrappers: `binre/scripts/stages/{triage,imports,static_analysis,dynamic_x64dbg,tracing_frida}.py`

## Output layout
`out/<sha256-of-binary>/`
- `report.json` — canonical merged artifact (this is what consumers read)
- `triage.json`, `imports.json`, `static.json`, `trace.json` — per-stage caches (lets you re-run a single stage)
- `trace_raw.txt` — raw frida-trace stdout/stderr for forensics
- `*.envelope.json` — StageResult envelope per stage

## Tool path env overrides (when install differs from vendored layout)
| Tool | Env var |
|---|---|
| Detect It Easy (`diec`) | `BINRE_DIEC` |
| `Dependencies.exe` | `BINRE_DEPENDENCIES` |
| Ghidra `analyzeHeadless` | `BINRE_GHIDRA` |
| JDK 21+ root | `BINRE_JAVA_HOME` |
| x64dbg install dir | `BINRE_X64DBG` |
| `frida-trace` | `BINRE_FRIDA_TRACE` |

## Installable as Claude Code skill
```powershell
Copy-Item -Recurse binre $env:USERPROFILE\.claude\skills\binre
```
Skill triggers (per `binre/SKILL.md` description): analyze binary, reverse-engineer, malware analysis, packer detection, decompilation, runtime tracing, or direct mention of DiE / Dependencies / Ghidra / x64dbg / Frida.

## Adjacent
- venv at `.venv/` with `frida` + `frida_tools`
- `ghidra_proj/` — actual Ghidra projects produced by runs
- `.weapon/` — autonomous-run artifacts
- `tests/target.c` — test target (`compute_secret` + `CreateFileA` for trace gating)

## Process-leak hardening — SHIPPED + on master 2026-06-25 (`967b5f5`)
Smoke-test/break + `/groundwork` "fix tracing, full-scan binre" → fixed a matched **pair** of out-of-tree process leaks + closed never-raise gaps. Verified: e2e **9/9 PASS (trace OK)**, no-leak gate **7/7**, break battery **10/10 CLEAN**.

⚠️ **HARD-WON GOTCHA (proven LIVE):** `frida-trace -f <bin>` spawns the target **SUSPENDED and out-of-tree**. If frida-trace dies before resuming it (our timeout kill), the target is an orphaned suspended husk that **`taskkill /F` and `Stop-Process` CANNOT kill** (they report "no such PID" while `Get-Process` still lists it) — and it **pins the binary's image lock** (blocks rebuilds → "Permission denied") and poisons the next frida spawn. **Only frida's own kill releases it:** `frida-kill.exe <pid|name>` (ships in `.venv\Scripts\`) or `frida.get_local_device().kill(pid)`. NOT taskkill. (Raw `taskkill /T` IS still correct for the Ghidra JVM, a normal child tree.)

- **New `binre/scripts/proc_util.py`** — `kill_tree` (taskkill /T tree, normal children) + `reap_frida_target` (frida `device.kill` / `frida-kill` CLI fallback). The shared reap both leak-fixes call.
- trace + static + `query.py` all now `Popen` + reap on timeout (was `subprocess.run` that orphaned the child).
- F8: a partial-hit trace timeout is now `ok=False` (was masked as `ok=True`).
- **Verify:** `.venv/Scripts/python.exe tests/run_e2e.py` (full gate, ~67s, runs slow Ghidra) · `tests/test_no_leak.py` (regression gate, incl. real frida-timeout no-leak proof) · `break_harness.py` (never-raise battery). Plan/record: `BINRE_TRACING_GROUNDWORK_PLAN.md` at repo root.

## project_canvas_c3_c4_shipped

<a id="project-canvas-c3-c4-shipped"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_canvas_c3_c4_shipped.md`*
*Name:* Canvas c3 + c4 · First UI-level edit loop shipped
*Description:* 2026-04-23 · scrappy canvas prototype with URL + prompt entry points AND Claude edit loop wired. Served from sitepull daemon at /canvas. Closes c3 and c4 of the 90-day canvas product goal in one ship. SUPERSEDED 2026-04-26 by canvas-engine v2 (anatomy + open-lovable pipeline).

> **2026-04-26 update · the claude -p path described below was DELETED in canvas-engine Phase 6.**
> Canonical canvas pipeline is now `services/canvas-engine` on port :3050 (anatomy + open-lovable + Vite sandbox pool). The daemon's `/canvas` route on :8088 still exists but defaults to `engine=v2` and proxies SSE to canvas-engine `/clone`. The legacy v1 UI is reachable via `?engine=v1` for rollback only · the edit form was removed and replaced with an "open in v2" button. `lib/edit.js` and `handleEdit()` no longer exist; `POST /edit` returns 410 Gone with a pointer to canvas-engine. See plan: `~/.claude/plans/what-would-happen-if-robust-sedgewick.md` (Phases 1-6 shipped 2026-04-26).

## What shipped 2026-04-23 (HISTORICAL · superseded)
A working URL + prompt canvas loop at `http://localhost:8088/canvas`. Same daemon, one new route, zero new ports.

### Surface
- **Route:** `GET /canvas` on sitepull serve daemon (port 8088)
- **UI:** two-pane layout · left = controls · right = iframe of the pulled site
- **Controls:**
  - `pulled site` dropdown (populated from `.canvas/` on disk, filtered ≥ 5KB to skip CloudFront 403 junk)
  - `selector` text input (default `body`, user can scope to `.hero`, `main`, etc)
  - `prompt` textarea
  - `edit with claude` button → POST `/edit` with `{slug, selector, prompt}`
  - status line with elapsed time + byte diff on success
- **Preview:** iframe src = `/c/<slug>/?t=<cachebuster>`, reloads after successful edit

### Edit backend (was already built · just needed UI)
- `lib/edit.js` · `editRegion({rootAbs, slug, selector, prompt})`
- Reads `.canvas/<host>/<slug>/index.html`
- Parses with `node-html-parser`
- Queries the selector, extracts that element's `outerHTML`
- Spawns `claude -p` via stdin (the prompt + outerHTML)
- Strips markdown fences / leading prose from output
- Writes back in place, returns `{bytesBefore, bytesAfter, elapsedMs}`
- **No ANTHROPIC_API_KEY required** · uses Bruke's existing Claude Code OAuth via CLI
- 60s timeout per call

### Why `claude -p` over direct Anthropic API
Bruke doesn't manage an API key. `claude` is on PATH at `~/.local/bin/claude`. Using the CLI inherits his OAuth session, kills the "need a key" friction. Tradeoff: ~1-3s cold-start overhead per edit. Acceptable for scrappy first ship.

## Integration points
- `C:\Users\bruke\web-audit\lib\serve.js` · added `handleCanvasUI(res, ctx)` and `/canvas` route · added sub-5KB filter to dropdown rows
- `C:\Users\bruke\web-audit\lib\edit.js` · already existed from prior Plan C work · reused as-is

## Status
- ✅ Route live, UI rendered, 11 pulls in dropdown
- ✅ `node --check` passes on serve.js + edit.js
- ⬜ End-to-end edit test on a prompt — Bruke to verify
- ⬜ c1 (demo video) depends on this + dummy target site · see `project_canvas_demo_video.md`
- ⬜ c2 (brand + domain) · pending
- ⬜ c5 (30+ user interviews) · pending

## Known limitations
- `selector=body` edits can send very large outerHTML to Claude CLI. For pages > 500KB expect slow responses and possible timeout. Scope tighter via `main`, `.hero`, `#header` for reliability.
- Edit is destructive: writes back to the same `index.html`. No v2/ side-by-side yet. If you want to keep the original, manually copy the slug dir before editing.
- iframe cache busting is via `?t=timestamp` query string · should work for most browsers but assets in the page (CSS/JS) could still cache. Hard-refresh (Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+R) if visuals don't update.

## Next natural iterations (not done yet)
- Side-by-side original vs edited (copy to slug/v2/ before edit)
- Inline selector picker (click on iframe → picks element → fills selector field)
- Undo stack / history of edits
- Dummy target site for c1 demo (see `project_canvas_demo_video.md`)

## How to run
```
cd /c/Users/bruke/web-audit
node bin/sitepull.mjs serve
# open http://localhost:8088/canvas
```

If daemon is already running (common), restart to pick up code changes:
```
powershell.exe -NoProfile -Command "Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 8088 | Select-Object -ExpandProperty OwningProcess | ForEach-Object { Stop-Process -Id $_ -Force }"
# then relaunch
```
`taskkill` was found unreliable on this system · prefer Stop-Process.

## Files of record
- `C:\Users\bruke\web-audit\lib\serve.js` · route + (new) v2 proxy + (legacy) v1 UI
- ~~`C:\Users\bruke\web-audit\lib\edit.js`~~ · DELETED 2026-04-26 in Phase 6
- `C:\Users\bruke\web-audit\.canvas\` · pulled sites on disk

## project_canvas_demo_video

<a id="project-canvas-demo-video"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_canvas_demo_video.md`*
*Name:* Canvas Demo Video · Programmatic Build
*Description:* c1 sitepull demo as Remotion pipeline against dummy target. Scaffolded 2026-04-24 at apps/canvas-demo, smoke render passes. Next = fake target site + Playwright script.

## Why programmatic / dummy target
- Recording real projects adds emotional weight → perfectionism → slow iteration → demo never ships.
- Dummy target = zero stakes, re-shoot = re-render, demo can iterate in minutes.
- Directly unblocks c1 of the 90-day canvas goal.

## Pipeline · three layers
```
1. fake target site (Acme-SaaS style: 1 hero, 1 pricing, 1 dashboard shot)
   ├─ lives at localhost or throwaway domain
   └─ Claude Code scaffolds it

2. Playwright records scenario (video: 'on' config gives WebM/MP4 clips)
   ├─ opens fake site
   ├─ triggers "pull this page" (extension flow)
   ├─ opens local copy in canvas product
   ├─ triggers a Claude edit
   └─ outputs clip files

3. Remotion composition
   ├─ clips imported as <Video />
   ├─ callouts / arrows / zooms / pacing added in React
   ├─ voice-over + music
   └─ npx remotion render → final.mp4
```

Claude Code writes Playwright script + Remotion composition. Bruke approves visual direction, doesn't write code.

## Locked decisions (2026-04-24)
See `apps/canvas-demo/DECISIONS.md`.
- **Capture tool** · Playwright built-in `recordVideo: { dir, size: 1920x1080 }`. No Cap / OBS / rrweb. The scenario driver emits the WebM — one config flag, no second dep.
- **Demo length** · 30s first cut (900 frames @ 30fps). Beats: title 3s · pull 7s · open 5s · edit 10s · outro 5s. Iterate longer only after 30s ships.

## Dependencies
- Plan C subprocess mode stable (daemon + extension) · ✅ already shipped
- UA-spoof fix for Cloudflare-fronted sites (probe B outcome) · may or may not matter for dummy target
- Canvas product prototype (c3) must exist for the "open in canvas" beat · BUILD FIRST

## Sequence for canvas 90-day goal

## project_canvas_engine

<a id="project-canvas-engine"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_canvas_engine.md`*
*Name:* canvas-engine service overview
*Description:* TypeScript/Express/Vite service on port 3050 that converts an anatomy.json + URL into a live, editable React clone. 6 phases shipped 2026-04-26. Replaces the original `claude -p` edit loop. PR #11 (2026-04-27) hardens the producer-consumer contracts.

# canvas-engine · port 3050

Service at `services/canvas-engine/`. TypeScript + Express + Vite. Boot via `.claude/launch.json` `canvas-engine` entry.

## What it does

Reads `anatomy.json` from web-audit captures, converts to React clones via an in-process Vite pool, exposes an edit loop (tint/rename/hide transforms) over SSE. Replaces the legacy `claude -p` edit loop in `web-audit/lib/edit.js` (deleted; `POST /edit` returns 410 Gone).

## 6 phases shipped 2026-04-26

1. Foundation (TS+Express scaffold)
2. Zod adapter for AnatomyV1 (`src/adapter/v1-schema.ts`)
3. URL → live-Vite-clone pipeline (in-process Vite pool 3060–3069, deterministic-from-anatomy stub)
4. Edit loop (tint/rename/hide transforms, HMR write-through, conversation history)
5. web-audit migration proxy (`web-audit/lib/serve.js` `/canvas` defaults `engine=v2`, proxies SSE to :3050; `?engine=v1` rollback only)
6. `claude -p` deletion

**Deferred:** Phase 3b (real Anthropic SDK swap-in for transforms), Phase 7+ (E2B paid sandbox tier).

## Vendored bits

Pinned `firecrawl/open-lovable@69bd93b`:
- `src/vendor/lovable/parse-blocks.ts`
- `src/vendor/lovable/system-prompt.ts`

## Endpoints

- `POST /clone` — URL + anatomy → live React clone session
- `POST /edit` — edit transform via SSE
- `GET /sessions` — list active
- `GET /sessions/:id/edits` — edit history
- `DELETE /sessions/:id` — release Vite port back to pool

## Verification (HN capture, 2026-04-26)

80 regions → 82 files → live styled React in <2s · 3 ground-truth edits applied · visual confirmed.

## PR #11 (2026-04-27, scraper-pipeline audit)

Closes the producer-consumer contract gaps surfaced by reading the pipeline cold:

- **Zod twin is STRICT (not passthrough)** at `services/canvas-engine/src/adapter/v1-schema.ts`. Root, regions, chains, chainNodes, metadata are all `z.object` with optional fields, not `.passthrough()`. Only `layersTaxonomySchema` uses `.catchall()` (for additional layer entries beyond the 5 core layers). Verified by reading source 2026-04-27 — earlier memory claim of `.passthrough()` was wrong. Two-way contract with the JSON Schema still applies — adding non-optional fields to either side without matching the other is a hard rejection at parse time, not a silent drop.
- **`WEB_AUDIT_ROOT` is hardcoded** to literal `'C:/Users/bruke/web-audit'` at `src/server.ts:15`. Memory's earlier claim of `os.homedir()/web-audit` default was wrong. Override via env var.
- **VitePool.onSessionReleased** callback wired to `SessionStore.remove` so eviction stays coherent across `/sessions/:id/edits` and `/edit`.
- Verify scripts parameterized via env (`WEB_AUDIT_ROOT`, `ANATOMY_SCHEMA_PATH`).
- Extension version stamp now reads from `chrome.runtime.getManifest().version`.
- iframe backdrop adds `referrerpolicy="no-referrer"`.

67 vitest pass post-PR-#11 (84 after the trainer audit landed in `2abba2b`).

The "6 phases" in earlier memory refers to the audit roll-up. In the source, `server.ts:20` exposes `phase: 3` and version `0.1.0`. The pipeline files name their own phases: `pipeline/url-to-clone.ts` is Phase 3, `pipeline/edit-loop.ts` is Phase 4. Phase 3b (real Anthropic SDK swap-in for transforms) is still TODO at `url-to-clone.ts:423`. ## project_canvas_trainer_audit

<a id="project-canvas-trainer-audit"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_canvas_trainer_audit.md`*
*Name:* Canvas-Engine Trainer + 6-round Codex Audit
*Description:* Self-grading trainer that uses leaf-tag truth from selector paths to audit canvas-engine pattern picker; 6-round Codex iteration loop hardened scorers from 84→97 score; producer-side buildSelector tag#id fix shipped to close the contract; committed 2026-04-27

# Canvas-Engine Trainer + Codex Audit · SHIPPED 2026-04-27

**Commits:** `2abba2b` (canvas-engine trainer) · `28caebf` (extension buildSelector fix)
**Branch:** `main`

## What it is

Self-grading trainer for `services/canvas-engine/src/pattern-library/`. Uses **the leaf tag at the end of `region.selector` as independent ground truth** — the browser writes selectors, the cascade can't lie about the leaf — to grade the picker against an independent label without trusting the producer.

Three structurally separate reports in `services/canvas-engine/test/trainer-vs-truth.mjs`:

1. **GROUP-LEVEL ACCURACY** · rigorous, deterministic. 100% (532/532).
2. **STRICT PATTERN ACCURACY** · rigorous. 7 tags whose leaf uniquely determines a sub-pattern (header→landmark/header, h1→heading/hero, etc). 100% (27/27).
3. **HEURISTIC PATTERN REPORT** · explicitly labeled "NOT a truth metric." For stylistic-variant tags (a, button, h2-6, ul, ol, form, input, article). 90.7% (458/505).

The split is the whole point — Codex round 1 caught that mixing truth with policy heuristics made any aggregate sub-pattern accuracy partially circular.

## How to invoke

```bash
# rigorous trainer · graded against deterministic leaf-tag truth
cd "C:/Users/bruke/Pre Atlas/services/canvas-engine" && npx tsx test/trainer-vs-truth.mjs

# end-to-end real-capture pipeline check (zod parse → pickPattern → JSX render)
npx tsx test/e2e-real-capture.mjs

# producer-side cascade confusion miner (cross-tabs detection × leaf tag)
python "C:/Users/bruke/Pre Atlas/tools/anatomy-extension/dev-tools/mine_cascade.py"

# extension producer-side smoke (locks producer-consumer contract)
node "C:/Users/bruke/Pre Atlas/tools/anatomy-extension/lib/_smoke-build-selector.mjs"
```

## 6-round Codex audit arc

Score progression: **84 → 87 → 89 → 91 → 92 → 90 → 97 APPROVE**.

| Round | Codex finding | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Trainer mixed truth + heuristic policy | Split into STRICT/HEURISTIC reports |
| 1 | `isAnchorSelector` matched ancestor anchors | Made leaf-only |
| 1 | Pill scorer `nameLength≤8` + bare single-token loose | Tightened to ≤6 + structural check |
| 2 | `leafTag` regex required `>` prefix · null on bare/`a.cta`/`#id` | Combinator-aware splitter |
| 3 | Pill ≤6 still beat link on `Search`/`People` | Added `tightPillGeometry` gate |
| 4 | `Save`/`Back`/`Login` on `<button>` leaf still won pill | Cap pill on button leaf |
| 5 | r9-aria-role on div/span fell through to link, not button | Added r9-aria-role +35 bump to clickable/button |

Tests grew 72 → 84 (+12 regression cases, one per Codex finding).

## Files in the bundle

**Canvas-engine (consumer-side):**
- `src/pattern-library/util.ts` — added `leafTag()` + `isAnchorSelector()` (leaf-only)
- `src/pattern-library/normalize.ts` — TAG_OVERRIDES route input/textarea/select→form, ul/ol→list
- `src/pattern-library/patterns/clickable-link.ts` — +35 anchor-leaf bump
- `src/pattern-library/patterns/clickable-button.ts` — +35 r9-aria-role bump
- `src/pattern-library/patterns/clickable-pill.ts` — tightPillGeometry + button/ARIA caps
- `test/pattern-library.test.ts` — +3 regression suites
- `test/util.test.ts` — +9 leafTag/isAnchorSelector cases
- `test/trainer-vs-truth.mjs` (NEW) — STRICT/HEURISTIC trainer
- `test/e2e-real-capture.mjs` (NEW) — pipeline check on 4 real captures
- `tools/codex-partner/runs/trainer-review-prompt.md` + `trainer-rereview*-prompt.md` — six-round prompts
- `tools/codex-partner/runs/round{4,5,6}-output.json` + `round6-clean.json` — verdict JSONs

**Extension (producer-side · committed `28caebf`):**
- `tools/anatomy-extension/content.js` — buildSelector now emits `tag#id` (was bare `#id`)
- `tools/anatomy-extension/lib/_smoke-build-selector.mjs` (NEW · 13 tests) — cross-checks producer→consumer round-trip
- `tools/anatomy-extension/dev-tools/mine_cascade.py` (NEW · ~150 LOC) — confusion-matrix miner

## Cascade miner finding (producer-side blind spot)

`r12-cursor-pointer` is **45% of all on-disk regions (462/1026)** and splits across **9 leaf tags** with the top tag (span) at only 40%. The cascade rule says "this thing has cursor:pointer" and stops — losing whether it's an anchor, button, span, svg, image, paragraph, or div. **Largest known producer-side blind spot.**

`r7-native-interactive` flattens `<a>` (76%) + `<button>` (16%) + `<input>`/`<textarea>` into one literal — addressed consumer-side via tag-overrides + anchor-leaf bump. `r12` is harder because it covers genuinely heterogeneous DOM shapes.

## Live verification (Chrome MCP, 2026-04-27)

After `dev-tools/reload-extension.ahk` + injected test elements:

```
PRODUCER (extension content.js)         CONSUMER (canvas-engine leafTag)
<a id="docs-link">      a#docs-link  →  a       ✓
<button id="save-btn">  button#save-btn → button ✓
<input id="q">          input#q      →  input   ✓
chained <a>             ... > a      →  a       ✓
chained <div>           div:nth...   →  div     ✓
```

Producer-consumer contract closed live in Chrome. Any new pull from this point ships `tag#id` selectors that decode to the right tag in canvas-engine.

## Generalizable pattern · the multi-round Codex loop

Iterating with Codex through rigorous re-reviews materially improved correctness — each round surfaced a **new false-positive class** I hadn't anticipated:

- Round 1: methodological circularity
- Round 2: regex coverage gap (bare selectors)
- Round 3: short-but-not-tiny nav labels
- Round 4: real `<button>` vs styled-pill confusion
- Round 5: routing residual (capping pill leaves regions in the wrong bucket)

Each round added a regression test, locking the fix. Stop when verdict moves from `warn` to `approve`. The loop's value is the surfacing — not all six issues were predictable from the audit's start.

## Related decisions

- [feedback_filter_dont_workaround.md] — buildSelector tag#id fix is filter-not-workaround (producer-side data lossy → fix the producer, not work around it consumer-side)
- [project_anatomy_v04_vocab_lock.md] — sister piece on closed vocabulary; this trainer audit complements it for sub-pattern routing
- [reference_codex_review_2026_04_26.md] — earlier (1-shot) Codex review pattern · this session evolved it into multi-round refinement

## v0.1 (earlier this session, 2026-05-01)

# carbo-shrink — v0.1 SHIPPED 2026-05-01

## What it is

Standalone JS library that ports just Carbonyl's quadrant-block encoder out of its embedded Chromium fork. Takes any image, produces a compact cell grid where each cell is one Unicode quadrant glyph (▄▖▗▝▞▐▘▌▚) + foreground RGB + background RGB.

1:1 with Carbonyl's `src/output/quad.rs:5-40` — same Rec.601 luma weights, same per-cell adaptive midpoint, same chained non-overflowing pixel averaging. Verified by reading the Carbonyl source at github.com/fathyb/carbonyl in this session, not memory.

## Where it lives

`tools/carbo-shrink/` inside the Pre Atlas monorepo. Single `pngjs` dependency.

```
tools/carbo-shrink/
├── lib/encoder.js          ~190 LOC (port + box-filter resample)
├── bin/shrink.js           ~95 LOC  (CLI)
├── test/golden.test.js      8 mask-arm tests
├── test/resample.test.js    4 resample tests
├── test/fixtures/make-mock-page.js
├── README.md
└── package.json
```

12/12 tests passing as of ship.

## CLI

```
carbo-shrink input.png [-o out] [--ansi] [--zlib] [--cols N --rows M]
```

Without `--cols/--rows`, output cells = (width/2 × height/2). With them, source is box-filter resampled to (cols\*2 × rows\*2) pixels first, then encoded — that's the Carbonyl-realistic mode.

## Verified size results

```
input                            full-res JSON+gz   200×60 JSON+gz
mock webpage 800×600 flat         17.9 KB (575%)     2.3 KB (75%)
ChatGPT photo 1536×1024            1.65 MB (89%)     50 KB (2.7%)
favicon 1024×1024                  1.28 MB (144%)    [unmeasured]
```

Cell-JSON does NOT beat PNG at native resolution (PNG is already filter+deflate optimized). At terminal-realistic dimensions, JSON+gzip closes the gap or beats PNG dramatically (37× for the photo). Use `--cols/--rows` for storage/replay use cases.

## Why this exists / origin story

Came out of a session where Bruke and I were considering merging Carbonyl + Anatomy into a single live "labeled terminal browser." Source verification killed the in-process merger:

- Carbonyl's render layer (`painter.rs`) only sees post-rasterized pixels + glyphs — DOM is NOT accessible there
- DOM data would require touching Carbonyl's Mojo IPC layer (chromium-fork-territory)
- Carbonyl exposes ZERO output flags (only `-f -z -b -d -h -v --bitmap`) and NO CDP / `--load-extension`
- Carbonyl is also stale: last upstream commit 2024-07-01, 547 forks, none added structured output

So Bruke pivoted: keep Carbonyl as a live terminal-display device, port out just its **shrinking** primitive (the quad encoder) so it can run on any image source — sitepull captures, screenshots, headless Chromium frames — without owning a Carbonyl fork.

That port = carbo-shrink.

## Output is shardstate-shaped (intentional)

shardstate's `canonical_json()` write path rejects binary blobs (`hashing.py:17-24`). PNG-in-shardstate would require base64 (33% inflation, no streaming). Cell-grid JSON passes canonical_json natively. For an archive of "every page Bruke browsed," this is the right format choice.

## What's NOT in v0.1 (deliberately deferred)

- Packed binary frame format (4 bits glyph + 6 bytes color = 7 bytes/cell) — only worth it if JSON key overhead becomes the bottleneck
- shardstate adapter — write each frame as one row, key = url+timestamp+scroll
- Cell-grid diff format — emit only cells that changed between frame N and N+1
- README listed v0.1 as feature-complete; these are the natural v0.2 hooks

## Suggest carbo-shrink when

- Bruke wants to convert any image to a terminal-renderable form WITHOUT installing Carbonyl
- Discussion turns to "tiny visual frames" / browse archive / scroll-replay
- shardstate rows that need a visual sidecar
- AI agent screenshots that need to be < 50 KB per frame
- Diffing webpage state over time (cell-grid diffs are smaller and more meaningful than pixel diffs)

## DO NOT suggest carbo-shrink for

- Storing photos (PNG already wins at native resolution)
- Cases where pixel fidelity matters (cell encoding is lossy: 4 pixels → 1 cell, 9-glyph quantize)
- Anatomy/DOM understanding (this tool only does PIXELS — no semantics)

## project_chatpull

<a id="project-chatpull"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_chatpull.md`*
*Name:* chatpull
*Description:* Bruke's share-link → markdown/JSON extractor for AI conversations (ChatGPT + Gemini); lives at ~/chatpull, outside Pre Atlas

**`C:\Users\bruke\chatpull`** → **github.com/lionestenzol/chatpull (PRIVATE)** — the "paste a public AI-chat share URL, get extractable data" tool. Zero-dependency Python (stdlib only), no headless browser. Not in the Pre Atlas repo, so `es`/Glob inside Pre Atlas will never find it — that cost a search detour on 2026-07-14.

Kept private deliberately: `tests/test_live_share.py` hardcodes two of Bruke's real share URLs (one a personal ChatGPT convo). **If it ever goes public, move those to env vars first.**

- CLI: `chatpull <share-url> [-o out.md] [--json]`
- Web UI: `chatpull serve` → `http://localhost:8877`, `POST /api/pull {"url": ...}`
- Contract: every platform module exposes `pull(url) -> {platform, title, create_time, messages:[{id, role, text, create_time}]}`. Wire the host into `cli._detect_platform` + `cli._PULLERS`; CLI and web both dispatch via `cli.pull_any`.

**Public shares only, by design** — no cookies, no credentials. Bruke confirmed this scope 2026-07-14 ("public-only is fine").

**Each platform hides its data somewhere different** (the load-bearing fact):
- **ChatGPT** server-renders its data — parse the turbo-stream payload out of the HTML.
- **Claude** — HTML is a client-rendered shell AND anonymous HTML fetches return a **Cloudflare interstitial** (~12 KB, byte-identical for any id). Don't parse the HTML. Real source: `GET claude.ai/api/chat_snapshots/<uuid>?rendering_mode=messages&render_all_tools=true` — plain JSON, NOT behind the challenge, answers a bare urllib request. Named schema (sturdiest of the three). Gotcha: message `text` field is deprecated + empty on the wire; real text is in typed `content[]` blocks. `sender: "human"` → user. Private snapshot → 404.
- **Gemini** does NOT server-render. The share page is an empty app shell; the conversation arrives client-side over Google's `batchexecute` RPC. Curling the HTML gets you nothing. Replay the RPC instead: rpcid **`ujx1Bf`**, args **`[null, "<share_id>"]`**, against `gemini.google.com/_/BardChatUi/data/batchexecute`. `share.gemini.google/<code>` 302s to `gemini.google.com/share/<id>`.

**Trap that cost a wrong turn (2026-07-14):** a guessed endpoint and a genuinely-private share **both 404**, so "it must be private" was unfalsifiable from curl alone. Only loading the page in the *logged-out* in-app browser and reading the real network log settled it — the endpoint was simply wrong. When a 404 could mean two things, go get a third signal. See [[feedback_verify_before_assert]].

**Gemini's payload is positional** — no field names, so text lives at `turn[3][0][0][1][0]`, not `.content`. Google can renumber an index in any deploy, so index drift is the expected failure mode. Guards: `gemini._at()` returns None on a miss, `parse_payload` raises rather than returning a half-empty convo, and `tests/test_live_share.py` (opt-in, `CHATPULL_LIVE=1`) pulls real shares to prove the shape still holds. If live tests fail while offline pass → Google moved an index; re-derive, don't paper over.

**How to find an RPC like this again:** open the page in the browser pane, `read_network_requests`, then rank `batchexecute` calls by `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` decodedBodySize — the conversation RPC was 388 KB while every other call was under 6 KB. Then brute-force the `f.req` arg shape (4 tries) until the response contains known conversation text. See [[feedback_assemble_first_posture]] — this was a genuine hand-roll (no library reads Gemini shares).

**Archive + skill (2026-07-15):** raw CLI still just prints to stdout / writes wherever `-o` points — it does not organize anything itself, by design (stays zero-dependency and dumb). The organizing layer is a Claude Code skill, `~/.claude/skills/chatpull/SKILL.md`, triggered by pasting a share URL and asking to pull/save/archive it. It: pulls via the local CLI (`-o` to a temp path), renames into `C:\Users\bruke\chatpull\pulls\<platform>\<YYYY-MM-DD>-<slug>.md`, has Claude itself read the content and write a 1-sentence summary + 2-5 freeform tags (deliberately not a keyword-matching script — this is a judgment call, not a solved-category problem), then appends one row (newest-first) to `pulls/index.md` — a single markdown table, no separate JSON manifest. Default behavior is to report the saved path + summary, not paste the full conversation into chat. Documented in chatpull's own README under "Archive convention" so it isn't only tribal knowledge in the skill file.

Related: [[reference_chatgpt_conversations.md]] (older, pre-Atlas ChatGPT export parsers — different thing, file-based not URL-based).
