## project_claude_bridge

<a id="project-claude-bridge"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_claude_bridge.md`*
*Description:* Cross-surface bridge between Claude web and local Claude Code via a synced Google Drive folder, with per-task code staging and a 30-min auto-poller.

# claude-bridge — web ↔ local CC channel through Google Drive

Built 2026-06-24. Bridges **Claude web** (hyper-focus bursts) and **local Claude Code**
(long-haul application) through a shared Google Drive folder `claude`
(id `1TyqXt80bOBacl8uwZwwqzNTJFcjz_r74`, local mount `G:\My Drive\claude`).

## Pieces
- **Skill** `~/.claude/skills/claude-bridge/SKILL.md` (committed to `~/.claude` master `e40ad01`).
  Two rules + a Step-0 surface probe: LOCAL CC uses the filesystem (Drive Desktop syncs it);
  WEB/REMOTE uses the Google Drive connector against the folder id.
- **Staging tool** `~/.claude/skills/claude-bridge/bridge.py` — `stage <task> <path>... --ask`
  exports git-archive snapshots (tracked files only, no `.git`) into `for-web/<task>/payload/`
  and maintains root `INDEX.md` + `index.json` manifest. `collect <task>` pulls web's output
  from `for-cc/<task>/result/` and prints `git apply` lines. `--ref` snapshots any branch
  without checkout (cheaper than a worktree). Only COMMITTED files stage.
- **Auto-poller** scheduled task `bridge-poll` (cron `*/30 * * * *`, notifyOnCompletion=false).
  Acts on open `for-cc/` handoffs CONSERVATIVELY — safe asks (read/analyze/code/tests) only;
  refuses destructive/outward/irreversible/ambiguous and leaves a `## Result` note instead.

## Lanes (named by WHO READS — renamed from to-cc/to-web after both the bootstrap prompt
   AND web-Claude misread direction): `for-cc/` (CC reads), `for-web/` (web reads),
   `shared/` (both). Handoffs are `YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM-<slug>.md` with from/to/date/status/slug
   frontmatter; reply-in-place (flip `open`→`done`, append `## Result`, never delete).

## Hard truths (relayed to Bruke)
- File SYNC is automatic (Drive Desktop); ACTING on files is NOT — a message ("check the
  bridge") or the poller triggers work. No background daemon.
- Routines only fire **while the CC app is open**; if closed when due, run on next launch.
- Web can only read what's PHYSICALLY in Drive — hence staging snapshots, not git URLs.
- Web→local is near-automatic (≤30min, app open); local→web stays pull (web has no cron
  we control). Web Project: instructions shape behavior but can't auto-fire on chat open.

## Open / next
- Web Project setup is the user's UI action (enable Drive connector + paste instructions).
- Protocol nuance to fold into web instructions: web should also re-check `for-cc/` for its
  OWN sent handoffs now marked `done` (reply-in-place results live there).
- Bridge folder contents (README/INDEX/handoffs) live in Drive, NOT git.

## project_command_audit_2026-06-25

<a id="project-command-audit-2026-06-25"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_command_audit_2026-06-25.md`*
*Name:* command-audit-2026-06-25
*Description:* Live audit of all 31 Atlas/personal slash commands — 5 bugs found+fixed, committed bb40db2; 2 follow-ups still open.

On 2026-06-25 Bruke asked to audit + smoke-test + break-test every Atlas/personal slash command (full live invocation, not static). Scope chosen: his own ~40 commands, not the legal/sales/HR/anthropic-skills plugin packs.

## Method that worked
- **13 CLI/MCP commands** smoke+break tested inline (real invocation + garbage input).
- **18 heavy orchestrators** run as **parallel background agents** (Agent tool, run_in_background) — NOT raw `claude -p`. A headless `claude -p --permission-mode bypassPermissions` is **blocked by the auto-mode classifier** (Bruke's own config forbids disabling approval gates), so background subagents under normal gating are the right "own terminal" equivalent. Each agent wrote a verdict file to scratchpad `orch-results/<name>.md` and returned ONE line to bound parent context.
- Shared smoke-test brief written once to a file; each agent prompt just pointed at it + its target. Sandboxed side-effecting skills (weapon, web-audit, handoff-out, claude-bridge) into scratch dirs.

## Result: 31 commands, all functional, 5 real bugs — ALL FIXED
- **F2** mini-ship/scan.py — cp1252 mojibake → UTF-8 stdout reconfigure. [[reference-windows-cp1252-encoding-gotcha]]
- **F3** jargonize/refresh-timer.ps1 — schtasks /Delete stderr leak on first run → try/catch + `*>$null`.
- **F4 (CRITICAL)** autopilot orchestrator.py:175 — `read_text()` (cp1252) crashed with UnicodeDecodeError on any task file with em-dash/arrow/emoji → `encoding="utf-8"`. Verified live against the strudel festival that crashed. NOT committed (festival-project isn't a git repo; lives at `~/festival-project/festivals/active/agent-cookbook-AC0001/orchestrator.py`).
- **F5** strategic-compact/SKILL.md — wired a phantom `skills/.../suggest-compact.js`; real hook is `~/.claude/scripts/hooks/suggest-compact.js`.
- **F6** web-audit/SKILL.md — documented `web-audit.mjs`; real binary renamed to `sitepull.mjs`.
- F1 (goal exits 0 on bad subcmd) was a **false alarm** — measurement artifact from piping through `head`; real exit is 2.

F2/F3/F5/F6 committed **bb40db2** on `~/.claude` (master), scoped add of exactly 4 files (the repo is full of unrelated dirty state incl `.credentials.json` — never `git add -A` there).

## Two open follow-ups (spawned as task chips, NOT fixed)
1. **autopilot phase-parser 0/0 false-complete** (task_d7042def) — silently reports "0/0 phases complete" for real festivals with phase folders (cycleboard-wiring, product-level-SS0002, atlas-verified-reality), so autopilot refuses to drive them. Phase-folder matcher too strict.
2. **continuous-learning has 0 instincts** (task_58bae492) — instinct-status/evolve/prune all work but the store is empty despite the hook supposedly observing sessions. Check if the instinct-creation hook is wired+firing.

## Notable: also cwd/env-sensitivity (works-as-designed, not bugs)
`/goal` `/status` need `Pre Atlas\.aegis-tenant-key` (401 elsewhere); `/fest` `/autopilot` need the festival-dir env to see festivals. search-stack + atlas-map work via in-process MCP even when their HTTP service ports (:3070/:3072) are down.

## 30-DAY REVIEW (2026-06-04 · FINAL scheduled pester)

**Ran autonomously. Bruke not present — this is the evidence-based half of the review; the conversation half waits for him.**

### What the evidence shows (the 5 items)
The 5 outward-facing items were: (A) cold email Anthropic/OpenAI, (B) LinkedIn/website positioning, (C) external observer test, (D) mine chat history for unmonetized IP, (E) stop undercharging / translating self down.

- **Git over the 30-day window (2026-05-04 → 2026-06-04): ~30 commits, ALL inward-facing engineering.** fest-reconcile + corpus archaeology, the dogfood swap-backlog audit (library swaps in aegis-fabric/cognitive-sensor/uasc-executor), atlas_explorer + galaxy WebGL view, canvas-engine image→code vision pipeline, portfolio-wide ship-evidence audit. Zero commits touch A–E. (Expected — A/B/C/E leave no git trace — but D would, and D shows nothing.)
- **Pester completion signal: all 4 scheduled pesters fired into an empty room.** Pesters 1 and 2 logged UNKNOWN ("user not present"); the memory file never received entries for 3 or 4. The system designed to force an answer never got to ask a live human once.

### Honest inference (not asserted as fact — Bruke must confirm)
Cannot confirm completion counts without Bruke. But absence-of-evidence + the shape of 30 days of work points hard at the **0–1 done** branch. **This is not a capacity problem** — the 30-day output is enormous and high-quality. It's a *direction* problem: every one of the 5 items requires **external exposure** (email a stranger, put your name in public, let someone judge the system, name a price). Every one of the ~30 commits is **internally safe** (refactor, audit, library swap, dashboard). The building is real, but it's also the exposure-shaped comfort zone. The wall the pester was built to push past is still standing because the pester (a) couldn't reach a live human, and (b) asked for the scary version of each move.

### Re-architected next 90 days — smaller, friendlier, exposure-graded
The original list asked for the 100% version of each item. Replace with the 1% version that still counts as a rep:
1. **D first (lowest exposure, you already own the tool).** Point competitor-monitor / chat-history at *yourself*: one session, output = a single markdown list "things I built that someone pays for." No sending, no judging. Pure inventory. This is the warm-up rep.
2. **C, scoped to one safe person.** Not "send the system." Send *one screenshot* of atlas_explorer or the galaxy view to one trusted person with the words "made this." That's the whole task.
3. **B, one line, one place.** Don't redo LinkedIn. Add one sentence to a bio you already control. Drafted line is in pester-2 SKILL.md.
4. **A and E are the boss fights — defer them behind 1–3.** A cold email and a price are easier to send *after* three small exposure reps have desensitized the flinch. Don't lead with the hardest one.

### Pester system verdict
The 4-stage scheduled pester **failed at its one job: it never reached a present human.** Don't rebuild the same architecture. If Bruke wants a v2, it must trigger when he's *actually in a session* (a SessionStart nudge, not a cron firing into the void), and it must ask for the 1% move, not the 100% one. Proposal logged here; needs his yes.

**Next step (requires Bruke):** open this file, confirm the real completion count (0–5), and pick item D as the single next rep. Everything else waits on that one number.

## project_continuous_learning_v2_wired

<a id="project-continuous-learning-v2-wired"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_continuous_learning_v2_wired.md`*
*Name:* project-continuous-learning-v2-wired
*Description:* continuous-learning-v2 instinct pipeline was never wired; root cause was a pyenv-win python3 shim + unregistered hook; now fixed + a Windows-safe one-shot analyzer added

The `~/.claude/skills/continuous-learning-v2/` instinct pipeline reported "No instincts found" / "Currently have: 0" for many sessions. Investigated + fixed 2026-06-26.

**Root cause was layered (all gates closed):**
1. Hook never registered — neither `settings.json` nor `settings.local.json` referenced `observe.sh`; not a plugin either. So observation capture never ran.
2. Entrypoint guard rejected this machine — `observe.sh` only allowed `cli|sdk-ts`; this is `claude-desktop`.
3. **The real silent killer:** `python3` on this box resolves to a pyenv-win shim (`~/.pyenv/pyenv-win/shims/python3`) that MANGLES multi-line `python3 -c` scripts (injects batch `|| goto :error`). Every multi-line python call in observe.sh/detect-project.sh failed. Plain `python` (`C:\Python313`) works. This is a general gotcha — any skill hardcoding `python3 -c '<multiline>'` breaks here.
4. Observer (the Haiku agent that WRITES instincts) was `enabled:false`, AND its hook lazy-start needs `flock`/`lockfile` (absent on Git Bash), AND `observer-loop.sh` gates claude off on Windows behind `ECC_OBSERVER_ALLOW_WINDOWS` (issue #295 hang — which does NOT repro here; `claude -p` works in ~12s).

**Fixes applied:**
- `config.json` observer.enabled=true; `observe.sh` entrypoint allows `claude-desktop`.
- Both `resolve_python_cmd` (observe.sh) + `_clv2_resolve_python_cmd` (detect-project.sh) now PROBE each candidate with a multi-line `-c` and skip mangled shims (portable; still prefers python3 on Linux).
- PostToolUse hook registered in settings.json (capture verified live across sessions). **PreToolUse hook still NOT registered — classifier blocked the settings.json edit as self-modification; Bruke must paste it manually** (mirror of the post entry with `pre`).
- New `agents/analyze-once.sh` + `agents/write_instincts.py`: a bounded one-shot analyzer (no daemon, no flock, no #295 gate). Design: the headless `claude --print` child is READ-ONLY and emits a JSON array to stdout; the bash wrapper writes the `.md` instinct files. This deliberately avoids `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` (forbidden by CLAUDE.md, and classifier-blocked) and Claude Code's protection of the `~/.claude` dir against headless Write. Run it manually: `bash ~/.claude/skills/continuous-learning-v2/agents/analyze-once.sh`.
- `instinct-cli.py`: added UTF-8 stdout/stderr reconfigure (status crashed with cp1252 UnicodeEncodeError on the confidence-bar glyphs — the [[reference_windows_cp1252_encoding_gotcha]] again).

**Verified:** analyze-once produced 4 instincts; `status` shows "4 total" and renders; `evolve` reads 4. The background daemon (`start-observer.sh`) remains Windows-gated by design — use analyze-once.sh instead on this machine.

*Name:* CycleBoard Wiring Festival

## Active Festival: cycleboard-wiring-CW0001

**Location**: `/root/festival-project/festivals/planning/cycleboard-wiring-CW0001` (WSL)
**Status**: Validated (100/100), 0% complete, 62 tasks
**Created**: 2026-03-25

## What It Does
Wires all disconnected CycleBoard elements to live data and functional behavior:
- Cognitive banner: show offline message instead of silent hide
- Strategic router: staleness warnings when data > 24h old
- Contingency buttons: actually switch day type (Running Late->B-Day, Low Energy->C-Day, etc.)
- Strategic reweighting: persist focus area weights to state
- Daily directive: display .txt content in Home screen
- Control panel: unify mode calculation with CycleBoard

## Phases
1. **001_PLAN** (planning): Map all disconnections, define exact changes
2. **002_BRAIN_DATA** (implementation): Cognitive banner + strategic router + staleness (5 tasks)
3. **003_CONTINGENCIES** (implementation): Wire contingency buttons (2 tasks)
4. **004_STRATEGIC_PERSIST** (implementation): Persist focus area weights (2 tasks)
5. **005_DIRECTIVE_SYNC** (implementation): Daily directive display + control panel sync (3 tasks)

## How to Continue
- Run `/fest next` to see the next task
- Run `/fest execute` to have Claude read and execute the next task file
- Run `/fest progress` to check overall status

**Why:** CycleBoard is the daily command center for Pre Atlas governance. Disconnected elements mean governance decisions from the Python pipeline never reach the UI.

**How to apply:** In next session, user will say `/fest execute` or similar. Read the task file and execute it against the CycleBoard code at `services/cognitive-sensor/cycleboard/`.

## project_dashboard_pipeline

<a id="project-dashboard-pipeline"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_dashboard_pipeline.md`*
*Name:* Dashboard rendering pipeline (md/json -> html)
*Description:* Plan for systematizing today/week dashboard rendering after the manual loop has earned it. Today's dashboard at C:\Users\bruke\OneDrive\Desktop\today-2026-04-29.html is the template seed.

# Dashboard rendering pipeline

## Origin
Built today's dashboard manually (2026-04-29) as the C-path delivery from the agenda-book convergence: a today.html-style standalone page Bruke fills in for a single day. He liked the shape and asked: *"what if there was a .md to html pipeline so when i need pages like this it could be systematic"* — surfacing the natural next step of making this rendering repeatable.

The dashboard at `C:\Users\bruke\OneDrive\Desktop\today-2026-04-29.html` is the canonical template seed. Inlined inPACT design tokens (Inter font, light theme, td-* classes), 40 checkboxes across 9 chapters, top-right progress meter, mission card, first-move CTA, end-of-day section, fast-version + do-not-do panels. Plus pullable state (URL hash + tab title + state pane + Copy state button) added in same session for agent readback.

## Three flavors

| # | Shape | Cost | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Chat-shape (zero code).** Bruke dumps the day in chat, Claude renders the dashboard, saves to OneDrive. Template is the recipe; Claude is the renderer. | 0 LOC | Default. Now. Aligns with "tools must beat paper" — no infrastructure. |
| **2** | **`.md` -> `.html` script.** Structured markdown (frontmatter for theme/mode/spine, `##` for chapters, `- [ ]` for rows, special blocks for fast-version / do-not-do / EOD). One Python script renders it through the existing template. mistune or python-markdown + Jinja2. | ~100 LOC | Earn it. After >=2 weeks of manual loop. |
| **3** | **JSON -> HTML template.** Skip MD parsing. Cleanest data shape, ugliest to author by hand (good for agent-authored input though). | ~60 LOC | If option 2 turns out to fight markdown's flexibility, or if Claude becomes the primary author. |

## Recommendation
Option 1 first. Graduate to option 2 only when:
- >=2 weeks of manual loop has run (per `feedback_tools_must_beat_paper.md`)
- The manual rendering has become a friction point worth automating
- The dashboard shape has stabilized (current shape may evolve over the first week)

If/when option 2 ships:
- Lives at `tools/dashboard-pipeline/` or similar (NOT in delta-kernel / cognitive-sensor / inpact)
- Single CLI: `python render_day.py today-2026-04-29.md` -> writes `today-2026-04-29.html` alongside
- Template extracted from current dashboard as `template.html` with placeholder slots
- MD frontmatter carries: date, week_of, mode, theme, spine[], posture_pills[]
- `##` headers become chapter titles, optional `> sub:` line becomes chapter-sub
- `- [ ] item` rows become checkboxes (with optional `* meta` indented for the gray sub-text)
- Special blocks via fenced code with language tag: ```fast-version, ```do-not-do, ```first-move, ```eod
- Pullability features (URL hash, tab title, state pane, copy button) baked into the template once, not re-implemented per page

## What this pipeline is NOT
- Not a new service. No port. No daemon.
- Not coupled to delta-kernel or atlas state. Optional later: emit signals to `/api/signals` if Atlas is up. Fail-soft.
- Not a substitute for the longer-arc consolidation (the architecture plan Bruke saved locally). This is a rendering tool, not a state engine.
- Not a replacement for the chat-shape. Option 1 stays available even after option 2 ships — for ad-hoc days where structured MD is overkill.

## Connection to other plan pieces
- `feedback_tools_must_beat_paper.md` — gates the move from option 1 -> option 2
- `feedback_database_agent_pair_shape.md` — the pipeline aligns with database+agent pair: the MD/JSON file IS the database, Claude (or the script) IS the agent
- `project_atlas_agenda_convergence.md` — the dashboard renders the agenda-book interaction shape; this pipeline is how that shape scales without writing new code each time

## project_delta_scp_v2

<a id="project-delta-scp-v2"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_delta_scp_v2.md`*
*Name:* project-delta-scp-v2
*Description:* Delta SCP v2 brownfield graph-memory upgrade — state-aware prune + flue + Supabase AST graph

Delta SCP v2 — brownfield upgrade landed 2026-06-21. **Committed** on branch `feat/delta-scp-v2-graph-memory` in the `pre-atlas` (hyphen) repo: `8dfee18` (vertical slice) + `489bdcc` (worker auto-populate). NOT merged to main; NOT pushed. Turns the static repo compressor into a state-aware memory filter. Core `compressTree` (the ~97% engine) left untouched per the build card; everything is additive.

**Worker wiring (489bdcc):** `fetchSourceFiles` extracted from `compressRepository` (one fetch feeds both compress + graph; compressRepository delegates, behaviour unchanged). `worker.tick` builds+persists the graph after a job durably completes — FAIL-SOFT (`populateGraph` swallows+logs; graph is a secondary index, never fails a complete job). Flag `SCP_GRAPH_AUTOPOPULATE` default true; needs migration 006 applied. Caught+fixed during commit prep: 2 NUL bytes in graph.ts keyId separators (git flagged it binary) → replaced with `|`.

**Critical gotcha:** delta-scp lives at `C:\Users\bruke\pre-atlas\` (hyphen), NOT the session cwd `C:\Users\bruke\Pre Atlas` (space). These are two different repo roots on disk. droplist lives in the *space* root. That's why the flue is file-based, not an import.

**What shipped (all pure cores tested, I/O thin per repo discipline — 35/35 tests, tsc clean):**
- `src/prune.ts` — state-aware anchor pruning. Anchors from `--anchor` paths / `--symbol` names / `--trace` file (extracts source paths from error text). Anchor+neighbor kept full; context collapses to stub. `anchored:false` => returns full map unpruned (never guts). Limitation: pure-mode "neighbor" = same directory, so flat repos (one src/ dir) get focus_ratio≈1 (no collapse). Fix = graph reader's true import-neighbours.
- `src/flue.ts` — `renderFlueMarkdown` (pure) + `emitToFlue` (writes .md drop to inbox). **Decision: file-inbox, NOT HTTP** — droplist works on a drop→packet grain, so zero edits to droplist; `SCP_FLUE_DIR` points at its watched inbox. HTTP POST deferred.
- `src/graph.ts` — `buildGraphRows` (pure: reuses existing regex extractor for nodes + `imports` edges resolved within repo) + `persistGraph`/`readFocusFromGraph` (thin Supabase I/O). **tree-sitter seam documented** for calls/inherits/implements edges (regex can't do those — said so plainly, didn't fake fidelity).
- `migrations/006_scp_ast_graph.sql` — Gemini's ast_nodes/ast_edges/view schema, hardened: `gen_random_uuid()` not `uuid_generate_v4()` (no extension needed, matches 004), enums wrapped in idempotent DO-blocks (migrate runner re-runs). Auto-applied (runner globs `*_scp*.sql`).
- CLI flags `--anchor/--symbol/--trace/--prune/--md/--flue`; old positional contract preserved.

**Ingestion bridge / graph sync (`ef4b8ae`, 2026-06-21):** Gemini proposed a per-file `syncFileGraph` (delete-and-replace keyed on a file-watcher). REJECTED that shape — flagged per Bruke's "trust your judgment": (1) there's no file-watcher; delta-scp is a whole-repo queue worker. (2) Per-file sync silently drops ALL cross-file `imports` edges (only sees the current file's inserted nodes, so A→B can't resolve B). (3) It mapped edges by `name` alone → basename collisions (`src/b/index.ts` vs `src/c/index.ts`). (4) CASCADE makes it decay (re-syncing B gives new UUIDs, deleting A→B). KEPT the valid kernel — idempotent delete-and-replace fixes a real orphan bug in my upsert (deleted files/symbols never got removed). Implemented at REPO scope, not per-file: delete repo's nodes (CASCADE clears edges) → insert all → resolve edges by FULL identity (file_path|name|node_type, never name-only) → insert. Adopted the one good idea from the proposal: `insert().select()` returns ids in one round trip. Added `resolveEdgeRows` (pure, tested: no-collision + drop-unresolved) + node dedup by identity (plain insert needs it; TS decl-merge/Py overloads). Lesson: per-file graph sync is wrong for whole-repo workers; edge resolution MUST use full node identity. 3 branch commits total: `8dfee18`+`489bdcc`+`ef4b8ae`. tsc clean, 38/40.

**Live-verified end-to-end 2026-06-21.** Migration 006 applied to live Supabase (project `aweolzawdxanxfmfsxgq`). Two self-cleaning smoke scripts, both green:
- `scripts/verify_graph.ts` (`909bba2`) — 6/6 direct `persistGraph` checks: writes nodes+cross-file edges, idempotent, delete-and-replace removes stale rows (drop file → 6→4 nodes), `readFocusFromGraph` one-hop closure, clean teardown.
- `scripts/verify_worker_e2e.ts` (`2774039`) — 4/4 FULL worker-loop checks: enqueue → `tick()` claim→compress→complete→auto-populate → cleanup. delta-scp/src = 20 files, 111 nodes, 40 edges.

**Bug the e2e caught (`2774039`):** `resolveJsImport` didn't rewrite TS-ESM `.js` specifiers to `.ts` source, so import edges silently dropped in every TS-ESM repo (delta-scp/src showed 0 edges). Fixed (strip ext, try `.ts` first) → 0→40 edges. Lesson: synthetic unit corpora used extensionless imports and missed this; the live run on real TS-ESM source exposed it. Regression test added.

5 branch commits: `8dfee18`+`489bdcc`+`ef4b8ae`+`909bba2`+`2774039`. tsc clean, 39/41 (2 integration skipped).

**Not committed to main / not pushed** — Bruke reviews. Remaining: tree-sitter edges (calls/inherits/implements — regex only does `imports`); HTTP flue target; wire the flue into the worker (currently graph auto-populates but flue is CLI-only). Deferred: tree-sitter true-AST extractor; HTTP flue target; wiring graph populate into the worker pipeline. Related: [[project-droplist-packet-engine]] (the Chainer), [[feedback-assemble-first-posture]] (tree-sitter named as the AST upgrade, not hand-rolled).

## project_delta_scp_web_ui

<a id="project-delta-scp-web-ui"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_delta_scp_web_ui.md`*
*Name:* project-delta-scp-web-ui
*Description:* delta-scp SaaS web UI (Vite+React) shipped on a Supabase-free adapter over the live :3012 engine

# Delta SCP web UI · SHIPPED (uncommitted) 2026-06-25

Ship of the Tier-1 [[project-claude-tools-saas-triage]] delta-scp product: "paste a repo URL → token-cheap symbolic skeleton for LLM context," leading with the **graph-memory layer** as the differentiator (not the compression — commoditized by repomix/gitingest).

## What's built (two pieces, two repos)
- **Front-end:** `C:\Users\bruke\Pre Atlas\apps\delta-scp-web\` — Vite + React SPA on **:5174**. Repo-URL input + example chips · context-budget cards (raw/skeleton/saved/% reduction) + interactive token-budget slider with model-window chips (8k/32k/128k/200k/1M) · graph-memory panel (node/edge counts, node-type breakdown, "most-connected files = read first" hub ranking, import-edge list) · grouped file tree with expandable per-file symbols · copy-to-clipboard (JSON + Markdown). `vite.config.js` proxies `/api` → `:3012` (no CORS).
- **Backend adapter:** `C:\Users\bruke\pre-atlas\services\delta-scp\src\demo-server.ts` — NEW file, **zero edits to existing backend**. Thin Supabase-free gateway that REUSES the real engine (`fetchSourceFiles`+`compressTree` from source.ts/compressor.ts, `buildGraphRows` from graph.ts) and returns the same `compressed_state` shape the production worker writes, plus the AST graph the real gateway (`server.ts`) does NOT expose over HTTP. Run: `npx tsx src/demo-server.ts` (listens :3012, no creds). Optional `SCP_API_KEY` Bearer.

## Why the adapter (key recon finding)
The real `server.ts` :3012 gateway is **Supabase-backed** (async job queue) and the service `.env` has **all creds blank** — so `npm start` 503s/can't enqueue. The compression engine itself is pure/offline. Bruke chose (AskUserQuestion) the thin sync adapter + surface graph from engine output. UI polls `GET /jobs/:id` until terminal, so it works UNCHANGED against either the adapter (immediate complete) or the real async queue (pending→complete) once creds are filled.

## Verified live (preview panel, real repo)
`sindresorhus/yocto-queue.git` → 8 files, 5 langs, **89.3% reduction (2.4k→252 tok)**, graph 9 nodes/2 import edges. Copy-to-clipboard confirmed `ok:true` (1739-char JSON) under a TRUSTED preview_click gesture — headless synthetic `.click()` and direct `clipboard.writeText` are sandbox-blocked (NotAllowedError), not a code bug; `lib/clipboard.js` has an execCommand fallback.

## Gotchas hit
- Windows entry-guard bug: `import.meta.url === \`file://${process.argv[1]}\`` never matches on Windows (backslash vs `file:///C:/`). Use `pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href`. (Same latent bug in the real server.ts.)
- preview_start rejects cwd outside the project root → web app lives in `Pre Atlas\apps\` (not next to the service in `pre-atlas\`). Frontend/backend split.
- Editing App.jsx triggers Vite full reloads that wipe React job state mid-verification; renderer also crashed to chrome-error once. Restart preview for a clean renderer; drive via example chips (controlled-input `preview_fill` doesn't update React state → Compress stays disabled).

## Hardened for public use + COMMITTED 2026-06-25
- Adapter: helmet headers, `express-rate-limit` (15 req/5min per IP, standard `RateLimit-*`+`Retry-After`), reject-when-busy concurrency gate (3, fast 429 not queue), tighter caps (maxFiles 4000 / 8MB total / 512KB file), 32kb body, x-powered-by off. SSRF guard (`validate.ts`) verified: `http:`/192.168/169.254 all 400. All env-overridable (`SCP_RATE_MAX`/`SCP_MAX_INFLIGHT`/etc). Burst-tested: 5×400 then 429.
- UI WCAG pass: ARIA live regions (loading/error=alert/health/verdict=status), aria-label on input + buttons, aria-expanded on dir/file toggles, aria-valuetext on slider, aria-hidden on decorative glyphs, :focus-visible outlines, prefers-reduced-motion, contrast-bumped muted vars. Verified live via eval.
- Commits: Pre Atlas `4592ccb` (UI + launch.json), pre-atlas `87e4679` (adapter + helmet/rate-limit deps). React auto-escapes all repo-derived text; no dangerouslySetInnerHTML.

## Not done
No hosting/deploy, no auth wall (strangers use it; rate-limit is the abuse control — optional `SCP_API_KEY` Bearer available). Graph layer surfaced read-only from engine output (no dedicated HTTP endpoint). Real Supabase async-queue path (`server.ts`) untested (no creds in `.env`). Worktree `Pre Atlas` still has many unrelated pre-existing dirty files (not mine).

## project_droplist_lifecycle_bricks

<a id="project-droplist-lifecycle-bricks"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_droplist_lifecycle_bricks.md`*
*Name:* project-droplist-lifecycle-bricks
*Description:* DropList project-lifecycle spine — bricks 1-4 built+smoke/break-tested 2026-06-25 (66 tests, §D 1-3 + B11 TOCTOU closed); NOT yet official — next = Merge + Run live + Use

**The arc:** Bruke wants DropList to be the thing he runs projects ON — not fest (hates that it
sticks in WSL). Recon (first-hand, 2026-06-25) proved DropList already IS ~70% of fest: the
`drop → packet → DAG → done_condition → tool-verify` engine is fest-style planning (phases/tasks/
Definition-of-Done) in pure Windows Python, no WSL. The 3 real gaps were: headless mode, cron,
and a prompt daisy-chain. Built all of it as a lifecycle spine:
`capture → plan → schedule → advance → check-in → act → track`.

**ALL 4 BRICKS BUILT + SMOKE/BREAK-TESTED 2026-06-25** on branch
`experiment/droplist-remediation-2026-06-15`. Suite **65 passed** (was 23 at session start, +42
tests). Built via two ultracode Workflows (design→build→adversarial-verify); each brick's DoD =
prove *mechanism→outcome*, not "200 OK". **NOT yet official** — see the Official-ization section
at the bottom. Commits this session: `343aeb4`(hydra) · `e44b3a4` · `fe03142` · `fc4e992`(DoD) ·
`08b5719`(2 break-found bugs) · `d8f199f` · `8e702e8`(§D 1-3) · `e0b4bde`. Core commits:

- **`e44b3a4`** — Brick 1 (mark-off) + Brick 2 (headless tick) + Brick 3 OS-cron scripts.
- **`fe03142`** — Brick 3 core (scheduler) + Brick 4 (daisy-chain).
- **`fc4e992`** — the DoD doc (see below).

**Brick 1 — Mark-off + Checklist** (PKT-011, `services/droplist/PACKETS/011_*.md`). New endpoints
in `server.py`: `POST /api/dag/{id}/node/{node_id}/complete` (242), `/reopen` (338),
`GET /api/dag/{id}/checklist` (313). Reuses `dag_update.apply_review` (mark-done + dependent-wake
+ flip-complete). `test_markoff.py` (11). This is the keystone — before it there was NO way to
check a task off from outside the engine.

**Brick 2 — Headless tick / living entity.** `droplist/daemon.py` + `droplist/__main__.py`:
`python -m droplist.daemon [--once] [--interval N]` (cron-drivable, HTTP-free) advances ready work,
materializes recurring, flags stale. `advance_dag` extracted from `graph_engine` (single engine
path). Server lifespan spawns a thread gated by `DROPLIST_DAEMON=1`. `test_daemon.py` (6).

**Brick 3 — Cron.** OS half = `scripts/{run,schedule,unschedule}_droplist_daemon.ps1` (Windows
Task Scheduler → `daemon --once`). In-repo half = `droplist/scheduler.py`: `due_jobs(schedules,
now, last_run)` w/ strict-prev-fire dedup, via **croniter** (assemble-first; added `croniter>=2.0`
to pyproject). `test_scheduler.py` (13), all on passed-in datetimes never real clock.

**Brick 4 — Daisy-chain** (the headline new capability). `droplist/chain_runner.py` + protocol
`chains/*.json` `{trigger:{on:cron|condition,expr}, steps:[{prompt,target_query,expect}],
on_report:{action:drop|complete_node|emit_signal}}`. Runner selects target tasks over DAG state,
runs step prompts through `llm.py` heuristic (no API key), writes `chain_reports.jsonl`, then
TAKES the action (drop = new packet; complete_node = flip done + wake deps; emit_signal). Wired
into `daemon._run_once` fail-soft. Example shipped: `chains/daily_ready_nudge.json`. Entrypoints
`tick(now)` / `run_chain(...)` / `validate_chain`. `test_chains.py` (9) proves report→action
creates state that didn't exist before.

**SMOKE/BREAK RUN DONE 2026-06-25** (`services/droplist/SMOKE_AND_DOD.md`, committed `fc4e992`,
is the runnable contract). Ran the full §B smoke (S1–S4) + §C break battery (B1–B12) via an
in-process TestClient harness on a scratch data dir → 14/14. Break-testing found and fixed **2
real bugs** (commit `08b5719`, suite now **63 passed**):
1. **Noise bug** — `run_chain` computed a per-step `ok` gate + `all_target_ids` but never used
   them, so a cron chain fired its `on_report` action even with ZERO matching targets (spurious
   "clarify intent" drops). Fixed: action only fires on real targets + passed expectations; cron
   window stays unconsumed so it fires when work appears. Regression test added.
2. **Inert example chain** — `daemon._run_once` advanced ready nodes to done BEFORE running
   chains, so `daily_ready_nudge` (targets `has_ready_node`) could never fire via the daemon.
   Fixed: chains run BEFORE advance.

**§D CLOSED 2026-06-25 (commit `8e702e8`, suite 65 passed):**
1. ✅ Scheduler wired into `daemon._run_once` — `load_schedules`/`due_jobs`/`mark_run` now have a
   live consumer; due `schedules.json` entries (drop/run_chain/tick) fire each pass against the
   LIVE data dir, dedup'd via `schedule_runs.jsonl`. A scheduled `drop` = templated cron reminder.
2. ✅ `do_not_reopen` lock 409 branch now tested.
3. ✅ Reopen no longer hand-rolls re-derivation — extracted `dag_update.recompute_states` as the
   single source of truth; both `apply_review` and reopen call it (no drift).

**§D#6 / B11 TOCTOU CLOSED 2026-06-25 (commit `fe44b06`, suite 66 passed):** `complete_node`'s
load→check→mutate→save→append was not atomic, so two concurrent `/complete` POSTs on the same node
both passed the not-done check and each appended a `node_completed` audit row (final DAG json stayed
correct — transitions idempotent — but the audit log double-counted; both returned `updates` instead
of one getting `already_done`). Fixed: extracted await-free `_complete_node_core` guarded by a
per-DAG `threading.Lock` (also holds vs the daemon thread). The in-lock not-done check is now the
compare half of an atomic compare-and-set; the race loser re-reads done → `already_done`. Regression
`test_concurrent_completes_yield_exactly_one_event` fires 2 real threads, asserts exactly one audit
row + one `already_done`; proven to fail pre-lock (2 rows) and pass post-lock.

**§B-S1 doc≠code CLOSED 2026-06-25 (in commit `fe44b06`, docs only, suite still 65).** The old
§B S1 smoke told you to `POST /api/drop` and read the DAG back — but that path NEVER leaves a
hand-markable plan: with default env a drop becomes a clarify packet with NO DAG
(`intake.py:78` gates DAG-build on `DROPLIST_ATLAS_SIGNALS_URL`), and when that var IS set
`graph_engine.run_graph_from_packet` calls `advance_dag` which auto-runs the DAG straight to
`complete` (`graph_engine.py:269`) — no `ready` node survives. Chose fix (a): rewrote S1 to
**seed** a `running` DAG with a `ready` head via `storage.save_dag` (the `test_markoff.py` shape,
a `python - <<'PY'` heredoc sharing `DROPLIST_DATA`), then drive it through the live API. Verified
end-to-end vs a live uvicorn server on a scratch data dir (seed→/api/dags→/checklist→complete
N1/N2/N3→complete). Also fixed stale S0 count (62→65). No code touched. (Note: a concurrent "@"
auto-committer folded these doc edits into `fe44b06` rather than a standalone commit.)

**STILL OPEN (hardening/posture, NOT inert-code bugs — deliberate backlog):**
4. Scheduler is naive-UTC, no DST/timezone test (croniter supports tz; whole system is naive-UTC).
5. Write endpoints are open (no token) — per-spec, local single-user; revisit at multi-user boundary.

**MAKE IT OFFICIAL — the gap between green-tests and load-bearing (Bruke asked 2026-06-25).**
Built ≠ Official. **Official = Merged + Running + Used.** PROGRESS 2026-06-25 (67 tests now):
- **Used ✅** — drove a REAL plan live through the server on :3073: seeded `DAG-OFFICIAL` (DropList's
  own official-ization as a 5-task plan), `GET /checklist` rendered it, `POST /N1/complete` → N1 done
  + N2 woke ready, `/api/brief` surfaces it. DropList now tracks its own official-ization.
- **Merged 🟡 — clean path ready: PR #24** https://github.com/lionestenzol/pre-atlas/pull/24
  is **MERGEABLE** (conflict-free, 80 files, +14.6k, 67 tests green on clean main). Built via an
  isolated `git worktree` off origin/main (never touched the dirty main tree): overlaid
  `services/droplist` + 3 daemon `.ps1` + `contracts/schemas/Signal.v1.json` (a hidden cross-dep —
  droplist's atlas-signal validation loads it; main's was older/narrower, this widens enums,
  backward-compatible set-monotone). DropList was NEVER on main → add-only → no conflicts. **Awaiting
  Bruke's merge click.** The big experiment-branch **PR #23 is CONFLICTING** (355 files, 101 commits,
  bundles everything) — PARKED as a separate future task ("tackle #23 + all the big things" later);
  do NOT need it to make DropList official — #24 is the path.
- **Running ✅** — always-on daemon INSTALLED + verified 2026-06-25: Windows Task Scheduler task
  `PreAtlas-DropList-Daemon` (logon + every 5 min, `-RunLevel Limited` no-admin, runs
  `scripts/run_droplist_daemon.ps1` → `python -m droplist.daemon --once`, logs to
  `services/droplist/daemon_cron.log`). Triggered live → exit 0, clean tick
  (`materialized=0 advanced=0 chains=0`), DAG-OFFICIAL untouched (autopilot:false held through a
  real pass). Single arm — the :3073 API server has no `DROPLIST_DAEMON=1` thread. Unschedule via
  `scripts/unschedule_droplist_daemon.ps1`. DAG-OFFICIAL N1(run-live)+N2(always-on) marked done
  live; N3(merge) now the ready next move (= merge pr#23).
- **Per-plan autopilot flag BUILT** (`e8bd3f6`): Bruke chose "Both — per plan". Daemon
  `_advance_stored_dags` skips any DAG with `autopilot:false` (hand-authored checklist you mark off);
  default True = pipeline DAGs auto-advance (non-breaking). DAG-OFFICIAL set `autopilot:false` so the
  daemon won't auto-complete it. Future create-plan front door defaults hand-authored → false.
Remaining to finish official: (2) install always-on cron/autostart [now SAFE w/ autopilot flag],
(3) MERGE pr#23, (4) UI front door [the one real BUILD], (5) decide auth + DST. Original path:
1. **RUN it live, end-to-end, with real data** (highest-signal proof): boot :3073, seed/drop a
   REAL plan, mark a task off via `/complete`, start `daemon --once`, provision a real
   `data/schedules.json` (e.g. a Mon-09:00 backlog-review drop), watch the `daily_ready_nudge`

2. **RUNNING / always-on**: install the Windows Task Scheduler cron

   ([[project-atlas-autostart-broken]] / `start_atlas.ps1`), or run the server with

5. **Decide the 2 §D deferrals**: auth (mirror `X-Atlas-Token` like atlas-map?) + DST/tz.

## REVIEW 2026-06-26 (32-agent adversarial, every finding verified) — 21 confirmed / 6 refuted (6 HIGH, 9 MED, 6 LOW)
Triggered by Bruke "look at the code babes" — the committed-but-UNREAD concurrent-builder code finally got a
real review. **Lesson: a green test suite is NOT a code review.** I had committed seqs 02-04 trusting 84
passing tests without reading them; the review proved the spend guard (Task F's whole point) was bypassable
for real dollars. Fan out adversarial verify on multi-session code BEFORE trusting commits.
[[feedback-agent-report-distrust]] [[feedback-verify-at-head-before-acting]]

**FIXED + committed `f6c97d0` (money-critical cluster; + `test_budget_guard.py`, 99 tests pass):**
- HIGH `llm.py` complete(): `litellm.completion_cost`→0 for unmapped PAID models (openrouter/auto) → logged
  $0 → `_today_ai_cost` never accumulates → daily budget never trips → unbounded spend. Now usage*rate
  fallback for non-ollama unmapped models (`_usage_cost`/`_rate_for`; over-count = safe).
- HIGH `llm.py` call_json(): hardcoded Sonnet $3/$15 regardless of MODEL → Opus output ($75/Mtok) under-counted
  5x. Now per-model rate.
- MED `server.py`: user `max_tokens` unclamped → one call blows the budget. Now `_clamp_tokens`→[1,4096]. +
  guarded `DROPLIST_DAILY_AI_BUDGET` float parse (bad env crashed the server at import).
- HIGH `DropList.spec`: bundled only `webview`, NOT litellm/fastapi/uvicorn → `/api/ai/*` CRASHES in the frozen
  exe (window opened, AI path dead — the earlier "run-verified" only checked server-up + `import litellm`, NOT
  the AI endpoint). Now `collect_all` litellm/fastapi/uvicorn → **exe must be REBUILT to pick this up**.

**3 HIGH — FIXED + verified live, committed `55e05ca` (2026-06-26):**
- ✅ `line.html` a11y dialog — `mountSheet()` now `role=dialog` + `aria-modal`, focus moved into first field,
  Escape-to-close, Tab focus-trap, focus restore on close. Verified via :3073 browser eval (role/aria + Escape).
- ✅ `line.html` hydrate data-loss — `hydrate()` is now merge-not-replace: local-only jobs (non-serverBacked,
  not in engine set, non-done) are concatenated + deduped by id, not wiped by `S.jobs=`. Verified: an injected
  offline-capture job SURVIVES a hydrate (count stable, no dupe).
- ✅ `sw.js` stale shell — network-first for HTML shells (`isShell`: navigate/document/`/`/`/chain`), cache-first
  kept for immutable icons; CACHE `v1→v2` so activate purges the stale shell. Verified: only `droplist-shell-v2`
  present, SW registered + controlling.

**REMAINING — next session (verified file:line, NOT yet fixed):**

  consistent; TestClient httpx deprecation is latent-only.

## Keystone (the bug the audit found — RESOLVED)
The audit's gating finding: `ui/line.html` was a disconnected localStorage twin — `tryWireLive(data)` called
on an undefined function, all state in `localStorage['droplist:state:v1']`, zero writes to engine routes.
FIXED: `line.html:1654` `EngineClient`, `:1706` `tryWireLive=()=>EngineClient.hydrate()`, real writes to
`POST /api/drop` (:1682) + node-complete (:410); localStorage demoted to cache. UI now SERVED —
`server.py:143` `GET /`→line.html, `:149` `/chain`→chain.html. Was gating all 4 bars; no longer.

## SaaS hedge (locked constraint, NOT a task)
Target = local-first installable app, NOT hosted multi-tenant SaaS (that's a storage+auth+deploy rewrite,
premature). Keep all state behind `storage.py` and all writes behind `auth.py` so a future hosted upgrade =
swap those two impls (JSONL→Postgres+tenant_id; shared-token→per-user JWT), not a rewrite. Already hardened
(keep): server-side key custody, constant-time X-Atlas-Token guard, narrowed CORS.

## Config surface (env knobs — now documented in services/droplist/README.md)
`DROPLIST_LLM` (heuristic|anthropic, legacy triage), per-provider keys (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY`/
`GEMINI_API_KEY`/`OPENROUTER_API_KEY`/`OLLAMA_BASE_URL` — picker only offers providers with a key present),
`DROPLIST_DAILY_AI_BUDGET` (default $5, 0=off), `DROPLIST_AI_RATE` (default 30/minute), `DROPLIST_PORT`
(default 3073), `DROPLIST_DATA`, `DROPLIST_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, `DROPLIST_DAEMON`.

`droplist-market-ready-DM0001` task .md files reconciled to git truth (`fest_status` + DoD ticked w/ notes);
`fest validate` PASSES. `fest progress` still 0% — binary limitation (counts only `fest task complete` + 20

## project_droplist_packet_engine

<a id="project-droplist-packet-engine"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_droplist_packet_engine.md`*

# DropList Packet Engine

**Home:** `services/droplist/` (moved into Pre Atlas 2026-06-08 from `C:\Users\bruke\OneDrive\Desktop\RA DAG files 4\mini-ship-1\mini-ship-1\`).
**Branch:** `claude/main-triage-26f4a5`. Untracked addition; not yet committed.
**Status:** all 4 acceptance gates green on Windows. The MVP ladder is retired in this repo.

## What it is

A capture-to-execution system. Drop messy human input -> one validated `WorkPacket` -> execution DAG -> tool-connected nodes with verified `done_condition` -> persistent operating state (entities, recurring, do-not-reopen lock) -> cross-domain command brief.

Built over four MVPs (1: contract / 2: engine / 3: governance / 4: operating layer). All MVPs shipped, all four acceptance gates pass. README has the full surface.

## The doctrine

`DOCTRINE.md` is the 6-line always-loaded preamble. `BIBLE.md` is the canonical reference (§§1-15). The Bible IS the manifesto Bruke wrote in chat 2026-06-07 — the MVP ladder generated the discoveries, the Bible captures them, and going forward all new work is an Execution Packet in `PACKETS/`, not a new MVP.

Six doctrines:
1. The graph has authority.
2. No node is done without evidence.
3. No tool action runs without a node.
4. No dependency is removed without reason.
5. No completed core is reopened unless validation fails.
6. Do not optimize interface before proving state.

## File map

| Path | Role |
|---|---|
| `BIBLE.md` | Living §1-15 contract |
| `DOCTRINE.md` | 6 lines, always quoted |
| `PACKETS/00N_*.md` | Execution Packets (the unit of work) |
| `droplist/` | The Python package (26 modules) |
| `test_drops.py / test_graph.py / test_tools.py / test_persist.py` | The 4 acceptance gates |
| `demo_data/` | Reference outputs from a real run |

## Session 2026-06-07 (3 packets executed; 1 code fix; 2 saves)

| Packet | Status | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| PKT-001 remove router orphan | ABORTED | Pre-flight grep caught wrong premise. `router.py` is load-bearing for `completion.py:185` dispatch via `selected_workflow`. 0 code touched. |
| PKT-002 windows portable python | SUPERSEDED | `shutil.which()` returned `.BAT` shim path on pyenv-win; broke allowlist. Masked failure looked like 3/4 gates passed. |
| PKT-003 invocation-probe python literal | DONE | `subprocess.run([cand, "--version"])` at module load returns bare command word. All 4 gates green. |

- OQ-1 ... OQ-10 (original)
- OQ-11 MVP 1 + MVP 2-4 are two parallel pipelines (router-driven completion vs domain-driven graph). Decision deferred until a consumer needs convergence.
- OQ-12 `packet.current_node` (router-DAG vocab) is a different concept from graph node IDs. Rename / remove only if Atlas reads it.
- OQ-13 ✓ resolved by PKT-003.

## Why this exists in Pre Atlas

DropList is the intake atom for Atlas. `WorkPacket.memory_update` + `entities/*.json` + `command_brief.build_brief()` are the shapes Atlas substrate can subscribe to. The n8n_webhook in MVP 3 is the explicit Atlas seam. Living under `services/droplist/` puts it next to its consumers (`cognitive-sensor`, `optogon`, `delta-kernel`).

## Stop 4 · PKT-006 retry buffer (SHIPPED 2026-06-16, commit 088821b)

**What landed:** New `services/droplist/droplist/retry_queue.py` (309 LOC) + `services/droplist/test_atlas_retry.py` (681 LOC, 10 cases). Wired enqueue into `atlas_signal.emit_signal` (HTTPError ≥500 + URLError/OSError branches) and drain into `graph_engine._maybe_emit_atlas_signal` (prune → drain → re-POST → mark_attempted, BEFORE current settle emit).

**Design honored:**
- `MAX_ATTEMPTS = 5` ([retry_queue.py:38](services/droplist/droplist/retry_queue.py:38))
- `BACKOFF_SECONDS = [60, 300, 1800, 7200, 43200]` = 1m/5m/30m/2h/12h ([retry_queue.py:37](services/droplist/droplist/retry_queue.py:37))
- `TTL_DAYS = 7` ([retry_queue.py:39](services/droplist/droplist/retry_queue.py:39))
- Dedup by `signal_id` ([retry_queue.py:175-177](services/droplist/droplist/retry_queue.py:175))
- Persistence at `$DROPLIST_DATA/signal_retry_queue.jsonl` ([retry_queue.py:36](services/droplist/droplist/retry_queue.py:36))
- Atomic file rewrite via `tempfile.mkstemp + os.replace` ([retry_queue.py:77-108](services/droplist/droplist/retry_queue.py:77)) — Windows-safe, crash-proof

**Behavior split (correct):**
- 4xx response → **NOT** enqueued (client-side bug, non-retryable)
- 5xx response → enqueued
- URLError/OSError → enqueued (DNS, timeout, conn refused)
- `DROPLIST_STRICT_EMIT=1` + invalid signal → returns BEFORE POST try-block, queue untouched (Stop-3 contract preserved)

**Test coverage (10 cases, all green):**
| Case | Covers |
|---|---|
| a | success path: no queue write, no retry events |
| b | failure → enqueue with attempts=0 + full payload preserved |
| c | drain on next settle: replay + retire + log success |
| d | max-attempts exhausted: drop + `signal_retry_exhausted` event |
| e | TTL prune at T+8d: `signal_retry_pruned` with reason=ttl_expired |
| f | dedup: same signal_id twice = 1 entry, second returns False |
| g | strict-mode validation failure → no enqueue (Stop-3 contract) |
| h | 4xx response → no enqueue (non-retryable) |
| i | 5xx response → enqueue (symmetric counter to h, catches comparator-flip) |
| j | integration: `_maybe_emit_atlas_signal` end-to-end drain + new emit |

**Verification (this session, 2026-06-16):** code-recon Verify mode produced 14-claim verdict table, all ✅. Full pytest gate green (23 passed in 19.00s). Commit message claims 33/33 across all runners (pytest 23 + standalone `test_atlas_signal.py` runner).

**Hardening beyond spec:** atomic rewrite (vs naive truncate), symmetric 4xx/5xx test pair, integration test (j) exercises production wire site, payload preservation assertion in case (b) guards against signal_id+url-only regression.

## project_external_code_tools_vs_stack

<a id="project-external-code-tools-vs-stack"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_external_code_tools_vs_stack.md`*
*Description:* DeepWiki/Cursor/Repomix/CodeSee vs my own code-understanding stack — verified comparison + OSS-shape findings + open decision

Compared 4 external code-understanding tools against my own arsenal (delta-scp, code-recon, groundwork/gw, lattice, seam, repo-inventory). Verified at HEAD, not from memory — first pass overstated the gap (good catch by the "don't act on soft claims" rule).

**Verified facts (file evidence, 2026-06-26):**
- `delta-scp` is signatures-only by design (`services/delta-scp/src/compressor.ts:5,9`; `extractSymbols()` :133) AND already git-clones remote repo URLs (`src/demo-server.ts:37` "real git clone", `:155` fetchSourceFiles; validateRepoUrl). So my "zero coverage for external repos" claim was WRONG — delta-scp ingests external repos by URL.
- `lattice` uses Cytoscape but vendored as committed loose JS (`apps/lattice/cytoscape-dagre.js`, `cytoscape-cose-bilkent.js`), NOT an npm dep (package.json deps = null). `gw map` ALSO draws an interactive Cytoscape system map (`~/TOOLS.md:68`). So I have TWO Cytoscape code-map tools = CodeSee already cloned twice.
- CodeSee is DEAD (shut Feb 2024 → GitKraken May 2024). GitKraken did NOT rebuild the auto codebase-map; it shipped "Automations" (workflow) and lists code-viz as "coming soon". GitKraken's "visualization" = commit/branch history graph, not codebase structure. Private repos need Pro $4.95/user/mo. GitKraken is a dead end for the automatic-map want.

**OSS-shape findings (search-stack, star counts real):**
- DeepWiki → `AsyncFuncAI/deepwiki-open` (17.0k★, MIT, active). THE one real upgrade: self-hosted, works on PRIVATE repos, supports Ollama/local models. Removes the public-only blocker that made hosted DeepWiki useless on Pre Atlas. Cost: heavy self-host (Next.js + Python + model endpoint).
- Repomix → `yamadashy/repomix` (26.2k★) IS the reliable OSS standard; no upgrade exists. The ONE thing it does that my stack doesn't: pack FULL file contents to a single file (mine are signatures-only). code2prompt/gitingest are near-identical peers.
- CodeSee → `MaibornWolff/codecharta` (463★) closest reliable heir; dependency-cruiser/madge (JS). None beat my own `gw map`/lattice for my needs.
- Cursor → already installed (`AppData\Local\Programs\cursor`); orthogonal (editor, not a map producer). OSS clones: void/continue/aider.

**INSTALLED ALL (2026-06-27, "install all" directive — verified, not assumed):**
1. ✅ `repomix` 1.15.0 global (`npm i -g`) + MCP `repomix` registered in `~/.claude.json` (`npx -y repomix --mcp`).
2. ✅ DeepWiki hosted MCP `deepwiki` (HTTP `https://mcp.deepwiki.com/mcp`) — public-repo NL Q&A.
3. ✅ `gw map` auto-refresh wired: `C:\Users\bruke\Pre Atlas\.git\hooks\gw-map-refresh.sh` (lock-guarded, backgrounded, never blocks/fails commit) called from `post-commit` (alongside existing sync_bridge). `.gitignore` updated (`.groundwork/`, `.gw-map-refresh.log`, `.gw-map.lock`). Validated: one run regenerated `system-map.html` (47 subsystems, exit 0). Hooks are local-only (not version-controlled).
4. ✅ `deepwiki-open` self-host at `C:\Users\bruke\deepwiki-open` — fully LOCAL/PRIVATE via Ollama: embedder `nomic-embed-text`, generator `qwen2.5:7b` (both already pulled; edited `api/config/embedder.json` + `generator.json` default_model), `.env` PORT=8001 + OLLAMA_HOST, backend deps in `.venv` (uv). Frontend `npm install` done (next binary present). **Bug fixed (code-as-furniture):** `api/api.py:401` called removed `app.add_websocket_route` → changed to `app.add_api_websocket_route`; backend then booted HTTP 200 on `/`, `/docs`, `/openapi.json`. NOTE: deepwiki-open is git-dirty (3 config edits + 1 bug fix), NOT committed.
   - **Start it:** backend `cd C:\Users\bruke\deepwiki-open && .venv\Scripts\python.exe -m api.main` (:8001); frontend `npm run dev` (:3000). Open :3000, paste a repo URL, check "Local Ollama". Ollama must be serving. Frontend dev server NOT live-smoked (deps verified, not run).
5. Cursor — already installed (`AppData\Local\Programs\cursor`), no action.

## project_fest_windows_native

<a id="project-fest-windows-native"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_fest_windows_native.md`*
*Name:* project-fest-windows-native
*Description:* The /fest CLI now runs as a native Windows binary (no WSL); where the binary, source, data, and rebuild steps live.

# fest → native Windows binary (WSL removed) — 2026-06-25

`/fest` used to get stuck because the skill wrapped every command in
`wsl -d Ubuntu -- bash -c "..."` and the WSL2 VM cold-boot would hang. Fixed by
building a **native Windows `fest.exe`** and migrating the data off WSL.

**Locations:**
- Binary: `C:\Users\bruke\bin\fest.exe` (on persistent user PATH as `fest`)
- Live data (single source of truth): `C:\Users\bruke\festival-project\`
- Go source (copy of WSL `/root/fest-install`): `C:\Users\bruke\fest-win\src\`
- Vendored private deps (`camp`, `obey-shared`): `C:\Users\bruke\fest-win\deps\`
- Full status doc: `C:\Users\bruke\fest-win\FEST_WINDOWS_PORT.md`
- Skill (rewritten, WSL removed): `~/.claude/skills/fest/SKILL.md`

**Code fidelity:** 963/965 source files byte-identical to upstream; only `go.mod`
(2 `replace` directives for the private deps) and `go.sum` differ. Straight
recompile, no logic changes.

**Rebuild:** `cd C:\Users\bruke\fest-win\src; $env:CGO_ENABLED=0; & "C:\Users\bruke\scoop\apps\go\1.26.3\bin\go.exe" build -o C:\Users\bruke\bin\fest.exe .\cmd\fest`

**Stress-tested (~45 adversarial inputs):** no crashes/panics/corruption.
Concurrency-safe (no dup IDs), input-sanitizing (illegal chars/reserved names
slugified), git-integrated. Two findings:
- **CRLF** — native fest writes CRLF; source/Linux data is LF. FIXED via
  `C:\Users\bruke\festival-project\.gitattributes` (`* text=auto eol=lf`).
- **TUI hang** — bare interactive `fest create festival` (no args) hangs headless
  with no TTY. Always use the flag form; skill already prescribes it. Not fixable.
- **`fest promote` source-unlink lock (2026-06-25 OPEN → FIXED 2026-07-07, native-only)** —
  root cause: `AtomicStatusChange` (`internal/commands/status/atomic.go`) does an
  `os.Rename` (or, for dungeon statuses, `MoveToDateDirectory` → `copyAndDelete`'s
  `os.RemoveAll`) directly on `festivalPath`. On Windows, a directory that is (or
  contains) any process's current working directory cannot be renamed or removed —
  and `fest promote` is naturally run from inside the festival being promoted. WSL
  build didn't hit this because Linux has no equivalent restriction. Result before
  the fix: festival copied to the new status dir but the source left as an empty
  shell, status_history not updated. Fixed by adding `escapeCwdIfInside(festivalPath,
  festivalsRoot)` at the top of `AtomicStatusChange` — steps the process's cwd out to
  `festivalsRoot` (always exists, untouched by the move) whenever cwd is `festivalPath`
  itself or a descendant, before either move strategy runs; no-op when cwd is already
  outside. Covered by new tests `TestAtomicStatusChange_CwdInsideFestival`,
  `TestAtomicStatusChange_CwdInsideNestedSubdir`, `TestEscapeCwdIfInside` in
  `atomic_test.go`. Rebuilt `fest.exe` and verified live: promoted festival

## project_file_inspection_nudge_hook

<a id="project-file-inspection-nudge-hook"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_file_inspection_nudge_hook.md`*
*Name:* project-file-inspection-nudge-hook
*Description:* PreToolUse advisory hook: nudges Bash cat/head/tail/grep/find calls toward Read/Grep/Glob, evidence-scoped from transcript-index findings

Built 2026-07-16 as script #3 (final) from the [transcript-index](project_transcript_index.md) pattern-clustering pass: `~/.claude/scripts/hooks/pre-bash-file-inspection-nudge.js`, registered as a `PreToolUse` Bash hook in `~/.claude/settings.json` (profiles `standard,strict`). Not a new tool — an enforcement gap. The Bash tool's own docstring already says "avoid find/grep/cat/head/tail/sed/awk/echo, use the dedicated tool instead"; ~140 calls in the corpus did it anyway. This encodes that existing policy as an automatic advisory.

**Why an advisory, not a hard block:** the documented policy has an escape hatch ("unless explicitly instructed or after you have verified a dedicated tool cannot accomplish the task") that a hook can't evaluate — it can't know whether that verification already happened. A wrong block costs real work; a wrong warning costs nothing. Matches this repo's existing hook convention (`pre-bash-git-push-reminder.js` is the same shape: stderr message, passthrough, exit 0) rather than the harder `pre-bash-commit-quality.js` gate style.

**Scope is evidence-grounded, not policy-speculative.** Checked actual corpus frequency before matching anything: `cat` (28 calls), `head`/`tail` (15+28), `grep` non-recursive single-file (50) — all real and matched. `sed -i`: 0 occurrences — excluded. Primary `awk`: 1 occurrence, and it was a legitimate text-scan (route-definition grep-like usage), not a file-edit substitute — excluded. `echo` with a `>` character somewhere in the command: 362 hits, but every sample checked was an incidental `>` elsewhere in a compound command (URL, format string, unrelated redirect), not a real `echo "..." > file` write — excluded. Three matchers shipped (cat/head/tail → Read, grep → Grep, `find -name` → Glob), three considered and dropped for lack of evidence.

**Deliberately narrow matching to avoid false positives:** only fires on simple single-purpose invocations — a bare command optionally piped into one trailing `head -N`/`tail -N`. Explicitly excluded: redirects (`>`), chaining (`&&`/`;`), command substitution (`` ` `` / `$(...)`), `tail -f` (live-stream follow, Read can't replace it), recursive `grep -r` (real multi-file search), and — the specific bug this design avoids — hyphenated filenames (`cat foo-bar.txt` must still fire; an earlier draft using a single big regex with a `(?!.*-)` lookahead would have silently failed to match any hyphenated path, which is common in this repo's kebab-case naming). Rewrote as a tokenizer instead of one big regex specifically to fix that.

**Tested:** unit-tested `checkCommand()` directly against 7 positive shapes (all correct tool + tip) and 10 negative shapes (all correctly silent, including the hyphenated-filename case and the deliberately-excluded `wc -l`). Then full stdin→stdout/stderr round trip with a properly JSON-escaped payload (an early test with hand-escaped shell JSON was itself malformed and silently no-op'd — not a hook bug, a test-harness bug, caught by checking `JSON.parse` behavior directly rather than trusting passthrough stdout as proof of a successful parse). Then through the real `run-with-flags.js` wrapper end-to-end, confirming: warning fires under `standard`/`strict` profiles, stays silent under `minimal` profile, stays silent when explicitly disabled via `ECC_DISABLED_HOOKS`, and stdout is byte-identical to input in every case (hooks must never mutate the tool call).

**Caveat:** hook registration in `settings.json` takes effect on session start — this session won't enforce it live; the next one will.

**How to apply:** No action needed — it's automatic. If it ever misfires, add `pre:bash:file-inspection-nudge` to `ECC_DISABLED_HOOKS`, or drop it to `strict`-only in settings.json if `standard`-profile sessions find it too noisy.

This closes out all three proposals from the transcript-index pattern-clustering pass. Related: [transcript-index](project_transcript_index.md), [memory-write tool](project_memory_write_tool.md), [register-preview-server tool](project_register_preview_server.md).

Yesterday (2026-06-27) FL 3.5.6 was fully reversed via `/groundwork brownfield` + binre/Ghidra: engine unpacked (5,313 fns, `CreateFruityInstance @0x5560bc`), plugin SDK ABI proven, `.flp` v3 format cracked (20-byte note records, pyflp reads FL3 wrong), headless 3xOsc render verified (13/13 pitches ±0.1 cents). Bearings: `fl356-sandbox\MASTERY_CAPSULE_fl356_2026-06-27.md` + `DEFINITION_OF_DONE.md` (all 7 DoD gates ✅).

Current renderer = Python host driving plugin voices into a master float buffer (`unpack-runtime/fl356_engine.py:283` master, `:317-322` mix). ⇒ **Route A: implement the sampler as a pure-Python voice mixed into the same master buffer.** Drum one-shots = native pitch = stdlib `wave` only, no new deps (engine is zero-dep). Pitched 808s later = assemble `resampy`/`scipy`. Route B (drive engine's internal sampler fn from Ghidra dump) REJECTED: not SDK-exposed, high-risk, nil drum fidelity gain.

**"Import wavs" = text event:** `.flp` embeds sample paths as text, e.g. `%\DrumKits_Old\Basic\General\C_HC.wav` (`%` = `Samples\` macro). Parser must capture that event id (S1a; classic-FLP candidate id=196). **Resolver needs basename-fallback:** C_HC.flp's `DrumKits_Old\` is gone but `C_HC.wav` exists at `MAINDIR\Samples\Packs\Extra\C_HC.wav`.

Proof target: `MAINDIR\Loops\Cool stuff\C_HC.flp` (real drum project: KICK/C_HC/BLIP/SHOT/BASS channels). Run all gates with python32 (`C:\Users\bruke\tools\python32\python.exe`). ## project_fl3_cli_prototype

<a id="project-fl3-cli-prototype"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_fl3_cli_prototype.md`*
*Name:* FL 3.5.6 CLI Prototype
*Description:* Working 32-bit Python ctypes host that loads FL Studio 3.5.6 native plugins and renders audio. Bypasses the packed engine via the documented plugin SDK ABI.

# FL 3.5.6 CLI — Plugin Host Prototype

**Location:** `C:\Users\bruke\fl356-sandbox\unpack-runtime\` — `fl356_engine.py` (library) + `fl356_render.py` (CLI) + `melody_render.py` (demo) + `fruity_host.py` (SDK reference)
**Status (2026-05-16):** WEAPON-CLOSED. End-to-end JSON-song → WAV CLI works. `python32 fl356_render.py --song <s.json> --out <s.wav>` renders deterministic 16-bit stereo WAV. 6/6 completion criteria PASS (FFT-verified polyphony). Path to `.flp` end-to-end is gated on a custom FL 3 note-record parser (pyflp reads FL 3 notes as garbage).
**Proofs:**
- `proto_3xosc.wav` — 2 s sustained C4
- `melody_3xosc.wav` — 5 s scale ascending + chord, 3-voice polyphony

## Architecture (verified)

- FL.exe = tiny Delphi shell (unpacked, 84 KB)
- FruityLoopsEngine.dll = ASPack-packed orchestrator. **Not needed. Did not unpack.**
- `Plugins/Fruity/Generators/*.dll` + `Plugins/Fruity/Effects/*.dll` = 30+ plugins, **all unpacked Delphi 5**
- All plugins export **ONE function: `CreatePlugInstance(TFruityPlugHost*, TPluginTag) -> TFruityPlug*`** — Image-Line's documented public plugin SDK ABI
- TFruityPlug vtable has 20 slots: DestroyObject, Dispatcher, Idle, SaveRestoreState, GetName, ProcessEvent, ProcessParam, Eff_Render, Gen_Render, TriggerVoice, Voice_Release, Voice_Kill, Voice_ProcessEvent, Voice_Render, NewTick, MIDITick, MIDIIn, MsgIn, OutputVoice_ProcessEvent, OutputVoice_Kill (in that order)

## Key gotchas

- Must run in 32-bit Python (plugins are 32-bit). Use `C:\Users\bruke\tools\python32\python.exe` (3.12 embeddable + pip + pefile).
- Host must allocate 6 wavetables (sine/tri/square/saw/anasaw/noise, 16384 floats each) and pass pointers via `host.WaveTables[0..5]`. 3x Osc reads these directly — NULL causes access violation.
- Host must allocate TempBuffers (4 × BLOCK_SIZE × 2 floats, contiguous).
- TFruityPlug uses `_stdcall` (WINFUNCTYPE in ctypes). First arg is always `this`.
- `Eff_Render` and `Gen_Render` are no-ops on 3x Osc (it's a hybrid generator with FPF_UseSampler bit). The voice-based path is `TriggerVoice → Voice_Render → Voice_Release`.
- Init order: SetSampleRate → SetBlockSize → SetEnabled(1) → SetPlaying(1) → TriggerVoice
- **CRITICAL:** `TLevelParams` layout is keyed on the plugin's `Flags`. If `FPF_NewVoiceParams (1<<21 = 0x200000)` is set → all-float. If NOT set (3x Osc 3.5.6's case, flags = 0x41d) → `TLevelParams_Old` with `int Pan`, `int Pitch`. A `float Pitch=200.0` interpreted as int is 0x43480000 ≈ 1.13 billion cents = silence. Pitch=0 happens to work either way because zero bytes parse the same.
- For sequential note triggers in the same channel, call `Voice_Release` then `Voice_Kill` — release alone leaves the voice in release-tail state, which doesn't matter for sound but slot reuse is cleaner.
- **CRITICAL CTYPES BUG:** The plugin holds a POINTER to the `TVoiceParams` struct passed to `TriggerVoice` and reads from it later (during Voice_Render). If the Python wrapper for that ctypes Structure is garbage-collected, the memory may be reused and the plugin will read corrupt data — typically observed as "earlier voices' Pitch silently resets to 0 when a later voice is triggered." Fix: keep all `_TVoiceParams` instances alive in a list for the duration of the render. See `fl356_engine.py vp_keepalive`.
- WiseUnpacker (`mnadareski/WiseUnpacker` v2.1.0) extracted the original Wise-format installer at `C:\Users\bruke\Dropbox\Cool Edit and Fruityloops\Cool Edit\Cool Edit and Fruityloops\FruityLoops 3.5.6 Full.exe`. Unpacked tree at `C:\Users\bruke\fl356-sandbox\MAINDIR\`.

## Public OSS resources needed for full CLI

- **pyflp** (`demberto/PyFLP`) — `.flp` parser. **Patched for Python 3.12** at `C:\Users\bruke\tools\python32\Lib\site-packages\pyflp\_events.py` (PR #204 backport, since pyflp v2.2.1 release predates Python 3.12). Parses FL 3.x headers/patterns; channel parse fails on `KeyError: ChannelID.GroupNum (145)` for some v3 files (not all); `proj.arrangements` always fails on FL 3 files (`NoModelsFound`). **NOTE PARSER IS BROKEN FOR FL 3** — returns garbage for positions/keys/velocities (e.g. pos=2155897920, key=C2563) on every FL 3 file tested. pyflp's `NoteEvent` decoder assumes FL 12+ byte layout. A custom v3 parser is required to drive the melody scheduler from real `.flp` data. Known-clean parse test case (channels + patterns, not notes): `MAINDIR\Loops\Cool stuff\C_HC.flp` (v3.4.0, 180 BPM, 10 channels, 13 patterns — all Samplers, no plugins).
- **FL Studio SDK headers** (`Martymoose98/FLStudioSDK` C++ or `tonikasoft/fpsdk` Rust) — public plugin ABI
- **LAME** — MP3 encode (already shipped in MAINDIR as `LAMEenc.dll`)

## Remaining work (engineering, ~1 week)

1. `SaveRestoreState` to load `.fst` preset files (FL has 1183 of these in `Samples/`)
2. Use pyflp to parse a `.flp` → channels + notes + pattern timing
3. Note scheduler that maps pattern events → TriggerVoice calls at sample-accurate offsets
4. Multi-plugin mixer (sum buffers)
5. Variable-length WAV writer

## Lineage / dead ends tried first

- Engine-DLL unpack attempts: Unipacker, AspackDie 1.41 + 2.2, orcastor/unpack — all failed (ASPack 2.42 with anti-RE behavior). DON'T retry; not needed.
- Runtime LoadLibrary unpack via thread+terminate: crashed in stub (likely needs FL.exe's COM/window context). Abandoned.
- VST2 hosting `FruityLoops VSTi.dll`: loaded successfully but chunk format is 32 bytes (not .flp) and engine still required. Abandoned.

The plugin-SDK path is the right answer; everything else was a distraction.

## project_fl_studio_help_corpus

<a id="project-fl-studio-help-corpus"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_fl_studio_help_corpus.md`*
*Description:* FL Studio offline help corpus + Gopher recon — Gopher's brain is cloud-side, but its local reference drop (Help/html/gopher/) is writable and now seeded with 3 unified manuals

# FL Studio help corpus → Gopher (Track A SHIPPED) + own corpus (Track B)

Installer `flstudio_offlinehelp.exe` (Desktop, 172 MB) deposits into
`C:\Program Files\Image-Line\FL Studio 2025\Help\`. Entry point is
`Help\index_offline.html` — at the **Help root, NOT inside `html\`** (there is no
`index.htm` in `html\`; that trips everyone up).

**Corpus:** 463 HTML (6.8 MB, ~60% content-to-chrome — nav lives in a separate
frame, so pages are clean) + 8 PDFs (110 MB).

## Gopher (FL Studio's built-in LLM agent) — what recon proved

- **Cloud brain, local mouth.** NO Gopher model/index/app code ships anywhere in
  the install. Docs call it "an internal web page inside FL Studio" and
  `System\Internet\error-offline.html` exists. **You cannot read what Gopher knows
  from the inside** — its corpus is server-side.
  (Caveat: the confirming step — string-searching `FLEngine_x64.dll` for endpoints —
  was blocked by a safety classifier. High-confidence inferred, not proven.)
- **It rides the MIDI scripting API.** Their words: it "works the same way MIDI
  scripting does." Gopher = LLM + the documented control surface. Not magic.
- **Its action surface is closed and fully enumerated** in
  `Help\html\gopher\gopher_control.htm`: transport, channel rack, mixer, playlist,
  plugins, piano roll (incl. *generating Python scripts*). Project access is
  gated by an explicit Approve/Deny prompt.
- **The one local seam:** `Help\html\gopher\readme.txt` says material dropped in
  that folder is referenceable by Gopher but hidden from normal docs. Their own
  `flkey2_unified_manual.htm` (67 KB, one whole Novation manual in one file) proves
  the **`*_unified_manual.htm`** convention is real and in use.

## The bigger insight — three corpora, one system

| Corpus | Tells you | Missing |
|---|---|---|
| `FLEngine_x64.dll` → [[reference_fl2025_function_db]] (56,508 fns) | what the code **does** | the names |
| Help: 463 HTML + 8 PDFs | what features are **called** | the code |
| Scripting API docs + embedded CPython 3.12 | what can be **driven** | — |

Join 1+2 → **names for the unnamed functions**. Add 3 → you can *act*, not just read.
The scripting API is the real prize, not the prose:
`midi_scripting.htm` (**205 KB**, the biggest doc in the corpus — Device API
reference), `pianoroll_scripting_api.htm` (36 KB), `fl_studio_remote_scripting_api.htm`.
FL ships **`Shared\Python\python312.dll`** — the runtime is in the box.

## Track A — SHIPPED 2026-07-14

Installed into `Help\html\gopher\` (needs **UAC elevation** — Program Files):
`harmor_unified_manual.htm` (256 KB) · `sytrus_unified_manual.htm` (127 KB) ·
`started_unified_manual.htm` (110 KB). 56 MB of PDF → 493 KB clean HTML (~115×).

Builder: scratchpad `gopher/build.py` (parameterized — new sources are a config edit).
Pipeline: **markitdown** (`pip install "markitdown[pdf]"`; PDFs are text-layer, NOT
scans — extraction is clean and even rebuilds parameter tables) → strip page furniture
(per-page copyright footers, running headers, bare page numbers, dot-leader TOC) →
**reuse the PDF's own TOC as the heading vocabulary**, matching titles back into the
body to rebuild real `<h2>/<h3>` structure (Harmor: 270/270 headings recovered).
Verified: 92/93/100% word retention (loss == the furniture we meant to drop), 0 dead
anchors, 0 unclosed tags.

**STILL UNPROVEN — the canary test.** readme.txt only *asserts* Gopher indexes local
drops; that's a vendor claim, not observed behavior. Verify by opening FL Studio →
Gopher tab → ask something ONLY in the Harmor PDF:
*"In Harmor's chorus, what are the settings for the Surround flanger preset?"*
Manual says **Order 9, Depth 7%, Speed 16%, Cross -51%, Mix 90%.** If Gopher returns
those, the drop works. If it waffles, the folder is decorative — stop investing there.

## Track B — corpus SHIPPED, join reframed 2026-07-14

`scratchpad/gopher/trackb_corpus.py` builds **`fl_help.sqlite`** (9.7 MB): all
**477 docs** (469 HTML + 8 PDF, 6.27M chars) via markitdown → **SQLite FTS5**
(`docs` + `docs_fts`, content-table + trigger). Verified: `MATCH 'surround flanger'`
ranks the Harmor content top. Offline, no server dep (search-stack :3070 was DOWN;
memory-hub :3071 up but unused). Built in 95s.

**KEY FINDING — the docs↔binary name-join is a DEAD END.** `FLEngine_x64.dll` is
symbol-stripped; its 56,508 function names are auto-generated (entry-address style),
so they do NOT overlap human-readable help titles. Title↔fn_name matching yields
only false positives (short tokens: entry/equal/_Put). **Do not chase this axis.**

**The join that WORKS: concept-based via the curated `flp_*` tables.** fl2025 DB has
`flp_event_map` (31 rows, real names: `ChannelID.Cutoff`, `FLP_NewChan`),
`flp_handler_roles` (9), `flp_loader_map` (32) — a hand-curated SEMANTIC layer.
`concept_bridge` in fl_help.sqlite splits those camelCase names into concepts and
FTS-matches the corpus → 71 rows from 36 seeds (e.g. `cutoff → filter plugin docs`).
Mixed precision — it's a demonstrated seam/seed, NOT finished. Next: rank/filter
by bm25 threshold + expand curated flp_* coverage (only 72 named events so far).

Related: [[project_fl_studio_mcp_port]] · [[reference_fl2025_function_db]] ·
[[reference_binre_re_stack]] · [[feedback_assemble_first_posture]] (markitdown named
before any hand-rolled parser).
