## project_fl_studio_mcp_port

<a id="project-fl-studio-mcp-port"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_fl_studio_mcp_port.md`*
*Name:* project-fl-studio-mcp-port
*Description:* FL Studio MCP vs REAPER MCP — why a wholesale port is impossible, and the measure:beat positioning upgrade that IS worth doing (Phase 0 done; FL exposes tsnum/tsden)

# FL Studio MCP — targeted port of REAPER's measure:beat positioning

Bruke uses **both** REAPER and FL Studio. Compared `karl-andres/fl-studio-mcp`
against `wegitor/reaper-reapy-mcp` (already installed + working, see
[[reference_reaper_reapy_mcp]]). Outcome: **do NOT port the REAPER repo wholesale**;
lift one narrow slice instead.

## The architectural asymmetry (why a wholesale port is impossible)

| | REAPER | FL Studio |
|---|---|---|
| Python model | **External automation server** — `python-reapy` runs a socket server *inside* REAPER; outside code drives it live with the full ReaScript API | **In-app sandboxed scripting only** — event-driven `device_*.py` (OnMidiMsg) + piano-roll `.pyscript`. No socket, no free-running process, no pip into FL's interpreter |
| Ceiling | Load any VST by name, insert media, full item/take/envelope model | **Cannot load plugins. Cannot create patterns.** Curated subset only |

That asymmetry is *why* fl-studio-mcp resorts to dropping JSON files + faking a
`Ctrl+Alt+Y` keystroke (FL must be the focused window or notes silently don't land).
It is not bad engineering — it's the only channel FL gives you.

**So:** capabilities → can't port (FL API wall). Transport/socket → can't port (FL has
none). Only the **positioning math + test discipline** are portable.

Cross-DAW work = **MIDI-file / stem handoff** (compose in REAPER or straight from
Claude → export `.mid` → drag into FL), NOT a live API bridge.

## Local install state

- Cloned `~/tools/fl-studio-mcp`, branch **`local/onedrive-paths`**, commit **`fb8df3d`**.
- **NEVER PUSH** — upstream is `karl-andres/fl-studio-mcp`, a third-party repo
  ([[feedback_never_push_to_repos_not_owned]]).
- That commit re-roots all 4 path helpers at `~/.fl-studio-mcp/`. They hardcoded
  `~/Documents/Image-Line/FL Studio/Settings`, which **does not exist here** —
  Documents is OneDrive-redirected (real path: `~/OneDrive/Documents/Image-Line/...`).
  JSON IPC is scratch, not FL config, so a dedicated local dir is correct *and* keeps
  the rapidly-rewritten JSON off OneDrive (sync could lock a file mid-write).
- Subdir split preserved (`Hardware/FLStudioMCP` vs `Piano roll scripts`) — both
  channels use the filename `mcp_response.json` and must not collide.

## PHASE 0 — DONE. FL exposes the time signature.

Ground truth is on disk, no install needed:
`C:\Program Files\Image-Line\FL Studio 2025\System\Config\Piano roll scripts\Piano roll script reference.txt`

```
Score:  PPQ    # ticks per QUARTER note (read-only)
        tsnum  # project time-sig numerator   (read-only)
        tsden  # project time-sig denominator (read-only)
Note:   time, length  # TICKS
        velocity      # 0.0-1.0  (NOT REAPER's 0-127 — do not port that)
Marker: tsnum, tsden  # time-sig MARKERS exist -> mid-song sig changes are readable
```

**This killed the planned `beats_per_bar` param / 4/4 fallback — unnecessary.**

### The port is strictly MORE correct than its source

REAPER's `src/utils/position_utils.py` hardcodes 4 quarter-notes per bar:
```python
time = RPR.TimeMap2_QNToTime(project.id, (measure - 1) * 4 + full_beat - 1)
#                                                       ^^^ 4/4 ONLY
```
→ REAPER's positioning **silently mis-places every note in 6/8, 3/4, 7/8.**
FL reads the real signature, so the port does not inherit the bug.

### Locked math (format `"measure:beat,fraction"`, 1-based measure+beat, fraction = ms 000-999 of a beat)

```
ticks_per_beat = PPQ * 4 / tsden      # tsden=4 -> quarter; tsden=8 -> eighth
ticks_per_bar  = tsnum * ticks_per_beat
ticks = (measure-1)*ticks_per_bar + (beat-1)*ticks_per_beat + (fraction/1000)*ticks_per_beat
```

## PHASE 1 — DONE. Commit `5898aa5` on `local/onedrive-paths` (never pushed).

`~/tools/fl-studio-mcp/src/fl_studio_mcp/utils/positioning.py` — pure,
stdlib-only, zero deps:
- `measure_beat_to_ticks(pos, ppq, tsnum, tsden)`
- `ticks_to_measure_beat(ticks, ppq, tsnum, tsden)`
- `measure_length_to_ticks(length, ppq, tsnum, tsden)`

`~/tools/fl-studio-mcp/tests/test_positioning.py` — 27 tests, all pass, run via
plain system `python -m pytest` (`PYTHONPATH=src`, no `uv sync`, no install
auth — pytest 9.0.2 already present system-wide). Covers: origin/boundary/
fraction cases in 4/4 · explicit 6/8 case proving `tsnum*ticks_per_beat` (288)
≠ a hardcoded-4-beats REAPER-style formula (192) · round-trip ticks↔measure
across 4/4, 6/8, 3/4, 7/8 · malformed-input / 0-based / fraction-overflow
rejection (`ValueError`).

## NEXT — Phase 2 (needs Bruke's authorization — modifies the in-FL pyscript)

Wire measure support into `ComposeWithLLM.pyscript`'s `add_notes_to_piano_roll`
/ `add_chord_to_piano_roll` (currently crude `int(ppq * note_time)` quarter-note
math). Inline a copy of the Phase 1 math (FL's pyscript sandbox can't import
from `src/`) and **FAIL LOUD if `flp.score` markers include a time-sig change**
mid-song, rather than silently mis-placing notes past that point — see
"Known limit to guard" below.

## Blockers (Phase 2+ only)

1. **loopMIDI NOT installed** — hard prereq for the FL MCP's MIDI trigger. Third-party
   GUI installer; **Bruke must run it himself** (Claude does not download/run installers).
2. **`uv sync` on the external repo was DENIED** by the auto-approver (installs/builds
   untrusted third-party code). Phases 2-4 need Bruke's **explicit authorization**.
   Wiring it into Claude's MCP config also means Claude auto-launches that code every
   session — a knowing opt-in.

## Known limit to guard (code-as-furniture)

Phase 1 uses the **project** time signature only. FL supports **time-sig markers**
(mid-song changes). The pyscript must **FAIL LOUD if ts markers are present**, not
silently mis-place notes. Marker-aware bar-mapping = optional Phase 5.

Related: [[reference_reaper_reapy_mcp]] · [[feedback_assemble_first_posture]] (this is a
port of proven code, not a hand-roll) · [[feedback_never_push_to_repos_not_owned]]

## project_four_part_pipeline_topology

<a id="project-four-part-pipeline-topology"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_four_part_pipeline_topology.md`*
*Name:* project-four-part-pipeline-topology
*Description:* Verified (first-hand, 2026-06-21) topology of the Drop List / Cycle Board / Atlas / Lattice vision vs. actual code — and where Supabase really is

Bruke's "4-part stateless multi-agent pipeline" vision (Drop List → Cycle Board → Atlas → Lattice on a Supabase Realtime bus) does **NOT** exist as described. First-hand code-recon verdict (rg/Read/es, not subagents — subagents confabulated the delta-scp claim):

**Actual topology = delta-kernel hub-and-spoke (SQLite, :3001), one-way HTTP seams:**
- Drop List = SoT ✓ — `services/droplist/` Python, append-only JSONL, :3073. Emits Signal.v1 via `atlas_signal.py:286` (urllib POST).
- Atlas seam ✓ — delta-kernel `app.post('/api/signals/ingest')` server.ts:1806. Agent routing real: `node_router.py:16-48` (human/tool/reasoning), `directive.ts:44` (target_agent claude_code|optogon|human).
- Lattice = UI ✓ — `apps/lattice/index.html:2585` fetches delta-kernel `/api/lattice/viewmodel` (server.ts:2004), built by `lattice-projection.ts` from idea_registry + droplist signals.
- Cycle Board = a VIEW, not an engine — localStorage SPA syncing to delta-kernel `/api/cycleboard` (server.ts:2343/2352). Zero droplist refs, zero realtime/websocket.

**BUSTED parts of the vision:** Cycle Board doesn't consume Drop List; nothing uses Supabase Realtime; no event bus ("Atlas intercepts events" is one-way writes, not interception); Lattice isn't wired to delta-scp.

**Supabase reality (machine-wide `es @supabase`):** NOT in droplist or cycleboard, either repo. NO python supabase pkg. The only legit install is `pre-atlas\services\delta-scp\node_modules\@supabase` (delta-scp compresses repos→symbolic map, uses Supabase for AST graph migration 006). Other installs are unrelated projects (ask-jamaal-v0 = "Jamaal V0", my-habit-tracker, STRUDEL, n8n, global supabase CLI). Bruke's hosted Supabase web projects are PAUSED — separate from this pipeline.

**TWO-REPO SPLIT (load-bearing):** `C:\Users\bruke\Pre Atlas` (space) = the 4 parts. `C:\Users\bruke\pre-atlas` (hyphen) = delta-scp (+ duplicate cycleboard/droplist copies). The Supabase/TS substrate the vision leans on lives in the OTHER repo. Phase 4 (Lattice mesh) requires resolving this split.

Spec to wire it (if pursued): Supabase shared `drop_list` (UNIQUE input_hash = atomic dedup, the only real justification — multi-device) + `events` table as the Realtime bus, behind a `DROPLIST_STORE` adapter (Repository pattern, JSONL default). See [[project-droplist-packet-engine]] and the intake chainer (POST /api/drop, committed 3117dec). Meta-lesson: do verification recon FIRST-HAND; subagents fan out breadth but confabulate causal stories.

## project_glyph_dedup_experiment

<a id="project-glyph-dedup-experiment"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_glyph_dedup_experiment.md`*
*Name:* glyph dedup experiment (closed inconclusive)
*Description:* 2026-05-03 experiment in minidocs-shape-engine/experiments/glyph_dedup/ — dual descriptor (raster SHA + skeleton-graph topology). Aborted, key lesson logged.

# Glyph dedup experiment — CLOSED INCONCLUSIVE

**Status:** ABORTED 2026-05-03 at /weapon task 4/6. Built code stays. Numbers in `experiments/glyph_dedup/report.json`.

**Location:** `minidocs-shape-engine/experiments/glyph_dedup/` in worktree `interesting-chaum-2944a8`.

## What was built

- `dedup.py` — extract glyphs via cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats, compute BOTH raster_hash (sha1 of canonical 32×32 binary) AND topology_hash (n_strokes, n_junctions, n_endpoints, n_holes, top-3 angles, top-3 length ratios via cv2.ximgproc.thinning + skeleton graph trace).
- `make_corpus.py` — synthetic 2-clean + 1-noisy pages via PIL DejaVu Sans 18pt + Gaussian noise σ=6.
- Two contact sheets: `top_glyphs_raster.png`, `top_glyphs_topology.png`.

## Why aborted

Pipeline works. Numbers don't pass the floors I set:

```
                          FLOOR    GOT       SIGNAL
clean-clean topology      0.70    0.6250 ❌  content-bound (pages have different letters)
noisy-clean topology      0.30    0.1750 ❌  synthetic noise too harsh
clean-clean raster                0.6429    baseline
noisy-clean raster                0.0306    baseline
                                            
                                  topology gave 5.7× improvement on noise
                                  but absolute numbers below thresholds
```

## Key lesson (use in future scoping)

**Don't validate descriptors on synthetic corpora.** The bottleneck wasn't the descriptor — it was the test corpus:

- Two PIL-rendered pages with different content → cross-page jaccard ceiling is bound by content overlap (~36 shared / 56 total = 0.64). No descriptor can break that ceiling.
- Synthetic Gaussian noise (σ=6, 15% brightness bias) is harsher than real scan noise. Real PDFs are essentially noise-free; real scans are mild.
- The right test is real data: born-digital PDF rasterized at 200 DPI vs photographed/scanned version of same.

## What this fed into

Plan file `~/.claude/plans/splendid-tumbling-sundae.md` — the layered build supersedes this experiment. Phases 1-3 (Leptonica + FAISS + DINOv2) explicitly skip rolling our own descriptor; the lesson here is why.

## Mission state

Aborted weapon mission state at `experiments/glyph_dedup/.weapon/` (mission.json, plan.md, progress.jsonl, spec.md). Useful for future weapon postmortems on what went wrong (synthetic-corpus design, fixed acceptance floors before seeing data).

## project_groundwork_cli

<a id="project-groundwork-cli"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_groundwork_cli.md`*
*Name:* project-groundwork-cli
*Description:* Standalone recon→map→plan CLI (gw) lifted from Pre Atlas audit tooling, runs on any repo without the Claude harness

**groundwork-cli** — a real installable CLI (`gw`) that points at any repo / local codebase and emits (1) a current interactive `system-map.html` and (2) a proof-gated `plan.md`. Lives at `C:\Users\bruke\groundwork-cli\` (own git repo, init'd, **uncommitted** as of 2026-06-25). Built this session from Bruke's ask: "analyze the Pre Atlas system map + flesh out a standalone program for repos/local codebases = `/code-recon` + `/fest` standalone." He chose: map+plan / real installable CLI / deterministic-first + LLM optional.

**Assemble-first, not invented.** The recon+map engine already existed in Pre Atlas: `audit/build_system_index.py` (subsystem indexer, was hardcoded to `REPO_ROOT`) + `audit/imports/_build_map.py` (Cytoscape cose-bilkent map generator, already `--root`-aware, docstring "build a system map for any repo"). I lifted `_build_map.py` wholesale into `groundwork/build_map.py` (repointed its asset source dirs to the package's `assets/`; it already has CDN fallback), wrote a de-hardcoded `indexer.py` (auto-detects monorepo dirs → top-level code dirs → single-package fallback; generic port detection; dropped the start_atlas.ps1 coupling), wrote a NEW `planner.py` (reuses `build_map.compute_insights`+`compute_churn` → tasks with verifiable DoD; `--fest` scaffolds a festival shell best-effort), `cli.py` (`gw index|map|plan|run|doctor`), `pyproject.toml` (console_scripts `gw`), README. Vendored cytoscape.min.js + cose-bilkent into `groundwork/assets/` for offline render.

**Verified live 2026-06-25:** `gw run` on Pre Atlas → **37 subsystems** detected (vs the stale hand-typed "3 services" in `system-map.html`), 12 responding on a port, plan = 11 untested/2 stale/10 hotspots. Self-run green too. Output lands in `<repo>/.groundwork/` (add to .gitignore). Windows cp1252 console glyph crash fixed (ASCII output + utf-8 stdout reconfigure) — [[reference_synthetic_github_api]] unrelated; the cp1252 footgun matches the project's Windows-quirk pattern.

**Known rough edges / roadmap:** (1) FIXED 2026-06-25 (same session) — LOC over-count from generated/data files. Bruke flagged "looking at strudel repo?"; verified-busted (STRUDEL is a separate sibling at `C:\Users\bruke\STRUDEL`, zero nesting in Pre Atlas, no strudel entry in index). Real cause: `atlas_clusters.json` (3.0M lines) + `.bak.json` counted as LOC, and anatomy-research's vendored repos (browser-use/firecrawl/vendor-singlefile) outside node_modules. Fix in indexer.py: DATA_EXT (.json not counted for LOC), SKIP_FILE (.min/.bak/-data.js/lock excluded), LOC_SIZE_CAP 2MB, prune VENDOR_DIRS + nested-.git dirs. Result: cognitive-sensor 4.3M→64K LOC, anatomy-research 2761→1 file. (2) No inter-subsystem edges yet (import-graph absent) → orphan/fan-in-hotspot findings skipped; plan leans on fs+git signals only. (3) `--fest` creates festival shell, doesn't populate task files from plan.md. (4) LLM enrichment is a stub seam (no API call). (5) not committed; not packaged to single binary.

This is the `groundwork` skill's pipeline (delta-scp/[[project-groundwork-skill]] → code-recon → fest) made standalone. Relationship: skill stays the in-harness orchestrator; `gw` is the harness-free product. Sibling effort [[project-skill-pytools]] (`C:\Users\bruke\skill-pytools`) ports the recon *toolkit* per-tool to Python — distinct from this map+plan *product*; don't conflate or rebuild either.

# /groundwork skill — verified plans from real code (2026-06-25)

A SKILL.md at `~/.claude/skills/groundwork/SKILL.md` that orchestrates three
existing native-Windows tools into one pipeline. **No new code — pure
orchestration** (assemble-first doctrine).

**The pipeline:**
- Stage 0 ORIENT — **delta-scp** (svc `C:\Users\bruke\pre-atlas\services\delta-scp`, port 3012): cheap whole-repo symbolic skeleton → pick candidate regions. Optional; skip for small scopes.
- Stage 1 RECON — **code-recon** skill: file:line evidence trail (locate/trace/audit/prior-art), absence claims multi-angle. The trace graph = parallelism structure.
- Stage 2 PLAN — **fest** (native `C:\Users\bruke\bin\fest.exe`): festival whose tasks cite real file:line, sequence numbers from the dep graph, DoD written as *provable* claims. Flag form only (TUI hangs headless).
- Stage 3 PROOF-GATED DONE (`/groundwork verify`) — code-recon verify against the DoD before `fest task complete`. No done without proof (same as DropList `done_condition`).
- `/groundwork drift` — re-recon task citations vs current code; flag moved/vanished code.

**Why it became possible NOW:** all three run native on Windows after the
[[project-fest-windows-native]] port. Before, fest-in-WSL + recon-on-Windows-repo
couldn't share a working directory. The Windows port was the enabler.

**Doctrine baked in:** "subagents confabulate, verify first-hand" — every fest task
inherits code-recon's anti-confabulation rule. Plans grounded in evidence, not hunches.

Registered + live (appears in skill list). delta-scp service may need starting for Stage 0.

<a id="project-groundwork-stack-roadmap"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_groundwork_stack_roadmap.md`*
*Description:* The 4-tool 'eyes before hands' loop (bearings/code-recon/groundwork/fest) — DoD + roadmap + the one critical-path move that closes it.

**The four tools are ONE system, not four:** an "eyes before hands" operating loop with one law — *no claim without evidence, no "done" without a tool-verified proof.* DoD + roadmap doc: `C:\Users\bruke\.claude\GROUNDWORK_STACK_DOD_ROADMAP.md` (authored 2026-06-28, grounded in verified current-state).

**The loop:** `bearings` (WHERE AM I — zero-LLM git+transcript digest) → `groundwork` (MAP+VERIFY+PLAN — chains delta-scp→code-recon→fest) → `code-recon` (WHAT'S REAL — 7-section evidence trail, every claim file:line) → `fest` (THE WORK + PROVE DONE — proof-gated DoD) → back to `bearings` (where did we land).

**Current state (verified 2026-06-28):** code-recon = solid/solo-trustworthy (orient gate `scripts/orient.mjs` FRESH/STALE, 6 modes). groundwork = good but stages hand-run (3 stages + brownfield mastery mode added this session, see [[reference_binre_re_stack]]). fest = solid but proof-gate is *advisory*. bearings = shipped v0.1.0 PUBLIC but *loop-blind* (see [[project_bearings_external_package]]). The loop runs end-to-end **by hand** (proven on STRUDEL this session); the gap is automation + a shared proof substrate.

**THE critical-path move (highest leverage, do first):** **hard-gate `fest task complete` on a passing `code-recon verify`.** This converts "no done without proof" from doctrine into structure — everything downstream depends on trusting a "done." Then: (2) code-recon emits a machine-readable evidence ledger (JSON sidecar), (3) groundwork emits one content-addressed dossier per run (capsule+festival+ledger under one hash), (4) bearings reads loop state.

**Roadmap horizons:** H0 now (shipped) · H1 harden each tool solo · **H2 close the loop via ONE shared content-addressed evidence ledger** · H3 self-reinforcing (feed tool-outcome ledger combo/synergy signal back so groundwork learns where to look) + optional productize (bearings + groundwork-cli already PUBLIC).

**Assemble-first / non-goals:** H2's substrate **already half-exists** — reuse the [[project_stack_integration_seam]] `seam.v1` Receipts + [[project_tool_lattice_architecture]] content-addressing + [[project_tool_outcome_ledger]]; do NOT build a 2nd ledger. One orient gate not two (groundwork Stage 0 defers to code-recon's `orient.mjs`). bearings stays zero-LLM. No UI until the loop closes (TGT law). Every brownfield capsule re-verifies at HEAD (STRUDEL's `.audit` was 3mo stale — the failure mode to design against).

**Open framing fork (ask the user):** trajectory primary = *close the loop* (internal leverage) vs *ship the stack as products*. Doc treats loop-closing as primary, productization as H3 secondary.

Every task DoD is tool-provable (file:line or passing test), verified at HEAD. Working dirs are junctions under `festival-project/projects/`.

## project_hh_trp_dataset

<a id="project-hh-trp-dataset"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_hh_trp_dataset.md`*
*Description:* WaivOps HH-TRP trap drum-loop dataset — Hungarian NMF assignment shipped, timing repair shipped-but-partially-stale, two-rater benchmark annotation tooling shipped. Festival HT0003 session 3 in progress, phase-gate confirmation pending.

<system-reminder>Superseded 2026-07-14 — the prior version of this memory (label_fix.py "ATTEMPTED, NOT solved") is now WRONG. Hungarian assignment was validated GO and shipped to production. Read below, not that.</system-reminder>

WaivOps **HH-TRP** = 15,000 trap drum loops, on disk at `C:\Users\bruke\STRUDEL\hh_trp_index\`. Dataset/schema facts (dataset source, 8-bar/32-beat verification, key map, JSON label shape) are unchanged from the original spike — see git history of this file if needed. This entry covers **festival HT0003** (`festival-project\festivals\active\hh-trp-audit-delegation-HT0003`), which executes the WaivOps audit's engineering-owned Task Delegation Plan against the real codebase.

## Festival status (2026-07-14, mid-session-3)

**Fully gated + approved (Sessions 1-2), 6/10 phases:** `001_DEDUP`, `001_INSTRUMENTATION`, `001_NMF_VALIDATION`, `002_SPLIT`, `002_NMF_PRODUCTIONIZE`, `002_TIMING_REPAIR`. All committed to STRUDEL as `6de3ab58f`.

**Session 3, in progress:**
- `003_ANNOTATION_TOOLING` — real work done, verified, committed (`d771aceb8`). All 5 sequence tasks (main + 4 gates) marked complete in fest. **Phase-gate Step 1 asked, NOT yet confirmed by Bruke** — resume by re-presenting the Required-Outcomes checklist and asking to advance.
- `003_BASELINE_FREEZE` — real work done, verified, committed (`d771aceb8`, plus 2 inline fixes at `c87ba925b`/`5c9a05ea3` from the freeze agent). **fest bookkeeping NOT STARTED** — main task + 4 gates + phase gate all still pending in fest.
- `004_CONTROLLED_COMPARISON` / `005_ML_REASSESSMENT` — `non_coding_action` phases, not started. Their entire job is to *record* BLOCKED/DEFERRED (per the audit's own governance rule: no ML claim until a controlled comparison runs on the frozen benchmark). Trivial once 003 closes.

**Resume point for a fresh session:** confirm `003_ANNOTATION_TOOLING`'s phase gate (4-step `fest workflow advance`/`approve`), then run `003_BASELINE_FREEZE`'s full bookkeeping chain (already-verified real work, just needs `fest task completed` × 5 + phase gate), then 004/005.

## What shipped (Sessions 1-2)

**002_NMF_PRODUCTIONIZE — GO, shipped.** `batch_grooves.py`'s `ASSIGNMENT_METHOD="hungarian"` (default) wires `label_fix.py`'s `hungarian_assign()` (`scipy.optimize.linear_sum_assignment` vs. measured spectral centroid) into production, replacing the old positional `freq_rank` sort. Validated: 60.8% (233/383) vs. ordinal's 37.1% (142/383) against ground truth, never worse on any of 9 validation files, 0.0% repeated-run disagreement (NMF decomposition here is deterministic — no `random_state` ever pinned). Confidence persisted per lane (`exp(-cost)` scale). Rollback flag present.

**002_SPLIT — shipped.** Group-aware `split_train`/`split_val`/`split_calibration`/`split_frozen_test` manifests via `sklearn.GroupShuffleSplit`, keyed on `duplicate_groups` (from 001_DEDUP: 151 rows/60 near-dup groups + 14,849 singletons). Zero cross-split leakage, verified.

**002_TIMING_REPAIR — shipped, BUT SEE FRESH FINDING BELOW.** Added `lane_offsets_ms_filtered`/`kick_lag_ms_filtered`/`anchor_lag_ms_filtered` columns (raw columns untouched — 7 other consumers depend on raw semantics). `emit_strudel.py` now sources `.late()` from filtered fields when present.

## Fresh finding (Session 3, `003_BASELINE_FREEZE`) — the misattribution filter was a no-op the whole time

Freezing the baseline against the 1,500-loop `split_frozen_test` manifest surfaced that `filter_misattributed_hits_nmf.py`'s "drop rate" was an artifact, not real filtering. **Verified directly**: across all 1,500 frozen-test loops, `hit_matrix_filtered` is byte-identical to `hit_matrix` — 0 of 320,494 hits dropped. The filter judges each hit against a re-run of the *same deterministic NMF + same onset detector on the same audio* that produced the hit, so every hit confirms itself by construction — a tautology.

The historical ~4.87% drop rate came from a different bug: `filter_misattributed_hits_nmf.py` (pre-fix) split `loops.drums` in **raw** order with no freq-rank sort, then matched it positionally against `decompose(sort=True)`'s frequency-sorted components. Wrong for **6,345 of 15,000 kits** (independently recounted, exact) whose raw order ≠ freq-rank order. The "drops" were mislabeled-lane noise, not misattribution detection. Fixed inline (`c87ba925b`): all three consuming stages (`filter_misattributed_hits_nmf.py`, `recompute_filtered_lane_offsets.py`, and `batch_grooves.py` itself) now route through one shared `production_lane_order()`.

**Consequence: 002_TIMING_REPAIR's DB backfill is partially stale.** Of 6,779 `grooves` rows carrying `*_filtered` timing values, only 1,500 (the ones re-run during the freeze, under the corrected Hungarian pipeline) are trustworthy. The remaining **~5,279 rows were computed under the pre-fix, inconsistent lane-ordering code** and should not be relied on. The code is fixed; a corpus-wide re-run to refresh the stale rows is a separate ~5-hour operation, explicitly out of scope for this festival (matches `002_NMF_PRODUCTIONIZE`'s own quality standard: "does not re-run the full 15,000-loop corpus as part of this phase"). Not yet spun out as a task — do that when picking this back up, or ask Bruke if he wants it done inline.

## Annotation tooling (Session 3, `003_ANNOTATION_TOOLING`)

The real annotation UI (`LabelWorkflow.tsx`) was NOT third-party — it's Bruke's own component, but it was stranded gitignored inside `audits/flowmusic.app/` (a web-audit capture of an unrelated app whose Vite/React harness he'd vendored as scaffolding). Every row in `gt_onsets.jsonl` traces back to it via its `session` field. Decision made with Bruke (AskUserQuestion): **fork it into tracked source** at `hh_trp_index/scripts/hand_transcribe/ui/` rather than edit the gitignored original — now version-pinnable, builds clean under `tsc --strict`.

Built: persistent annotator identity (localStorage, replacing per-tab `crypto.randomUUID()`), the 3-way identity/mixture/confidence field split (replacing one overloaded "Unsure/Mixture" button — this recovers the 33%/267-of-806 of existing ground truth that conflation made unusable), server-enforced two-rater independence (403 on peek, 409 on post-finalize revise — verified via raw curl, not UI-only), blind server-shuffled adjudication, `ANNOTATION_PROTOCOL.md`, and a fixed `select_gt_sample.py`.

**`select_gt_sample.py` had its own real bug**: it drew samples via `set()` iteration (line 216, no seed) — CPython randomizes string-hash order per process, so every run drew a *different* 56-loop sample. For a "frozen benchmark" festival, an unreproducible sampler is a foundational defect. Fixed with sorted iteration + `random.Random(--seed)`. New draw: `benchmark_manifest.json`, 150 loops, all inside `split_frozen_test` (0 leaked into `train`), 0 duplicate-group collisions, reproducible.

Also fixed: a label-literal drift bug — the UI posted `"unsure/mixture"` but every downstream consumer filtered `"Unsure / Mixture"` (title case), so those rows would've silently scored as a nonexistent instrument class with no error raised.

**Known gap, spun out as a separate background task** (`task_77175e69`, running in a separate local session as of last check): the 150 benchmark loops have candidates but 0/150 have `gt_cluster_ids` entries or cached WAVs — `within_loop_cluster_bootstrap.py` is still hardcoded to the legacy 56-loop pool. No rater can open a benchmark loop until this is pointed at the new manifest. Check that session's outcome before assuming the annotation program can start.

## Process/doctrine notes worth carrying forward

- **`fest`'s queue is strictly serial** regardless of which phases are logically independent — dispatch parallel background agents for the real engineering work, but expect to feed `fest task completed`/`fest next` one task at a time, in whatever order fest's own pointer wants, once each is independently verified.
- **Recon-ledger receipts must live at `<festival-dir>/.audit/recon-ledger.jsonl`** (not the target repo's own `.audit/`), with `file_line` paths relative to the *festival* directory (e.g. `../../../STRUDEL/...`), not repo-relative or absolute — both of the latter silently fail the gate.
- **Background agents will stall claiming "waiting for a Monitor notification"** — no such mechanism exists for their own detached child processes. Resume via `SendMessage` with an explicit correction to poll synchronously (`until <check>; do sleep N; done`) before ending their turn.
- **`fest commit` doesn't fit this repo shape** (not `fest link`-registered) — plain scoped `git commit` used instead, by explicit user authorization each session (never assumed to carry forward).
- **Gitignore parent-directory trap**: `!path/to/file` inside an ignored parent directory does nothing — git only re-includes files whose *directory* isn't itself excluded. Fix shape: `!dir/`, then `dir/*`, then `!dir/specific-file`. Bit us with `output/` hiding the frozen-baseline artifacts (a governance deliverable that must be version-controlled) — same class of bug as `LabelWorkflow.tsx` being stranded in gitignored `audits/`. Pattern to watch for: check whether "generated output" directories are quietly swallowing files that are actually permanent records.
- **Agent-report distrust paid off repeatedly this festival**: every major finding (the tautological filter, the nondeterministic sampler, the 6,345-kit ordering bug, the label-literal drift) was independently re-verified against real disk/DB state — sometimes catching the agent's report was *right* when a lazy re-check would've missed it (e.g. a bad first-pass JSON parse made it look like the 150-loop draw had 0/150 inside frozen_test, until the parsing bug was found).

## project_hydra

<a id="project-hydra"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_hydra.md`*
*Name:* project-hydra
*Description:* HYDRA — snake that crawls live GitHub hop-by-hop, eats capability \"stones\" into a tail; v1 shipped 2026-06-24

**This is one head of a deliberately multi-headed HYDRA** — the **intake head** (the repo-engine
that pulls capabilities in). Other heads exist for other functions, e.g. a deterministic-execution
head (LangGraph + Pydantic-schema dev-pipeline concept, see [[project_hydra_gemini_dev_os_concepts]]).
Don't read "HYDRA" elsewhere as a naming collision with this file — same organism, different organ.

**HYDRA** = a snake.io-style game reframed as a GitHub crawler. Head sits on a repo (a dot);
you aim at a neighbor dot, hop, and eat a *stone* (one capability). Stones accumulate as the
tail = a treasury of intelligence meant to pair with [[project-droplist-remediation-plan]] /
[[project-item-backbone]] (DropList/Lattice).

**Lives at** `apps/hydra/hydra.html` — single self-contained file, hits `api.github.com`
directly (CORS-enabled, 60/hr unauth, optional token field → 5000/hr). Launch config `hydra`
added to `.claude/launch.json` (http-server on :8898). README at `apps/hydra/README.md`.

**v1 SHIPPED & verified 2026-06-24** (uncommitted, on branch experiment/droplist-remediation-2026-06-15).
Done-condition met: seed repo → live neighbors → aim (mouse) → hop (click/Space) → stone in
tail (catalog depth). Browser-verified end-to-end: turso → turso-client-php → libsql-client-ts,
2 hops, snake advanced, stones persisted to localStorage `hydra_v1`, zero console errors.

**Edge rules (Directions):** Tour-a-mind (owner repos), Wander (topic search), Bloodline
(forks+parent), Tour-me (lionestenzol) — all live. Drill (manifest deps) + Climb (dependents)
stubbed as v2 (Climb has no clean GitHub JSON API). Switching direction mid-run re-surfaces
neighbors = "the snake turning."

**Stone schema** (deterministic, catalog depth): id/from_repo/type/name/what/pairs_with/
signal(stars→1-5)/language/stars/url/caught_at. Type heuristic-guessed, click-to-cycle in UI.
Export-JSON button = the bridge to DropList/Lattice (schema already matches a Lattice item).

**Origin:** Bruke had a working slither.io MVP at `OneDrive/Desktop/snake_io.html`, then pasted
the full "HYDRA — The Menu" spec (directions / stone types / absorb depth menus + worked turso
example + crisp v1 done-condition). Built fresh, reusing the snake's canvas/body/eyes rendering,
swapping free-roam for graph-crawl.

**Auto-explore SHIPPED 2026-06-24:** "▶ Auto-explore" toggle — snake picks highest-signal
*unvisited* neighbor, hops on its own, rotates direction (Tour→Wander→Bloodline) when a region
is exhausted, pauses when GitHub rate ≤2. Pace ~6s/hop unauth, ~2.6s with token.
**KEY BUG FIXED:** hop's `waitAnim()` originally resolved via `requestAnimationFrame` — rAF is
PAUSED in background/headless tabs (incl. the preview MCP), so the first hop pushed its stone
then hung forever at the await, wedging `busy=true` and freezing all later ticks. Fix: drive
hop completion with `setTimeout` (fires when backgrounded), keep rAF only for visual interp in
loop() via `hopAnim.start/dur`. Same background-rAF gotcha that bit the wall thumbnail work.
Also added startAuto double-start guard. Verified: auto hopped turso→libsql unattended.

**DropList pairing SHIPPED 2026-06-24 (catalog depth):** each stone → a drop via
`POST http://127.0.0.1:3073/api/drop` (droplist `server.py`, CORS `*`, body `{raw, source}`).
Per-stone ⇪ send + bulk "→ DropList" + "auto→DropList" checkbox (feeds auto-explore stones
hands-free). DropList bouncer returns `secured`/`dropped`; HYDRA badges each stone. `offline`
badge if :3073 down (nothing lost, resend later). Verified browser→:3073: OPTIONS preflight +
3× POST 200, all "secured". DropList intake is TEXT (`raw` string), not structured — chain_intake
bouncer decides. NOTE droplist `chain_intake` is a few seconds/drop, so bulk send is slow-ish.

**COMMITTED 2026-06-25** (was uncommitted all along): `343aeb4` carries v1 + auto-explore +
DropList pairing + 3 bug fixes from a web engine-review (via [[skill-claude-bridge]]). Fixes:
(1) manual `hop()` pushed duplicate stones when a repo re-surfaced — dedupe by id on eat +
gray out already-eaten dots; (2) `drawSnake` overwrote the last trail node AND pushed the head,
dropping the leaving-node→head segment (body popped loose mid-slither) — keep node, append head
once; (3) `aimedNeighbor()` recomputed in both draw passes per frame — compute once in `loop()`.
All 3 verified live (preview MCP, real api.github.com, dedup proven: same repo hopped twice = 1
stone). Web's full review saved at `apps/hydra/REVIEW-web-2026-06-25.md`.

**Bridge share 2026-06-25:** whole Hydra picture staged to the `claude` Drive folder via
[[skill-claude-bridge]] (`for-web/hydra/`, git-pinned to `343aeb4` via bridge.py). Web read it,
returned the review above, flipped handoff to done.

**v2 growth path** (web's recommended order, not yet built): #1 add `via` + `from_parent` to the
stone schema so the tail becomes an EDGE list (what Lattice wants — ~10 lines); #2 Extract depth
(pull actual code into a vault DropList draws from); then Drill (pure-client: parse manifest deps,
hop npm/crates registry repo URLs) before Climb (needs a thin proxy — no CORS-able GitHub
dependents JSON). v3 Integrate = wire a capability into the stack live.

**EATING-FOR-REAL (delta-scp throughput) — built+verified 2026-06-25, UNCOMMITTED.**
Bruke rejected catalog-stones as "read-only, no throughput — I want Pac-Man eating stuff." So
eating now = DIGEST. Chain: HYDRA browser -> HYDRA engine (apps/hydra/engine.py, stdlib
http.server :8899, holds delta-scp Bearer key server-side, CORS open, caches full digest to
apps/hydra/vault/) -> delta-scp demo-server (pre-atlas\services\delta-scp\src\demo-server.ts,
:3012 — a PARALLEL session built this: Supabase-free, SYNCHRONOUS POST /jobs {repo_url} returns
{job:{compressed_state,graph}}, ~1-5s, clones via git). On eat, stone gets digest =
{file_count, symbol_count, languages, top_files[{path,symbols,names}], graph}. Browser-verified
e2e: ate sindresorhus/type-fest -> 461 files / 2808 symbols / graph 2821-979 / real type names
in the tail. UI: engineUrl field + 🧬 digest checkbox (default on), digest block per stone,
"digesting…"/"⚠ engine offline" states. Launch cfgs added: hydra-engine :8899, delta-scp-demo
:3012. delta-scp .env has LIVE secrets (don't print). Big repos = slow clone (eat small first).
Followed feedback-built-at-me lesson: proved real digest lands before calling done, not HTTP 200.

STILL read-only/notes: DropList wire (shallow "clarify intent" noise — Bruke flagged it; left
in, NOT the throughput). Separation-of-interests design discussed (Scout->Librarian->Editor
gate->Architect proposal->Builder) but NOT built — the gate + proposal stages are the real
missing pipeline after digestion. v2: Drill/Climb edge rules, wire digested stones into Lattice
/ the gate->propose->integrate pipeline, then v3 Integrate (wire a capability into the stack live).

## project_hydra_gemini_dev_os_concepts

<a id="project-hydra-gemini-dev-os-concepts"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_hydra_gemini_dev_os_concepts.md`*
*Description:* CUBCORE64, Sass Wars, RIPrReap — real Bruke projects/concepts, confirmed 2026-07-15, first surfaced to Claude via a pulled Gemini share transcript. Distinct from apps/hydra (the GitHub-crawler HYDRA).

**Confirmed real (2026-07-15), not Gemini invention.** A pulled Gemini share transcript
(`chatpull/pulls/gemini/2026-07-15-claude-code-an-autonomous-developer-agent.md`) named
three of Bruke's projects that don't appear anywhere else in this memory system:
**CUBCORE64**, **Sass Wars**, **RIPrReap**. Claude initially flagged them as possible
Gemini hallucination (per [[feedback_argued_from_priors_not_evidence]] discipline — unfamiliar
+ unverifiable = flag, don't assert). Bruke corrected: *"those are real, not gemini making it
up they are concepts discussed."*

**What's still unverified:** the *functional descriptions* of these three, below, come from
Gemini's own synthesis inside that conversation — Gemini was reasoning generically about how
they'd plug into a proposed "HYDRA" dev-pipeline architecture, not reciting something Bruke
stated as fact. The names are confirmed real; the specific mechanics Gemini attributed to them
are NOT independently verified against any code or spec Claude has seen. Ground these against
real source (or ask Bruke directly) before citing the mechanics as fact:
- **CUBCORE64** — per Gemini: a symbolic/binary payload packer, alternative to standard zip
  libraries, for compressing symbolic binary files or optimizing local data storage.
- **Sass Wars** — per Gemini: a developer tournament platform — microVM sandboxes, timed
  coding rounds.
- **RIPrReap** — per Gemini: the commercial SaaS/production arena for this ecosystem, branded
  "a grim reaper riding a hydra."

**NOT a naming collision (corrected 2026-07-15) — this is the intended design.** Claude initially
flagged "two HYDRAs" as a collision. Bruke corrected: HYDRA is deliberately many-headed — that's
the point of the name. `apps/hydra/hydra.html` (the shipped snake.io-style GitHub crawler) is the
**intake head** — the repo-engine that pulls capabilities in. The LangGraph + Pydantic-schema +
deterministic-Python-scripts system discussed in the Gemini transcript is a **different head of
the same HYDRA** — the **deterministic execution system** head. These are two organs of one
body, not two projects sharing a name by accident. CUBCORE64/Sass Wars/RIPrReap are concepts
Bruke has been developing for a while, part of this same multi-headed ecosystem — not isolated
one-offs. When more heads surface, expect the same pattern: distinct function, same organism.

**Why this matters:** the Gemini conversation's proposed architecture substantially overlaps
with the already-locked, file:line-verified [[project_langgraph_skill_lattice]] plan (combo.py
as the real Schema-Librarian-equivalent router, seam.v1 Receipts as the real contract layer).
If CUBCORE64/Sass Wars/RIPrReap are meant to plug into that pipeline, the locked plan is the
grounded version to build from — not Gemini's from-scratch reasoning, which didn't have
visibility into what already exists in this stack.

## project_hydra_s_supagetti_naming

<a id="project-hydra-s-supagetti-naming"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_hydra_s_supagetti_naming.md`*
*Name:* project-hydra-s-supagetti-naming
*Description:* Naming/branding for the modernization-suite SaaS: HYDRA-S (serious house brand) + Supagetti (playful self-serve door); 3-head tier menu; naming due-diligence done

Working brand for the [[project_modernization_suite]] SaaS (packaging the code-recon + binre + delta-scp + sigil engine as a delivered "understand -> modernize -> ship, sealed" service), decided 2026-07-13:

- **Two-brand split.** **HYDRA-S** = serious enterprise/house brand (deliberately echoes the existing [[project-hydra]] GitHub-crawler -> framed as "the many-headed engine"). **Supagetti** = playful self-serve funnel / front-door (Tier I). Names are downstream of the wedge; recommended launch wedge = Tier II.
- **"Three heads of one hydra" menu** (same engine, escalating human wrap + guarantee): I Supagetti = self-serve cleanup ~$19-39/mo; II Working head = managed ship-and-keep-shipping ~$499-1,999/mo; III Deepwater head = enterprise no-source/binary decompile + due-diligence $15k-150k/engagement. All directional/placeholder.
- **Visual identity:** committed dark "Gyarados" black + water-teal + serpent-blue; Supagetti gets a warm whisker-gold accent as the one warm door. Menu + GTM artifact: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/a4f243b0-719a-4ba3-8584-fb19802a4ad7
- **Due-diligence (2026-07-13):** "Hydra" is crowded (THC-Hydra, Ory Hydra, Meta hydra.cc) but all in *other* categories -> validates the name, doesn't block. Dev-software trademark lane looks open (only live HYDRA mark = Lam Research semiconductor hardware; the software ones are dead). The one namesake `sibyllinesoft/hydra` (6 stars, MIT) is a **Claude Code context/agent framework, NOT a modernization product** -> adjacent lot, not a competitor; differs on delivery (they expose the recipe MIT; HYDRA-S keeps it server-side + sealed) and has zero no-source/binary capability. "Supagetti" is a free string but sits in a crowded "supa-" dev-tool lane (Supabase, Supacode). The code-modernization *slot* is effectively empty.

**Why:** names were the live question this session; the moat is sealed server-side delivery + the no-source capability, so the brand must carry "delivered outcome," not "a tool you run." **How to apply:** if resuming the SaaS thread, treat HYDRA-S / Supagetti + the 3-tier menu as the working frame (not locked). Don't position on context/agents (that's the namesake's lane); differentiate on sealed delivery + binaries. Working names + placeholder pricing -> confirm with Bruke before treating as final. Relates: [[project_atlas_potential_assessment]], [[feedback_assemble_first_posture]].

**>>> NEXT SESSION (chosen 2026-07-13) = BUILD THE PROOF.** Operational truth-test of the whole SaaS thesis: can the line ship a sealed, working package without babysitting? (That, not branding, is the business risk.) Plan:
0. Lock plan (WHAT/WHY/done-condition) FIRST - no building without it ([[feedback_no_building_without_locked_plan]]).
1. Pick ONE genuinely messy vibe-coded repo (Bruke picks: his own half-built thing, or find a representative public one). Test the **Supagetti / Tier I self-serve** flow specifically - the tier that MUST be fully automated.
2. Verify the unified pipeline still runs at HEAD before trusting (this memory is days old): `C:\Users\bruke\binre\modernize.py` (root dispatcher; source-dir/git-URL -> delta-scp with `SCP_EXTRACTOR=treesitter`; binary -> binre). delta-scp at `pre-atlas/services/delta-scp`; its `.env` has LIVE secrets - don't print.
3. Run end-to-end -> dossier (symbolic_map + dependency_graph + MODERNIZATION_REPORT.md) + sealed sigil artifact = the Tier I deliverable prototype.
4. INSTRUMENT THE BABYSITTING - log every human-intervention / failure / manual-fix point. That tally IS the real output.
5. Gap-check output vs the promised Supagetti menu (structure map . plain-English read . dead code cleared . secrets flagged . deployable package . sealed receipt).
**Done when:** one real repo -> sealed deliverable, plus a written tally of (a) deliverable-vs-promised-menu gaps and (b) every human-touch point -> verdict on the operational thesis + top-3 gaps to close. The engine is [[project_modernization_suite]]; small/medium target first (big repos = slow clone).

**>>> PROOF DONE (2026-07-13).** Ran `binre/modernize.py` on `C:/Users/bruke/URBANNOMAD` (own vibe-coded JS PWA): exit 0, ZERO in-run babysitting, deterministic 3-file dossier. Verdict: the line's DISCOVERY half ships unattended and is genuinely good (accurate structure map + fan-in/out/size hotspots w/ file:line + readable roadmap), but the Supagetti menu OVERSELLS the SHIP half - **today it delivers a _dossier_, not a _modernization_** (no dead-code removal, no secrets scan, no deployable package, no sealed sigil bundle - just a sha256 field). Honest Tier-I product that exists NOW = "instant codebase X-ray," not "we fix+ship sealed." Full write-up: `scratchpad/proof-run/PROOF_VERDICT.md`. Top-3 gaps were: (1) silent HTML/file skips, (2) C/binary-tuned risk read blind to assignment-form JS XSS, (3) dossier != package.
**Gap #1 CLOSED same session:** delta-scp (`pre-atlas/services/delta-scp`) now (a) analyzes HTML - extracts inline `<script>` JS via already-shipped `tree-sitter-html.wasm`, delegating to the JS extractor with line-offset (`htmlLine = jsLocalLine + rawText.startRow`); (b) fails loud on every skipped file - `walk()` records skips, dossier §1 prints `coverage: N file(s) NOT analyzed (unsupported type): ...`, receipt gains `skipped_files`. Files edited: source.ts (walk->WalkResult + SkippedFile + fetchSourceFilesDetailed), compressor.ts + source.ts (html in EXT_LANGUAGE/INCLUDE_EXT), treesitter.ts (html LANGS + inlineScripts), modernize.ts (thread skipped). tsc clean; 64 tests pass (+1 new HTML line-offset test). Live re-run: URBANNOMAD files 16->17 (index.html now in), skipped_files=3 (png×2·css×1) NAMED. **Committed+pushed** `09f9e50` on `pre-atlas` (hyphen) repo, branch `feat/delta-scp-v2-graph-memory` at `lionestenzol/pre-atlas` (his own account, [[reference_lionestenzol_owned_account]]).

**Gap #2 CLOSED same track (2026-07-14), commit `93966d0`, pushed.** Risk scanner was blind to the two dominant JS/web sink shapes: (a) `el.innerHTML = x` is an ASSIGNMENT, invisible to a call-only scan; (b) `document.write(x)`/`child_process.exec(x)` are MEMBER calls, invisible to a query that only matched bare-identifier callees. Added `extractPropertyAssignmentsAst` + `extractQualifiedCallEdgesAst` in treesitter.ts (JS/TS, HTML inherits via existing delegation), narrow denylists in modernize.ts (`DOM_SINK_PROPS`={innerHTML,outerHTML}, `RISKY_QUALIFIED_TARGETS`={document.write,writeln} — deliberately NOT generic `.href`/`write` to avoid flooding false positives on ordinary code). Also fixed (found incidentally, code-as-furniture): assignment caller-attribution was reporting "(module scope)" for sinks inside anonymous forEach/map callbacks instead of the nearest named function — added `nearestNamedFunctionName`, scoped to assignments only (didn't touch call-graph attribution / fan-in-fan-out, which other tests depend on). Verified: tsc clean, 73/75 tests (2 pre-existing env skips), 8 new tests incl. a precision check. Live proof: reconstructed URBANNOMAD's exact historical S1 innerHTML-in-forEach finding from its own AUDIT_REPORT.md as a fixture — dossier now correctly reports `.innerHTML assigned in updateZoneLogsUI` + `document.write called by debugDump`. Re-ran against CURRENT (already-remediated) URBANNOMAD: 10 review-first innerHTML sites now surface (up from 0) — correctly framed as a review-candidate list (AST pattern match, no taint tracking), not a vulnerability proof.

**Gap #3 CLOSED (2026-07-14), commit `f71e4c8`, pushed** (squashed from two local-only commits `b026a19`+`aead583` after GitHub push protection correctly flagged a Stripe-key-shaped test fixture — neither prior commit ever reached origin, so the squash was safe; fixed by building the fake key via `.join('_')` so no git blob contains the literal pattern, approved explicitly by Bruke after the auto-mode classifier flagged the first attempt as scanner evasion). The dossier was a MAP (3 loose files), not the promised deliverable ("dead code cleared · secrets flagged · deployable package · sealed receipt"). Assembled, didn't hand-roll: `secretlint` (MIT, recommend preset) for secrets scan via `@secretlint/node` programmatic API (writes the already-collected analyzable files to a scratch dir since the engine needs real fs paths, not in-memory content) — new report §5. `findDeadCodeCandidates` (zero-fan-in functions from the EXISTING call graph, no new deps) — new report §6, explicitly review-first/not-auto-removed (an exported API or `onclick=` HTML wiring shows zero fan-in too — the call graph structurally can't see it). `archiver` (v8, zero native deps) zips the 3 dossier files into `dossier.zip` — the actual openable deliverable. Then the EXISTING `sigil` CLI (`C:\Users\bruke\sigil\`, already on PATH, sha256-verified byte-identical round-trip) seals that zip into `dossier.zip.sgl` — real content-addressed receipt, not a hand-rolled format. Sealing is fail-soft (sigil is an external binary, not vendored — missing PATH degrades to `sealed:false`, doesn't fail the run). Scoped OUT: actually deleting dead code (too destructive/needs judgment) and scanning `.env`/`.pem` files (filename-pattern, not extension-based — named limitation, honestly surfaced via the existing skip-tally rather than hidden). Verified: tsc clean, 84/86 tests (2 pre-existing env skips), live proof via the real `binre/modernize.py` dispatcher against URBANNOMAD — `dossier.zip` opens cleanly (`testzip()==None`), `dossier.zip.sgl` independently re-verified byte-identical via `sigil unpack`+sha256 compare (didn't just trust the tool's own `verified:true`). `possible_secrets:0` (correct, no hardcoded creds), `dead_code_candidates:16` (several visibly false-positive from onclick=/DOMContentLoaded wiring — exactly what the section's caveat anticipated).

**All 3 gaps from the 2026-07-13 proof-run are now closed and pushed** (HTML-skip `09f9e50`, JS-sink `93966d0`, deployable-package `f71e4c8`). The automated Tier-I line now genuinely produces: structure map + risk surfaces (calls+assignments) + secrets scan + dead-code candidates + a real zip + a sealed, verifiable receipt — unattended, in one `modernize.py` run. What's still NOT true: nothing is auto-fixed/auto-shipped (all findings are review-first by design), and `.env`/key-file secrets scanning is unbuilt.

**SECOND-REPO CONFIRMATION (2026-07-14).** Ran the exact same unified `modernize.py` dispatcher against `C:\Users\bruke\STEMai` — deliberately a different shape than URBANNOMAD (polyglot Python+JS+HTML audio-processing tool, heavy media/data payload: 120 analyzable files + 54 correctly-named-and-skipped mid/wav/png/m4a/log/zip files, vs. URBANNOMAD's single-language JS PWA). Result: exit 0, **zero babysitting**, all sections held up on genuinely new code:
- Coverage: 54 skipped files individually named by extension (`mid×17 · wav×16 · png×12 · m4a×5 · log×2 · gitignore×1 · zip×1`) — gap #1 fix generalized, no silent drops.
- JS-sink detection (gap #2): found 5 real `.innerHTML` assignment sinks across 3 HTML files. Independently spot-checked one file (`beat_grid.html`) via raw `grep -ni innerhtml` — exact match (2 occurrences, both correctly attributed to their enclosing function: `loadSessionJSON` line 395, `renderGrid` line 716) — no false positives, nothing missed.
- Deployable package (gap #3): `dossier.zip` (17,418 bytes) passed Python `zipfile.testzip()`; `dossier.zip.sgl` independently re-unpacked via `sigil unpack` and sha256-compared byte-for-byte against the original zip (didn't just trust the tool's own `verified:true`) — MATCH.
- Secrets: `possible_secrets: 0` — true negative (repo has no exposed creds), correctly caveated re: `.env`/key-file blind spot.
- Dead-code: 20 candidates found, several correctly matching the documented false-positive shape (`main()` entry points in standalone scripts like `build_ir.py`/`capture_onsets.py`) — the caveat text is doing its job, not covering a bug.

**Verdict: the three gap-fixes are not overfit to URBANNOMAD.** Two independent repo shapes now confirm the Tier-I self-serve thesis — a structurally different, messier polyglot target produces a genuine sealed deliverable with zero manual intervention.

**THIRD-REPO RUN (2026-07-14) — found a real precision bug (GAP #4, OPEN, not yet fixed).** Ran `modernize.py` against `services/delta-kernel` (66 TS files, 1,357 symbols, 1,400 call edges — real production code, not vibe-coded, deliberately the first mature single-language TS backend tested). Exit 0, zero babysitting; coverage/JS-sink/package/secrets/dead-code sections all held up exactly as on the first two repos, including 8 correct `.innerHTML` findings in `src/ui/*.html` and `src/core/*-live-test.html`.

BUT: **11 of 19 risk findings were false positives.** `this.db.exec(DDL)` (better-sqlite3's schema-exec method, `src/atlas/preferences-store.ts:65`, `src/atlas/signals-store.ts:88`, `src/cli/sqlite-storage.ts:54+`) got flagged as `exec`/injection risk. Root cause: `RISKY_CALL_TARGETS` (`modernize.ts:34-45`) includes bare `'exec'`, and gap #2's fix made `extractCallEdgesAst`'s member-call branch (`treesitter.ts:107-110`) capture *any* `.methodName()` call's property name with **no receiver check** — so `db.exec()`, and by the same logic `RegExp.exec()` or Python's `json.loads` (vs. genuinely-dangerous `pickle.loads`), all collide with the exec-family denylist entries (`eval, exec, execsync, system, popen, spawn, spawnsync, execfile, loads`). The 6 real `child_process` findings (`execSync`/`spawn` in `atlas-ai.ts`, `atlas.ts`, `gate_client.ts`) stayed correctly caught — this is a precision/false-positive bug, not a missed-detection regression.

**Fix shape (scoped, not yet built):** split `RISKY_CALL_TARGETS` into (a) an unambiguous set safe to match against ANY call shape (C string/memory ops, dynamic-loading, `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` — these essentially never collide with unrelated method names) and (b) a collision-prone "exec-family" set (`exec/execsync/spawn/spawnsync/execfile/system/popen/loads`) that should only fire when RECEIVER-QUALIFIED to a known-dangerous namespace — extend the existing `qualifiedCallQuery`/`RISKY_QUALIFIED_TARGETS` pattern (already used for `document.write`) with pairs like `child_process.exec`, `child_process.spawn`, `os.system`, `os.popen`, `subprocess.call/run/Popen`, `pickle.loads`, `pickle.load`, `marshal.loads`. Python's `LangSpec` currently has no `qualifiedCallQuery` at all (only JS/TS do — `treesitter.ts:70-93` vs `94-133`) so this also requires adding one for Python's `(call function: (attribute object: (identifier) attribute: (identifier) @callee))` shape. Multi-file change (treesitter.ts + modernize.ts + tests), correctly deferred rather than rushed given context state when found.

**GAP #4 CLOSED same session (2026-07-14), commit `496af28`, pushed clean.** Bruke said "fix it now" right after the deferral was banked — overrode the park decision. Implementation: split `RISKY_CALL_TARGETS` into an unambiguous set (matches any call shape — C string/memory ops, dynamic loading, `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`) and a new `EXEC_FAMILY_TARGETS` set (`exec/execsync/spawn/spawnsync/execfile/system/popen/loads`) that only matches as a BARE identifier call. Member/attribute calls with those names now require a specific known-dangerous receiver via `RISKY_QUALIFIED_TARGETS` (extended with `child_process.*`/`cp.*`/`os.system`/`os.popen`/`subprocess.*`/`pickle.*`/`marshal.loads` pairs). To make this possible, `treesitter.ts`'s `callQuery` was split into distinct `@callee.bare`/`@callee.member` captures (new `AstEdge.qualified` boolean) for TS/JS/Python, and `qualifiedCallQuery` — previously TS/JS-only, capturing only bare-identifier receivers — was (a) broadened to capture the WHOLE receiver chain including `this.x.y` (fixes the exact `this.db.exec` false positive) and (b) added to Python for the first time (`(call function: (attribute) @qualified)`).

Re-verified live on all 3 proof-run repos: **delta-kernel 19→14** (the 5 `this.db.exec()`/`.exec()` false positives gone, all 6 real `execSync`/`spawn` child_process findings + 8 `.innerHTML` preserved exactly). **STEMai 5→8** — not a regression: 3 NEW genuine `subprocess.run(cmd,...)` findings in `stem_pipeline.py` surfaced as a side effect of Python finally having qualified-call detection at all (it had none before this fix) — verified against real source (`stem_pipeline.py:304,340,372`). **URBANNOMAD unchanged at 10** (pure-JS repo, no exec-family calls, untouched as expected — correct null result). `tsc --noEmit` clean, 89/91 tests (2 pre-existing skips, +7 new precision tests), zip+seal independently re-verified byte-for-byte on all three repos post-fix.

This is the third of the proof-run's repo tests — not originally planned as a fixed count, but each new repo shape found something real (STEMai confirmed generalization; delta-kernel found gap #4). All 4 gaps from the original proof-run plus the second/third-repo discoveries are now closed. No further repo runs currently planned.

## project_inpact_assessment

<a id="project-inpact-assessment"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_inpact_assessment.md`*
*Name:* inPACT Assessment Purpose
*Description:* The in-PACT assessment serves 4 roles — lead magnet, baseline for personalization, immediate value delivery, and engagement hook

The in-PACT assessment has four explicit purposes:
1. **Lead magnet** — drive engagement toward signup/waitlist
2. **Baseline capture** — provide a starting configuration for personalized user experience
3. **Immediate value** — the results must deliver standalone insight worth the user's time
4. **Engagement hook** — peak interest in going deeper with the full system

**Why:** These four jobs shape every design decision — question count, depth, scoring, results format. Generic results fail jobs 3 and 4. No data persistence fails job 2.

**How to apply:** When working on the assessment or results page, every change should serve at least one of these four purposes. Results page is currently the weakest link — it fails all four jobs (static, generic, disconnected from pillars).

## project_inpact_curriculum_embodiment

<a id="project-inpact-curriculum-embodiment"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_inpact_curriculum_embodiment.md`*
*Name:* inPACT as embodiment of the Impacts curriculum
*Description:* The big vision for inPACT — every page is a manifestation of one or more lessons from Bruke's 20-lesson Impacts curriculum. The 8 Steps page (method.html) is lesson 3 of 20. Nineteen more to go.

# The Vision

inPACT is not a productivity tracker. It is the **personal version of the Impacts curriculum**, the 20-lesson methodology that produced the #1 sales rep in the country and that Bruke adapted for himself while working 4 jobs + school. Every page in inPACT should be the manifestation of one or more binder lessons. The product is the curriculum, made executable.

**The 8 Steps page (method.html) is one lesson out of 20.** It's the most central one and the right place to start — the rest hang off it — but the other 19 each need the same treatment: capture Bruke's teaching voice, ask how he applies it, build the page in his words, ship it.

# Hard Rules (learned tonight, must apply to every future lesson)

1. **No em dashes in UI.** See `feedback_no_em_dashes_in_ui.md`. Use `·`, `:`, `.`, `?`, or hyphen.
2. **Never invent the operational meaning of a binder concept.** See `feedback_ask_before_translating_methodology.md`. Always ask Bruke how he teaches it before writing.
3. **Default to Bruke's personal version**, not the sales-floor version. See `user_8steps_context.md`. He developed his version under 4 jobs + school, no manager. inPACT customers are closer to that than to a sales rep with a manager.
4. **The headline principle is intentionality, not discipline.** Every lesson translation should preserve the "did you choose it on purpose, did it get the result?" framing.
5. **Tone**: cleaned-up substance with raw teaching lines preserved as quotes attributed to Bruke. The product is public-facing so profanity gets pulled back, but the teaching is uncompromising. Real motto examples in placeholder text use generic stand-ins so customers can use them as-is.

# Architecture: localStorage shape (becomes future API shape)

```
inpact:profile               (set once, persists across days)
  { mission: string, motto: string }

inpact:daily:YYYY-MM-DD      (per day)
  { date, dayType (A|B|C),
    winTarget,                                    // Step 5
    p1, p1why, p2, p2why, p3, p3why,             // Steps 3+7
    x1, x1d, y1, y1d, ... (3 rows),              // X/Y goals
    lever,                                        // Lesson 1: Plan Your Attack
    block1..block4 (bool),                        // Step 4 reps
    resetMove,                                    // Step 6
    reflection,                                   // Step 3 input for tomorrow
    updated_at }
```

When inPACT eventually wires to delta-kernel (see `MEMORY.md` Atlas section), this shape becomes the request payload. No data migration needed. Currently zero backend.

# Why this matters (for future Claude sessions reading this)

Bruke's 11% Atlas compliance rate is the bottleneck of his entire system. The system already knows what to do. He doesn't listen to it. The reason: there's no external standing — no manager, no peer, no customer who depends on him following it.

**Shipping inPACT is the move that closes that gap**, because the moment one paying customer is using it, Bruke can't be 11% on the methodology he's selling. The discipline comes from being the example, not from being told. He said earlier in this session: he trained the #1 rep — he wasn't the #1 rep. The discipline came from teaching, not being taught. inPACT is the same shape. Building it for others creates the standing he can't create for himself.

Every lesson page we add is one more thing he can't half-ass without contradicting himself in public. **That's the actual value of the curriculum embodiment vision.** It's not "more pages." It's "more accountability surface."

## project_inpact_onboarding_ai_assistant

<a id="project-inpact-onboarding-ai-assistant"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_inpact_onboarding_ai_assistant.md`*
*Name:* inpact-onboarding-ai-assistant-idea
*Description:* Future feature — an optional AI helper/assistant for the \"official\" inPACT onboarding, plus a broader in-app AI assistant. Captured at Bruke's request 2026-05-24. NOT built; idea/deferred.

## What Bruke asked for
2026-05-24, right after we shipped the one-click "Use the starter and start now" onboarding: "make a note so there's an AI helper or something for official onboarding, optional, and AI assistant."

Two linked ideas:
1. **Optional AI helper for the official onboarding** — when inPACT gets a polished/"official" first-run (vs. today's quick wizard + static starter draft), offer an AI assistant that guides setup conversationally. Must be **optional**: the user can always skip it and use the manual wizard or the one-click starter.
2. **In-app AI assistant** — a broader assistant inside inPACT (beyond onboarding) to help plan and run the day.

## Why it fits
- The hardest onboarding fields are the personal ones (Mission, Motto, monthly A-Z milestones). The current static starter draft is generic; an AI helper could interview the user and produce *their* words instead of placeholder text. This directly serves the "remove friction / I have no mind to fill it out" need that drove the one-click starter.
- Keep it OPTIONAL — Bruke said optional explicitly. Some sessions he wants zero interaction (one-click starter); the AI helper is for when he *wants* guided personalization.
- Respect the standing rule: never write operational meaning for binder concepts without asking (see feedback_ask_before_translating_methodology.md). An AI helper drafts, the user confirms.

## How it could connect (do NOT build yet)
- inPACT's real backend is the Atlas stack: delta-kernel :3001, cortex :3009, optogon :3010, cognitive-sensor.
- CycleBoard (the parent app) already has `ai-context.js` (LLM read state) + `ai-actions.js` (LLM take actions). An inPACT AI assistant should reuse/port that pattern, not invent a new one.
- Onboarding writes these fields, which an AI helper would produce before the existing `finish()`/state-write runs: `st.Today.mission`, `st.Today.motto`, `st.AZTask[]`, `st.WeeklyPlan`, `st.Today.daily[today]`, `st.onboardingDone`.

## Current state this layers on (shipped 2026-05-24)
- `apps/inpact/onboarding.html` — 4-step wizard + top Quick Start banner: "Use the starter and start now" (`quickStart()` = `prefillStarter()` + `finish()`, one click) and "Fill the fields, let me review first" (`prefillStarter()`).
- `STARTER` object in onboarding.html = grounded starter draft (organize GitHub, daemonize Atlas stack, run inPACT daily, ship canvas-engine vision path, close an Optogon PR).
- `prefillFromState()` makes reopening Setup load current saved values (edit-safe, no wipe; title flips to "Edit your setup").
- Persistent **Setup** button (wand icon) in inPACT sidebar footer (`index.html`) → `onboarding.html`, summonable anytime.

## Status
IDEA / DEFERRED. Not built. Trigger to build: productizing inPACT onboarding, or Bruke says "build the onboarding AI assistant."

## project_inpact_pricing_model

<a id="project-inpact-pricing-model"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_inpact_pricing_model.md`*
*Name:* inPACT pricing model · locked 2026-04-15
*Description:* The pricing call for inPACT. Free lessons, paid execution, premium Public Integrity Toggle. Three independent opinions (me + Codex + DeepSeek) converged on Model A. Do not relitigate this without new information.

# The locked pricing model

**Decided 2026-04-15 after three independent opinions converged.**

## Model A · Free lessons, paid execution, premium integrity

### Free (public, indexed, no account required to read)
- All 20 lesson pages in `inpact-site/`
  - `method.html` · 8 Steps (lesson 3)
  - `followup.html` · Life of an App (lesson 2)
  - `today.html` (read-only view of planning surface — lesson 1)
  - 17 skeleton pages for the remaining lessons
- `curriculum.html` · the public index/marketing front door
- Readable, linkable, quotable, shareable, SEO-indexed
- No gating. No `?dev=1`. No email wall. Just public content.

### Paid (account required at point of execution)
- `today.html` as a working tool (tap counts, toggle actions, save reps, track X/Y)
- `followup.html` as a working tool (real follow-up tracking with the 5-stage pipeline)
- History · the audit trail that proves you did the thing
- Streaks · compliance visualization across days/weeks
- Mastery model · the "had it taught to me / can teach it" dual-axis tracking from the original binder checklist. This was what selected winners in the Impacts curriculum. It is inPACT's power feature.
- Tier: TBD · likely $10-20/mo

### Premium (+$10/mo on top of paid)
- **Public Integrity Toggle** · opt-in public streak profile, initials only
- Users opt to have their compliance publicly visible
- Bruke is the first user. His profile is the demo. His 11% becomes visible.
- This is the feature that cannot be forked by a copycat because it depends on reputation accumulating in real time.
- Source of this idea: DeepSeek, 2026-04-15. The sharpest single contribution in the three-way deliberation.

## Why this is locked

### Three independent opinions agreed

1. **Claude** (me): Free content is marketing, tool is the business. Moat = embodiment and voice.
2. **Codex**: Same call. Added the rule "account required at point of execution, not at point of reading. Let the ideas spread frictionlessly, charge when the user wants the system to hold them to the standard."
3. **DeepSeek**: Same call. Added the Public Integrity Toggle. Framed it as *"a lie detector for your own compliance. Scrapers can't copy the shame of a public 11% adherence rate."*

### The mechanism this protects

Bruke's own memory-file context: his 11% compliance with his own system is the bottleneck. The only mechanism that closes it is external standing · public commitment. Paywalling the teaching strangles visibility, which strangles standing, which strangles the accountability mechanism the whole product depends on. Free lessons keep the standard maximally visible.

### The moat is not the content

The 20 lessons are field-tested and real, but a scraper gets text, not application. The moat is:
- Bruke's voice and teaching lines
- The audit trail the tool captures (history, streaks, mastery)
- The embodiment · lessons turned into tappable daily surfaces
- The founder's public compliance record (the Public Integrity Toggle realization)

A competitor can copy the 20-lesson text tomorrow. They cannot instantly generate Bruke's reputation or a user's 18-month audit history.

## The funnel

**Read → Believe → Run → Retain**

- **Read** · free lesson pages. Discovery, SEO, social share, quote attribution.
- **Believe** · "this is exactly the standard I'm missing." Conviction, not curiosity.
- **Run** · land on `today.html`. Account created *here*, not earlier. User pays because they want the system to hold them to the standard.
- **Retain** · streaks, history, mastery model. Dependency forms. Churn drops.
- **(Optional) Public tier** · when users want to stake their reputation on their compliance.

## Rules this implies for future building

1. **Never gate a lesson page.** All 20 lessons are forever public.
2. **Never require email to read.** Only at the point of execution.
3. **Skeleton pages ship public** with source quotes and "in development" markers. They are marketing surface, not dev surface.
4. **The pricing page must reflect this model** · free curriculum, paid tool, premium public toggle.
5. **Bruke ships his own public integrity profile first.** This is non-negotiable. He is the demonstration of the feature, and the feature is how the product closes his personal compliance gap.
6. **Do not relitigate this pricing model** without new market evidence (e.g. actual paying users telling us the model is wrong, or a competitor successfully eating the market with a different model).

## What still needs to be decided

- Exact dollar amounts for paid tier and premium tier
- Trial length (7 days? 14 days? lifetime free read-only?)
- Whether the free tier of the execution tool exists at all, or if execution is always paid from day one
- How the Public Integrity Toggle profile pages actually render (which URL path, what shows, what's configurable)
- Team/coaching tier for later

Those are product details. The pricing *shape* is locked.

## project_item_backbone

<a id="project-item-backbone"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_item_backbone.md`*
*Name:* project-item-backbone
*Description:* The \"make it one thing\" spine — unifying droplist/cycleboard/inpact/lattice/today items into one backbone. Brick 1 (unified read) shipped 2026-06-20.

**THE central work Bruke has asked for since 2026-06-15: "islands to country", "i want all my shit connected... droplist atlas lattice cycleboard today, it should be one thing."** Not a surface choice (he rejected that) — ALL surfaces become windows onto one shared item backbone.

**Why it's not one thing today (confirmed 2026-06-20):** every surface keeps items in its own store + shape:
- droplist → `services/droplist/data/dags/*.json` (`{dag_id, goal, status}`) + `entities/*.json` (`{entity_id, name, open_nodes}`) — his farm data lives here (goats: Doe, etc.)
- cycleboard → `services/cognitive-sensor/thread_cards.json` (326 cards, `{convo_id, title, classification}`)
- inpact → `apps/inpact/projects.json` (42, `{name, band, lang}`)
- today → browser localStorage (no file)
- `governance_state.json` = the MODE/hub state, NOT items (mode/risk/north_star/lanes)
delta-kernel is already the designated hub (all map edges point at it) but nothing routes through it as shared item-state.

**The bar for "done" (stop saying "go use it" before this):** one real item visible in droplist AND cycleboard AND today AND inpact as the SAME thing, actionable from any, updates everywhere.

**Brick ladder:**
1. ✅ **Unified READ — SHIPPED `e8f7767` (2026-06-20).** `services/atlas-map-api/src/atlas_map_api/items.py` + `GET /items?source=` aggregates all stores → one shape `{id, source, kind, title, status, updated, ref}`. Read-only, fail-soft per source. **Live: 373 items (droplist 5, cycleboard 326, inpact 42). Tests 36/36.** · **4th source SHIPPED 2026-06-25 `8e702e8`† (via /groundwork): `festival` — `festival_items()` reads `~/festival-project/festivals/**/fest.yaml` (workspace via `ATLAS_FESTIVAL_DIR` env, default `~/festival-project`, since festivals live OUTSIDE repo_root unlike the other 3). PyYAML (already in venv). Read-only (WRITABLE_SOURCES still droplist-only). Live by_source now {droplist, cycleboard, inpact, festival 21}=399. pytest 120 green (+4 festival tests incl. 16-case hostile-input break battery, all fail-soft). Accepted limitation: yaml.safe_load expands anchors (billion-laughs DoS theoretically possible) but fest.yaml are trusted local files, not network input — not hardened. Planned via fest festival FI0001 at `festivals/planning/festival-item-backbone-wire-FI0001`. NOTE: autopilot skill de-WSL'd same session — now runs orchestrator.py via native `python`. †CAVEAT: a concurrent session/autopilot active on branch `experiment/droplist-remediation-2026-06-15` did a broad `git add` and swept these 3 files into `8e702e8` (a *droplist*-labeled commit) — work is in HEAD intact + verified (pytest 120, live by_source.festival=21), just bundled under the wrong message; did NOT rewrite history while another process writes the branch. Planning festival FI0001 promoted to `dungeon/completed/2026-06-25` (hit the [[project-fest-windows-native]] promote-unlink bug, cleaned by hand).**
2. ✅ **A surface CONSUMES the feed — SHIPPED `5c2701b` (2026-06-20, via /weapon + ultracode).** `apps/lattice/index.html` `loadBackbone()` fetches :3072/items, maps to lattice's item shape (`bb:`-prefixed ids, project=source, links:[]), unions into items/projects. All 373 render grouped by source in tree/graph/sidebar (droplist 5, cycleboard 317, inpact 42; the goat "Doe" shows in lattice now). KEY: lattice is a Replicache client (syncs delta-kernel :3001 viewmodel); the merge is folded into the `onData` handler via `window.mergeBackbone*` helpers so a delta-kernel recovery re-unions instead of wiping it. Adversarial 2-agent review caught the onData-wipe blocker (fixed+proven) + "undefined"-time defect + statusPill XSS-hardening, all pre-commit. Graceful if :3072 down. apps/lattice/index.html is hand-maintained (NOT generated by _build_map.py — that's system-map.html), so edits are durable.
3. ✅ **Write-through — SHIPPED `50599e9` (2026-06-20, /weapon + ultracode).** `items.set_item_status('bb:droplist:<id>', status)` + `POST /items/{id}/status`. droplist-only (others 422), status-field-only, all other packet fields preserved. Path-traversal guarded (native id `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,80}` BEFORE path build), atomic write (tempfile+os.replace), .bak, 5MB read cap. lattice `setItemStatus` routes `bb:*`→`setBackboneStatus`→POST→re-pull. **Verified live: flipped a real droplist packet status FROM lattice, source file changed (fields preserved), restored to original — user data unchanged.** droplist's own API is read-only (no write endpoint) so the backbone writes the store file directly. Security review: traversal guard airtight, nothing blocked. Tests 42/42. **KNOWN follow-up (HIGH, deferred): write + /map/start|stop|restart endpoints have NO auth — loopback + CORS cover local dev, but need an X-Atlas-Token (env token, viewer passes it) before reliance beyond local viewer. Deferred over env-gated-off-by-default false security.**
4. ⏳ **Shared identity** — link the same logical item across surfaces (a droplist packet ↔ inpact project ↔ today task) so it's one record seen many ways, not 4 records.

**Meta-lesson Bruke drove home 2026-06-20:** stop ending turns with "done, go use it" + a 3-door menu — that IS the endless-doors disease reproduced. Build the connective spine, not more islands. Don't declare done until items cross surfaces. Related: [[project-triage-loop-trace]] (the dormant act-loop), [[project-atlas-gps-substrate]] (the systems map — sibling unification, for CODE not items), [[project-droplist-packet-engine]].
