## project_itunes_noutube_port

<a id="project-itunes-noutube-port"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_itunes_noutube_port.md`*
*Name:* itunes-noutube-port
*Description:* iTunes library -> NouTube (YouTube) playlist port project, lives outside Pre Atlas repo at C:\\Users\\bruke\\iTunes-Titles-Backup\\

Separate project, NOT in the Pre Atlas repo — lives at `C:\Users\bruke\iTunes-Titles-Backup\`.
Built in a prior session (files dated 2026-07-13 to 2026-07-20) that explicitly wrote a
handoff doc (`PORT_TO_NOUTUBE.md`) for "next chat" instead of relying on memory — good
practice, but it left this project invisible to Pre Atlas's own MEMORY.md since it's a
different folder entirely. Discovered 2026-07-21 via `es` disk search after Bruke asked
"why don't I see any result" for work he remembered being done.

**What it actually is:**
- `iTunes_Library.html` / `iTunes_Library_Titles.csv` — full parsed iTunes library (2,414
  songs, 31 playlists) from the encrypted `.itl` file, via `export_itunes_titles.py`.
- `iTunes_to_NouTube.html` — a static **catalog/launcher page**, one "▶ open in NouTube"
  link per playlist using the `noutube://www.youtube.com/watch_videos?video_ids=...` deep
  link protocol (confirmed via grep — zero `<audio>`/`<iframe>`/player elements in the file).
  It was never designed to embed a player; it hands off to the separately-installed
  **NouTube desktop app** (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\noutube-desktop\noutube.exe`, a
  third-party YouTube/YT Music Electron wrapper, ad-free). That handoff design is
  explicitly documented in `PORT_TO_NOUTUBE.md`.
- `headless_import.py` — a DIFFERENT, deliberately-named "headless" automation script.
  Uses Chrome DevTools Protocol against `noutube.exe --remote-debugging-port=9222` to
  inject playlist bookmarks directly into NouTube's IndexedDB, "bypassing the UI
  entirely." This is about populating saved-playlist folders programmatically, **not**
  about audio playback. The word "headless" here is CDP/automation jargon, unrelated to
  "plays with no visible controls" — a real naming collision that caused confusion.
- `consolidated/` (newest, 2026-07-20) — the actual finished data product: one
  deduplicated dataset (SQLite + CSV + JSON) joining the library + YouTube resolution +
  cover art. 2,299/2,414 tracks have a confirmed video match; 115 are search-only.

**Why it looked "broken"/"headless" to Bruke:** clicking a playlist link in the catalog
HTML fires the `noutube://` deep link, which activates the external NouTube app — 7
`noutube.exe` processes were confirmed running at investigation time. Audio plays inside
NouTube's own window (which should have normal YouTube-style controls, being a real app),
not inside the HTML catalog page — the catalog page itself has no player UI by design, so
if NouTube's window doesn't come to the foreground it reads as "plays with no controls."
This is inferred from the architecture, not confirmed by watching the window behavior
directly — worth checking live if this resurfaces.

**Why this repo's search came up empty:** [[reference_windows_cp1252_encoding_gotcha]] is
unrelated — the real reason is simply that this project is outside `Pre Atlas` entirely,
so repo-scoped tools (Glob/Grep in this cwd) and this repo's MEMORY.md never had it. `es`
(Everything, whole-disk) was required to find it. See [[feedback_use_es_tool_by_default]].

**How to pick this thread back up:** read `PORT_TO_NOUTUBE.md` for the original handoff
context, `consolidated/README.md` for the current data state. Open question left in the
handoff doc: which of the 3 approaches (temp-playlist deep-link / YouTube Data API OAuth /
browser automation) to use for making playlists *permanent* in Bruke's real YouTube
account, since the deep-link approach only creates temporary `watch_videos` playlists.

## project_langgraph_skill_lattice

<a id="project-langgraph-skill-lattice"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_langgraph_skill_lattice.md`*
*Description:* LOCKED PLAN — LangGraph as the missing executor under combo.py's Thompson bandit; skills become graph nodes, seam.v1 Receipts become graph state. Plus the 2026-07-14 orchestration audit that grounded it.

**Bruke, 2026-07-14: "i think all my tools need lang graph… lang graph can also go on lattice and connect my skills."** He was right, and I was wrong to push back at first. Plan locked in `Pre Atlas\docs\LANGGRAPH_SKILL_LATTICE_PLAN.md`. **ALL 7 SEQUENCES DONE incl. live proof, 2026-07-16 — plan closed.**

## Seq 7 shipped (2026-07-16) — Supervisor, closes the plan. Found+fixed 2 real bugs live.

`tools/lattice/supervisor.py` (stdlib `urllib`, fail-soft) registers a lattice run as a
delta-kernel `type: "system"` job (`metadata.kind: "lattice_resume"`) before it starts.
`run_chain.py` gained `--supervised`/`--job-id`/`--delta-kernel-url`/`--job-timeout-ms` + a
`--demo` mode (3-step zero-cost synthetic chain, extracted to `tools/lattice/demo_steps.py` and
shared with Seq 6's viewer so there's one implementation). On the TS side,
`services/delta-kernel/src/governance/lattice_resume.ts` (new, pure, hermetically tested) decides
whether a retried job is a lattice run and builds the `run_chain.py --resume --job-id ...
--supervised` argv; `governance_daemon.ts`'s existing every-minute `runWorkQueue()` dispatches to
it for anything `checkTimeouts()` retries. Not a new ActionType/capability (`TRUST_BOUNDARY.md`)
-- reuses the existing `system` job type and `/api/work/*` routes.

**Live-testing found and fixed two real bugs before this was called done — [[feedback_code_as_furniture]] in action, not just noted:**

1. `supervisor.py` sent no `Authorization: Bearer` header at all -- every call would 403 against a
   real running delta-kernel with a `.aegis-tenant-key` on disk (confirmed live: it IS running
   with a tenant key). Fixed by fetching `/api/auth/token` (the one open route) before every POST.
2. **A genuine pre-existing architecture bug, not introduced by this sequence**: `server.ts` and
   `GovernanceDaemon` each constructed their OWN `new WorkController(repoRoot)`. Both load the
   on-disk ledger once at construction and never see each other's writes again -- a job registered
   via `/api/work/request` (server.ts's instance) was **permanently invisible** to the daemon's
   `checkTimeouts()`, which was checking its own separately-loaded, stale-forever copy. This
   silently broke retry for ANY job registered through the API, not just lattice ones -- found only
   because Seq 7's live test watched `elapsed_ms` climb past `timeout_ms` for 70+ seconds across
   multiple daemon ticks with zero retry log lines. Root-caused by reading the raw
   `work_ledger.json` directly (confirmed `timed_out?: True` in the data) before suspecting the
   daemon's OWN in-memory state, not the data, was stale. Fixed at the root -- `GovernanceDaemon`'s
   constructor now accepts an optional shared `WorkController`; `server.ts` constructs one first
   and passes it to both `getDaemon()` and its own routes. A reload-before-read patch would have
   left the real problem (two independent writers to one file, racing on every mutation, not just
   checkTimeouts) in place; sharing the instance removes it entirely.

**Live-verified literally, not just hermetically**: registered a supervised `--demo` run, found its
real OS PID via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`, `Stop-Process -Force`-killed it mid-chain
(confirmed via an EMPTY stdout log -- receipts only print at the very end, so empty = genuinely
killed before completion), then did nothing else and waited for the real governance-daemon cron
(`*/1 * * * *`) to fire on its own. Unedited log trace:

```
[Daemon] Work queue: 1 job(s) retried after timeout: j_9g9thkqdmrmyony2
[Daemon] lattice_resume: re-launching thread_id=seq7-live-demo4 for retried job j_9g9thkqdmrmyony2
[Daemon] lattice_resume: job j_9g9thkqdmrmyony2 resume completed
```

Confirmed past the log line: a direct `graph.aget_state()` query against the checkpoint sqlite
showed all 3 demo steps `ok` in the final state (not just a clean process exit code), and
`/api/work/status` showed the job cleared from `active`. **Zero manual `--resume` invocation for
this particular thread_id** -- the daemon did the entire resurrection on its own, which is the
plan's literal DoD: "Kill a run; the queue's checkTimeouts() resurrects it without human action."

19 new hermetic tests: `test_lattice_supervisor.py` (10 -- request/complete shapes, fail-soft on
unreachable/malformed responses, the auth-header regression itself), `lattice-resume-tests.ts` (7
-- pure `isLatticeResumeJob`/`buildResumeArgs`/`resolveLatticePython`, split out of
`governance_daemon.ts` specifically so they're testable without constructing a full daemon), and
`governance-daemon-shared-workcontroller-tests.ts` (2 -- the shared-instance regression test,
reproducing the exact bug class via a monkey-patched `checkTimeouts` spy AND a real
registered-then-retried job run through `daemon.runJob('work_queue')`). Full suite: 258/258
Python, 8/8 TS test files green (fabric 4/4, work-controller 6/6, routing 6/6, lattice-droplist
14/14, artifact-schema 11/11, api 14/14, lattice-resume 7/7, daemon-shared-controller 2/2).

**Not committed yet as of this memory write** -- see git status before assuming pushed/committed.

**THE PLAN IS CLOSED.** All 7 sequences done with live proof: Seq 1 (receipt store) → Seq 2
(skill adapters) → Seq 3 (LangGraph spine) → Seq 4 (bandit node) → Seq 5 (learn loop) → Seq 6
(live viewer) → Seq 7 (supervisor). The original gap -- "a learned policy over skills with no
durable executor" -- is closed: skills are graph nodes, receipts are durable state, the bandit
routes, the ledger learns from lattice-driven runs too, a browser watches it live, and a killed
run resurrects itself without a human.

## Seq 6 shipped (2026-07-16) — live browser viewer, real bug found+fixed in the UI

`tools/lattice/viewer_server.py` (FastAPI: `POST /run`, `GET /structure/{thread_id}`,
`GET /stream/{thread_id}` SSE) + `tools/lattice/viewer.html` (Cytoscape + `cytoscape-dagre`)
render a `build_chain_graph` run LIVE: node colors go gray→blue→green/red as each `@task`
actually completes, streamed off `graph.astream(..., stream_mode="updates")`.

**Assemble-first call, reasoned through explicitly**: `apps/lattice/index.html` is a large,
UNRELATED production surface (work-item viewmodel UI over delta-kernel, its own locked 3-week
plan — see its `NEXT_SESSION_HANDOFF.md` — Replicache write-paths). Extending/forking it for
this would be scope creep into a system with its own moat and verdict ("compose, don't fork,
don't relitigate"). The correct reuse is the vendored `cytoscape.min.js`/`cytoscape-dagre.js`
LIBRARIES (mounted read-only at `/vendor`), not the app — confirmed by actually reading that
app's own locked plan before deciding, not assumed from the name match.

**Non-obvious mechanism, verified not assumed**: `stream_mode="updates"` fires TWICE per node
— once for the inner `@task`'s raw return, once for the node's `{"receipts":[...]}` state patch,
both under the SAME node-name key. `_is_node_update()` filters to only the state-patch shape.

**Live-verified in an actual browser** (`preview_start` + Browser pane tools, not curl/
TestClient): demo chain (3 synthetic nodes, one deliberately built to fail) → confirmed via
direct Cytoscape state inspection (`cy.nodes().map(n=>({id:n.id(),classes:n.classes()}))`) that
2 nodes ended `"ok"` and 1 ended `"error"`, exactly matching each step's real outcome. Then ran
a REAL `code-recon` call through the actual UI (clicked buttons, typed into fields) and
confirmed that node ended `"ok"` too.

**Found and fixed a real bug via the live run, not caught by any hermetic test**: the first UI
draft used one `"skill:prompt, skill:prompt"` text field with comma/colon delimiters. A prompt
containing a natural comma ("In one sentence, what does...") silently mis-parsed into a bogus
second pair, failing with `unknown skill(s)`. Root-caused by reading `document.getElementById(
...).value` directly (not guessed from the error text alone), fixed by replacing the fragile
delimiter scheme with a proper skill `<select>` + a plain prompt field, then re-ran the EXACT
same comma-containing prompt through the fixed UI and confirmed `code-recon` → `"ok"`. Screenshot
tool timed out repeatedly this session (environment quirk, `read_page`/`javascript_tool` worked
fine) — direct Cytoscape state inspection was the proof, arguably stronger than a screenshot
since it's unambiguous rather than requiring visual interpretation.

4 hermetic tests cover pure logic only (node-name disambiguation, the updates-stream filter,
demo-step receipt shapes) — the FastAPI/SSE routes themselves are proven by the live browser run,
not a mocked TestClient, since background-task SSE streaming is exactly the kind of thing that
looks right under a mock and breaks for real. 246/246 full suite green.

at a glance but is actually `hydra-engine`. Added a `lattice-viewer` launch.json entry.

## Seq 5 shipped (2026-07-16) — close the learn loop + decided the ledger-vs-get_state_history question

`tools/lattice/ledger_feed.py` mirrors `tools/seam/run.py`'s `_append_ledger`/`_ledger_rows`
**exactly** — same row schema, same objective ok+sha256 reward convention (a receipt counts as
a win iff `status=="ok"` AND it has a `sha256`, else a loss), same `SEAM_LEDGER=1`/
`SEAM_LEDGER_PATH` gate. Reused, not reinvented — furniture already in the house
([[feedback_assemble_first_posture]]). Wired into `run_chain.py`, called automatically after
every `ainvoke`.

**Decided the plan's own "OPEN QUESTION FOR SEQ 3"** (should the JSONL ledger just *be*
`graph.get_state_history()` across threads?) — **No**, checked three reasons before deciding,
not assumed: (1) **coverage** — the ledger spans every session (interactive+seam+lattice);
`get_state_history()` is scoped to one thread, so swapping would blind `combo.py` to the much
larger non-lattice volume (209 real rows today, lattice has produced a handful); (2) **reward
semantics** — a checkpointed Receipt has no reward field, only status/error; the ledger's reward
is sentiment+shipped/retried fusion or seam's objective convention, neither derivable from a
bare checkpoint; (3) **coupling** — `combo.py` is deliberately stdlib-only, coupling it to
LangGraph's internal checkpoint schema buys nothing. Unification = lattice runs FEED the same
ledger seam feeds, not "replace the substrate."

Lattice rows are **`seq`-shaped** (`a>b`), not `cofire`: each row gets a DISTINCT `request` key
(unlike seam's one-shared-request-per-manifest), so combo.py's turn-chunking treats each node
completion as its own turn and derives ordered transitions — matching what a linear chain graph
actually does. The bandit's own receipt (`tool="bandit"`) is EXCLUDED from the feed — it always
immediately precedes whatever it routed to by construction, so it's not a learnable transition,
just noise that would pollute genuine skill-to-skill combos.

**8 hermetic tests** (`services/atlas-map-api/tests/test_lattice_ledger_feed.py`, 242/242 full
suite green) prove the DoD literally via `combo.evaluate()` on a synthetic lattice-shaped
ledger. **Found and fixed a real flakiness bug en route** — worth remembering as a pattern:
`evaluate()`'s holdout split hashes session id, and Python's `hash()` is salted per PROCESS
(`PYTHONHASHSEED`), not per test — a small fixture (12-vs-3 sessions) failed 2/5 explicit seeds.
Root-caused via a direct diagnostic print of the full `evaluate()` result dict (not guessed):
the failing seed's 30% holdout draw contained ZERO losing-arm sessions, so
`random_expected_reward` accidentally tied `combo_expected_reward` — a genuine tie, which
`evaluate()`'s own docstring calls honest reporting, not a bug to route around. Fixed by scaling
the fixture (200-vs-60 sessions) until a zero-draw is vanishingly improbable; **stress-tested
0/30 failures across seeds 0-29** before trusting it, not just re-running until green.

**Live-verified end to end** (real `code-recon` call, `SEAM_LEDGER=1` pointed at a scratch file
— NOT the real ledger, to avoid polluting production data with a test run): produced a real
Receipt, `run_chain.py` printed `lattice: fed 1 row(s) to the tool-outcomes ledger`, and the
appended row's content was confirmed byte-for-byte against `_ledger_rows`' schema by reading the
scratch file directly. Scratch artifacts (sqlite checkpoint + jsonl) deleted after, not committed.

ledger_feed.py`, `tools/lattice/run_chain.py` (wiring), `services/atlas-map-api/tests/
test_lattice_ledger_feed.py`, `tools/lattice/README.md` (Seq 5 section), plan-doc Seq 5 row +
the OPEN QUESTION section marked decided.

## Seq 4 shipped (2026-07-16) — bandit node (Design Constraint 1)

`tools/lattice/bandit.py`: `make_bandit_node(seed, load_combos)` draws once from `combo.py`'s
Thompson-sampled ranking over tool-combination arms — imports `~/.claude/scripts/ledger/
combo.py` directly (`combo.pick_combo(combo.build_combos(combo.router.load_rows()))`), doesn't
fork it — wraps the draw in a `@task`, records it as a `seam.v1` Receipt, writes the chosen
combo to `state["next_combo"]`. `route(state)` is a pure function used as the conditional edge:
reads `next_combo` back out, zero randomness. This is the exact split Design Constraint 1
demands — LangGraph edges get re-evaluated in ways that aren't checkpointed the way nodes are,
so a draw living in an edge would silently re-randomize on replay; living in a node, it can't.

**6 hermetic tests** (`services/atlas-map-api/tests/test_lattice_bandit.py`, 240/240 full suite
green) prove the plan's own DoD wording directly, not a paraphrase: `aget_state_history()` shows
the drawn arm recorded as a receipt; a downstream crash + `ainvoke(None, config)` resume
produces the **identical** arm — proven via a call-counting `load_combos` stub that stays at
exactly 1 invocation across the crash/resume boundary (mechanism-level proof, not "the output
looked the same"). Plus cold-start safety: empty ledger → `next_combo=None` → routes to
`"done"`, no crash.

**Live-checked against the real ledger** (pure local Thompson sampling — no API cost, no
`claude-agent-sdk` involved): 209 real ledger rows → 176 derived combos → ran the actual node
through a real `StateGraph` and got a genuine `seam.v1` Receipt (`status: "ok"`, real 64-hex
`sha256`) with the top-ranked arm (`code-recon>groundwork-cli`, score 0.9999, n=12) correctly
written to `next_combo`.

**COMMITTED 2026-07-16, `989aa30`** on `feat/atlas-setup-ui` (not pushed) — `tools/lattice/
bandit.py`, `services/atlas-map-api/tests/test_lattice_bandit.py`, `tools/lattice/README.md`
(Seq 4 section), plan-doc Seq 4 row.

**>>> NEXT: Seq 5 — close the learn loop.** Every node completion should append a ledger row

## Seq 3 shipped (2026-07-16, hermetic) — LangGraph spine

`tools/lattice/graph.py`: `build_chain_graph(steps, order, checkpointer)` compiles a linear
`StateGraph` (`State.receipts: Annotated[list[dict], operator.add]`); each node `await`s exactly
one `@task`-wrapped `StepFn` (name + zero-arg sync/async callable returning a Receipt dict) —
Design Constraint 2 compliance. Not yet wired to a real `StepFn` (Seq 2's `invoke_skill` or a
Seq 1 `/seam/call`) — that's a one-line adapter, deliberately deferred, not blocked.

**Installed `langgraph` 1.2.9 + `langgraph-checkpoint-sqlite` 3.1.0 into
`services/atlas-map-api/.venv` only** — new `lattice` pyproject extra. Checked reverse deps
FIRST (`importlib.metadata`): the machine's **global** Python already has `langchain` 0.3.x +
`langchain-community`/`-chroma`/`-ollama` pinned to `langchain-core<1.0.0`, for unrelated tools.
`langgraph` hard-pins `langchain-core>=1.4.7,<2` — a global install would have broken those.
Confirmed the conflict before installing, didn't assume isolation was needed or safe.

**Two more plan-shorthand corrections, verified against installed source (not assumed):**
`durability` is an `ainvoke()`-time kwarg, not `compile()`-time. And since every node here is
async, the checkpointer must be `langgraph.checkpoint.sqlite.aio.AsyncSqliteSaver` — the sync
`SqliteSaver` the plan names raises `NotImplementedError` against an async graph (confirmed by
running it and reading the actual error, not by reading docs).

**5 hermetic tests** (`services/atlas-map-api/tests/test_lattice_graph.py`, 228/228 full suite
green) prove the actual DoD: (1) normal linear run; (2) crash-in-node-then-resume via
`ainvoke(None, config)` — node-level Pregel durability, doesn't re-run the completed node;
(3) **two `@task` calls inside ONE node**, crash between them — the specific case Design
Constraint 2 warns about (a node re-running its own body on resume must not re-invoke an
already-completed nested task) — proves the first task's underlying function runs exactly once
while the second genuinely retries; (4) receipt `sha256` identical across the crash/resume
boundary, matching the plan's own DoD phrasing ("proven by receipt CIDs being identical, not
regenerated"); (5) resume survives a **genuinely fresh `AsyncSqliteSaver` connection** against
the same on-disk file (not the same in-process Python object) — the hermetic proxy for an
actually-killed-and-restarted process, since a real `kill -9` test isn't practical in pytest.

## The finding (6-agent forensic recon, all file:line verified)

**You have a learned policy over skills with NO durable executor.**
- `~/.claude/scripts/ledger/combo.py` — Thompson bandit over skill *tuples*, **beats random** (+0.19..+0.57), learns from `tool-outcomes.jsonl` (**203 rows**, live). But it can only `suggest`. A human fires the skills by hand.
- `tools/seam/run.py:478` — **every stage binds the same input arg; NO stage consumes the prior stage's output.** seam fans out, it does not chain.
- `tools/seam/run.py:387` — receipts are `print()`ed. Zero persistence.
- `atlas-map-api/seam.py:57-107` + `server.py:678` — **mints proper `seam.v1` Receipts and throws them away.** Only durable trace of any gateway call = a per-capability integer counter (`call_counter.py:56`).
- **ZERO orchestration libraries anywhere in the stack** (every manifest checked: delta-kernel has only `node-cron`; optogon/uasc/cortex/cognitive-sensor/atlas-map/search-stack/groundwork/delta-scp/fest/lattice → none). ~30 hand-rolled coordination points; almost none crash-resume.
- Worst breaks found: optogon persists session state every turn but **`list_active()` has ZERO production callers** (`session_store.py:151`) — nothing ever resumes it. uasc-executor crash → `runs` row stuck at `'running'` **forever**, no reaper. cortex **never persists the LLM-authored plan** (`loop.py:23-38`). `OPTOGON_SIGNAL_EMIT` is an **in-memory list with no outbox** (`signals.py:24`) — turning it on today ships a lossy bus.
- **`fest` is the ONE thing that genuinely resumes** — event-sourced JSONL replay, event log is authoritative and repairs a stale summary (`fest-win/src/internal/workflow/localstore/store.go:347,369,483`). The pattern is already proven in his own hand; it's just wired to methodology steps, not tool calls.

## The two design constraints that would have been silent bugs

From primary docs (`docs.langchain.com/oss/python/langgraph/durable-execution`). **Non-obvious. Get these wrong and every resume corrupts.**
1. **The bandit must be a NODE, not an edge.** LangGraph *replays* on resume. A Thompson draw in an edge function **re-draws differently on every replay**. Draw inside a node → write the arm to state as a receipt → edge is a pure `lambda s: s["next_tool"]`.
2. **Every skill/CLI call goes in `@task`.** Resume restarts the interrupted node **from its beginning** — a node that shells out then dies **re-runs the shell command**. Only `@task` results are checkpointed. **The Receipt `sha256` is the natural idempotency key** — Bruke's content-addressing already solves LangGraph's worst footgun.

## Verified mechanism
- **`claude-agent-sdk`** (py) / `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk` (ts). **Skills ARE forceable**: `ClaudeAgentOptions(skills=["code-recon"], setting_sources=["user","project"])`. Typed node return via `output_format={"type":"json_schema","schema":...}` → `message.structured_output`. Bound with `max_budget_usd` + `max_turns`. **No official LangGraph↔Agent-SDK integration exists** — this is net-new wiring.
- **`langgraph` 1.2.9** — does NOT require `langchain` (the abstraction layer) but **DOES hard-pin `langchain-core`**. Can't shed it. Zero-LLM nodes work (plain Python fns) but are off the documented path.
- `SqliteSaver` = `langgraph-checkpoint-sqlite` 3.1.0. **Must pass `durability="sync"`** — default `"async"` has a crash-loss window.
- **LangGraph is NOT Temporal**: in-process, single-language, **no auto-resume** — something external must call `graph.invoke(None, config)`. **delta-kernel's work queue is that supervisor** (already does claim/heartbeat/timeout).
- Studio needs a LangSmith account → skip. `stream_mode="updates"` + `draw_mermaid()` = free, offline, full observability.
- Set `LANGGRAPH_STRICT_MSGPACK=true` day one (compromised checkpoint DB = code-exec vector).

## Non-goals (trust boundary)
`delta-kernel` governance spine (`routing.ts`: "LOCKED — deterministic only. No AI."), `uasc-executor` (token set closed by source, `schema.sql:55-65`), `fest` (Go, and already resumable). Never import `langchain` proper.

## Process lesson (this session)
A recon agent claimed "no Thompson router, no combo.py, zero hits repo-wide" — it had scoped only to Pre Atlas; the tree lives in `~/.claude/scripts/ledger/`. **I nearly wrote a memory 'correcting' a memory that was right.** [[feedback_agent_report_distrust]] earned its keep. Also: I initially pushed back on LangGraph on the grounds that "you don't have a routing problem" — that was me deciding his problem for him, and I'd overstated LangGraph as Python-only (it has a TS lib) and framed combo.py-vs-LangGraph as either/or when **combo.py plugs IN as the router**. He pushed back and was right.

## Reconciled against a Gemini chat (2026-07-15)

A Gemini conversation ([[project_hydra_gemini_dev_os_concepts]] — the "deterministic execution"
head of the multi-headed HYDRA) independently proposed a near-identical architecture ("Prep &
Pack": deterministic scripts + Pydantic schemas + a "Schema Librarian" router + LangGraph
coordinator) without visibility into this stack. Reconciliation verdict: **it adds zero new
scope.** Every component maps onto something already locked here and more grounded:
Schema Librarian = `combo.py` (built, proven, just needs Seq 4's node promotion); Pydantic
schemas = `seam.v1` Receipts + `output_format` (Seq 2, and the receipt `sha256` solves an
idempotency problem Gemini's version never surfaced); deterministic scripts = the existing ~8
skills with real ledger `n`, invoked via `@task` (Gemini proposed writing new bespoke scripts —
an assemble-first violation). Gemini's naive version, if built as stated, would have hit all
four footguns this doc already defused: bandit-in-edge replay bug, non-`@task` skill calls
re-running on resume, default `durability="async"` loss window, and no named external
supervisor (this doc already identifies delta-kernel's work queue as that supervisor, Seq 7).

Two genuinely small additions worth folding into node design when Seq 3 gets built (not new
phases, not scope changes): (1) a Planner/Executor/Reviewer role lens for node prompts — roughly
what "skills become graph nodes" already implies; (2) cache-friendly node-prompt ordering
(static content first, dynamic input last), relevant because Honest Cost #3 says every node is
a full agentic session. `repomix`/`CntxtPY`/`Aider` (Gemini's suggested repo-mapping tools) are
redundant with `delta-scp`, already in this stack — don't adopt a second one without comparing.

## project_lattice

<a id="project-lattice"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_lattice.md`*
*Name:* project-lattice
*Description:* Single-file responsive HTML task tracker (tree/graph/time views) at C:\\Users\\bruke\\Downloads\\lattice.html. Personal prototype, not yet in monorepo.

Built 2026-05-28 in a long iterative session with Bruke. One self-contained HTML file (~1500 lines, no external deps · inline SVG icons in place of Tabler webfont).

**File:** `C:\Users\bruke\Downloads\lattice.html`, alongside the original mockups `lattice-ui_1.html` (desktop) and `lattice-narrow.html` (mobile) that Bruke had downloaded.

**Origin:** Bruke dropped two mockups, asked to compare. Realized mid-session they were the same app at two breakpoints, not competing designs. Asked to merge into one responsive build. The build evolved through six iterative requests:
1. Merged responsive: ui_1 desktop chrome at ≥600px, narrow's single-column layout below, data-driven from one items array (so dataset never forks)
2. Timeline sub-modes: list / 7-day / calendar with pill sub-switch under the timeline tab
3. Today button: topbar, jumps to today's anchor in whichever sub-mode is active
4. Zoom feature: ctrl+wheel + +/- buttons + keyboard +/- to scroll between sub-modes (list=most-zoomed-in, calendar=most-zoomed-out)
5. Airtable-style polish: inline `+ new item` row per section, right-click context menu (status / show in graph / delete), color rails on rows
6. Color rails removed per [[no-color-rails-on-rows]] (Bruke: "i hate the strip it gives claude code made this")

**Data shape (single source of truth):**
- `items[]`: `{ id, title, project, status, time, links: [{kind, to}] }`
- `events[]`: `{ date, day, time, kind, project, itemId, desc }`
- `projects[]`: `{ id, name }`
- `status`: open | active | blocked | done | dropped
- `link.kind`: blocks | spawned | relates
- `event.kind`: add | status | link | done

14 hand-crafted items across 6 projects (lattice/optogon/atlas/inpact/property/music) using Bruke's real work (ship lattice v0, audit v2 spec, USDA 502 timeline, dark-edm strudel set, etc.). 11 events spanning may 25-28 2026.

**Views and interactions:**
- Tree: grouped by project, inline `+ new item` per section, click item → graph, right-click → context menu
- Graph: 1-hop neighborhood around focus item (hand-tuned layout for #4 desktop + #4 mobile · fallback ring for other centers). Click node to recenter.
- Timeline: list (day-grouped flat) · 7-day (column grid desktop / stacked mobile) · calendar (month grid with kind-colored dots per day). Click cal-day-with-events → drops to list mode.

**Mutations work end-to-end in memory:**
- Add: inline edit row → enter saves, escape cancels, blur commits
- Status change: right-click → status option → re-renders tree+sidebar+counts
- Delete: right-click → delete → strips inbound links from other items
- All updates flow through `rerenderAll()` to keep tree+sidebar+graph+timeline in sync

**Known gaps (not built):**
- No persistence; refresh wipes mutations and resets to demo data
- `TODAY_ISO = '2026-05-28'` hardcoded; real `new Date()` is one line
- Topbar "add" button is decorative
- Graph layout only hand-tuned for centerId=4; fallback ring works for other centers but isn't optimal
- Sidebar status/project filters affect tree but ignore timeline (counts, week column events, calendar dots)
- Calendar `has-events` cell click only routes to list mode but doesn't scroll to that day

**The realization that ended the session (project-defining):**
Bruke noticed the surface felt like "anti-Atlas" → landed on Atlas-in-the-background, lattice-on-the-frontend → "ingesting thousands of conversations will look like magic" → "atlas plus lattice is OP". Lattice is the experience layer Atlas's substrate (cognitive-sensor / perception / triangulation / optogon / cortex / 47 contracts / 6-mode machine) was always trying to enable. See [[project-atlas-lattice-seam]] for the NEXT BUILD target.

**Background still running at session end:**
- Bash `begbri7s7`: `python -m http.server 8799 --bind 127.0.0.1` in `C:\Users\bruke\Downloads`
- Playwright browser (last navigated to lattice.html)
- 13+ throwaway PNGs (`lattice-*.png`, `timeline-*.png`, `today-*.png`, `zoom-*.png`, etc.) in repo root from verification screenshots; auto-mode blocked bulk delete

**Audit-trail link:** the no-em-dash rule [[no-em-dashes-in-ui]] was honored throughout (the original ui_1 had one em dash in the sr-only heading; replaced with colon).

## project_libsql_spine_decision

<a id="project-libsql-spine-decision"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_libsql_spine_decision.md`*
*Name:* libsql-spine-decision
*Description:* Turso fork resolved — libSQL is the delta-kernel spine, Turso-Rust parked; proven green behind DELTA_DB_DRIVER flag

**Decision (2026-06-25):** For the "rip queue" Turso integration, the spine is **libSQL** (tursodatabase's production better-sqlite3-compatible synchronous fork), NOT the beta Turso-Rust engine. Turso-Rust is parked as a lead alongside flue/agentfs.

**Why:** (1) `delta-kernel/src/cli/sqlite-storage.ts` + `src/atlas/preferences-store.ts` use only the synchronous better-sqlite3 core API → `libsql` npm is a near-drop-in. (2) libSQL already ships production native vectors + embedded-replica sync (what Lattice/Ledger need). (3) Turso-Rust's headline `BEGIN CONCURRENT` (MVCC) is *contraindicated* — the hub serializes writes on purpose to keep the delta hash-chain fork-free (sqlite-storage.ts header); Pre Atlas is single-writer hub-and-spoke ([[project-four-part-pipeline-topology]]), no concurrent-write bottleneck. (4) beta engine under a deterministic governance core violates code-as-furniture + CLOSURE/HIGH-risk. (5) `--mcp` already covered by atlas-map/[[project-self-describing-surfaces]].

**How to apply:** Driver shim at `services/delta-kernel/src/cli/db-driver.ts` (`makeDatabase`). Default = better-sqlite3 (no behavior change). `DELTA_DB_DRIVER=libsql` routes through libSQL. Wired at both connection sites. **Proof 2026-06-25 (green):** core 4/4, test:work 5/5, test:lattice-droplist 14/14, test:artifact-schema 11/11 — identical on both drivers. `test:api` 9/9 red on BOTH drivers (pre-existing 401 auth-harness gap, spawned as a task, NOT a libSQL regression). `libsql` added to delta-kernel devDependencies. **Default NOT flipped** — flipping the deterministic hub's default driver is a separate deliberate move for a non-CLOSURE day.

**State 2026-06-25 ~20:45 — UNCOMMITTED** on `feat/atlas-setup-ui`: 4 changes (new `src/cli/db-driver.ts`; wirings in `src/cli/sqlite-storage.ts` + `src/atlas/preferences-store.ts`; `package.json`/lock libsql dep). Files persist on disk through compaction. **Next steps:** (1) commit scoped, **excluding `services/crucix` submodule** (never bulk `git add -A`); (2) flip default → libsql on a non-CLOSURE day after a soak period; (3) rip-queue item 2 = compare codebase-memory-mcp's node/edge/event schema vs Lattice's items/links/events. The `test:api` 9×401 failures are a pre-existing, driver-independent auth-harness gap → spawned as task_f9d3dc9e (fix the harness to send a Bearer token, not the auth gate). Repos all verified real this session; see [[synthetic-github-api]] for the metadata-fabrication caveat.

**Re-verified 2026-06-26 (via autopilot driving fest [[project_atlas_leverage_integration]] AL0002):** all 5 suites green on BOTH drivers — core 4/4, **test:api 9/9** (✅ the 2026-06-25 9×401 auth-harness gap is RESOLVED: the Bearer-token fix is committed in `51c52cc`, which ALSO banked the db-driver shim + sqlite/preferences storage wirings → the prior "UNCOMMITTED" state above is now **COMMITTED** on `feat/atlas-setup-ui`), test:work / lattice-droplist / artifact-schema exit 0. Resolved `libsql` **0.5.29**. The only red this run was a transient `test:api` **port-race** in back-to-back driver-matrix runs (3 isolated libSQL runs were 9/9 green) — NOT a libSQL defect; logged as a test-isolation follow-up. **libSQL drop-in PROVEN**; default driver still better-sqlite3 (flip is a separate deliberate move).

## project_m3p_to_fract

<a id="project-m3p-to-fract"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_m3p_to_fract.md`*
*Name:* m3p-to-fract bridge
*Description:* First open-source converter from Mandelbulb 3D .m3p parameter files to Mandelbulber 2 .fract — v0.1 shipped 2026-05-12 via /weapon at C:\Users\bruke\m3p-to-fract\

# m3p-to-fract

**Location:** `C:\Users\bruke\m3p-to-fract\`
**Status:** v0.1 SHIPPED 2026-05-12 via `/weapon` (10/10 tasks, 10/10 tests).

Converts Mandelbulb 3D `.m3p` parameter files to Mandelbulber 2 `.fract` settings files that Mb2 CLI renders headlessly.

## Why this exists (the pivot)

Earlier in 2026-05-11 session: Bruke asked about modernizing MB3D ("update 2003 to 2026 standards" → "faster + pipeline + web rendering + API" → "or extract logic into a Blender plugin"). Two parallel research agents converged on **wrap Mandelbulber 2, don't port MB3D**:
- Mb2 already implements ~85-95% of MB3D's 461 formulas natively in C++ + OpenCL with multi-GPU
- One Mb2 formula is literally `menger3_m3d.cl` — a deliberate MB3D port
- Mb2 has 6-slot hybrid composition (math-isomorphic to MB3D)
- Mb2 has a rich `--nogui` CLI with `--keyframe`, `-O KEY=VALUE`, multi-GPU `-G`
- **`.m3p → .fract` converter does NOT exist.** Mb2 issue [#630](https://github.com/buddhi1980/mandelbulber2/issues/630) "import MB3D parameter files" has been open since 2019 with no PR.
- The closest prior art on the planet was Bruke's own `parse_m3p.py` in the mandelbulb3d skill.

So the smallest valuable ship was: **the missing converter**. This project IS that.

## Architecture

```
input.m3p (MB3D binary)
        |
        | parse_m3p.parse_blob() -- header + 6 slots
        v
   { Width, Height, dXmid/Ymid/Zmid, dZoom, dFOVy, Iterations, slots[6] }
        |
        | translate_header() -- main_parameters dict
        | translate_slots()  -- formula_map.json lookup, KeyError if unmapped
        v
   { main_parameters, fractal_1..N }
        |
        | build_fract_dict + parse_fract.dump_fract()
        v
output.fract (Mb2 INI text, comma-decimal)
        |
        | mandelbulber2.exe --nogui --format png -r WxH --output OUT.png
        v
output.png
```

## Formula coverage (v0.1)

14 mappings in `data/formula_map.json`:
- **9 of 10 built-in iFnr** (iFnr=7 'test' has no Mb2 equivalent and is intentionally unmapped)
- **5 popular custom `.m3f` names** by string match: `_HybridBulbox`, `ABoxModKali`, `AmazingSurf`, `_MengerSponge`, `_Mandelbox`

Mb2 canonical IDs in `data/mb2_formula_ids.json` (474 entries, regenerated from `all_fractal_list_enums.hpp` by `tools/extract_mb2_ids.py`).

## Cuts (v0.1 → v0.2+)

- Multi-slot hybrid composition (6-slot mixing) — Mb2 supports it natively, just needs proportional iter math
- Per-slot fractal params (`byOptionType[16]` / `dOptionValue[16]` → Mb2 `[fractal_N]` body)
- Camera rotation / up vector / DOF / AO
- `.m3f` custom-formula port to Mb2 OpenCL kernel (precedent: `menger3_m3d.cl`)
- Animation / keyframe support (Mb2 has `-K --keyframe` + `-F --flight` already)
- Text-share `Mandelbulb3DvN{...}` paste parsing (parse_m3p.py supports it; CLI doesn't expose it)
- Blender plugin (separate ship; Houdini contrib `hou_mandelbulber.py` is the template — `subprocess` + file I/O pattern)

## Bugs fixed inline (Code-as-Furniture)

1. **Unicode arrow in docstring broke cp1252** during `--help` output on Windows. Replaced all `→` with `->` in source.
2. **4-line `.fract` preamble triggered "Format not specified" rejection.** Mb2's `settings/CSettings.cpp` expects exactly 3 leading comment lines: `# Mandelbulber settings file` / `# version N.NN` / `# only modified parameters`. Trimmed extra comments.

## Validation

- `python -m pytest tests/ -v` — 10 tests, all pass:
  - `test_header_basic`, `test_header_skips_zeros`
  - `test_mb2_ids_loaded`, `test_formula_map_loaded`
  - `test_slot_translation_builtin`, `_custom_name`, `_unmapped_raises`
  - `test_convert_roundtrip` (real `.m3p` fixture)
  - `test_convert_missing_file_raises`
  - `test_cli_unmapped_exits_1`
- End-to-end: `python m3p_to_fract.py fixtures/builtin_mandelbulb.m3p out/e2e.fract` → `mandelbulber2 --nogui ... out/e2e.png` → 12,877-byte valid PNG.

## Strategic position

This is the **bridgehead** between two ecosystems Bruke uses heavily:
- MB3D: 461 community formulas + thousands of `.m3p` shares (fractalforums.com)
- Mb2: GPU-accelerated, headless, scriptable, multi-platform

With the bridge in place, the next ships are unlocked:
- **Blender plugin** = `subprocess.run(mandelbulber2 ...)` + `bpy.ops.image.open()` — pattern is Mb2's existing Houdini contrib script
- **Web/API** = Express/Hono wrapping `mandelbulber2 --nogui`
- **Animation farm** = `--keyframe` + frame-range on a render box
- **Catalog/intel** = bulk-convert MB3D History/ folders to Mb2 then batch-render

## Files

```
m3p-to-fract/
├── m3p_to_fract.py            CLI: translate_header, translate_slots, build_fract_dict, convert, main
├── data/
│   ├── mb2_formula_ids.json    474 entries (Mb2 enumFractalFormula)
│   └── formula_map.json        14 entries (MB3D -> Mb2)
├── vendor/
│   ├── parse_m3p.py            from ~/.claude/skills/mandelbulb3d/scripts/
│   └── parse_fract.py          from ~/.claude/skills/mandelbulber2/scripts/
├── tools/
│   └── extract_mb2_ids.py      regen mb2_formula_ids.json from Mb2 source
├── tests/
│   └── test_translate.py       10 tests
├── fixtures/
│   └── builtin_mandelbulb.m3p  vendored from MB3D History/
├── out/                        gitignored
├── .weapon/                    spec.md, plan.md, progress.jsonl, mission.json, closed.md
├── .gitignore
└── README.md
```

## project_mandelbulb3d_skill

<a id="project-mandelbulb3d-skill"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_mandelbulb3d_skill.md`*
*Name:* Mandelbulb3D skill build
*Description:* Built the mandelbulb3d skill end-to-end via /weapon on 2026-05-10. Skill at ~/.claude/skills/mandelbulb3d/. Source ground truth cloned at C:\repos\mb3d-source\ (github.com/thargor6/mb3d, 1030 files, LGPL).

# Mandelbulb3D skill (2026-05-10, SHIPPED via /weapon)

**Skill location:** `~/.claude/skills/mandelbulb3d/`
**Source repo:** `C:\repos\mb3d-source\` (github.com/thargor6/mb3d, LGPL, Andreas Maschke + others)
**Mission state:** `C:\Users\bruke\.claude\skills\mandelbulb3d\.weapon\` (spec, plan, progress, audit, closed.md)

## What the skill teaches

Progressive disclosure: top-level `SKILL.md` (105 body lines) routes to four scope docs in `reference/`:
- `parameters.md` (253 lines) — `.m3p` binary format (TMandHeader10 + THeaderCustomAddon), text format Mandelbulb3DvN{...}, 3-bytes-to-4-chars custom-alphabet encoding (`./0-9A-Za-z`), 40+ named header fields by byte offset
- `formulas.md` (284 lines) — 10 built-ins, ~30 community formulas across ABox/ASurf/Mandelbulb/Menger/Sierpinski/Quaternion/Aexion families, 15+ transforms, 6 hybrid recipes, visual-goal mapping
- `workflow.md` (265 lines) — 6 UI tabs, preview vs full param tables, postprocess chain, lighting/AO/DOF/stereo, 5 target-visual recipes, 11 failure modes, perf budget
- `jit-formulas.md` (363 lines) — `.m3f` structure (`[OPTIONS]`/`[SOURCE]`/`[CODE]`/`[END]`), Pascal source surface, TIteration3D state record, 50+ math primitives, DE convention, worked example AbsFoldRotateBox, 6 idiom patterns

Plus `examples/parameters/INDEX.md` (80 source-repo scenes categorized), `examples/parameters/anatomy_of_a_share.md` (byte-level walkthrough), `examples/formulas/jit_walkthrough_kalezobox.md` (annotated real JIT formula), `scripts/parse_m3p.py` (pure-stdlib parser, handles both binary and text formats, round-trip validated).

## Critical correction made during build

Original plan assumed `.m3p` was a text-INI format with sections like `[Mand3D]`. WRONG. `.m3p` is a packed binary `TMandHeader10` (840 bytes) + `THeaderCustomAddon` (~1136 bytes). The shareable text format is `Mandelbulb3DvN{...}` (where N is header version, currently 18) which encodes the same bytes 3-to-4 in a custom base64 variant. Pivoted in-flight; documented in `.weapon/inventory.md`.

## Why: Use it like it's native

Bruke wanted Claude to operate MB3D fluently, not just answer general questions. The skill encodes operational mastery: pick the right formula for a visual goal, hybridize correctly, decode a fractalforums.com paste, author a custom `.m3f`. Every claim cites a source file:line in `C:\repos\mb3d-source\` (no hallucination from training data).

## How to apply

## project_mb3d_blender

<a id="project-mb3d-blender"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_mb3d_blender.md`*
*Name:* mb3d-blender addon
*Description:* Blender 4.x addon that renders MB3D .m3p files inside Blender by chaining m3p_to_fract + mandelbulber2 --nogui. v0.1 shipped 2026-05-12 via /weapon at C:\Users\bruke\mb3d-blender\

# mb3d-blender

**Location:** `C:\Users\bruke\mb3d-blender\`
**Status:** v0.1 SHIPPED 2026-05-12 via `/weapon` (10/10 tasks in 5 parallel rounds, 5/5 tests, headless register-check `REGISTER_OK` in Blender 4.5).

Blender 4.x addon that brings Mandelbulb 3D `.m3p` scenes into Blender as Image data-blocks via the m3p_to_fract converter and Mandelbulber 2 CLI. Closes the MB3D modernization arc: 2003-era Delphi GUI -> 2026 Blender + GPU-accelerated headless renderer.

## What it does

Sidebar panel in 3D Viewport ("MB3D" tab). Pick a `.m3p`, click Render. Two subprocess calls under the hood:
1. `python <m3p_to_fract>/m3p_to_fract.py <m3p> <fract>` — format conversion
2. `mandelbulber2.exe --nogui --format png -r WxH --output <png> <fract>` — render

Result PNG loads as `bpy.data.images.load(...)`. Use as texture, compositor input, reference image.

## Pattern

Pure subprocess + file I/O — identical shape to Mb2's official Houdini contrib (`contrib/vfx_tools/houdini/scripts/python/hou_mandelbulber.py`). No FFI, no bindings, no Blender-specific Python deps.

## Architecture

```
mb3d_blender/                  installable Blender package
├── __init__.py                bl_info + AddonPreferences + MB3D_OT_render + MB3D_PT_main + register/unregister
└── runner.py                  pure-Python; importable in pytest WITHOUT bpy
```

`runner.render_m3p(m3p_path, png_path, m3p_to_fract_dir, mb2_path, width, height) -> Path`
  - Fail-loud pre-checks on all three paths (FileNotFoundError with which one + how to fix)
  - Both subprocesses use `check=True, capture_output=True`
  - Combined stderr surfaces in a `RuntimeError` if either stage exits non-zero
  - Returns the PNG path on success

`runner.resolve_defaults() -> {mb2_path, m3p_to_fract_dir}` — env vars `MB2_EXE` / `M3P_TO_FRACT_DIR` with hardcoded fallbacks.

Inside `__init__.py`:
- `MB3DAddonPreferences` — two `StringProperty` paths, displayed in `Edit > Preferences > Add-ons > MB3D Render`
- `MB3D_OT_render` — operator `mb3d.render`, props `m3p_path` / `png_path` / `width` / `height`
- `MB3D_PT_main` — panel in `VIEW_3D > UI > MB3D`, draws a file picker + sliders + Render button
- bpy imports are guarded by `try/except ImportError` so the module loads cleanly for pytest

## Validation evidence (Blender 4.5 headless)

```
VERIFY_RESULT: {
  "bl_info_name": "MB3D Render",
  "bl_info_version": [0, 1, 0],
  "blender_required": [4, 0, 0],
  "operator_registered": true,
  "panel_in_types": true,
  "op_classes": ["MB3DAddonPreferences", "MB3D_OT_render", "MB3D_PT_main"],
  "status": "REGISTER_OK"
}
```

Smoke render: `fixtures/builtin_mandelbulb.m3p` -> 12,923-byte valid PNG.

## Install

```
python build_zip.py            # -> dist/mb3d_blender.zip (4,173 bytes)
```
Then `Edit > Preferences > Add-ons > Install...` -> pick the zip -> enable.

## Cuts (v0.2+)

- Bulk batch render of MB3D `History/` (separate CLI ship)
- Animation / keyframe rendering (Mb2 supports `-K/-F`; addon doesn't expose yet)
- Multi-slot hybrid UI (blocked at the converter layer; lift when m3p_to_fract v0.2 lands)
- GPU/CPU toggle
- Cycles material translation (render output -> procedural material vs. flat texture)
- Live progress streaming

## Files

```
mb3d-blender/
├── mb3d_blender/
│   ├── __init__.py            Blender side (bl_info + classes + register)
│   └── runner.py              pure Python; pytest target
├── tests/
│   ├── __init__.py
│   └── test_runner.py         5 tests, all bpy-free
├── tools/
│   └── verify_register.py     `blender --background --python` register check
├── fixtures/
│   └── builtin_mandelbulb.m3p byte-identical from m3p-to-fract
├── dist/
│   ├── mb3d_blender.zip       installable
│   └── smoke.png              proof render
├── build_zip.py
├── README.md
└── .weapon/                   spec, plan, mission, progress, closed
```

## Strategic position

Final piece of the MB3D-to-Mb2-via-Blender arc:
1. **binre** — RE pipeline used to first analyze MB3D's Delphi binary
2. **m3p_to_fract** — format converter (the missing bridge identified in issue #630)
3. **mb3d-blender** — UI surface bringing MB3D scenes into Blender's creative pipeline

With this ship, Bruke can render any of his MB3D `.m3p` files directly inside Blender, composite them with characters/scenes/sims/lighting, and export from Blender's standard pipelines. The original "MB3D in 2026 standards / faster / pipeline / web rendering / API / or extract logic into Blender plugin" arc CLOSES with the Blender plugin path delivered.

## Parallelization

This was the first /weapon mission to execute with explicit parallel rounds per `feedback_weapon_parallel_when_deps_allow.md`:
- 5 rounds vs 10 sequential
- Round 2 wrote 5 files in one message; Round 3 ran 3 validations in parallel

## project_mb3d_uia_driver

<a id="project-mb3d-uia-driver"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_mb3d_uia_driver.md`*
*Name:* MB3D UIA Python driver
*Description:* Working programmatic control of Mandelbulb3D via Python uiautomation. End-to-end render+save via .m3p file. Replaces the AHK approach that couldn't see TSpeedButtons.

# MB3D UIA driver (2026-05-10 SHIPPED)

**Script:** `~/.claude/skills/mandelbulb3d/scripts/mb3d_driver.py`
**CLI surface:** `mb3d_driver.py {render,stop,probe} ...`
**Companion CLI:** `~/.claude/skills/mandelbulb3d/scripts/mb3d.py` (parse + edit + render proxy)

## What it does

End-to-end programmatic render of a `.m3p` scene file:
1. Attach (or launch) MB3D
2. Click "Open" tab via UIA
3. Coord-click the "Open m3p" TSpeedButton via SendInput at DFM-known offset
4. Detect Win32 #32770 file dialog owned by MB3D's pid
5. Type filename + Enter
6. Click "Calculate 3D" via UIA (real TButton)
7. Poll for render completion
8. (optional) Click "Save pic" tab + PNG/BMP TSpeedButton, fill Save As, wait for file

Tested 2026-05-10: full pipeline 11–17 sec for a 320×240 iter=30 Chrystal render+save. BMP output at expected size.

## Why it took so long

Six failed runs before the breakthrough. Bruke's pushback "you need something making sure only good code is coming out" forced a probe gate. The probe revealed everything was findable — but the actual click silently failed.

Root cause found in source: **`Mand.pas:1163 DisableButtons`** sets `Button5.Enabled := False` while a render is in progress. The probe sees the button visually but doesn't catch enabled state. My clicks during the run hit a disabled button → silent no-op.

Fix: `stop_active_render(win)` before any Open/Save attempt. Clicks Stop button if MB3D is rendering, waits for Calculate 3D to reappear, then proceeds.

## Key architecture insights

- **TSpeedButton is a TGraphicControl** — no HWND, invisible to UIA enumeration. UIA can address `TabItemControl` (tab headers, which are real TWinControls) and `ButtonControl` (real TButtons like Calculate 3D), but NOT TSpeedButtons.
- **For TSpeedButtons: coord-click via SendInput** at offsets known from the `.dfm` source file. Foreground must be MB3D first (`_force_foreground` uses AttachThreadInput trick).
- **find_window via Win32 EnumWindows for class `TMand3DForm`** — `auto.GetRootControl().GetChildren()` misses owned-popup MB3D forms; Win32 EnumWindows + `auto.ControlFromHandle` is more reliable.
- **find_dialog via class `#32770`** — modern Win32 CommonItemDialog; must exclude `THintWindow` (the tooltip class that flashes button hint text — that bit me once).
- **Don't filter MB3D main window by title** — MB3D rewrites the title to the loaded scene basename (e.g., "smoke_seed" after loading smoke_seed.m3p). Match by class only.
- **Render-done polling: 200ms interval, 3 consecutive Calc3D readings** — at small image sizes the Stop→Calculate3D transition is <1s; slow polling misses it.

## How to apply

When user wants to render an MB3D scene programmatically:
1. Bring MB3D up (or let driver launch it)
2. Use mb3d.py to edit/create the .m3p (parse_m3p + apply_mutations)
3. Call `mb3d_driver.py render <m3p> --save <out.bmp>`

For new GUI-driving sessions: ALWAYS run `probe` first. The probe is the gate — if it shows green and the render still fails, look in the SOURCE for state-dependent disabling (DisableButtons pattern).

## Validation file

`~/.claude/skills/mandelbulb3d/.weapon/render_proof/uia_smoke_v2.bmp` — real Chrystal fractal render, 160×120, 57654 bytes, via the full UIA pipeline.

## project_memory_write_tool

<a id="project-memory-write-tool"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_memory_write_tool.md`*
*Name:* project-memory-write-tool
*Description:* Deterministic script that mechanizes the memory-save protocol (frontmatter file + MEMORY.md index line)

Built 2026-07-15 as script #1 from the [transcript-index](project_transcript_index.md) pattern-clustering pass: `~/.claude/scripts/memory_write/memory_write.py`. Mechanizes the two-step memory-save protocol (write frontmatter'd file to `memory/` + append one-line pointer to `MEMORY.md`) that every session's system prompt already documents by hand.

**Why:** `MEMORY.md` was the single most cross-session-touched file on the machine — 94 distinct sessions, 528 Read/Write/Edit hits (transcript-index evidence). The save procedure is a fixed template, not a judgment call, so it was the clearest script-vs-LLM-turn win of the three proposed. Deliberately scoped narrow: the script only mechanizes the WRITE (frontmatter formatting, filename slugging, section-correct index-line insertion, idempotent skip on rerun). Deciding *what's* worth saving and *how* to word it stays a judgment call made by the session — not automated away.

**Usage:**
```bash
python memory_write.py --type {user,feedback,project,reference} --name <slug> \
    --description "<one-line>" --body "<text>" | --body-file <path> \
    [--title "..."] [--hook "..."] [--section "## Heading"] [--dry-run]
```
Filename = `{type}_{snake_name}.md`, frontmatter `name:` = `{type}-{kebab_name}` — matches the existing corpus convention exactly (verified against `project_transcript_index.md` before writing). Index line auto-placed under a per-type default section (`project`→Active Work, `feedback`→Working Style, `user`→User Profile, `reference`→References), overridable via `--section`. Reruns with the same `--name` overwrite the file's content and skip re-adding the index line if already present (idempotent — this exact memory file + its MEMORY.md line were written/updated by the script itself as the live test, not a synthetic smoketest).

**How to apply:** Reach for this instead of manual Read-MEMORY.md + Write + Edit whenever saving a memory in this project. Two proposals remain from the same clustering pass: `register-preview-server.py` (`.claude/launch.json` entries, 33 sessions/95 hits) and a PreToolUse hook for the cat/tail/grep-via-Bash anti-pattern (~140 calls that should have used Read/Grep).

Related: [transcript-index](project_transcript_index.md), [DoD on every message](feedback_every_message_has_dod.md).

## project_mini_ship

<a id="project-mini-ship"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_mini_ship.md`*
*Name:* mini-ship slash command + cross-session hook
*Description:* /mini-ship one-round shipping loop · V0.1+V0.2 ACTIVE (6 ships landed 2026-05-02; D1-D5 all closed including D5 filter-repo anti-pattern) at .claude/commands/mini-ship.md + tools/mini-ship/scan.py + Stop hook

`/mini-ship` slash command + cross-session head-hunt loop. **V0.1+V0.2 ACTIVE — 6 successful ships landed 2026-05-02; D1-D5 all closed (kill criterion exceeded 4×).** Originally shipped 2026-05-02 in worktree `elegant-black-71ab26`.

## V0.2 architecture (3 pieces)

1. **Scanner** at `tools/mini-ship/scan.py` (~225 lines, pure stdlib). Runs in <2s. Inspects 3 hardcoded REPOS (Pre Atlas, atlas, web-audit) for git status + ahead-of-remote, reads MEMORY.md for ship-candidate keywords (UNCOMMITTED/PENDING/awaiting merge/etc), reads last 5 entries from `tools/mini-ship/log.jsonl`, derives ranked candidates by tier (S<A<B) + est_min ascending. Writes `~/.claude/mini-ship/fleet.json`. Fail-soft: exits 0 on any error.
2. **Stop hook** in `.claude/settings.local.json` (worktree-scoped, NOT gitignored — user manages locally). `async: true`, `timeout: 10`. Runs scanner after every session ends so next session has fresh fleet.json.
3. **Consumer** = `/mini-ship` slash command updated. Step 1 reads `~/.claude/mini-ship/fleet.json`; if FRESH (<2h old) and has candidates, uses top candidate as SHIP CARD seed; else falls back to step 3 local-git scan. Step 7 re-runs scanner after each ship so feedback loop stays warm.

## V0.1 contract (carries forward)

Read git state + last-3 entries from `log.jsonl` → pick smallest shippable atom (<30 min, user-visible bias, skip pure refactors, max 5 files) → print SHIP CARD → wait for `y` → do work + stage only touched files (never `git add -A`) + conventional commit (no `Co-Authored-By` per global setting) + append round to JSONL log + ask one-line feedback → append feedback to log entry. **No push.** No tests for the tool itself.

## Why it exists

Bruke's recurring frustration is hesitation tax — "everything I've been hesitating, I've seen other people do." This compresses every shipping decision to **1 keypress + 1 sentence of feedback per round** and persists across sessions. Slot-fits feedback rules: "Ship small, iterate fast", "Ship the loop, not the dead form", "Database + agent pair is the target shape" (the JSONL log + fleet.json = database, slash command + scanner = agent).

## Gate-skip note (V0.1 → V0.2)

V0.1's kill-criteria said V0.2 should wait for ≥3 successful V0.1 rounds before promoting. Bruke explicitly overrode this on 2026-05-02 ("the hook continues to the next conversation, and we build and have a direction") — the gate was deliberately skipped after Fleet 01 / S1 (atlas hallway) shipped successfully (1 round, not 3). Honest record kept here so future-me doesn't think the gate was forgotten.

## How to apply

- Both V0.1 and V0.2 are **ACTIVE** as of 2026-05-02 — graduated from BETA after 4 clean ships (S1 atlas hallway, S1.5 mini-ship build itself, S2 .gitignore housekeep, S3 absolute-log-path fix).
- **Kill criteria (historical, met):** the original gate said /mini-ship had to beat just-asking-Claude in 2-3 rounds OR be deleted. It cleared 3-of-3 on S2 and 4-of-4 on S3. Gate retired. If quality regresses (picks feel wrong ≥2/3 rounds, or feedback round becomes friction), revisit by re-applying the gate as a watch-criterion rather than a kill-criterion — adjust scan.py's `derive_candidates` instead of deleting the tool.
- **Hook may need `/hooks` reload** after first install if Claude Code's settings watcher wasn't watching `.claude/` at session start. settings.local.json existed before this session so it should pick up automatically; if not, user opens `/hooks` once.
- `.claude/settings.local.json` contains the hook + ~172 permissions entries. **Not gitignored** in this repo. If user wants to share the hook config across worktrees, copy the `hooks` block manually.
- Do NOT add CycleBoard journaling, SQLite log, multi-atom batching, auto-push, tests-for-mini-ship, or external-greenfield-research (V0.3+) until V0.2 lands ≥3 successful rounds.
- If global promotion needed (move to `~/.claude/commands/` + `~/.claude/settings.json`): namespace the log by repo path (`~/.claude/mini-ship/<slug>/log.jsonl`) and update REPOS list in scan.py to be config-driven.
- Adjacent skills to **not** confuse it with: `weapon` (whole-project finisher, bigger scope · used to ship Fleet 01/S1), `autopilot` (fest orchestrator, reads festival state not code), `project-finisher` (planner only, doesn't execute), `checkpoint` (just snapshots state).

## project_minidocs_glyph_cas

<a id="project-minidocs-glyph-cas"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_minidocs_glyph_cas.md`*
*Name:* MiniDocs glyph-CAS layered build
*Description:* Glyph-level content-addressable store for document images; layered architecture with Leptonica jbclass as base + 5 boosts roadmap; plan file at ~/.claude/plans/splendid-tumbling-sundae.md

# MiniDocs glyph-CAS — layered build

**Status:** PLAN APPROVED 2026-05-03. Pre-execution committed; phase 1+ executes in fresh context.

**Plan file:** `~/.claude/plans/splendid-tumbling-sundae.md` (full phase breakdown, file paths, verification per phase). Fresh sessions read this first.

**Worktree:** `C:\Users\bruke\Pre Atlas\.claude\worktrees\interesting-chaum-2944a8` · branch `claude/interesting-chaum-2944a8`. Work lives in `minidocs-shape-engine/`.

## Core idea

Document = `(template_id, [(glyph_id, x, y, θ), ...])` against a shared, queryable shape dictionary. Cross-document indexing, visual-phrase grep, reconstruction by overlap. No OCR.

## Why this shape

Verified in source (2026-05-03): Leptonica `src/jbclass.{h,c}` (BSD-2, last commit 2026-04-18) already produces the (page, class, x, y) stream + template-mosaic PNG. `jbAddPages()` grows the dictionary across N input images. JBIG2 family already solved the storage half; the gap is the queryable-index layer on top.

## Phase plan (5 boosts, 7 phases)

```
0  pre-execution checklist                    DONE 2026-05-03
1  Leptonica jbclass cffi-wrap                DONE 2026-05-04 (dedup 0.8517 >= 0.85 gate)
2  FAISS index over template hashes           DONE 2026-05-04 (cross-corpus self-lookup PASS)
3  pretrained DINOv2 encoder                  DONE 2026-05-04 (USABLE — closest pairs are visually similar letters)
4  hybrid: raster hash + neural fallback      DONE 2026-05-04 — SHIP (cross-font dedup 1.0 at NT=25)
5  tiny VQ-VAE codebook                       SKIPPED — phase 4 ship gate hit
6  contrastive font-invariant encoder         SKIPPED — phase 4 ship gate hit
```

## Phase log

- **2026-05-04 — phase 1 GREEN.** Leptonica cffi-wrap binds to Tesseract-bundled `libleptonica-6.dll` (no vcpkg/conda/source build needed — Tesseract was already installed). 2-page born-digital corpus → 236 components → 35 templates → 0.8517 dedup. Visual mosaic at `experiments/leptonica_spike/jbout.templates.preview.png` shows clean letter shapes. Run details + decisions in `experiments/leptonica_spike/log.md`. Defaults: `JB_CORRELATION` over `JB_CONN_COMPS`, `thresh=0.8`, `weightfactor=0.6`.
- **2026-05-04 — phase 2 GREEN.** `RasterHashIndex` (faiss IndexBinaryFlat over 32×32 canonical, +sha1 dict) and `EmbeddingIndex` (IndexFlatL2 stub for phase 3+) at `src/leptonica/index.py`. Cross-corpus spike vs new corpus_b: 35 templates each, self-lookup PASS for all 35 A templates, 1 exact-hash hit (2.86%), mean k=1 Hamming 31.1 bits / 1024 (median 20, max 173 — manually verified outliers are real "no counterpart in haystack" cases). Commit `5adc5d9`.
- **2026-05-04 — phase 3 GREEN.** `Dinov2Encoder` wraps `facebook/dinov2-small` (384-dim CLS) with 1-bpp -> RGB-stacked 224x224 NEAREST upscale at `src/leptonica/encoder_dinov2.py`. 35 templates encoded on CUDA in 0.92 s (26 ms/template), determinism PASS (L2=0). Top-5 closest non-self pairs are all visually-similar letters: (./dot-frag, l/i, b/p, q/p, f/t); closest L2 4.7 vs median 31.6. Qualitative read: USABLE for glyphs — phase 4 hybrid will use DINOv2 as the neural fallback. Commit `b98e141`.
- **2026-05-04 — phase 4 SHIP.** `HybridIndex` at `src/leptonica/hybrid_index.py` chains exact-sha1 → raster-Hamming@30 → DINOv2-L2@25 → new. Three-corpus spike (A=Helvetica content X, B=Helvetica content Y, C=Times content X) shows A vs C cross-font dedup = **1.0 at NT=25** (28 templates routed via neural, 7 via raster_hamming, 0 unmatched). A vs B same-font hit 1.0 at NT=40, 0.94 at NT=20. Found + fixed FAISS-IndexFlatL2-returns-squared-L2 bug in `EmbeddingIndex.search` mid-run; first sweep reported 0.20 cross-font with the squared comparison hiding all neural matches. Per plan stop condition (>=0.95 cross-font → SHIP), **phases 5 (VQ-VAE) and 6 (contrastive) are SKIPPED** — the layered build's architectural surface is closed. Caveats: small corpus (N=35), identical text across A/C (cleanest cross-font), no real scan tested.
- **2026-05-04 — phase 4 stress test MARGINAL.** Five-font corpus (Helvetica, Times, Courier, Georgia, Verdana) at ~100 templates each, ~4.5KB long passage covering full alphabet+digits+punctuation, NT=25 fixed. Cross-font dedup: Verdana 1.000, Courier 0.938, Times 0.872, **Georgia 0.798 ← worst** (just 0.002 below the 0.80 GREEN floor). Ship-at-1.0 claim from the original phase-4 spike was over-confident; at scale the hybrid lands at MARGINAL. Phase 5 not unambiguously needed (3/4 pairs cleared 0.80) but not unambiguously safe to skip either. Next moves before considering phase 5: inspect the 25 Georgia "new" templates for genuine new shapes vs encoder failures, sweep NT once more on this richer corpus. See `experiments/stress_spike/log.md` for full read.
- **2026-05-04 — Georgia investigation + NT sweep VERDICT WIDER-NT-RESCUES.** All 25 Georgia "new" templates have nearest-A L2 in [25.07, 34.50] (median 27.02) — none genuinely new shapes, all just above the NT=25 cliff. NT sweep on the stress corpus: smallest NT where all 4 pairs >= 0.80 is **NT=27.5** (Times 0.952, Courier 0.990, Georgia 0.919, Verdana 1.000); at NT=30 all pairs >= 0.97; at NT=35 all pairs hit 1.000. Encoder is fine; the original NT=25 calibration (from the 35-template toy spike) was just barely too tight when faced with serif fonts that have heavier stroke modulation. Phase 5 NOT needed for this corpus shape. `HybridIndex.neural_threshold` default stays at 25 per spec (per-deployment calibration); downstream consumers handling Georgia/Bodoni/Didot-family fonts should set NT=30 at construction. See `experiments/georgia_investigation/log.md`.
- **2026-05-04 — Scale + precision probe VERDICT PRECISION-DEGRADES-AT-SCALE.** First test that measured precision (matched_label == query_label via tesseract single-char OCR), not just dedup. ~28KB Alice fragment per font, 5 fonts, 94-201 templates each (Helv 96, Times 201, Courier 115, Georgia 190, Verdana 94). **Dedup holds at scale (>= 0.86 for all pairs at NT=27.5).** **Precision is much lower than dedup** — even on the cleanest font pair (Verdana), only 0.75 of high-confidence-labelled matches are right-letter; Times/Georgia ~0.10-0.17, Courier ~0.38. Spot-check at NT=30 shows the bare numbers are mixed: real DINOv2 cross-font errors (R↔P, I↔n shape collisions) AND tesseract mislabelling on the index AND non-letter fragments (vertical-bar stems from CONN_COMPS) all contribute. Two big architectural findings: (a) `JB_CONN_COMPS` produces wildly different template counts across fonts (96 Helv vs 201 Times same text — corpus shape is itself font-dependent, not just content), (b) the "phases 5/6 unnecessary" line from the original phase-4 ship was too strong; precision-only metrics surface a real failure mode at scale. Three responses worth evaluating before scaling production: switch to `JB_CHARACTERS` mode, add tesseract OCR post-validation to the hybrid, OR train phase-5/6 encoder. None built in this mission per spec. See `experiments/scale_precision/log.md`.

Optogon orchestrates phases 2+3 in parallel. Phases 4-6 sequential, gated on measured outcomes.

## Stop conditions

- Phase 1 dedup ≤ 0.50 on clean PDF → abort, calibrate Leptonica thresholds.
- Phase 4 hybrid ≥ 0.95 cross-font → STOP, ship. Don't train models we don't need.
- Phase 5 VQ-VAE training > 1 day on laptop → reassess.

## Verified source references (load-bearing)

- `leptonica/src/jbclass.h:47-86` — JBCLASSER struct with pixat, naclass, napage, ptaul, dahash.
- `leptonica/src/jbclass.c:444-473` — jbAddPages() cross-document API.
- `leptonica/src/jbclass.c:1917-1966` — jbDataWrite() emits PNG mosaic + text data file with (ipage, iclass, x, y) per glyph.
- `pdfminer/converter.py:233-263` — render_char keeps Unicode but receives raw cid; ~50-line patch for vector-PDF half.

Verification done by Bruke and Claude on 2026-05-03 with cloned repos at `C:\Users\bruke\AppData\Local\Temp\jbig2-research\leptonica\`.

## Why ALL 5 boosts (not lean)

Bruke chose full ceremony over lean spike (2026-05-03 AskUserQuestion). The 5-layer architecture is the target end state; phases 5+ may not execute if phase 4 hybrid achieves ≥0.95 cross-font dedup — but the plan covers them so the layered architecture is documented end to end.

## What this kills

- Hand-rolled skeleton-graph topology descriptor (the aborted experiment in `experiments/glyph_dedup/`). 1990s technique, the field abandoned it for reasons. Use Leptonica's correlation matcher + (later) learned encoders instead.
- Synthetic Gaussian-noise corpus testing. Real PDF + real scan corpus from phase 4 onward.

## project_mirofish_merge

<a id="project-mirofish-merge"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_mirofish_merge.md`*
*Name:* MiroFish Merge into Cognitive-Sensor
*Description:* Plan to fold MiroFish prediction engine into cognitive-sensor pipeline and Atlas visualization — kill separate service

## MiroFish → Cognitive-Sensor Merge (planned)

MiroFish was rewired from a fake "swarm simulation" (LLM calls in a for-loop) into a real prediction engine on 2026-04-02. The prediction algorithms work but MiroFish runs as a separate service (:3003) with its own Neo4j Docker container, which adds friction instead of removing it.

**Why:** User wants a cohesive system with no manual code running. Separate services contradict this. Predictions should flow through the existing refresh.py → daily_payload → CycleBoard pipeline.

**How to apply:** Next session should merge MiroFish into cognitive-sensor:

### What exists (services/mirofish/src/mirofish/)
- `prediction/loop_predictor.py` — KNN outcome prediction for open loops (deterministic, no LLM)
- `prediction/pattern_detector.py` — 5 pattern types via Neo4j graph queries (recurring, spiral, catalyst, abandonment, domain)
- `prediction/mode_simulator.py` — "what if" mode transitions using real atlas_config thresholds
- `prediction/insight_engine.py` — orchestrator combining all three
- `graph/neo4j_client.py` — expanded with Conversation/Topic schema, vector search, pattern queries
- `graph/conversation_chunker.py` — turn-aware chunking
- `graph/conversation_extractor.py` — topic extraction via Ollama qwen2.5:7b
- `graph/ingester.py` — ConversationIngester reads cognitive-sensor data into Neo4j
- `graph/embedder.py` — Ollama nomic-embed-text (unchanged)
- `ingest_state.py` — incremental ingestion tracker (~/.mirofish/ingest_state.json)

### Current state
- 55 of 1,397 conversations ingested into Neo4j
- 524 nodes (conversations + topics), 501 DISCUSSES edges
- Mode simulator works — reads real cognitive_state.json
- Neo4j Docker container: `neo4j-mirofish` on ports 7474/7687 (password: mirofish123)
- Ollama models installed: qwen2.5:7b (4.7GB), nomic-embed-text (274MB)

### Merge plan
1. Move `prediction/` modules into `services/cognitive-sensor/prediction/`
2. Wire into `refresh.py` pipeline (after loops + classifications, before export)
3. Add prediction data to `daily_payload.json` → CycleBoard gets predictions
4. Add prediction layer + topic network to Cognitive Atlas HTML
5. Keep Neo4j but start it from `refresh.py` or `run_all.ps1` (not separate service)
6. Delete `services/mirofish/` service (keep api.py patterns for reference)
7. Remove MiroFish from mosaic port map

### Key design decisions confirmed
- No LLM for prediction — Ollama only during ingestion (extraction)
- Cognitive-sensor is read-only source, Neo4j is the graph store
- All predictions carry evidence strings (auditable)
- Mode simulator uses real atlas_config.ROUTING thresholds

# Modernization Suite — 4 tools → one legacy-modernization product

**Goal (Bruke, 2026-07-07):** take `/code-recon`, `binre`, `sigil`, `delta-scp` to peak potential as standalone B2B tools that help companies understand + modernize + fortify legacy code. Product shape = a pipeline: **decompose → understand/prove → (decompile if no source) → compose/carry → deliver a modernization dossier.** The seam (`seam.v1` Receipts, sigil sha256 join key — [[project_stack_integration_seam]]) is the productization spine.

**Verified current state (4 parallel Explore recons, 2026-07-07):**
- **delta-scp** (`pre-atlas/services/delta-scp`) — pure REGEX symbol extraction, top-level only, IMPORTS-edges-only (calls/inherits/implements stubbed; tree-sitter named but UNBUILT at `graph.ts:8-12`). All-files-in-RAM, 20K-file/50MiB guardrails. CLI works standalone (no Supabase). TS/JS/Py mature; C/C++/C#/PHP recognized-but-NO-patterns (legacy langs produce zero symbols).
- **code-recon** (`~/.claude/skills/code-recon`) — Node orchestrator; `orient.mjs` gate (FRESH/STALE/MISSING) + `ledger.mjs` (seam.v1). Ladder is PROSE, not invoked in code. Hard dep on delta-scp at `C:/Users/bruke/pre-atlas/services/delta-scp` (override `DELTA_SCP_DIR`). atlas-map optional. ~70% standalone.
- **binre** (`C:\Users\bruke\binre`) — 5 stages, never-raises, portable via env overrides. Emits report.json (NO seam_version). Recon found gaps: no decompiled-source export, no dependency graph, no modernization report, no unpacking, large-binary timeouts.
- **sigil** (`C:\Users\bruke\sigil`) — solid, 6/6 tests, SGL1 content-address enforced. Load-all-in-RAM (fine for maps, not 2GB). Missing: streaming, Nuitka onefile.

## Phase 1 SHIPPED (2026-07-07): binre modernization layer
`C:\Users\bruke\binre\binre\scripts\modernize.py` + `tests/test_modernize.py` (24/24 PASS). Pure post-processor over an existing report.json (no Ghidra re-run). Emits under `out/<sha>/modernize/`:
