Prints a seam.v1 receipt to stdout. **Proven live:** 3x Osc.dll (Borland Delphi 5, 2208 funcs 100% decomp, 4958 call edges, 5 risky imports) + graceful zero-decomp on Mandelbulb3D.exe (13641 funcs, 45309 edges, Embarcadero Delphi detected). NOT committed yet (binre is its own git repo).

## Phase 2 STARTED (2026-07-07): delta-scp tree-sitter AST core — engine SHIPPED
Commit `46d9e6a` on `feat/delta-scp-v2-graph-memory` (pre-atlas repo). New `services/delta-scp/src/treesitter.ts` + `treesitter.test.ts` (7/7; full suite 46 pass/2 pg-skip; tsc clean).
- **Lib decision (assemble-first):** `web-tree-sitter` (WASM, no node-gyp — portable for B2B) + `tree-sitter-wasms` (precompiled grammars). **ABI GOTCHA:** tree-sitter-wasms 0.1.13 grammars are built with tree-sitter-cli 0.20.8 (ABI 13/14); web-tree-sitter 0.26 broke the dylink ABI (~0.25). **Pin `web-tree-sitter@0.22.6`** to match. 0.22 API = CJS default export, `Parser.Language.load()`, `lang.query()` (NOT the 0.26 named `{Parser,Language,Query}` / `new Query()`).
- **Exports:** `extractSymbolsAst(content,lang)` (symbols w/ exact lines incl. legacy langs regex returns 0 for), `extractCallEdgesAst(content,lang)` (caller→callee via enclosing-function walk = the `calls` edge regex can't produce), `supportsAst`, `astLanguages`. Query config keyed by lang: `{wasm, symbolQuery (captures @sym.<kind> on name node), callQuery (@callee), funcNodeTypes}`. Languages done: **c, python, typescript, javascript** — adding cpp/java/csharp/go = one wasm name + 2 query strings each.
- **Proven:** C source → regex `extractSymbols` returns `[]`; AST returns add/compute/main + `Point` struct + `compute→add`/`main→compute` call edges. Additive/opt-in: unknown lang → empty, regex core untouched.
- **WIRING DONE (commit `5efaa26`):** flag `config.extractor`/`SCP_EXTRACTOR=treesitter` (default regex, behaviour unchanged when unset). `compressor.ts` factored `assemble()` + async `compressTreeAsync` (AST where `supportsAst`, per-file fail-soft to regex, WASM lazy-loaded via dynamic import). `source.ts compressRepository` + `worker.tick` route through it. `graph.ts` refactored to shared `buildGraphRowsWith` core; `buildGraphRows` unchanged (regex), new `buildGraphRowsAst` emits AST symbol nodes + real `calls` edges (caller→callee resolved same-file first else unique repo-wide; ambiguous dropped; edge dedup now full-identity keyed). `worker.populateGraph` uses it when treesitter. `src/wiring.test.ts` (6) + full suite **52 pass / 2 pg-skip, tsc clean**. **Live CLI proof:** `engine.c` symbols `[]` (regex) → `[Osc struct, clampi, process]` (treesitter).
- **LANGUAGE COVERAGE DONE (commit `d292764`):** added cpp/csharp/java/go query sets to `treesitter.ts LANGS` — queries verified empirically against the real grammars first (probe script), not guessed. Now 8 langs: c, python, typescript, javascript, cpp, csharp, java, go. Each = grammar wasm + symbol query (`@sym.<kind>` on name node) + call query (`@callee`) + `funcNodeTypes`. cpp captures inline methods via `field_identifier` declarator; `namespace`→type_definition in KIND_TO_NODE_TYPE. `treesitter.test.ts` +4 (symbols+call edge per lang), suite **56 pass/2 skip, tsc clean**. Live CLI proof (regex→AST symbol counts): widget.cpp **0→4**, Shape.cs **0→3**, Shape.java **1→3**, main.go 2→2 — the legacy C++/C# gap (0 symbols) fully closed.
- **SOURCE-REPO DOSSIER DONE (commit `65dde8f`):** `src/modernize.ts` + `modernize.test.ts` (7) — source-side analogue of binre's modernize. `npm run modernize -- <repo>` (SCP_EXTRACTOR=treesitter for call graph). Writes `symbolic_map.json` + `dependency_graph.json` (fan-in/out) + `MODERNIZATION_REPORT.md` (identity/legacy-flags · structure · hotspots [core-utils by fan-in, orchestrators by fan-out, god-files] · risk surfaces · roadmap). Content-addressed receipt (`modernize.v1`, sha256 of map minus wall-clock). Risk surfaces from RAW call pairs (strcpy/eval/system are exactly what edge-resolution drops). **Gotcha fixed:** Windows ESM entry guard `file://${argv[1]}` never matches a backslash path → main() never ran; use `path.resolve(argv[1])===path.resolve(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url))`. Suite **63 pass/2 skip, tsc clean**. Live dogfood on delta-scp/src: `loadConfig` top core-util (fan-in 12), `modernizeRepo` top orchestrator (fan-out 11), 120 call edges, 0.065 ratio — accurate.
- **UNIFIED ENTRY DONE (binre commit `91e6a5c`):** `C:\Users\bruke\binre\modernize.py` (repo-root dispatcher, distinct from `binre/scripts/modernize.py`) + `tests/test_modernize_unified.py`. ONE command auto-detects the asset and routes: source dir/git-URL → delta-scp (subprocess `npx tsx src/modernize.ts`, SCP_EXTRACTOR=treesitter, `cmd /c npx` on Windows, delta-scp via `$DELTA_SCP_DIR` default `C:/Users/bruke/pre-atlas/services/delta-scp`); PE/ELF/Mach-O binary → binre (in-process `binre.scripts.modernize`, runs orchestrate first if no cached report unless `--no-analyze`). Normalizes both into `{tool,status,asset_type,engine,target,sha256,dossier_dir,report,engine_receipt}`. `detect_asset`: git-URL regex, dir check, binary magic; fails loud on lone non-binary file / missing. **Live proof, one CLI:** `delta-scp/src` dir → delta-scp engine (120 call edges); `3x Osc.dll` → binre engine (Delphi 5, 2208 sources). Detection/normalize tests all pass. Run with binre's `.venv` python.
- **PRODUCT SPINE COMPLETE:** decompose→understand→deliver proven end-to-end on BOTH asset types through one entry point. binre `6cd3b7e` (binary dossier) + `91e6a5c` (unified); delta-scp `46d9e6a`+`5efaa26`+`d292764`+`65dde8f` (tree-sitter core→wire→8 langs→source dossier).
- **NEXT (do fresh, lower-leverage now):** (b) make treesitter the DEFAULT once perf validated on a large repo (currently opt-in); (d) large-repo scaling (streaming/incremental — the 20K-file/50MiB ceiling, all-in-RAM); grammar adds php/ruby/rust/swift/kotlin (tree-sitter-wasms has them). Also: put the unified `modernize.py` on PATH / register as an atlas-map seam surface if it becomes a daily tool.

## Phase 3 STARTED (2026-07-07): using the suite for real — injection sweep across Pre Atlas

Dogfooded the unified `modernize.py` + manual code reading against real Pre Atlas services. Found and fixed 3 real vulnerabilities so far:
1. **Shell injection**, `services/delta-kernel/src/cli/atlas-ai.ts` `compoundWork`'s codex invocation — `execSync` with string-interpolated prompt. Fixed via `resolveCodexEntry()` bypass pattern (see [[reference_windows_safe_exec_pattern]]). Commit `93f33fb`, **pushed** to `origin/feat/atlas-setup-ui` (space-path `Pre Atlas` repo).
2. **Safe-but-fragile hardening**, same repo, `atlas.ts` `stopAll`/`startAll` — were safe today (hardcoded inputs) but built shell-strings via `execSync`/`exec`. Hardened to argv-array `execFileSync`/`spawn` with `shell:false` (safe by construction). Commit `908c930`, **pushed**.
3. **Git argument injection** (CVE-2017-1000117-shaped, distinct class — see [[reference_git_argument_injection_pattern]]), `services/delta-scp/src/source.ts` `cloneRepo()` — missing `--` before the untrusted `repoUrl` positional arg to `git clone`. Fixed. Commit `e190577` in the **hyphen-path** `pre-atlas` repo, branch `feat/delta-scp-v2-graph-memory` — **pushed** to `origin/feat/delta-scp-v2-graph-memory` (2026-07-07; PR not yet opened, no target branch specified).

**Critical lesson, twice-proven:** the automated `RISKY_CALL_TARGETS` scanner in `modernize.ts` reported delta-scp's own source as "0 risky surfaces" — both times, deterministically. It was wrong. Finding #3 was only caught by manually reading `source.ts` after the scanner said clean. The scanner does callee-*name* matching on the AST call graph; it cannot express "does this positional arg have a `--` guard," and has real naming gaps (`execFileAsync` doesn't match `'execfile'`; Python's `subprocess.run/call/Popen/check_output/check_call` are entirely absent from the target list). **Known gap, not yet fixed**: add Python subprocess names to `RISKY_CALL_TARGETS`, or accept the scanner can't cover Python and always pair it with manual `grep -n "subprocess\.(run|call|Popen|check_output|check_call)"` + `shell\s*=\s*True` on Python services.

### Sweep COMPLETE across all 15 remaining services (2026-07-07)

Dispatched 6 parallel security-reviewer agents (grouped by language: 4 Python groups, 2 Node groups) covering every unswept service, each instructed to manually read code rather than trust the `RISKY_CALL_TARGETS` scanner. Every finding was independently re-verified by reading the actual source before fixing (never acted on an agent claim alone — [[feedback_agent_report_distrust]]). 8 real vulnerabilities found and fixed, all verified with real payloads/TestClient calls, all pre-existing test suites re-run green (droplist 8/8 relevant, search-stack 27 passed, openclaw 30/30, uasc-executor 11/11 — cortex/memory-hub have no pre-existing suites, verified via direct TestClient/unit calls instead):

1. **CRITICAL — cortex** (`services/cortex/src/cortex/`): `POST /tasks/submit` had ZERO auth, bound `0.0.0.0`. `TaskIntent.GENERATE_ARTIFACT` (a public enum value) has no template, so any task with that intent unconditionally hits `_claude_decompose`, whose prompt explicitly told the LLM `shell_exec` was a legal `action_type` — and the executor ran whatever `command` came back via `asyncio.create_subprocess_shell` with zero sanitization. Also `api_call`'s `url` had no host allowlist (SSRF), and `EXECUTE_DIRECTIVE`'s template ran arbitrary `instructions` through the paid Claude API with no gate at all (cost-abuse, deterministic not probabilistic). Fixed: Bearer-auth middleware reusing the existing `.aegis-tenant-key`/`DELTA_API_KEY` shared secret (same pattern as delta-kernel — dev mode with no key file stays open); `DECOMPOSITION_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` allowlist (`api_call, claude_generate, state_update, noop` only — LLM-decomposition path can no longer emit `shell_exec`/`file_write`/`uasc_command`, whole plan rejected if it tries); host-origin allowlist on `_handle_api_call`. `main.py`, `agents/planner.py`, `agents/executor.py`.
2. **CRITICAL — uasc-executor** (`services/uasc-executor/`): `_execute_shell` (`executor.py`) did naive `{var}` → `str.replace` interpolation into `subprocess.run(cmd, shell=True)`, fed by `POST /exec`'s free-text `inputs` (HMAC-authenticated, but any authenticated caller's free-text `goal`/`context` = RCE). Proven live-exploitable with the shipped `BRIEF_v1.json` profile. Fixed: new `_interpolate_shell` rejects any substituted value containing a shell metacharacter (`&|<>^"%\`$;\n\r`) before substitution — verified with a real payload (`x" & echo PWNED > pwned.txt & echo "` → rejected; clean goals still interpolate). Same denylist applied as defense-in-depth directly in `build_brief.py`'s `generic_actions()` (the downstream function this upstream fix already makes unreachable with malicious input, but a future refactor could add another path to it). `run_actions.py`'s `run_action_direct` (also `shell=True`) was NOT independently patched — its `payload` strings legitimately contain `&&`/`|` from static templates, so a blanket metachar filter there would break legitimate functionality; it's protected transitively by the upstream `_interpolate_shell` fix cutting off the only path that generates a malicious `payload`.
3. **HIGH — search-stack** (`services/search-stack/src/search_stack/`): `POST /memory/save`'s `drop_to` was a fully unvalidated outbound URL with the response reflected back (SSRF + read primitive, no auth on the whole FastAPI app). Fixed with a loopback+configured-host allowlist (`server.py`). Also `rg`/`fd`/`es`/`gh search` CLI calls passed the unauthenticated search query as a bare positional with no `--` guard (e.g. `q="--files"` turns a text search into filesystem enumeration) — same CVE-2017-1000117-shaped class as the delta-scp git fix. Fixed all 4 with `--` (verified empirically that `es.exe` and `gh` both honor it: `es -x` errors "Unknown switch", `es -- -x` treats it literally; `gh search --help` documents `--` explicitly). `providers/repo_search.py`, `providers/local_file.py`, `providers/github.py`.
4. **MEDIUM — openclaw** (`services/openclaw/src/openclaw/delta.py`): `/approve <text>` chat command's raw text went unencoded into a URL path segment (`f"{delta_url}/api/actions/confirm/{action_id}"`), using openclaw's own broadly-scoped delta-kernel bearer token — a value containing `/`/`?`/`#` could redirect the authenticated POST to a different route. Fixed with `urllib.parse.quote(action_id, safe='')`.
5. **HIGH — droplist** (`services/droplist/droplist/storage.py`): `GET /api/dag/{dag_id}` (unauthenticated by design — GET reads stay open) built a filesystem path via `os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "dags", f"{dag_id}.json")` with zero validation. Starlette's path-param regex excludes `/` but not `\`, and Windows treats `\` as a separator too — a backslash traversal payload could read any `.json` file. Fixed with an ID-shape regex (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,80}$`, matching the real `DAG-<8hex>` shape) at the single `load_dag` choke point (closes both `/api/dag/{id}` and `/api/dag/{id}/checklist`). Verified: traversal payloads rejected, real DAG still loads.
6. **MEDIUM — memory-hub** (`services/memory-hub/src/memory_hub/`): `POST /save` wrote directly into droplist's shared data store with ZERO auth, while droplist's own equivalent write route requires `X-Atlas-Token`. Fixed by adding a new `auth.py` mirroring droplist's exact shared-secret pattern (same `.atlas-write-token` file, same repo-root resolution — verified both services resolve to the identical token) and gating `/save` with it. Verified end-to-end via TestClient: no/wrong token → 401, correct token → 200.
7. **LOW — memory-hub** (`stores.py`): `atlas_query.py` subprocess call passed the search query as a bare positional with no `--` guard — not exploitable today (verified: `atlas_query.py search` requires exactly one positional, so a flag-shaped query just leaves it unfilled and argparse errors → empty result) but structurally incidental, not deliberate. Fixed with `--` anyway; verified `atlas_query.py` errors without `--` and correctly treats `--root=...` as literal text with it.
8. **Clean, verified by manual read (no fix needed):** cognitive-sensor, optogon, perception, triangulation, canvas-engine, aegis-fabric (re-verified after the scanner's earlier "0 risky" — this time by actually reading every file, genuinely clean, no exec-family calls exist at all), crucix (2 `exec()` sites but both string sources are operator/env-controlled, not remotely reachable), ws-gateway (zero CLI-invocation surface), atlas-map-api (already hardened from an earlier adversarial review — `build_argv`'s `--` sentinel is the reference implementation of the exact pattern applied everywhere else in this sweep).

**Total this session across both phases: 11 real vulnerabilities found and fixed** (3 in delta-kernel/delta-scp + 8 here), spanning shell injection, git/CLI argument injection, SSRF, path traversal, missing auth, and an LLM-mediated RCE chain. Every fix was verified against a real payload or a real TestClient/unit-level call before being considered done — never trusted an agent's or a scanner's claim alone.

**Committed and pushed** — 6 commits, `Pre Atlas` (space-path) repo, `feat/atlas-setup-ui` → `origin/feat/atlas-setup-ui`: `1d56bef` (cortex), `7a50cea` (uasc-executor), `2321b95` (search-stack), `e97d97c` (openclaw), `5afb34b` (droplist), `d75c22e` (memory-hub). **Injection sweep across all of Pre Atlas is now COMPLETE** — every service checked, every real finding fixed and shipped.

Relates: [[reference_binre_re_stack]] · [[project_sigil_substrate_codec]] · [[project_delta_scp_v2]] · [[project_groundwork_stack_roadmap]] · [[feedback_assemble_first_posture]] · [[reference_windows_safe_exec_pattern]] · [[reference_git_argument_injection_pattern]] · [[feedback_agent_report_distrust]].

*Name:* nih-plug VST3 bootstrap
*Description:* Rust → VST3 → FL Studio dev loop. Clone, build dir, FL scan path, install helper, preserved tutorial builds. Forward-references session 2/3.

# nih-plug VST3 dev loop

**Location:** `C:\Users\bruke\nih-plug` (cloned 2026-05-16 from https://github.com/robbert-vdh/nih-plug)

## Build pipeline

- `cd C:\Users\bruke\nih-plug && cargo xtask bundle gain --release` → writes to `target\bundled\gain.vst3`
- First clean build: ~30 s (way faster than the tutorial's "several minutes" — Rust 1.83 + MSVC was already warm)
- Incremental rebuilds: ~4 s after code edit

## FL Studio is wired to scan `target/bundled` directly

Bruke added `C:\Users\bruke\nih-plug\target\bundled` to FL's VST3 search paths (Options → File settings → Manage plugins → +). **Consequence: zero install/copy/UAC friction.** Every rebuild is live on next FL rescan or restart. No `Copy-Item` to `C:\Program Files\Common Files\VST3` needed.

## Session 1 (2026-05-16, COMPLETED)

Per `~/.claude/plans/concrete-steps-in-order-idempotent-dusk.md`. Steps 1-2 already done on this machine (rustc 1.83.0, VS Community 2022 with VC++ x64). Executed steps 3-8 of the pasted tutorial: clone → build → install → verify in FL → rename `const NAME` → rebuild → distortion (`*sample = *sample * *sample`) → rebuild. Final state in `plugins/examples/gain/src/lib.rs`:
- Line 114: `const NAME: &'static str = "Bruke Gain";`
- Line 182: `*sample = *sample * *sample;` (compiler warns about unused `gain` smoother var — expected, harmless)

## Preserved tutorial artifacts

`C:\Users\bruke\nih-plug\builds\step{4,7,8}\gain.vst3` — three pinned states (clean, renamed, distortion). Useful for replaying the loop or A/B comparisons without re-editing source.

## Install helper (rarely needed now)

`C:\Users\bruke\nih-plug\install-vst3.ps1 -Step {step4|step7|step8|current}` — copies the chosen artifact into `C:\Program Files\Common Files\VST3\`. Needs elevation. Only used during session 1 when scan-path config wasn't yet set up.

## Friction gotchas (learned the hard way)

- **UAC can't be driven from the sandboxed shell.** `Start-Process -Verb RunAs` silently fails; Windows native `sudo` (1.0.1) is on PATH but elevation prompts don't surface to me. **Implication for future:** if a future step legitimately needs admin (uninstall, system-wide install), pre-write the elevated command for Bruke to paste — don't try to drive it from here.
- **FL holds the VST3 DLL memory-mapped while running.** Even admin `Remove-Item` returns "Access denied" if FL64.exe or PluginManager.exe has the file open. Close FL before any deletion of an installed plugin.

## Forward references (not in scope yet)

- **Session 2** (per tutorial): read-only — clone Surge XT + Airwindows, trace one small effect line-by-line (suggestions: `Console`, `Pop`, `BassDrive`).
- **Session 3** (per tutorial): build a real effect Bruke would actually use, by copying `plugins/examples/gain` into a new folder and writing DSP in `process()`.

Optional add-ons deferred at end of session 1:
- VS Code: `code --install-extension rust-lang.rust-analyzer` (not installed yet)
- `pluginval` from https://github.com/Tracktion/pluginval/releases (not installed yet)

## Phase 1 — Tour player (Screen-Studio easing)

Lifted `mathUtils.ts:cubicBezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)` (the OpenScreen "Screen-Studio feel" curve) into:

| File | Lift |
|---|---|
| `C:\Users\bruke\web-audit\templates\anatomy-template.html` | Tour btn + scroll + pan-zoom on `.dash-inner` wrapper. Leader lines hide during tour. |

| `apps/canvas-demo/src/Main.tsx:121` | One-line swap `Easing.out(Easing.cubic)` → `Easing.bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)`. |

**Verified end-to-end**: probe captured the chained zoom across 5 beats: scale ramped 0.89 → 3.80 → 1.20 → 3.89 → 0.89 → reset, each beat 700ms scroll + 1800ms dwell + 400ms cleanup. Cleanup zooms back to identity. Test fixtures + Playwright recordings at `_research/openscreen-lift-test/recordings/tour-{extension,webaudit}.{mp4,webm}`.

## Phase 2 — Sitepull --record + batch + bug fixes

Three follow-on lifts on top of `web-audit/` (the sitepull npm package source at `C:\Users\bruke\web-audit\`):

### A · `sitepull --record` flag
- New flag (boolean, requires `--browser`)
- `lib/browser.js:makeBrowserFetcher` accepts `recordVideoDir` + `singlePage: true`
- `lib/audit.js`: spawns dedicated recording fetcher ONLY for the crawl phase (probe phase noise excluded). Forces `concurrency=1` + persistent page → ONE continuous video, no white-flash gaps.
- Output: `<outRoot>/crawl.webm`. Cleanup auto-stitches multi-clip outputs with ffmpeg if multiple clips exist (fallback to single-largest).

### B · `sitepull --stealth` flag
- Wires `playwright-extra` + `puppeteer-extra-plugin-stealth` (`npm install --save-dev` in web-audit).
- Patches `navigator.webdriver`, `window.chrome.runtime`, `navigator.plugins`, WebGL vendor/renderer, permissions API, iframe.contentWindow.
- Defaults UA to realistic Chrome 121 string in stealth mode (vs default `sitepull/0.2; +browser-mode`).
- **Verified** on `bot.sannysoft.com`: baseline 21 passed / 9 failed → stealth 31 passed / 0 failed. The 9 fixed checks: Plugins.PluginArray, HEADCHR_CHROME_OBJ, HEADCHR_PERMISSIONS, HEADCHR_IFRAME, plus 5 others.

### C · Batch script
- `scripts/batch-pull.mjs` runs sitepull --record on a list of URLs, produces per-target `crawl.{webm,mp4}` + an `index.html` table with play links.
- Default targets: example.com, Wikipedia, Hacker News.

### Bug fix · Windows path sanitization in `lib/util.js:urlToRelPath`
- URLs with `:` in the pathname (Wikipedia `/wiki/Wikipedia:Contents`, GitHub project boards, Confluence) crashed `mkdir` on Windows.
- Patched: per-segment sanitize of `:?*"<>|` → `_`. Wikipedia + similar now vendor cleanly.

## Test infrastructure

- `_research/openscreen-lift-test/render-fixture.mjs` — renders web-audit fixture from current template

- `web-audit/scripts/bot-test.mjs` — bot.sannysoft.com baseline-vs-stealth
- `web-audit/scripts/bot-test.mjs --stealth` — re-run after stealth changes

## Watch-me labeling (extension only)
Separate lift on top of the same dwell algorithm: `tools/anatomy-extension/content.js` got a `[watch me]` button next to `[auto-label]`. Captures cursor at 20Hz during browsing, runs dwell detection (450-2600ms within 0.02 normalized threshold), creates labels at dwell points using existing `buildSelector` + `deriveLabel`. *Name:* Optogon delegate_to_codex path
*Description:* Optogon state machine that wraps the Codex handoff. 6-node path (qualify → classify → route_gate → run → output_gate → close) at services/optogon/paths/delegate_to_codex.json. CLI wrapper at tools/codex-partner/delegate.py is the one-line entry point. 94 Optogon tests passing. Shipped 2026-04-25.

# Optogon path · delegate_to_codex · SHIPPED 2026-04-25

Wraps the Codex handoff as an Optogon state machine instead of an ad-hoc dispatcher.

## Path

`services/optogon/paths/delegate_to_codex.json` — 6 nodes:

```
qualify → classify → route_gate → run → output_gate → close
```

## Handlers (in `services/optogon/action_handlers.py`)

- **`classify_codex_intent`** — regex-maps Bruke phrasings to `(skill, sandbox, framing)`. Preserves raw casing for the prompt sent to Codex; lowercased copy used for matching. Routes back to Claude on `anthropic_overlap` (e.g., docx/pdf/pptx/touchdesigner) or `no_match`.
- **`run_codex_exec`** — `subprocess.run` via `codex.cmd` shim. Sandbox-honoring (read-only / workspace-write). Optional `--output-schema` with jsonschema validation on the response.

## HTTP entry

`POST /session/run` walks the whole path one-shot. Single request in, structured JSON out.

## CLI wrapper (Pattern D)

`tools/codex-partner/delegate.py` is the one-line Bruke-friendly entry point:

```bash
python tools/codex-partner/delegate.py "<intent>" [--cwd X] [--schema path]
```

- Auto-starts Optogon if down
- Returns pure JSON
- Zero manual steps
- Documented as Pattern D in `~/.claude/skills/codex-delegate/SKILL.md`

## Tests

94 Optogon tests passing — 63 existing + 31 new for the path (classifier coverage + sandbox honoring + schema validation + anthropic-overlap routing).

## Related

- [reference_codex_bridge.md](reference_codex_bridge.md) — Codex CLI v0.118 reference
- [reference_codex_pentest.md](reference_codex_pentest.md) — sandbox empirics
- `~/.claude/skills/codex-delegate/SKILL.md` — the dispatcher skill that decides when to call this

## project_optogon_path_runtime

<a id="project-optogon-path-runtime"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_optogon_path_runtime.md`*
*Name:* project-optogon-path-runtime
*Description:* Optogon — FastAPI path-runtime (node-graph FSM) service, live on :3010, committed; has a latent closed_at bug

**Optogon** = "the path runtime that closes directives through node graphs" (its own pyproject.toml:4). Python/FastAPI **dialogue-path engine** at `services/optogon/` in Pre Atlas. Drives a declarative JSON node-graph (state machine) turn-by-turn, executes work at each node (optionally via Codex), emits signals, and strictly rations questions asked.

**Status (2026-06-25, verified first-hand):** RUNNING live on `:3010` (`uvicorn optogon.main:app`, boot entry `.claude/launch.json:157`) · MERGED (36 files tracked, commits `82c86de` sitepull wiring, `a5d2eb4` /session/run fix) · 12 test files. Built by festival `optogon-stack-OS0001`, completed **2026-05-27**.

**Core concept:** a "path" is a JSON node-graph FSM (`paths/ship_inpact_lesson.json`, also `commit_a_file`, `triage_fs_loop`, `delegate_to_codex`). Node types: `qualify` (gather/infer keys) → `execute` (tool_call/transform/side_effect, with reversible+risk_tier) → `gate` → `approval` → `close` (emits CloseSignal). Edges carry conditions + priorities.

**Distinctive thesis — pacing as a HARD constraint** (`response_composer.py:1-16`): max 1 question/turn, per-node token budget, no node_id/session_id leaks; violations replaced with fallback. Close-loop phase 004 measures whether it learns to ask *fewer* questions over repeat runs via a user-preference store. LLM optional (Claude if `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, else deterministic fallbacks).

**Endpoints** (`atlas.surface.json`): GET /health /paths /session/{id} /signals · POST /session/start /session/{id}/turn · **/session/run** (one-shot autonomous drive, caps 30 turns, auto-Codex — for agent callers like codex-delegate). Full build spec at `doctrine/fest_staging/optogon-stack/` (4 phases: CONTRACTS → SERVICE → INTEGRATION → CLOSE_LOOP).

**closed_at bug — FIXED+COMMITTED 2026-06-25 (`9f922f5` on `feat/atlas-setup-ui`; 4 files +116/-8).** Was: `_handle_close` set node-scoped `ns["closed_at"]` + `state["_close_signal"]` but NOT session-level `state["closed_at"]`, and `store.close()` had zero callers → SQLite column never written → `list_active()` returned closed sessions forever. Fix: `_handle_close` now sets `state["closed_at"]` (node_processor.py:325); `store.update()` persists the column when set (session_store.py:119-125, single write path); `main.py:_is_closed()` workaround removed (trusts `closed_at`); `store.close()` kept + documented as manual force-close API. Regression test `tests/test_session_close_propagation.py` covers both sinks. **96 pytest green; red→green proven; 5/5 adversarial verifiers verified.** Built via /groundwork fest `optogon-close-fix-OC0001` (all 3 tasks marked completed).

**⚠️ ENV FOOTGUN:** the editable `optogon` install pointed at a STALE worktree (`.claude/worktrees/vigorous-hoover-3db0c2/...`), so tests/live-server imported old code. Re-ran `pip install -e .` from main `services/optogon` to repoint. Live :3010 (started earlier) still runs worktree code in memory — **restart to load the fix.**

```
seed_preferences.py  (schema 0.2)
   │  feedback_*.md  +  user_*.md  +  cognitive-sensor profile reports
   ▼
proposals/preferences.proposed.json    ← review before promoting
   │
   ▼  promote_preferences.py  (NEW, urllib only)
   │
POST /api/atlas/preferences  ─▶  delta-kernel preferences.db
                                          │
                                          ▼  fetch_preferences()  (already wired pre-2026-04-27)
                                          ▼  inject_preferences_into_context()
                                          ▼
                            session.context.confirmed / .inferred
```

## Tier mapping (in promote_preferences.py)

| Source                                | key prefix                           | source     | confidence |
|---------------------------------------|--------------------------------------|------------|------------|
| feedback_*.md                         | `feedback.{slug}`                    | explicit   | 0.95       |
| user_*.md memory files                | `profile.user_memory.{stem}`         | explicit   | 0.90       |
| cognitive-sensor analyses (curated 9) | `profile.cognitive_sensor.{stem}`    | inferred   | 0.80       |

Confidence is paired against `AUTO_CONFIRM_CONFIDENCE = 0.85` in
`services/optogon/src/optogon/config.py`. ≥ 0.85 lands in
`context.confirmed`; below lands in `context.inferred`.

## Curated cognitive-sensor allowlist

Pulled (9): MY_COGNITIVE_PROFILE, DEEP_PSYCHOLOGICAL_PROFILE, EMOTIONAL_PROFILE,
BEHAVIORAL_AUDIT, CONVERSATION_PATTERNS, DERAILMENT_FACTORS, LANGUAGE_LOOPS,
LEVERAGE_MAP, GROWTH_REPORT.

Excluded on purpose:
- BELIEF_CORE.md / BELIEF_RULES.md → raw conversation-quote dumps (1000+ lines
  of repeated transcript snippets). Noise > signal.
- STALL_SIGNATURES.md / ACCELERATION_PHRASES.md / DECISION_ENGINE.md → stub
  files (<10 lines), auto-skipped by `PROFILE_MIN_WORDS=20` guard.

## Live verification

End-to-end on 2026-04-27:
- `seed_preferences.py` produced 16 feedback + 12 profile signals = 28 entries.
- `promote_preferences.py` posted all 28; 0 failures (delta-kernel on :3001).
- Optogon session `sess_605b0cdca6f2` started against `commit_a_file` path:
  - `state.context.confirmed` had 22 keys (16 feedback + 3 user_memory + 3
    pre-existing ui_theme/severity/fs_kind from the d5b82cf-era close-loop).
  - `state.context.inferred` had 9 keys (all `profile.cognitive_sensor.*`).
  - Top derailment factor "Phone/Social Media (1,509 mentions)" visible to
    every action handler that reads
    `context['inferred']['profile.cognitive_sensor.derailment_factors']`.

## What's NOT wired yet

The preference data is now reaching session context, but Optogon does not
**use** it for routing or composition. Two remaining wires (intentionally
deferred):

1. **Router weighting** — `router.py` doesn't consult preferences when
   ranking paths. A path tagged "ship_small" doesn't yet get a boost from
   the matching `feedback.ship_small_iterate_fast` rule.
2. **Compose hook** — `teach_me_a_path` doesn't read profile signals when
   suggesting templates / triggers.

Both would build on top of the data that's now flowing.

## Files

- `tools/optogon-calibrate/seed_preferences.py` — schema 0.2 (feedback + profile)
- `tools/optogon-calibrate/promote_preferences.py` — NEW
- `tools/optogon-calibrate/proposals/preferences.proposed.json` — current proposal output

## How to re-run

```bash
# 1. Regenerate the proposal from current memory + cognitive-sensor state
python tools/optogon-calibrate/seed_preferences.py

# 2. Inspect (optional)
cat tools/optogon-calibrate/proposals/preferences.proposed.json

# 3. Boot delta-kernel
cd services/delta-kernel && npx tsx src/api/server.ts &

# 4. Promote — preview first, then for-real
python tools/optogon-calibrate/promote_preferences.py --dry-run
python tools/optogon-calibrate/promote_preferences.py
```

## project_optogon_signal_ingestion

<a id="project-optogon-signal-ingestion"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_optogon_signal_ingestion.md`*
*Name:* Optogon Signal Ingestion (Ship Target #1)
*Description:* End-to-end Signal.v1 transport from Optogon producers to InPACT, with delta-kernel as the broker. Persisted to SQLite, polled at 5 s, plain-language UI.

# Ship Target #1 · InPACT Signal ingestion

**SHIPPED 2026-04-27** on branch `claude/condescending-lumiere-d300af` (worktree at `.claude/worktrees/condescending-lumiere-d300af`). Open as a PR; not yet merged.

## What landed

- **delta-kernel · `services/delta-kernel/src/atlas/signals-store.ts`**: Replaced the in-memory ring buffer with a `SignalsStore` class backed by SQLite (`better-sqlite3`) at `~/.delta-fabric/signals.db`. Two tables: `signals` (with `resolved_at` / `resolved_action` columns) and `signal_resolutions`. Resolution updates the row in place — does not delete — so resolved approvals stay history-able.
- **delta-kernel · `src/api/server.ts`**: Added canonical `POST /api/signals` (kept `/api/signals/ingest` as one-release alias). Renamed life-signal routes to `/api/life-signals/{energy,finance,skills,network,bulk}` to free the canonical signal namespace. Updated callers in `cli/atlas.ts` and `cli/atlas-ai.ts` (17 references).
- **optogon · `services/optogon/src/optogon/signals.py`**: `emit()` now fires a daemon-thread POST to `${DELTA_KERNEL_URL}/api/signals` when `OPTOGON_SIGNAL_EMIT=1` (matches the three-switch ladder from `cortex_bridge.py`). Fail-soft: any HTTP error is logged, never raised; the path runtime is unaffected by a delta-kernel outage.
- **InPACT · `apps/inpact/js/signals.js`**: Polling 5 s base / 30 s backoff cap. Banner now surfaces only `approval_required` + `urgent` + `error` (Rule 6 dropped `completion`). `approval_required` cards are sticky (no auto-dismiss). `SOURCE_LABELS` map translates `optogon → "Conversation"`, `atlas → "Queue"`, `ghost_executor → "Executor"`, `site_pull → "Site capture"`, `claude_code → "Builder"`. `payload.data` is never read by the renderer — verified by static test + live DOM scrape. Public surface: `window.__inpactSignals = { poll, getLatest, sourceLabel, subscribe, resolve }`.
- **InPACT · `apps/inpact/js/screens.js`**: Home() renders a `<div id="ip-stream-list">` Stream block. Populated via `setInterval` polling for the element (chosen over MutationObserver because the renderer mutates the same subtree it would observe — feedback loop).

## Why: Where this fits in the doctrine

Per Rosetta Stone Contract 5: "InPACT is the human surface. It receives signals from every layer and surfaces what the user needs to see." Before this ship the Optogon stack was running blind — producers built `Signal.v1` payloads and threw them away locally. After this ship, InPACT renders them within ~5 s of emit, and approval-required signals block the producing thread (Optogon's `awaiting_approval` state in `node_processor.py`) until the user clicks an action option.

## Verified live

- POST canonical, alias, malformed (400), GET, resolve, resolve-twice (404), persistence-across-restart all green via `curl`.
- Browser: signal lands within 5 s, `approval_required` card sticky with two action buttons, click resolves and removes card. Stream block on Home shows non-attention signals with plain-language source. ZERO `node_id` / `path_id` / `session_id` in `document.body.innerHTML`. ZERO em dashes.
- Tests: 10/10 delta-kernel SignalsStore + 9/9 optogon (4 emit + 5 no-id-leak/no-em-dash) all pass. tsc clean (only pre-existing goal/Author errors in unrelated lines remain).

## Three real bugs uncovered while shipping

1. `app.get('/api/signals')` was registered twice in server.ts — first by Optogon Signal route, then by life-signals. Express resolved by registration order, so life-signals readout was silently broken in `cli/atlas.ts` and `cli/atlas-ai.ts` (energy / mental_load / sleep_quality always rendered `--`). Renamed to fix.
2. `node_processor.py` emits signals with `data={"node_id":..., "session_id":...}`. The InPACT renderer skips `payload.data` so the DOM stays clean, but the API response is not scrubbed. Logged for downstream callers — not blocking InPACT's Rule 5 because the doctrine concern is rendered HTML.
3. `today.html` is now a one-line redirect to `index.html`. Memory said "today.html IS the product per project_inpact_product_pivot.md" but the actual file got replaced. Stream block landed in `index.html` Home() screen.

## Locked decisions (confirmed by Bruke before exit-plan)

- Path collision → rename life-signals to `/api/life-signals`; Optogon Signal.v1 owns `/api/signals`.
- InPACT surface → stream block in Today screen of `index.html` (not the redirect file).
- Approval block semantics → sticky banner card on InPACT side; producing thread blocks via existing `awaiting_approval`. No full UI modal lock.

## Out of scope (parallel cloud agent shipping)

- `services/optogon/src/optogon/adapters/sitepull_adapter.py`
- `services/optogon/tests/test_sitepull_adapter.py`
- `services/optogon/tests/test_session_start_sitepull.py`
- `services/optogon/src/optogon/main.py` `POST /session/from_sitepull`

## Files touched

| File | Change |
|:---|:---|
| `services/delta-kernel/src/atlas/signals-store.ts` | Ring buffer → SQLite; resolve marks-not-deletes |
| `services/delta-kernel/src/api/server.ts` | `POST /api/signals` + alias; life-signals rename |
| `services/delta-kernel/src/cli/atlas.ts` | `/api/signals` → `/api/life-signals` |
| `services/delta-kernel/src/cli/atlas-ai.ts` | same |
| `services/optogon/src/optogon/config.py` | `DELTA_KERNEL_URL` + `SIGNAL_EMIT_ENABLED` |
| `services/optogon/src/optogon/signals.py` | Daemon-thread POST, fail-soft |
| `apps/inpact/js/signals.js` | 5 s poll, Rule 6 filter, plain-language labels, sticky approvals, stream renderer |
| `apps/inpact/js/screens.js` | `<div id="ip-stream-list">` block in Home() |
| `services/delta-kernel/src/tests/signals-store-tests.ts` | New: 10 tests |
| `services/optogon/tests/test_signals_emit.py` | New: 4 tests (validate, switch off, switch on, fail-soft) |
| `services/optogon/tests/test_signals_no_id_leak.py` | New: 5 static-source contract tests |

## Lessons for next ship

- I edited the main `Pre Atlas\` checkout instead of the `\.claude\worktrees\condescending-lumiere-d300af\` worktree for the first half of the ship. Codex caught it on the first sanity pass. Recovery: copied the 11 files into the worktree, reverted in main. Cost ~10 min.
- Setting `DELTA_DATA_DIR` without `DELTA_REPO_ROOT` makes server.ts compute repoRoot wrong (`path.resolve(DATA_DIR, '..')` → `~`). Always set both for non-default test configs.
- MutationObserver on `#main-content` with `subtree: true` and a child renderer that mutates inside it = infinite loop. Switched to `setInterval` element-identity check.

## Schema Conditional Gotcha (remember for next Optogon work)

Draft-07 `if/then` with `properties` ONLY fires the `then` when the property is actually present. If a property is optional, use `if: { required: ["key"], properties: { key: {...} } }` — the `required` in the `if` acts as a presence gate. Bit both ContextPackage.partial and Signal.payload.action_required during initial validation. Fixed in both. Same pattern will bite again anywhere a conditional depends on an optional field.

## project_orchestration_experiment_2026_06_15

<a id="project-orchestration-experiment-2026-06-15"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_orchestration_experiment_2026_06_15.md`*
*Name:* orchestration-experiment-2026-06-15
*Description:* One-time experiment comparing 4 orderings of code-recon + delta-scp + agents (A/B/C/D) on a fixed Signal.v1 trace task in services/droplist. Result, lesson, do-not-rerun.

One-time experiment (2026-06-15/16) comparing 4 orchestration orderings on a fixed task: trace Signal.v1 emission/consumption in `services/droplist`.

**Result: D (hybrid: map → parallel agents → verify) wins.** It was the only ordering whose skill combination produced 3 unique findings (`payload.data.links` doc drift, 9 untested payload sub-fields, synthetic action_option fallback risk) that A/B/C all missed. The other 3 orderings produced consensus baseline findings; on a clean codebase they're interchangeable.

**Do not re-run this experiment.** The orderings-comparison is a one-time exercise. Going forward:
- **Day-to-day audits:** pick D, run once
- **High-stakes audits driving real decisions:** run all 4 once on the specific question, take consensus as truth, scrutinize divergence
- **Never** re-run A vs B vs C vs D again to figure out which is better

**Why:** Three substrate variants ran (local Workflow without skill calls, cloud routines on `main` then on `claude/main-triage-26f4a5`, manual sessions with actual `Skill` invocations). Each surfaced different findings but D consistently produced the richest unique architectural insight. Skill cost is real (~5 calls + 4 parallel agents in D vs 4 for A/B and 7 for C) but pays off on causal and absence claims.

**How to apply:** When the user asks for a code audit / forensic scan / "find everything wrong with X" — default to D (hybrid). Invoke the actual `Skill({skill: "delta-scp"})` and `Skill({skill: "code-recon"})` tools — don't approximate with Glob+Grep. For audits whose output drives real decisions, run all 4 trials in parallel via the manual prompt pattern at `experiments/manual-skill-trial/prompts/`.

**Lessons surfaced:**
- C (sweep-first + strict verify) is a **precision specialist, not recall specialist.** It DROPS findings at verify rather than accumulating. Reach for C when stakes mean you can't ship a false positive.
- Local Workflow and cloud routines find DIFFERENT findings even on the same codebase — substrate matters (working tree vs committed state).
- Skills add real value on absence and causal claims; for presence-at-file:line they're overkill.

**Artifacts kept:** `experiments/manual-skill-trial/` (4 reports + 3 scp JSONs + README); cloud branches `experiment/cloud-orch-{A,B,C,D}-v2-2026-06-16` on the remote.

Related: [[verify-at-head-before-acting]] · [[droplist-remediation-plan]] · [[verified-audit-default]] · [[agent-report-distrust]]

## project_paperme_catalog

<a id="project-paperme-catalog"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_paperme_catalog.md`*

# PaperMe Catalog

**Status:** SHIPPED 2026-05-02
**Worktree:** `silly-thompson-1a5e28`
**Location:** `tools/paperme-catalog/`
**Source site:** https://paperme.pixzens.com/en

## What this is

One canonical exemplar of every paper template PaperMe offers, in three formats chosen for AI consumption:
- **SVG** — text-based vector, AI can read line spacing / grid math / cell sizes directly, also editable
- **PNG** — vision-model preview at A4 resolution
- **manifest.json** — flat queryable index: `{ id, value, name, rawLabel, category, svg, png, bytes }`

PDF and DOCX intentionally skipped (heavier, less AI-friendly).

## Result

| metric | value |
|---|---|
| templates captured | 43 / 43 |
| failures | 0 |
| categories | 14 standard · 18 new · 11 chinese |
| SVG total | 801 KB (260 b – 134 KB per file) |
| PNG total | 5.2 MB (78 KB – 450 KB per file) |
| manifest | 16 KB |
| run time | ~3 min headless |

`output/` is gitignored — regenerable via `npm run` (or `HEADLESS=1 npm run`).

## Architecture

Single Playwright script (`run.mjs`, ~150 lines, ESM, no build step) that:
1. Opens the SPA at `/en` once.
2. Locates the Paper Type `<select>` via `select:has(option[value="lined"])`.
3. Enumerates options as `{ value, label, disabled }`. Filters out disabled and `----` separators.
4. For each option: `selectOption()` → wait for preview SVG to render → click Export SVG → capture download → click Export PNG → capture download.
5. Writes `manifest.json` with category counts and a summary table.

Env knobs: `HEADLESS=1`, `SLOWMO=300`, `LIMIT=N`, `ONLY="lined,grid"`, `DEBUG=1`.

## What worked well

- **WebFetch + sitemap peek before planning.** Discovered "no per-template URLs, single SPA route" early. Saved time vs jumping into Playwright with wrong assumptions.
- **Plan mode with format-tradeoff table.** SVG/PNG/PDF/DOCX matrix made the format decision legible and let the user approve one shape rather than iterating.
- **Smoke test with `LIMIT=2` before full run.** First smoke test (30 sec) immediately revealed the cards-vs-dropdown mistake — failure was in the right place at the right time.
- **Text-based selectors (`getByRole`, option `value`).** No CSS-class brittleness. Survived selector retries without modification.
- **Header-byte structural check before visual check.** Reading PNG magic bytes (`89 50 4E 47`) and SVG headers (`<?xml`/`<svg`) takes ~50 ms across 43 files vs minutes of opening images. Did the cheap check first.
- **Failure quarantine in the loop.** Each template wrapped in try/catch, screenshot on fail to `output/failures/`, batch continues. The 3 separator-option failures didn't take down the run.
- **Slugify with explicit hyphen rule.** Filenames are predictable (`hexagonal-grid.png`) — the manifest stores both `id` (slug) and `value` (raw option attr) so AI can reference either way.

## What to do better next time

- **Look at the rendered page screenshot BEFORE writing the v1 driver.** I wrote a card-clicking driver based on the WebFetch markdown summary, then had to rewrite. The `DEBUG=1` screenshot path I added later should've been step 1 of exploration, not a debugging afterthought. Cost: ~10 min wasted on the v1 path.
- **Enumerate-then-categorize, not categorize-then-enumerate.** I hand-coded `STANDARD`/`CHINESE` name sets from the WebFetch description. The live site had templates I didn't anticipate (`pink_millimeter_grid`, `grid_lined_split`, `practice_paper`) — they got bucketed as `new` by default. Better: capture all, then ask the source for canonical groupings (or scrape group `<optgroup>` labels — separators in this site mark groups).
- **Don't second-guess "every type" + "best format you see fit."** I almost asked a clarifying scope question after the user had explicitly delegated both decisions. Spent extra round-trips on deliberation. When the brief is unambiguous, plan and ship.
- **Filter disabled options on first pass.** The `optgroup`-via-disabled-`<option>` pattern is common; should've added that filter to v1 instead of hitting it as a "failure."

## Reusable patterns extracted

- **SPA-driven artifact catalog template:** open page once → enumerate `<select>` options → switch state → intercept `download` events → manifest with byte sizes. Applies to any tool with template/preset switcher + export buttons (Figma exports, design tools, generators).
- **Format-tradeoff matrix in plan mode:** when "best format" is part of the brief, write a 4-column table (vision / structure / edit / verdict) so the choice is auditable and reversible.
- **Smoke test = LIMIT + DEBUG envs.** Build the full pipeline first, expose env-var caps for cheap iteration. Saves running 43 templates to find a selector bug.

## Critical files

- `tools/paperme-catalog/run.mjs` — driver
- `tools/paperme-catalog/package.json` — playwright dep, npm scripts
- `tools/paperme-catalog/README.md` — usage + manifest schema + AI-agent example
- `tools/paperme-catalog/.gitignore` — excludes `output/` (regenerable)

## Re-run

```bash
cd tools/paperme-catalog
npm install && npm run install:browser   # one-time
HEADLESS=1 npm run                       # ~3 min
```

## project_phantom_input_audit_plan

<a id="project-phantom-input-audit-plan"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_phantom_input_audit_plan.md`*
*Name:* phantom-input · 7-repo humanization audit (DEFERRED)
*Description:* Future-move plan to audit 7 humanization libs and synthesize a unified phantom-input library. Deferred until sitepull --humanize ships and creepjs measurement reveals whether mouse-shape (vs fingerprint/TLS/IP/session) is actually the bottleneck.

# Phantom-Input · 7-Repo Humanization Audit (DEFERRED · 2026-04-26)

## Status
**DEFERRED.** Triggered by Bruke 2026-04-26 as alternative to shipping `--humanize` with ghost-cursor. Decision: ship simple ghost-cursor version FIRST, measure with creepjs, run this audit ONLY if measurement shows mouse-shape is the actual bottleneck.

## Why deferred (vs killed)
The audit IS valuable when:
1. `--humanize` ships, creepjs trust score still <0.7
2. Diagnosis points specifically at velocity profile / Perlin noise / fatigue modeling — i.e. the dimensions ghost-cursor lacks
3. Real-target failures correlate with mouse-shape signals (not fingerprint/TLS/IP)

The audit is NOT valuable if:
- Creepjs hits >0.7 with ghost-cursor alone (audit's premise unproven)
- Creepjs failures correlate with fingerprint/TLS/IP/session (different layer; audit is wrong tool)
- Codex's diagnostic point: "mouse-shape realism is often not the dominant factor for trust scoring; environment consistency matters more"

## Factual issues to fix before running
The original doc contained claims that didn't survive verification:
- `saksham-personal/humanization-patchright` → **404, repo doesn't exist** at cited path
- `shy-mouse-playwright` → claimed "Playwright/Patchright mouse, Fitts's Law"; actual: **6 stars, 2 forks, 8 months old, last commit 2.5 mo ago.** Not "battle-tested"
- `ghost-cursor` → claimed ~3k stars; actual **1501**, last commit 10 days ago, 159 forks. Mature option is *more* mature than doc says
- Agent frontmatter model IDs `claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929` / `claude-opus-4-6-20250910` are stale; current is `claude-sonnet-4-6` / `claude-opus-4-7`

## Real differentiators worth chasing IF audit is triggered
What ghost-cursor lacks (per shy-mouse-playwright README, must verify in source):
- Velocity bell curve (fast middle, slow start/end)
- Perlin noise paths (vs pure bezier)
- Fatigue modeling (precision degrades over time)
- Polling rate variation (60-144Hz, temporally correlated)
- Micro-corrections / jerk smoothing

## Original audit doc reference
Full audit plan (7 target repos, parallel subagent dispatch, synthesis-architect agent design, phantom-input library structure) was provided by Bruke 2026-04-26. Key points:

**Target repos:**
1. `Xetera/ghost-cursor` (1501 stars, mature) — already shipping with this
2. `AB6162/shy-mouse-playwright` (6 stars, small) — claimed velocity/Perlin/fatigue
3. `riflosnake/HumanCursor` (Python, Selenium)
4. `sarperavci/human_mouse` (Python, Bezier+spline)
5. `patrikoss/pyclick` (Python, Bezier)
6. `saksham-personal/humanization-patchright` — **404 at cited path**, may need to find renamed/forked version
7. `Kaliiiiiiiiii-Vinyzu/patchright-python` (~2.2k stars, stealth context not input)

**Architecture**: Python+JS dual-target `phantom-input` library with core/, mouse/, keyboard/, behavior/, adapters/.

**API**: `phantom.click(page, sel)`, `phantom.type(page, sel, text)`, `phantom.scroll(...)`, `phantom.move_to(...)` with persona configs ("casual", "precise", "tired", "gamer").

## Trigger conditions for re-activation
Run this audit when ANY of the following:
1. creepjs trust score <0.7 after `--humanize` ships AND failure correlates with mouse-signal categories (not fingerprint/TLS)
2. A specific WAF (Akamai BMP, DataDome, PerimeterX) blocks sitepull and post-mortem points to mouse behavior
3. Bruke explicitly says "build phantom-input"

## Scope adjustments if reactivated
- **Cut Python side** unless there's a Python product target. Sitepull is JS; Python is +scope.
- **Verify each repo URL exists** before delegating audits. Replace dead repos.
- **Update model IDs** in agent frontmatter to current (sonnet-4-6, opus-4-7).
- **Diagnose-first**: before audit, identify which specific creepjs/WAF signal failed. Audit FOR that signal, not "best of all."

## project_png_substrate_seed

<a id="project-png-substrate-seed"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_png_substrate_seed.md`*
*Name:* PNG-Substrate Seed (from share 69f38391)
*Description:* Four-pattern PNG-as-substrate frame; arc fully closed 2026-05-01 with all 5 patterns shipped + live wire-in to cognitive-sensor at 2.41x speedup

# PNG-Substrate Seed

**SEED:** [PNG_SUBSTRATE_SEED.md](../../.claude/worktrees/trusting-germain-a57cb0/PNG_SUBSTRATE_SEED.md) (worktree `trusting-germain-a57cb0`).

**Source transcript:** ChatGPT share `69f38391-e470-83ea-8e82-3dead8c9a012`, 174 turns. Vendored at `web-audit\.canvas\chatgpt.com\chatgpt.com-aspwk1-mols19s6\`.

**Why:** The original conversation drifted from real PNG calculator into "AI-Symbolic Execution / AGI civilization" framing. The seed pulled out the four-pattern scaffold and discarded the inflated framing. **Read your own questions; ignore the responses.**

**How to apply:** Canonical reference for PNG-substrate questions. Don't re-extract the share; transcript is on disk at `conversation_full.md`. The arc is now CLOSED — only re-engage if a new external consumer appears or an empirical claim needs re-testing.

## Patterns (all shipped 2026-05-01)

| # | Pattern | Reference impl | Spec status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Lookup table | [apps/c121-png-calc](../../.claude/worktrees/lucid-bohr-24156a/apps/c121-png-calc) | `lut.v1` shipped |
| 2 | Bytecode VM | [apps/c110-png-vm](../../.claude/worktrees/lucid-bohr-24156a/apps/c110-png-vm) | `vm.v1` shipped |
| 3 | Execution telemetry | [apps/c110-trace](../../.claude/worktrees/trusting-germain-a57cb0/apps/c110-trace) (port 8897) | `trace.v1` shipped |
| 4 | Content-addressed integrity | [apps/c130-png-integrity](../../.claude/worktrees/trusting-germain-a57cb0/apps/c130-png-integrity) | `integrity.v1` shipped |
| 5 | FSM transition tables | [apps/c140-png-fsm](../../.claude/worktrees/trusting-germain-a57cb0/apps/c140-png-fsm) | `fsm.v1` shipped |

Spec README at `specs/png-substrate/README.md` marks all 5 SHIPPED.
Conformance vectors at `specs/png-substrate/conformance/` — 4/4 fixtures PASS via `run.py`.

## Scale tests (worktree `heuristic-buck-35f73f`)

`tests/png-substrate-scale/` measured each pattern at scale and the headline lessons:

- **PNG-as-data swap (full embeddings)** — KILLED. Per-row PNG read 0.6x slower than SQLite raw blob streaming. PNG is not a database.
- **PNG-as-shortcut (LSH index)** — WIN. K=14 / L=24 hyperplane LSH baked into `embeddings_lsh.png` (4.5 MB) gives **3.23x speedup at 95% recall@10** vs brute-force cosine over the same 84,848 embeddings. The non-obvious fix: bigger K/L AND replacing per-table `np.isin` with a 2^K boolean accept-array (~60% of query time vanished).
- **alpha-strip survival** — c110 `program.png` originally relied on alpha as validity marker; social-media re-encoders strip alpha and broke the VM. Fixed with pixel-value HALT sentinel + RGB=0 (M4).

## Live wire-in (the only one that's "in production")

`services/cognitive-sensor/lsh_search_helper.py` exposes `find_similar_messages(query_vec, top_k)`. Wired into `cluster_leverage_map.py::get_central_peripheral`. Rollback: `--brute` flag or `LSH_DISABLE=1` env var. Verify run: 95.0% recall · 2.41x speedup · PASS.

This is the SINGLE proof point that PNG-substrate isn't a toy. Brute-force cosine is still the default for every other call site in cognitive-sensor — by design (LSH only helps for k-NN over k>=10, not for all-pairs / NxN cosine matrices).

## Dispatch (mode router compiled to a 220-byte PNG)

`services/delta-kernel/src/core/routing-png/` ships a Python LUT generator + Node TS no-deps decoder. Parity test runs all 1,458 (mode, bucketed-signals) inputs through both `computeNextMode()` and `routePngLookup()` — currently 1,458/1,458 PASS. Use case is **portability**: any non-TS consumer can reproduce delta-kernel routing decisions without porting the rules.

**Drift-spotting bonus:** the compile pass surfaced that `MODE_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` is duplicated in `routing.ts:191` and `atlas-ai.ts:491`. The S1 entry in `SWAP_CANDIDATES.md` is the highest-ROI next swap.

## Future-swap survey

`tests/png-substrate-scale/SWAP_CANDIDATES.md` enumerates 3 strong fits, 2 marginal cases, and 4 explicit anti-fits. The anti-fits are the more useful half — they document the rule "`Record<X, Y>` is necessary but not sufficient for LUT-shape; check the values for unbounded predicates like `.includes('admin')`."

## Calibration findings (kept for base-rate)

- Conversation amplification ratio (original ChatGPT share): **38.7x**. Assistant pushback count: **0/87**.
- I committed the symmetric "small demo proves nothing" fallacy myself this session — see `feedback_dont_disclaim_what_wasnt_tested.md`.
- Codex parallel-batch dispatch (6 missions in `.weapon-batch/`): all 6 closed in ~2 min wall-clock. Dispatch.sh pattern is reusable for future fan-outs.

## Codex second-opinion pass (2026-04-30, pre-arc-close)

Confirmed four-pattern frame, parking decision, and c110+telemetry next-ship. Surfaced FSM as candidate fifth (now shipped) and the "demo family, not paradigm" framing distinction.

## project_register_preview_server

<a id="project-register-preview-server"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_register_preview_server.md`*
*Name:* project-register-preview-server
*Description:* Deterministic script that mechanizes .claude/launch.json dev-server registration, with port/name-collision guards

Built 2026-07-15 as script #2 from the [transcript-index](project_transcript_index.md) pattern-clustering pass: `~/.claude/scripts/register_preview_server/register_preview_server.py` + `/register-preview-server` skill. Mechanizes adding a new dev server to `.claude/launch.json` — previously a manual Read + Edit-insert-JSON-block, verified against 33 sessions / 95 hits on that exact file before building.

**Why:** Same clustering pass that produced [memory-write tool](project_memory_write_tool.md). Checked actual edit content (not just counts) before building — every `launch.json` edit followed the identical shape, so this was a legitimate scriptable duty, not a coincidence of frequency.

**Real bug found on first run, RESOLVED 2026-07-15:** `--list` against the live `launch.json` immediately surfaced two port collisions that predated this tool — port `3011` shared by `lattice`/`ws-gateway`, port `8897` shared by `audit-map`/`c110-trace`. Root-caused via git history + `atlas-manifest.yaml` before touching anything: `c110-trace` copy-pasted `audit-map`'s port on add (2026-04-30, `a0c87a8`); `ws-gateway` drifted from its original system-index port 3006 (now `inpact`'s) to 3011, silently colliding with `lattice`'s long-standing static assignment. Fixed by moving the two newcomers: `ws-gateway` → `3013` (+ `WS_PORT` env override, since its own source falls back to 3011 when the env var is unset — verified in `services/ws-gateway/index.ts` before assuming the outer `port` field alone would work), `c110-trace` → `8901` (both the outer `port` field and its `-p` runtimeArg, since that server's actual bound port comes from the http-server CLI flag, not the launch.json metadata). Both new ports confirmed unused anywhere in the repo before picking them. Propagated the fix to every living doc that stated the stale port as fact: `atlas-manifest.yaml`, `ATLAS_HEADLESS_MAP.md`, `ATLAS_MASTER_PLAN.md`, `services/FLEET_INVENTORY.md`, `services/ROADMAP.md`. Left `docs/archive/**` (dated snapshots) and the auto-generated `system-map-data.js` files untouched — those aren't hand-maintained sources of truth.

**Usage:**
```bash
python register_preview_server.py --list                          # read-only, shows all registered servers
python register_preview_server.py --name X --exe npm --args run,dev --port N [--cwd ...] [--env K=V] [--force] [--dry-run]
```
Guards: refuses name collision and port collision unless `--force` (this is what caught the two live conflicts above, and what will catch the next one before it ships). **Gotcha documented in the script itself:** `runtimeArgs` starting with a flag (`-m`, common for `python -m uvicorn ...`) requires `--args=...` (equals sign) — plain `--args -m,...` makes argparse mistake the value for another option and fail with "expected one argument". Found this by testing for real, not assuming.

**Tested:** `--list` against the real 42-entry file (read-only), dry-run preview, name-collision guard (real error against `droplist`), port-collision guard (real error against `delta-api`/3001), a full write round-trip (append + force-overwrite) on a scratch COPY before ever writing to the real file, and finally the two real fixes applied for real with `--force` + verified via a Python JSON round-trip (42 entries preserved, zero duplicate ports, `yaml.safe_load` on the manifest edit).

**How to apply:** Reach for this instead of manual Read+Edit whenever registering a new named dev server for `preview_start`. One proposal remains from the same clustering pass: PreToolUse hook for the cat/tail/grep-via-Bash anti-pattern (~140 calls that should have used Read/Grep).

Related: [transcript-index](project_transcript_index.md), [memory-write tool](project_memory_write_tool.md).

## project_screenshot_to_code

<a id="project-screenshot-to-code"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_screenshot_to_code.md`*
*Name:* screenshot-to-code-clone
*Description:* abi/screenshot-to-code cloned + its vision approach ported into canvas-engine as an image->code path; how to run both

Bruke cloned abi/screenshot-to-code and is folding its capability into the existing canvas-engine stack. Sequence he chose 2026-05-23: (1) run abi's standalone, (2) study its source, (3) integrate into canvas-engine — done in that order in one session.

**abi's app** lives at `C:\Users\bruke\screenshot-to-code` (Python/FastAPI backend :7001 + React/Vite frontend :5173; NOT in Pre Atlas). Run: backend `cd backend && poetry install --no-cache && poetry run uvicorn main:app --port 7001`; frontend `cd frontend && npm install && npm run dev`. yarn is NOT installed on this machine — use npm. Poetry's global HTTP cache was permission-corrupt (`AppData\Local\pypoetry\Cache`) — always pass `--no-cache`. Keys via UI gear icon (browser-only) or backend/.env; no LLM keys were in env.

**Its architecture** (Phase 2): agentic, not raw-dump. WS `/generate-code` → 4 parallel variants (asyncio.gather) → each an Agent with tools (create_file/edit_file). Prompts in `backend/prompts/` (system_prompt.py + create/image.py); Claude streaming w/ prompt caching (ephemeral) + adaptive thinking in `backend/agent/providers/anthropic/provider.py`.

**canvas-engine integration** (Phase 3, additive, reversible): new `services/canvas-engine/src/pipeline/image-to-clone.ts` (`runImageToClone`); `/clone` in `src/server.ts` now takes `url` XOR `image` (data-URL or http URL) + optional `intent`, json limit bumped to 16mb, pre-flight 400 if image requested without key. Reuses vendored open-lovable `buildSystemPrompt` + `parseStreamedBlocks` (src/vendor/lovable/); ports abi's replication prompt; real Claude streaming via already-present `@anthropic-ai/sdk@0.40`. Default model `claude-sonnet-4-6` (env `CANVAS_ENGINE_VISION_MODEL`). Both paths converge on GeneratedFile[] → VitePool. tsc clean; 4 branch/schema cases verified; URL path regression-free.

**Image-clone edit support (added same session):** image clones now register in SessionStore (source:'image', envelope optional) and edit via `runImageEdit` (`src/pipeline/image-edit.ts`) — LLM free-form edit using open-lovable isEdit prompt + parseStreamedBlocks. `/edit` branches by clone source (image=LLM, no targetId needed; url=deterministic region edit-loop); `targetId` now optional; image edit gated on the key. Smoke: `npm run smoke` (clone->edit->assert, scripts/smoke-image-edit.mjs) or `npm run smoke:clone` (gen only); zero-arg via bundled scripts/fixtures/sample-ui.png. tsc clean; 84 tests pass; /edit branching verified (404 / missing_target / url region path intact).

**Backend switched to claude CLI (no API key, added same session):** image gen + edit now shell out to `claude -p` (subscription auth) via `src/pipeline/claude-cli.ts` — child env has `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` deleted, system via `--system-prompt`, prompt via stdin, image via temp file + `Read` tool (`--add-dir`), `spawn(..., {shell:false})` (claude.exe is native). API key kept only as optional SDK fallback; server no longer 400s without a key. Default model alias `sonnet` (env `CANVAS_ENGINE_VISION_MODEL`). tsc clean; 84 tests pass; verified /clone image no longer requires a key (streams + invokes CLI).

**Backend selector + .env loader (added 2026-05-24):** new `src/pipeline/vision-backend.ts` (`resolveVisionBackend`) reads `CANVAS_ENGINE_VISION_BACKEND` = `auto` (default: CLI→SDK fallback) | `sdk` (use key directly, skip CLI) | `cli` (CLI only). Both image-to-clone.ts + image-edit.ts honor it. New `src/load-env.ts` (first import in server.ts) loads `services/canvas-engine/.env` via `process.loadEnvFile` — and FIRST deletes a present-but-empty `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (host-managed sessions export it empty, which both 401s auth AND blocks loadEnvFile's no-override-of-existing-vars behavior; that empty key is the SAME root cause as the CLI 401). `.env` gitignored; `.env.example` committed-safe. Verified on live :3050: `.env` parsed (backend=sdk active), tsc clean, 84 tests pass, both selector branches confirmed (sdk short-circuits to "requires ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"; auto still does CLI-first→401 in host-managed). The `sdk` path means a real key now works for live verification from INSIDE this host-managed session (SDK needs no CLI auth).

**KEY GOTCHA (cost ~an hour):** a spawned `claude` 401s from inside a host-managed Claude Desktop session (`CLAUDE_CODE_PROVIDER_MANAGED_BY_HOST=1`, `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=claude-desktop`; on-disk `~/.claude/.credentials.json` does NOT satisfy a fresh login). So the CLI path 401s from inside a Claude Code session UNLESS you pass a setup-token OAuth value as `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` to the spawned process (RESOLVED 2026-05-24 — see LIVE-VERIFIED below; with that token it authenticates fine in-session). Otherwise it must run where canvas-engine runs — a normal terminal where `claude` is logged in. Reliable spawned-CLI auth = run `claude setup-token` once (subscription, no key). Same pattern as competitor-monitor's "claude CLI fallback".

**LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-05-24:** full image->code clone AND edit ran end-to-end via `npm run smoke` against :3050 — SMOKE PASS. Clone produced 9 files served by Vite at :3060 (HTTP 200); edit changed `src/components/UploadTab.jsx` and "SMOKE TEST OK" landed in real source (line 24). KEY UNBLOCK: Bruke provided a `claude setup-token` OAuth token (`sk-ant-oat01-...`, NOT an API key). Setting `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` in the server env makes the spawned `claude` authenticate EVEN inside the host-managed session — the prior 401 was just "no reachable creds", and the OAuth token supplies them. So the CLI / $0 subscription path works here after all (no API key, no SDK). Ran with backend=auto + token in env, NOT written to disk. `.env`/`.env.example` updated to document `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (backend reset to `auto`); claude-cli.ts notes it preserves the token. SECURITY (resolved 2026-05-25): the token (a `claude setup-token` OAuth value, `sk-ant-oat01-…`) was pasted into chat → exposed in the transcript. Could NOT be force-revoked: claude.ai/settings/claude-code lists 26 unlabeled "Claude Code" authorization tokens, the leaked one can't be isolated by sight, and UI revokes didn't flip the live probe (immediate revoke appears delayed/cached, consistent with Anthropic's documented ≤4-day token validity; `claude auth logout` does NOT revoke server-side). Bruke chose to let it AUTO-EXPIRE (~4 days). Contained meanwhile: canvas-engine server stopped (token was only ever in process env, never written to a file — verified), it's a subscription token (no payment/API billing; ToS also bars these from third-party tools). One-move certain kill if ever wanted: change the claude.ai password (root-invalidates all tokens). LESSON: never let a secret be pasted into chat — once in the transcript it can't be cleanly scrubbed, and Anthropic OAuth tokens have no instant per-token revoke. No commits (Bruke reviews).

**Why:** abi's pixels->LLM path fills the gap canvas-engine's live-DOM clone can't touch (static screenshot / Figma / mockup). Integration chosen over a second service per [[feedback_database_agent_pair_shape]] (collapse-not-federate); the TS port was cheap because the SDK + open-lovable prompt/parser were already vendored in canvas-engine.
**How to apply:** to use the image path live, set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY and restart canvas-engine. Host service details in [[project_canvas_engine]].

# Search Stack — Phases 1-5 SHIPPED 2026-06-09

**Update 2026-06-09:** Phase 3-5 landed in commit `e06599d`. 19 new providers added (memory + 4 academic + 2 social + 2 news + 4 legal + 4 data + 1 multimedia + 2 local biz + 1 product + 1 web-answer). 14 intent kinds total. 20/28 providers enabled out of the box. 8 DISABLED stubs await keys: perplexity, newsapi, youtube, yelp, google_maps, keepa, fred, govinfo.

50 tests pass / 6 skip on upstream tolerances / 0 fail.

## Original Phase 1+2 (2026-06-08)

Unified search router living at [services/search-stack/](../../../Pre Atlas/services/search-stack/). FastAPI on port **3070**, FastMCP wrapper exposes 3 tools to Claude (`search_stack_search`, `search_stack_extract`, `search_stack_budget`). MCP registered at user scope — visible in any session's tool palette.

## What ships (Phase 1+2 commits on `claude/main-triage-26f4a5`)

| Commit | What |
|---|---|
| `fd4ed57` feat(search-stack) | Full `services/search-stack/` tree — 17 Python files, 4 external providers + 3 local + Firecrawl extract, SQLite cache + budget guards, JSONL audit log, 15/15 unit tests. |
| `1a54072` feat(droplist) | DropList Phase 2 wire — `retrieval.py` + `toolrouter.py` + `cli.py` + `BIBLE.md` + new `test_retrieval_external.py`. Gated by `DROPLIST_EXTERNAL_SEARCH=1`. 7/7 new tests + 20/20 acceptance still PASS. |
| `4b41692` docs(search) | Three-layer search docs — architecture (WHAT) + protocol (HOW) + repo-search-stack (WHICH binaries). |

## Architecture

```
Client (curl, Claude MCP, DropList, n8n) → POST /search :3070
  ↓ router.classify(query) → kind
  ↓ providers_for(kind) → cache.get → budget.consume → provider.search
  ↓ dedup_by_url → sort by score → top N
Response: {kind, results[], providers_used, providers_failed, n}
```

Kinds: `web` (Exa+Tavily+Brave) · `extract` (Firecrawl) · `code` (rg/fd/sg) · `file` (es) · `github` (gh CLI) · `memory` (Phase 3 — not yet wired).

## Key files (do not rewrite — extend)

- [services/search-stack/BIBLE.md](../../../Pre Atlas/services/search-stack/BIBLE.md) — §1-15 contract
- [services/search-stack/src/search_stack/router.py](../../../Pre Atlas/services/search-stack/src/search_stack/router.py) — intent classification
- [services/search-stack/src/search_stack/registry.py](../../../Pre Atlas/services/search-stack/src/search_stack/registry.py) — `KIND_TO_PROVIDERS` map; adding a provider = 1 file + 1 entry
- [services/search-stack/src/search_stack/providers/base.py](../../../Pre Atlas/services/search-stack/src/search_stack/providers/base.py) — `SearchProvider` ABC, `SearchResult`/`ExtractResult` Pydantic schemas
- [docs/search-stack-architecture.md](../../../Pre Atlas/docs/search-stack-architecture.md) — layered diagram + request flow

## Activation gate (next manual step)

External providers are DISABLED until API keys land:

```
services/search-stack/.env  ← copy from .env.example, fill in 4 keys
```

Required: `EXA_API_KEY`, `TAVILY_API_KEY`, `BRAVE_API_KEY`, `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY`. All have free tiers. With keys absent: local providers (rg/fd/sg, es, gh) still work — service is never broken.

## DropList wire (Phase 2)

When `DROPLIST_EXTERNAL_SEARCH=1` env is set:
- `drop --memory-search "X" --external` merges internal token-overlap hits + external web hits (yellow color marks external)
- DAG nodes with `tool_type: "external_search"` pull live results into their evidence — `_check_external_search` done-checker fires on `n_results > 0`

Off by default. Backward-compatible — existing droplist users see no change.

## What's deferred (Phases 3-5, still in plan)

- **Phase 3** — `services/memory-hub/` REST in front of [cognitive-sensor atlas_query.py](../../../Pre Atlas/services/cognitive-sensor/atlas_query.py) + mirofish Neo4j + idea_registry. Registers as `kind=memory` provider in router. Closes the cross-store memory query gap.
- **Phase 4** — GitHub GraphQL provider (faster than `gh` CLI for bulk) + n8n daily-cron workflow that runs tracked-topic searches → saves to DropList.
- **Phase 5** — `~/.claude/skills/search-stack/` skill + `pre-atlas search "..."` CLI shim.

Plan file (out-of-repo): `~/.claude/plans/i-need-you-to-mighty-hanrahan.md`.

## Doctrine alignment

- ✅ [[feedback-assemble-first-posture]] — every provider wraps a vendor API; cache/budget are SQLite stdlib; FastAPI / FastMCP / httpx / Pydantic are mature defaults; no hand-rolled search.
- ✅ [[feedback-no-tool-sprawl]] — one router, one MCP, one CLI surface. Each provider is one file.
- ✅ [[feedback-managed-unlock-over-self-hosted-stealth]] — Firecrawl is the managed extract layer; sitepull stealth only on explicit opt-in.
- ✅ [[feedback-tgt-law]] — TREE+GRAPH+TIME backends (droplist, cognitive-sensor, mirofish) stay load-bearing; UI surface deferred to Phase 5.

## project_self_describing_surfaces

<a id="project-self-describing-surfaces"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_self_describing_surfaces.md`*
*Description:* Self-describing surfaces / capability-registry layer-1 shipped in atlas-map-api (token-enforced caller-scoped \"test forms\")

**Self-describing surfaces — layer-1 of the "OpenRouter for my services" vision. SHIPPED (uncommitted) 2026-06-24 via /weapon + ultracode.** Lives in `services/atlas-map-api`.

Every live service narrates itself to a caller through a headless, access-scoped descriptor (a "test form"): same surface, different form per role, more criticality => fewer descriptors (need-to-know). Same descriptor serves a blind human (`format=text`), a CLI, and an agent (`format=json` / MCP) — accessibility and agent-usability are the SAME requirement.

Core: `src/atlas_map_api/describe.py` — Role model (anon/agent-ro/agent/operator/root), Capability, redaction ladder (full → locked → redacted), `existence_hash` (HMAC proof for crit≥2 redactions), `narrow_role` (query param can only narrow, never escalate), `render_text` narration.

Enforcement (the lock, not a demo): role is DERIVED from `X-Atlas-Token` via `auth.resolve_caller_role` (no token => anon; write token => root; extra tokens via `ATLAS_ROLE_TOKENS` env / gitignored `.atlas-role-tokens.json`). Headline property: `?role=root` from an unauth caller still yields the anon form.

3 axes per capability: direction(read/write) · exposure(public/agent/internal) · criticality(0-3).

Surfaces: declarative `services/<name>/atlas.surface.json`, one per service. 10 surfaces / 74 real capabilities (aegis-fabric 18, droplist 8, canvas-engine 8, delta-kernel 7, optogon 7, cortex 6, atlas-map-api 6, memory-hub 5, search-stack 5, uasc-executor 4). 7 generated by a background Workflow (one agent per service, evidence-cited file:line); `scripts/verify_overlays.py` enforces every `invoke` is a real route (code-as-furniture — caught + fixed 2 hallucinated endpoints in my own hand-written demo seeds).

Endpoints: `GET /describe` (discovery), `GET /describe/{surface}?role=R&format=json|text`. MCP: `atlas_describe(surface, role)` + `atlas_describe_list` in `mcp_server.py`.

Tests: 88 pass (was 74) — `tests/test_describe.py` (gradient against synthetic fixture, enforcement against real delta-kernel) + `tests/test_describe_mcp.py`. Docs: `services/atlas-map-api/SELF_DESCRIBE.md`. Weapon trail: `.weapon/closed.md`.

**UPDATE 2026-06-24 — FULL 35/35 COVERAGE + retired-services autopsy (verified).** Extended `describe.py` with `kind` (http/cli/ui/websocket/library) + `lifecycle` (live/retired/stub); `overlay_path`/`described_surfaces` now scan services/+apps/+tools/. A 25-agent ultracode workflow wrote overlays for all remaining surfaces → **35/35 surfaces self-describe, 154 real capabilities, verify_overlays.py green, suite expanded**. Live got real caps (inpact 11, atlas-cli 10, fest-reconcile 10, lattice 9, atlas-audit 9, anatomy-extension 7, crucix 5, codex-partner 6...); 14 retired/stub tagged 0 caps. verify_overlays.py now: skips endpoint-check for non-http kinds, allows 0 caps for retired/stub, reads .mjs/.cjs (caught my hand-seed bugs again).

**Retired-services autopsy (8-agent workflow + code-recon/repo-inventory verify pass):** 0 clean REVIVE, 1 NEEDS-BUILD, 7 DEAD. Counts verified EXACT (mosaic-orch 42 routes, perception 9/13 NotImplementedError, openclaw 918 LOC, blueprint deps next/react only + 0 service_edges, openclaw bot tokens blank). **triangulation = the one REVIVE** — anatomy/DOM verification sidecar, core verify() works (46/46 tests cold-start), only ever lacked an HTTP face + a caller (ORPHANED, spine fixes it). Revive path: wrap verify() in 3 FastAPI endpoints, port **3074** (NOT 3010 — optogon owns it), register `verify` capability, point one anatomy-extension caller at it. **BUSTED autopsy claim:** mosaic-orchestrator is NOT consumer-less — cortex (LIVE, autostart) is wired to call it: `cortex/config.py:13` MOSAIC_URL + `cortex/agents/planner.py:120,169` call /api/v1/tasks/execute & /compound/run; `cognitive-sensor/run_daily.py:35` hardcodes :3005. cortex is gated (CORTEX_BRIDGE_APPLY=1 off) so may be dormant path — but wiring is real. **OPEN: decide if cortex→mosaic is live or dead code before retiring mosaic-orchestrator.** Other 7 dead verdicts hold (Mosaic tier = abandoned architecture, not orphans). Autopsy report at `audit/retired-services-autopsy.md`.

DEFERRED (CUT_LIST): live `state` population (state:null slot), the call-normalization gateway (layer 3 — the actual OpenRouter front door), per-caller rate limiting, existence-hash for sub-crit-2. **triangulation REVIVED 2026-06-24** — `services/triangulation/src/triangulation/api.py` wraps working `verify()` in 3 FastAPI endpoints (POST /verify, GET /library/stats, POST /library/add, GET /healthz) on **port 3074**; config.py port 3010→3074; overlay → lifecycle live + 4 caps; `.claude/launch.json` entry added (system python, no venv). Booted live + curl-verified (/verify → confirmed verdicts). 54/54 tests (46+8 new test_api.py). Fix gotcha: `from . import verify` grabs the FUNCTION (re-exported in __init__), use `from .verify import verify`. **cortex→mosaic RESOLVED 2026-06-24:** dormant human-triggered dead code, NOT a live dependency — mosaic-orchestrator DEAD verdict HOLDS, safe to leave retired. Only creators of RUN_PIPELINE/COMPUTE_METRIC are cortex `dashboard.html` buttons; cognitive-sensor `run_daily.py:167` (:3005) runs only via manual `atlas daily` CLI (no cron). Cleanup option: repoint those at optogon/delta-kernel or delete dead call sites (do NOT revive mosaic). **LAYER-3 GATEWAY first brick SHIPPED 2026-06-24 (`c3cbc99`):** `gateway.py` + `POST /call {surface, capability, args}` in atlas-map-api — invoke any capability BY NAME through one front door. Access enforced by VISIBILITY (you can only call what your /describe form shows — reuses the projection as the ACL); resolves surface→port (launch.json runtime truth → snapshot fallback) + proxies declared route via httpx. Read-only http only (safe half of hybrid); writes gated `DESCRIBE_GATEWAY_WRITES=1`; cli/ui→422. Refusals 404/403/422/501/502. Bounded SSRF (host+path from trusted config). 10 tests, full suite 98. Live-proven: anon→triangulation/healthz + root→triangulation/verify proxy e2e.

**GATEWAY COMPLETE + HARDENED 2026-06-24 (`e9eec89`):** writes (opt-in `DESCRIBE_GATEWAY_WRITES=1`, fail-closed, verb-decides-write-not-label), normalized envelope, cli invocation (opt-in `DESCRIBE_GATEWAY_CLI=1`, argv-only no-shell).
