## project_shardstate_atlas_layer

<a id="project-shardstate-atlas-layer"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_shardstate_atlas_layer.md`*
*Name:* shardstate atlas layer
*Description:* shardstate audit + coordination layer is shipped in atlas/. Two operators (agent_local, agent_chat) write through atlas.db, lex-larger wins concurrent ties, doctrine drift refuses tick.

shardstate library has been given a purpose and shipped as the audit + coordination layer for the atlas/ substrate. Two atlas/ commits land it: `6550de2` (steps 1-7 + 3.5: helpers, mirror, drift gate, rebuild) and `4426cd4` (step 8: two-operator sync, AC6 conflict log, MCP_SETUP.md). Library spec on shardstate branch at commit `a7a0dbf`. Atlas integration spec in Pre Atlas worktree at commit `9f7b8f3`. 29 tests, 9 of 9 ACs satisfied.

**Operator pair:**
- `agent_local` — local `agent.py tick / decide / update` writes through `_mirror_to_store`
- `agent_chat` — Claude Project via shardstate-mcp stdio server (set up per atlas/MCP_SETUP.md, requires user-side claude.ai Settings → Connectors action)
- Lex-larger wins concurrent ties: `agent_local > agent_chat`, so local writes prevail in races. Loser preserved in `conflicts` table.

**Why:** atlas was previously two operators sharing files with no handshake — chat writes could be silently clobbered, no decision provenance, doctrine drift produced silent rule changes. shardstate gives time-travel reads, hash-pinned decisions, mechanical drift detection, deterministic conflict resolution.

**How to apply:**
- `atlas.db` is gitignored, regenerable via `python agent.py shardstate rebuild`. AC8 holds: rebuilt state_root matches live tick state_root.
- Every record in decisions.jsonl now carries `state_hash` + `state_root` + `doctrine_hash`. To replay any past state: `store.get("state", state=<state_root>)` or `GET /api/state/at/<state_root>`.
- After editing any `doctrine/*.md`, run `python agent.py doctrine accept` or `tick` will refuse with `doctrine_drift_detected`.
- After heavy chat writes, `python agent.py sync` pulls them into state.json (also runs automatically at the start of every tick).
- shardstate is an OPTIONAL dependency. If `pip uninstall shardstate`, `agent.py` runs exactly as before with no audit fields. AC9 enforces this.

**Files referenced:**
- atlas/agent.py — helpers in shardstate-layer block (_open_store, _mirror_to_store, _ensure_static_entities, _current_doctrine_hash, _sync_from_store) + cmd_doctrine, cmd_shardstate, cmd_sync
- atlas/serve.py — `/api/state/at/<root>`, `/api/decisions/by-hash/<root>` (mine, deferred · awaiting your hallway-endpoints commit to land first)
- atlas/MCP_SETUP.md — Claude Project connector setup
- atlas/tests/test_shardstate_layer.py — 29 tests
- shardstate/SPEC.md — library spec on `claude/shardstate-coordination-ZxEgN` branch
- (Pre Atlas worktree) SHARDSTATE_SPEC.md — atlas integration spec on `claude/magical-jang-7f9762`

**Storage shape (verified from source 2026-05-01):**
- Two SQLite tables: `nodes(content_hash PK, content_json, created_at)` and `entities(stable_id PK, current_hash, vclock_json, updated_at)` (`store.py:165-172`)
- All values pass through `canonical_json()` at `hashing.py:17-24` — uses `json.dumps(allow_nan=False)`. **Binary blobs are NOT supported.** PNG/binary payloads must be base64-encoded (33% inflation, no streaming, no partial reads). For visual frame storage, prefer cell-grid JSON (e.g. [carbo-shrink](project_carbo_shrink.md) output) over raw PNG.
- Three operations: `put`, `patch`, `append`. All synchronous, single calls. SQLite WAL provides atomicity.

## project_shot2code

<a id="project-shot2code"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_shot2code.md`*
*Description:* shot2code — deterministic screenshot→code SaaS; Slice 0 (IR→TSX core) shipped, vision front-end next

**shot2code** (working name, rename freely) — a screenshot→code product built as a
**deterministic compiler**, not an LLM blob. Repo: `C:\Users\bruke\shot2code` (own git, no remote yet, uncommitted as of 2026-06-26).

**Search-first verdict (the field):** [[feedback_assemble_first_posture]] — one project owns this category: `abi/screenshot-to-code` (73k★, MIT, FastAPI+React, vision-LLM). Everything else is tiny clones. emilwallner's 16k★ is the OLD 2017 Keras/pix2code research, not modern. So a custom build only earns it via a wedge.

**The wedge (Bruke chose, recommended):** deterministic IR pipeline. `screenshot → Layout IR → deterministic codegen` instead of abi's flat `image → LLM → code-blob`. The Codia insight: flat images have no hierarchy/reproducibility; the IR gives one. Determinism IS the moat → passes assemble-first condition #2.

**Provenance:** vision front-end = borrow abi's `backend/codegen`+`prompts` (commodity). Determinism doctrine = ported from [[project_anatomy_saas_compiler]] (`C:\Users\bruke\anatomy-saas\packages\compiler`, `@anatomy-saas/compiler`). NOTE: anatomy-saas runs the OPPOSITE arrow (TSX→diagram, an *analyzer*) — NOT a drop-in codegen lib. Reuse = the doctrine + Babel toolchain + byte-identical discipline, not the modules.

**Architecture:** `screenshot → [VISION: Claude structured output → IR, zod-validated] → Layout IR (moat) → [COMPILER: pure, LLM-free, byte-identical] → React+Tailwind .tsx → [optional POLISH]`.

**Slice 0 = DONE:** `src/ir.ts` (zod IR + closed style-token vocab), `src/tokens.ts` (deterministic tokens→Tailwind, fixed emission order), `src/emit.ts` (pure IR→TSX serialiser), `src/index.ts` (`compile`/`compileFromJson`), `src/cli.ts`. 5 vitest tests pass (golden via `toMatchFileSnapshot` + key-order-independence + JSX-escape + reject-malformed). `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Justified hand-roll:** no Prettier in emit path — a Prettier dep makes goldens churn on version bumps (makes determinism *worse*, not just later). Babel/ts-morph deferred until a slice needs AST transforms.

**NEXT = Slice 1 (vision):** Claude vision + structured output → `LayoutIR`, validate with `LayoutIRSchema`, hand to `compile()`. No new core code, just an IR producer. Then Slice 2 = optional LLM polish layer.

## project_sigil_substrate_codec

<a id="project-sigil-substrate-codec"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_sigil_substrate_codec.md`*
*Name:* sigil-symbolic-substrate-codec
*Description:* New standalone tool/project at C:\Users\bruke\sigil\ - content-addressable symbolic substrate codec. Brick 1 (zstd dictionary compression + PNG pixel carrier round-trip) SHIPPED + verified byte-identical. The convergence point of PNG-Substrate + glyph-CAS + delta-scp into ONE named format + a tool factory.

# sigil - content-addressable symbolic substrate codec

**Location:** `C:\Users\bruke\sigil\sigil.py` (standalone sibling of `binre`, OUTSIDE the Pre Atlas repo on purpose).

## Origin / what Bruke actually wants
Started 2026-06-26 from a fuzzy ask ("perception + compiling + reverse-compiling", "JIT and AOT tools", "pngs and pixels", "turn big codes into little macro codes", "dictionaries and indexes and references", "protocol or language - haven't decided"). De-jargoned, the real goal is a **tool factory**: use Claude Code to build + package fast, standalone, agent/human-accessible tools. The recurring theme is a **content-addressable symbolic substrate** - big content squeezed to small symbols via a shared dictionary, each symbol a hash/index reference, carried in an alternative skin (pixels in a PNG).

This is NOT new ground for Bruke - it's the convergence of three shipped projects:
- [[project_png_substrate_seed]] - PNG-as-carrier, 5 patterns shipped (lookup, bytecode VM, telemetry, content-address integrity, FSM). KEY LESSON honored: PNG-as-database = KILLED (0.6x vs SQLite); pixels win as a portable INDEX/CARRIER, not primary store.
- [[project_minidocs_glyph_cas]] - content-addressable store w/ shared dictionary (Leptonica + FAISS + DINOv2).
- delta-scp - repo -> small symbolic map (dictionary compression for LLM context).

## Decisions made
- **Protocol vs language: START as a protocol/format**, grow the language later. The "language" half already exists as a prototype (PNG-Substrate pattern #2, the c110 bytecode VM); bolt it on only where execution is needed.
- **Assemble-first stack** (no hand-rolled compressor): `zstandard` (dictionary compression + `train_dictionary`), `Pillow`+`numpy` (pixel carrier, RGB no-alpha so it survives re-encoders), `hashlib` (content-address), `Nuitka` (AOT -> standalone .exe; the factory's packaging step).

## SGL1 container format (defined in sigil.py docstring)
`magic(4) ver(1) flags(1) dict_id(4) orig_len(8) sha256(32) comp_len(4) | zstd frame`. Self-delimiting via comp_len so PNG pixel-padding is harmless. Two carriers, one format: `.sgl` (raw bytes) or `.png` (bytes painted to RGB).

## Status - Brick 1 SHIPPED + verified (2026-06-26)
CLI subcommands: `pack` (--png, --dict), `unpack`, `dict-train`, `info`.
Proof: packed sigil.py (7347 B) -> 2506 B (2.93x) -> 30x30 PNG -> unpack -> **SHA-256 byte-identical PASS**.

## PACKAGED + PUBLISHED + FINISHED (weapon mission CLOSED 2026-06-25)
- git repo at `C:\Users\bruke\sigil\` (branch **master**), remote **github.com/lionestenzol/sigil (PRIVATE)**.
- Commits: `a5ce66c` v0.1.0 -> `0b2357c` docs+CHANGELOG (this is where `ancestor/` got committed) -> `dc91271` test+harden (this weapon mission). **`dc91271` PUSHED to origin/master** (Bruke approved 2026-06-25); branch in sync.
- `pyproject.toml` -> setuptools console_script -> **`sigil` on PATH**; reinstalled **editable** (`pip install -e .`) so the command now tracks the repo source.
- **WEAPON mission (`.weapon/closed.md`, 6/6):** added `test_sigil.py` (5/5 PASS - container+PNG round-trips byte-identical, dict round-trip with **-80% payload over 128 similar records**, wrong-dict guard, dict-train guard). **FIXED** dict-train crashing with an opaque zstd traceback on too-little data (now guarded + fails loud). **FIXED** `roundtrip.py` (was a byte-for-byte copy of sigil.py; now the real worked pack->png->read->unpack example the README references). README Tests section + dict-train troubleshooting; CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]`; `.weapon/` gitignored.
- CORRECTION to earlier note: `ancestor/` is **TRACKED** (committed in `0b2357c`), and there were **2** commits not 1 - the prior "ancestor untracked, 1 commit a5ce66c" note was stale.

## Next steps (in order)
1. **dict-train demo** - ✅ DONE + verified: `test_sigil.py` trains a dict over 128 similar records, packs with `--dict`, measures **-80% payload** vs no-dict (the literal "dictionaries and indexes and references"). dict-train guarded against too-little-data crash.
2. **Package to `sigil.exe`** - ✅ DONE (outside this session) via pip/setuptools console_script; `sigil.exe` on PATH. OPTIONAL next: `nuitka --onefile` for a zero-Python single-binary.
3. **Generalize the factory** - lift the binre shape (one CLI + one JSON receipt + installable skill + atlas-map registration) into a repeatable "idea -> Claude writes it -> Nuitka packages it -> registered" pipeline.

## Located prior-art primitives (disk sweep 2026-06-26) - sigil's pluggable carriers
- **png-substrate spec + 5 conformance-tested patterns** - worktrees `trusting-germain-a57cb0\specs\png-substrate` + apps in `lucid-bohr-24156a\apps` (c121-png-calc=lut.v1, c110-png-vm=vm.v1 THE language layer, c130-png-integrity, c140-png-fsm) and `trusting-germain-a57cb0\apps`. These ARE sigil's carriers/patterns; absorb don't reinvent.
- **routing-png - SHIPPED, in main tree** `services\delta-kernel\src\core\routing-png\routing.lut.png` - mode-router compiled to a 220-byte PNG, 1458/1458 parity. The production proof.
- **LSH-index-in-PNG** `tests\png-substrate-scale\embed_swap\` (lsh_index_png_xpoly.py etc.) - 84k embeddings -> PNG index, 3.23x speedup. Compact-index carrier (the proven WIN; PNG-as-DB was KILLED).
- **JSONtoPNG.py** `C:\Users\bruke\Documents\SavedFiles\JSONtoPNG.py` - rough one-off: stores video edges/motion in PNG metadata ("Encoded Video") + RIFE frame-interp to reconstruct video. A metadata-carrier variant.
- **ST3GG (F5 steg)** `C:\Users\bruke\tools\ST3GG` - hide data inside an existing image; covert carrier option.
- **idea seeds** `Documents\SavedFiles\002 Novenary Convo.txt` (base-9 encoding), `003 Pixel Convo.txt`.
- **UASC-M2M** `research\uasc-m2m\` (in every worktree) -> promoted to `services\uasc-executor\` :3008. A compact machine-to-machine COMMAND token protocol (@WORK/@BUILD/@DEPLOY/@CLEAN/@WRAP/@CLOSE_LOOP/@SEND_DRAFT, HMAC-signed). Sibling of the "protocol / alternative comms" theme but for COMMANDS, not data-encoding.
- **OPEN lanes (nothing of Bruke's on disk):** QR (add `qrcode` lib = robust visual carrier, ~30 min) and Parallax (undefined; candidate = layered/multi-plane depth encoding). Both are net-new for sigil.

## DIRECT ANCESTOR found in conversation corpus (2026-06-26)
sigil is not new - it's the 16-months-later return of an idea Bruke built in early 2025.
- **Conversation #215 "PNG-based Calculator Overview"** dated **2025-02-25**, verdict MINE, 188 msgs / 598K chars / **281 code blocks** (longest 153 lines). Harvested at `services\cognitive-sensor\harvest\215_png-based-calculator-overview\` (summary.md, key_quotes.md, code_blocks.md, final_output.md, manifest.json). His own words (msg 1): "How the PNG Calculator Works - the core concept is that this calculator uses a pre-generated [PNG]...". Topics: execution / symbolic / intelligence.
- **The "something else based" he forgot = NOVENARY (base-9).** The project was an HTML calculator using **RGB PNG + Novenary (base-9) encoding**. Confirmed in `Documents\SavedFiles\002 Novenary Convo.txt` (base 9, Novenary-Based, calculator, PNG, HTML all co-occur) and `001_Keylogger convo.txt` (Novenary-based).
- Sibling corpus ideas in `cognitive-sensor\IDEA_REGISTRY.md`: #63 PNG-based Calculator Overview, **#233 Novenary Code Research Plan** (started, 2 sessions), #616 PNG calculator spec.
- **So sigil's DNA = alternative number base (novenary) + pixel carrier (RGB PNG) + a working HTML tool**, which Bruke prototyped in Feb 2025 and re-derived from scratch this session. The actual HTML lives un-extracted in `215_.../code_blocks.md` (0 of 281 blocks are in any repo - never committed). **EXTRACTED 2026-06-26 to `C:\Users\bruke\sigil\ancestor\`**: `png-calculator.original.jsx` (verbatim React, block 1) + runnable self-contained `png-calculator.html` (embedded base64 lookup PNG, vanilla JS, runs by double-click) + `build_png_calculator.py` (regenerates both). Scheme = PNG-substrate **lut.v1**: pixel(x,y) R=x+y G=x-y, pixel(x+100,y) R/B=x*y in 2 bytes; reader recovers sign of subtraction. Lookup self-check PASS 500/500.
- **delta-state**: no disk artifact (es filename sweep, 3 variants, empty). Likely refers to delta-kernel's state model (`services\delta-kernel\src\core\types.ts`, the 6-Mode system), not a separate project.

## project_sitepull_tier4_plan

<a id="project-sitepull-tier4-plan"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_sitepull_tier4_plan.md`*

# Sitepull · Tier 4 Behavioral Defeats · SHIPPED (2026-04-26)

## Status: SHIPPED 2026-04-26
`--humanize` flag plumbed end-to-end through `bin/sitepull.mjs` → `lib/audit.js` → `lib/browser.js`. Uses ghost-cursor 1.4.2's `path()` for bezier mouse paths + inline `overshoot()` port + multi-step decelerating scroll + jittered timing (1500-3500ms post-load wait, 200-800ms pre-nav delay). Cursor position tracked across pages. 35% of hops include overshoot.

### Verification numbers (creepjs.abrahamjuliot.github.io + bot.sannysoft.com)

| Mode | sannysoft pass/fail | creepjs like-headless | creepjs headless | HeadlessChrome leaks | composite stealth |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| baseline | 21/9 | 88% | 67% | 3 | 18.9/100 |
| --stealth | **31/0** | 56% | 33% | 2 | 48.1/100 |
| --stealth --humanize | **31/0** | 56% | 33% | 2 | **48.1/100** (identical) |

**Empirical finding (matches codex's prior diagnostic):** creepjs scores are dominated by fingerprint signals (UA, WebGL, Canvas, Audio, Timezone, Headless markers). Mouse-behavior delta is **zero** on creepjs. The 7-repo `phantom-input` audit would not have moved this number — its premise (mouse-shape is the bottleneck) is empirically not true on this target.

### What `--humanize` actually defeats
Not creepjs. Where it WOULD show up: **WAFs that explicitly score behavioral signals** (Akamai BMP, DataDome, PerimeterX). Creepjs measures static fingerprint, not behavior. A real Akamai-protected target would be the right test, but those don't expose scores publicly.

### Failed creepjs metric vs goal
Plan's stated target: `creepjs trust score >0.7`. Best achieved: 0.481 (creepjs composite), gap is fingerprint-side (HeadlessChrome UA leaks 2x, headless markers still detectable). Mouse-side is maxed for this target.

### Real-crawl smoke
`node bin/sitepull.mjs https://example.com --browser --stealth --humanize --out ./.tmp/humanize-smoke --no-smoke-test` → completes in 117s, recon+detect+probe+crawl+vendor all green. The 117s is humanize x 40 probe endpoints; future optimization could gate humanize to crawl phase only.

### Code changes
- `bin/sitepull.mjs`: `humanize` in BOOLEAN_FLAGS + opts + help text
- `lib/audit.js`: destructure + thread to both `makeBrowserFetcher` calls + warn-if-no-browser
- `lib/browser.js`: `humanize` param, `walkPath()` + `overshoot()` + `humanScroll()` helpers, jittered pre-nav and post-load waits, lazy-load ghost-cursor only when humanize is on
- `package.json`: `ghost-cursor: ^1.4.2` in devDependencies
- `scripts/bot-test.mjs`: rewritten with creepjs target, headless-detection signal extraction, `.tmp/bot-test-results.json` cross-run history

### Did NOT break
- `--stealth` alone (sannysoft still 31/0)
- `--record` (untouched)
- `--browser` POST/PUT path (uses APIRequestContext, no goto)

### Followup deferred (see project_phantom_input_audit_plan.md)
The 7-repo `phantom-input` audit is **not the next move.** Empirical data shows mouse-shape isn't the bottleneck on creepjs. If a future real-target failure correlates specifically with mouse signals (not fingerprint), THEN run the audit — but focused on the failing dimension.

---

## Real-WAF probe (2026-04-26 follow-up, 4 targets × 3 modes)

`scripts/waf-test.mjs` hits 4 single-GET targets across 3 modes. Results:

| Target | WAF | baseline | --stealth | --stealth --humanize |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DataDome homepage | DataDome (own) | 403 BLOCKED (1.4KB stub) | 403 | 403 |
| Vinted homepage | DataDome customer | 200 SERVED (2.2MB real) | 200 SERVED | 200 SERVED |
| Hermes homepage | DataDome customer (mislabeled Akamai initially) | 200 SERVED + DD script embedded | 200 SERVED | 200 SERVED |
| Imperva homepage | Imperva (own) | 200 SERVED | 200 SERVED | 200 SERVED |

**Verdict on --humanize value: zero observable benefit on these targets.** Every target produced identical responses across all 3 modes. Either we got in (3/4 sites) or we got blocked at the edge (DataDome's own page; same blocking signal in all 3 modes — TLS / IP / header-level rejection that flags before behavior is observed).

**What this proves and doesn't:**
- ✓ `--humanize` doesn't break anything — sannysoft 31/0 holds, real sites still served real content
- ✓ `--stealth`'s value is real but localized to fingerprint-level WAFs (sannysoft, basic webdriver checks)
- ✗ We could not reproduce a scenario where behavior matters — DataDome blocks before mouse moves; customer sites tolerate first GETs
- ✗ Multi-page crawl scenarios (where DD's embedded scoring kicks in over 5-50 pages) NOT tested. That's where --humanize would theoretically earn its keep, but testing it cleanly requires consent or a sandboxed target

**Honest assessment:** `--humanize` is now flag-shipped, plumbed through, doesn't regress anything. Its value remains theoretical until a real multi-page-crawl scenario surfaces where baseline gets challenged and humanize doesn't. Don't promote it as "defeats DataDome" — say "available for behavioral-WAF scenarios when one arises."

`scripts/waf-test.mjs` and `.tmp/waf-results.json` retained for re-runs against future targets.

### Verdict-classifier known issue
The current `scripts/waf-test.mjs` classifier flags "CHALLENGED" any time block-marker substrings (e.g. `captcha`) appear, but real homepages contain `captcha-delivery.com` script tags as embedded session monitoring (not as a challenge served). Better classifier: combine `<title>` content + size + actual challenge stub patterns (e.g. `var dd={'rt':'c'`) before deciding.

---

## Path B follow-up · residential-proxy 3-mode test (2026-04-26)

Created BrightData residential zone via Chrome MCP automation. Zone gave us a Burkina Faso residential IP (Orange ISP, ASN 37577). Ran waf-test through it in all 3 modes against the same 4 targets.

| Target | baseline-proxy | stealth-proxy | stealth-humanize-proxy run 1 | stealth-humanize-proxy run 2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DataDome.co | 403 (1.4KB stub) | 403 (1.4KB stub) | **200 (426KB real homepage!)** | ERROR (timeout) |
| Vinted | 200 (2.1MB, served) | ERROR | 403 (7KB block) | 200 (2.1MB, served) |
| Hermes | 200 (598KB) | 200 (597KB) | 200 (597KB) | 200 (597KB) |
| Imperva | 200 (289KB) | ERROR | ERROR | 429 (rate-limited) |

**Headline finding (positive but unconfirmed):** stealth+humanize+residential proxy on run 1 served DataDome.co's real homepage (426KB, title `DataDome | Bot Management & Agent Trust Platform`, 134 real-page markers) where baseline-proxy and stealth-proxy got the 1.4KB challenge stub. Title + content + zero `var dd=` stub markers confirm it was the real page, not a different challenge.

**Why we can't claim "humanize defeats DataDome":**
1. **Run 2 didn't reproduce.** Same flag combo, different IP rotation (BrightData rotates per request), the result was a network error.
2. **Confound: IP quality varies.** Each request through the residential zone gets a different residential IP from BrightData's pool. We can't isolate "humanize made it work" from "that IP just happened to be cleaner."
3. **N=1 success vs N=1 error is not statistical evidence.** Would need ~5+ runs per condition to claim signal.

**Honest read:** the test gave us ONE positive data point that --humanize has SOME signal when stacked with stealth + clean IP. Not strong enough to advertise as a feature, but consistent with the theory that ML-WAFs combine many signals (mouse + fingerprint + IP) and humanize is one input that can tip the score.

**For sitepull users:** if hard WAFs are the target, `--via brightdata` is deterministic and proven (50s, ~$0.005, real homepage). `--stealth --humanize --proxy <residential>` is cheaper but flakier and value is per-run unpredictable.

### Cost
~$0.40 of residential bandwidth used across the 3 runs (per BrightData $8/GB pay-as-you-go).
Zone: `residential` (Pay as you go, $8/GB), customer ID `hl_7983c3a5`. **Bruke instructed to revoke after session.**

# Original plan below

# Sitepull · Tier 4 Behavioral Defeats · Plan (2026-04-26)

## Context
Tiers 1–3 already shipped (see `project_openscreen_lifts.md`). Tier 4 = behavioral analysis: the WAF watches HOW you interact, not just WHAT requests you send. This is what catches `--stealth` runs even when `navigator.webdriver` is undefined.

## What tier 4 actually checks

```
SIGNAL                          DETECTION                  TYPICAL CHECKER
─────────────────────────────   ─────────────────────────   ────────────────────
mouse never moves                no mousemove events        Akamai, DataDome
clicks land on dead-center       always (X+W/2, Y+H/2)      PerimeterX
no scroll before click           page.click without scroll  Cloudflare bot mgmt
timing is robotic                exact 2500ms after load    Kasada
no focus/blur events             no input focus before nav  PerimeterX
no input typing rhythm           paste-only or <50ms gaps   Akamai BMP
identical session-to-session     same fingerprint always    DataDome ML
```

## What sitepull does today (post --stealth)
- Opens a page with `goto(url)`
- Waits `waitForLoadState('load')` + `waitForTimeout(2500)` (FIXED 2.5s, robotic)
- Reads `page.content()` — never moved the mouse, never scrolled, never clicked
- Closes page, opens next
- 100% predictable timing

Visible signals to a tier-4 WAF: zero mouse events, zero scroll, zero focus, identical 2.5s think time on every page. Trivially flagged.

## Strategy

```
APPROACH                   COVERS                    EFFORT     RISK
─────────────────────────  ────────────────────────  ─────────  ──────
A · jitter timing only     timing                    1h         low
B · + ghost-cursor mouse   timing + mouse paths      3h         low (needs npm dep)
C · + scroll + idle wait   above + scroll + idle     1d         medium
D · + click before nav     above + click events      2d         medium-high
                                                                (clicks may
                                                                 trigger nav!)
E · session warmup         above + history of normal 1w         high
                           pageviews                            (full UX shift)
```

Recommended: **A + B + C** as `--humanize` flag. D and E reserved for "I really need to scrape Cloudflare" cases.

## Implementation plan (A + B + C)

### Step 1 · Add `--humanize` flag
- `bin/sitepull.mjs`: new boolean flag in `BOOLEAN_FLAGS`, threaded through opts
- `lib/audit.js`: pass `humanize` to `makeBrowserFetcher`
- Help text: "simulate human mouse + scroll + variable timing (slows crawl)"

### Step 2 · Jittered timing (A)
- `lib/browser.js`: replace `await page.waitForTimeout(2500)` with `await page.waitForTimeout(1500 + Math.random() * 2000)` (1.5–3.5s range)
- Add a small random pre-navigation delay (200–800ms) so requests don't fire at exact same intervals
- ~10 lines

### Step 3 · Ghost-cursor mouse (B)
- `npm install --save-dev ghost-cursor` (works with Playwright via wrapper)
- After `page.goto` + `waitForLoadState`:
  - `await ghostCursor.move(page.mouse, randomViewportPoint())`
  - 2–4 random destinations across the viewport
  - Each move uses bezier interpolation with realistic acceleration
- ~30 lines + 1 dep

### Step 4 · Scroll behavior (C)
- After mouse jiggle:
  - `await page.mouse.wheel(0, 200)` (small initial scroll, like reading)
  - Pause 400–1200ms
  - Maybe one more scroll, smaller
  - Then read `page.content()`
- ~15 lines

### Step 5 · Verify
- Add a behavioral-detection target to `scripts/bot-test.mjs`. Candidates:
  - `https://abrahamjuliot.github.io/creepjs/` (most thorough public bot test, scores behavioral too)
  - `https://nopecha.com/demo/cloudflare` (real Cloudflare challenge, true negative if it loads the protected page)
  - `https://datadome.co/cookies/` (DataDome's own demo)
- Compare baseline vs `--stealth` vs `--stealth --humanize`. Target: creepjs trust score >0.7.

### Step 6 · Performance budget
- A + B + C adds ~3-5s per page (mouse paths + scroll + idle). On the HN crawl (8 pages) that's an extra 30s.
- Acceptable if --humanize is opt-in. Document it.

## Out of scope (defer until needed)
- **D · click before nav** — easy to break: clicking a random `<a>` triggers actual navigation, derails the crawler. Would need to filter to `[role="button"]` with no href + non-navigation handlers. Hard to do reliably.
- **E · session warmup** — pull a few benign pages first to build cookie history. Significant time cost, only useful for very aggressive WAFs.
- **TLS fingerprint (uTLS / curl-impersonate)** — different attack surface, defeats Cloudflare bot mgmt at a different layer. Worth exploring SEPARATELY if --humanize alone isn't enough.
- **Residential proxy integration** — `--proxy http://user:pass@host:port` already works in Playwright. Just document it in the README; no code change needed for tier 4.

## Files that will change
| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `web-audit/bin/sitepull.mjs` | Add `--humanize` to BOOLEAN_FLAGS + opts |
| `web-audit/lib/audit.js` | Plumb `humanize` to `makeBrowserFetcher` |
| `web-audit/lib/browser.js` | New `humanize` option in fetcher; jittered timing + mouse + scroll between `goto` and `content()` |
| `web-audit/package.json` | Add `ghost-cursor` dependency |
| `web-audit/scripts/bot-test.mjs` | Add creepjs target + tri-state comparison |

## Verification path
```
node scripts/bot-test.mjs                              # baseline
node scripts/bot-test.mjs --stealth                    # tier 1+2 (existing)
node scripts/bot-test.mjs --stealth --humanize         # tier 1+2+4 (new)

→ all 3 should produce HTML at .tmp/bot-test-*.html
→ then add creepjs to bot-test.mjs and run again
→ target: failed-checks count keeps dropping monotonically
```

## Estimate
~4-6 hours of focused work for A+B+C. Most risk is in ghost-cursor compatibility (last published 2023, may need patches for Playwright 1.45+).

## project_sitepull_via_brightdata

<a id="project-sitepull-via-brightdata"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_sitepull_via_brightdata.md`*
*Name:* sitepull --via brightdata · SHIPPED Phase 1
*Description:* Managed-fetcher backend for sitepull. Phase 1 SHIPPED 2026-04-26 (BrightData Web Unlocker via REST). Verified end-to-end with a 203-page crawl of DataDome.co. Phase 2 (Apify/Firecrawl/ScrapingBee) deferred.

# Sitepull · `--via <provider>` · Phase 1 SHIPPED 2026-04-26

## Status
**SHIPPED 2026-04-26** — same session that shipped --humanize. Pivot was fast: --humanize had no observable delta on real direct-IP WAF probes, while BrightData Web Unlocker defeats DataDome.co (the wall --humanize couldn't crack) on every call. Reframe held: sitepull's product is vendoring + replica generation + anatomy mapping — outsource the fetch.

## Headline result · 203-page crawl of DataDome.co
Ran `node bin/sitepull.mjs https://datadome.co/ --via brightdata --out ./.tmp/via-brd-datadome --no-smoke-test` (no probe/crawl skipping flags) and let it run to completion:

| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Pages crawled | **203 HTML pages** (depth 0-2 across the site) |
| Assets vendored | **4,949 files** (591.5 MB total) |
| Recursive CSS/JS asset pass | +91 files (1.1 MB) |
| Wall time | **70 minutes (4178.7s)** |
| Cost | ~$1 of BrightData Web Unlocker credits (~200 successful unlocks × $0.005) |
| Output | `./.tmp/via-brd-datadome/app/{server.js, package.json, public/*}` |

What was captured: blog posts, threat-research articles (`/threat-research/ai-traffic-report/`), product pages (`/agent-trust-management/...`), images, scripts, stylesheets — DataDome's full marketing footprint, runnable offline via `node server.js`.

This is the empirical proof that the architecture works at scale, not just on a single smoke-test page.

## Smoke-test (single-page) numbers for reference

| Approach | DataDome.co | Time | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| direct fetch | 1,464 bytes (challenge stub, 403) | ~2s | $0 |
| --browser --stealth --humanize (direct) | 1,464 bytes (same stub) | ~5s | $0 |
| --via brightdata (1 page, --no-probe --max-pages 1) | **438,200 bytes (real WP homepage)** | 50s | ~$0.005 |
| --via brightdata (full crawl, depth 2) | **203 pages · 591.5 MB · 4,949 assets** | 70 min | ~$1 |

Direct CLI corroboration: `brightdata scrape https://datadome.co/ --format html` returns the real WP homepage (3,582 lines, `wp-theme-bubble` markers).

## Architecture (as shipped)

| Layer | File | Behavior |
|---|---|---|
| CLI flag | `bin/sitepull.mjs` | `--via <provider>`, `--via-zone <name>`, `--via-country <code>` |
| Dispatch | `lib/audit.js` | When `via` is set, replace fetcher entirely. Warn if `--browser/--stealth/--humanize/--proxy/--record` also set (provider does the fetch — those become no-ops) |
| Provider | `lib/via-providers.js` (NEW · 143 LOC) | `makeViaFetcher(provider, opts)` dispatcher. BrightData branch reads creds from CLI config (`%APPDATA%\brightdata-cli\credentials.json` on Windows / XDG / macOS prefs). POSTs to `https://api.brightdata.com/request` with `{zone, url, format: 'raw'}`. Wraps response in sitepull's `{status, ok, headers, text, arrayBuffer}` shape |
| Test harness | `scripts/waf-test.mjs` | `--via <provider>` mode for cross-target comparison |
| Sibling flag | `lib/browser.js` | `--proxy http://[user:pass@]host:port` plumbed through Playwright `launch({proxy})`. Sets `ignoreHTTPSErrors: true` on context when proxy is set (residential proxies MITM HTTPS) |

## Decisions made
1. **Default zone**: `cli_unlocker` (auto-created by `brightdata login --api-key <key>`). Web Unlocker mode. ~$0.005/successful request.
2. **Conflict semantics**: `--via` overrides everything. Warning logged. Empirically right: provider's stack handles WAF.
3. **Cost guardrails**: NOT shipped. Web Unlocker only charges on success; trial $5 ≈ 1000 unlocks. Defer flag-based caps until someone burns a real budget.

## Apify Phase 2 · still deferred
Apify's actor model (URL → run actor → poll → get structured data) doesn't slot in as a simple drop-in fetcher. Phase 2 would wire `apify-client` (already in `web-audit/devDependencies`) + a generic "give me HTML" actor. Useful for sites with prebuilt actors (Amazon, LinkedIn, Insta) where you'd otherwise write the parser. Complementary to BrightData, not a replacement.

## Files touched
- `bin/sitepull.mjs` — added `--via`, `--via-zone`, `--via-country` flags + help text
- `lib/audit.js` — destructure `via/viaZone/viaCountry`, override-everything-else when `via` is set
- `lib/via-providers.js` — NEW (BrightData REST + Apify placeholder)
- `lib/browser.js` — added `parseProxyUrl()` helper + `ignoreHTTPSErrors: true` when proxy set
- `scripts/waf-test.mjs` — added `--via <provider>` mode
- `scripts/bot-test.mjs` — added `--proxy <url>` mode
- `package.json` — added `apify-client` and `ghost-cursor` to devDependencies
- Globally installed: `@brightdata/cli@0.1.8`, `apify-cli@1.4.1`

## Auth state (this session)
- BrightData: `brightdata login --api-key <key>` ran successfully. Auto-created zones `cli_unlocker` (Web Unlocker, trial) + `cli_browser` (Browser API, trial). Trial balance was $7.00; ~$1 spent on the 203-page crawl.
- Apify: `apify login -t <token>` ran successfully. Account: `dazzling_palace`.
- Residential zone: created via Chrome MCP automation (claude-in-chrome). Customer ID `hl_7983c3a5`. Used for the Path B residential 3-mode test in [project_sitepull_tier4_plan.md].
- **All tokens were pasted in chat — Bruke instructed to rotate after session.**

## Out of scope (deferred)
- Multi-provider routing across BrightData/Apify/Firecrawl/ScrapingBee
- Cost guardrails (`--max-cost`, page count caps)
- Caching layer for repeated fetches
- Custom country/region routing beyond `--via-country`
- These wait for actual usage data.

## project_skill_pytools

<a id="project-skill-pytools"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_skill_pytools.md`*
*Name:* project-skill-pytools
*Description:* Python ports of the code-recon / search-stack / groundwork skill toolkits — standalone, assemble-first; sibling to (NOT same as) groundwork-cli

**skill-pytools** at `C:\Users\bruke\skill-pytools\` — standalone Python versions of the tools inside three Claude Code skills, each folder self-contained/movable (carries its own `_common.py` harness). Built in a parallel session 2026-06-25 from Bruke's ask: "use es to itemize every claude skill I have, make a python version of each tool in my /code skills, put them all in a folder." Verified firsthand via README + tree (not git-checked for commit state).

**Three folders, each with per-tool modules + a RUNNER:**
- `code-recon/` — the recon ladder ported tool-by-tool: `es eza tokei fd rg` (discover/orient/find/grep), `sg semgrep` (structural/rule scan, **wrap-only**), `jq yq ctags bat delta tree_sitter`, `orient.py` (Step-0 delta-scp skeleton freshness gate, port of orient.mjs), and `recon.py` (RUNNER: doctor·orient·locate·trace·audit·prior-art).
- `search-stack/` — `router.py` (kind decoder), `client.py` (urllib client for the :3070 service), `kinds.py` (handlers + offline fallbacks for file/code/extract only), `search_stack.py` (RUNNER).
- `groundwork/` — `delta_scp.py` (orient), `code_recon.py` (DELEGATES via subprocess to `../code-recon/recon.py`), `fest.py` (wraps native fest), `groundwork.py` (RUNNER: full·verify·drift).

**Doctrine (assemble-first, made explicit):** (1) prefer the real binary via subprocess; (2) pure-Python fallback for common cases when the binary is missing (never hard-fail); (3) NO faking — `sg`/`semgrep` are wrap-only and degrade with a clear message vs fabricating. Force fallback with `--no-binary` / `SKILL_PYTOOLS_NO_BINARY=1`; `--json` on any tool gives a `Result` envelope. Honest fallback limits documented (es root-scoped not machine-wide; jq/yq only simple paths; recon is lexical not type-aware).

**Relationship to [[project-groundwork-cli]] (DON'T confuse / don't rebuild either):** both make the `/code-recon`+`/fest` pipeline standalone Python, but different shape. **groundwork-cli** = a packaged `gw` CLI that turns a repo into `system-map.html` + `plan.md` (lifts Pre Atlas's `build_system_index.py`+`_build_map.py`); the *map+plan product*. **skill-pytools** = the recon *toolkit* ported per-tool (the ladder itself as runnable Python + search-stack + a groundwork conductor). groundwork-cli is the deliverable map/plan; skill-pytools is the reusable tool library. They can converge later (gw could shell out to skill-pytools' recon for edges) but are not duplicates.

## project_sourcegraph_fit_system_map

<a id="project-sourcegraph-fit-system-map"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_sourcegraph_fit_system_map.md`*
*Description:* Where Sourcegraph actually fits in Atlas — above zoekt, above already-generated coarse import edges; fills symbol-level xrefs, NOT dependency graph

Question that came up 2026-07-24: "how does Sourcegraph fit with the system-map?" I first answered from priors that Sourcegraph would auto-derive edges and kill hand-curated manifest drift. That framing was **wrong** — recon corrected it.

**Ground truth (verified 2026-07-24):**

- `atlas-manifest.yaml:2-15` — GENERATED FILE, `python audit/build_atlas_manifest.py`. Mechanical facts (ports, routes, LOC, deps, seams, contracts) auto-re-derived from `audit/system-index.json` + `atlas-map.json` + `.claude/launch.json` + route sweep. Curated bits (UI calls, store shapes, roles) in `audit/manifest-overlay.yaml`. See [[project_atlas_manifest_yaml]].
- Import graph per service already extracted: `audit/imports/{aegis-fabric,cortex,delta-kernel,cognitive-sensor,…}.json` via `audit/imports/py_imports.py` (Python AST) + madge-shape for JS/TS.
- Zoekt live + `sym:` symbol search over Pre Atlas + pre-atlas + STRUDEL — see [[project_zoekt_windows_native]] and [[project_atlas_rag_context_engine]].

**So the coarse "who imports whom" graph is already automated.** Sourcegraph would NOT fill that; the manifest already has it.

**The actual Sourcegraph-shaped gap** (per your own doc `docs/archive/2026-07/SHIP_READINESS_AND_MARKET_2026-06-25.md:102`): "atlas-map … No language-aware code-intel; single-repo; no refs/defs; vs free Sourcegraph/Zoekt." Zoekt gives symbol *search* (`sym:`); it does not give **cross-repo xrefs / callers / def-ref navigation**. The manifest knows "cortex imports delta-kernel"; it does not know "line X in cortex/src/foo.ts calls `WorkController.commit` in delta-kernel/src/…". THAT is Sourcegraph's actual seam.

**Fit for future reference:**
- system-map = coarse edges (module-level imports, routes, ports, contracts) — already generated, keep as-is
- zoekt = text search + best-effort symbol search — already live
- Sourcegraph = fine edges (symbol xrefs, callers, def→ref jumps across repos) — the tier that neither of the above supplies

Not competing views of the same data — different resolutions of the same dependency graph.

**Why this memory exists:** to prevent me re-answering the same question from the "manifest is hand-curated" prior on future turns. See [[feedback_argued_from_priors_not_evidence]] — this was a live instance of that failure caught by /code-recon.

## project_spine_topology_verified

<a id="project-spine-topology-verified"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_spine_topology_verified.md`*
*Name:* project-spine-topology-verified
*Description:* First-hand code-recon of \"the spine\" (2026-06-22) + the triage→droplist keystone wire that was built to close it

First-hand `code-recon` (no subagents) of the 7 spine seams on 2026-06-22. The real topology is ~80% wired but NOT shaped like the arrow-sketch ("delta-kernel → intake → droplist → cycleboard → inPACT → atlas"). Verified with file:line:

**What's actually wired (✅):**
- **intake chainer → packet/DAG** — `droplist/server.py:148` POST /api/drop → `intake.py:33 chain_intake` → `engine.process_drop`. Tested `test_intake.py`.
- **droplist → delta-kernel → lattice** (the real "to lattice" path) — `droplist/graph_engine.py:236 _maybe_emit_atlas_signal` (after every settle) → `atlas_signal.py` POST `/api/signals/ingest` → delta-kernel signals-store → `lattice-projection.ts:204 isDroplistSignal` → `apps/lattice` loadViewmodel. **DORMANT unless `DROPLIST_ATLAS_SIGNALS_URL` is set** (`graph_engine.py:34`). One env var activates it.
- **inPACT ↔ cycleboard** — shared state via delta-kernel `/api/cycleboard` (`apps/inpact/cli/inpact.ts:96`).
- **inPACT → atlas** — `atlas-map-api/server.py:386 GET /items` → `items.py:118 inpact_items` reads `apps/inpact/projects.json`. The unifier `items.py:141-143` read-aggregates droplist DAGs + `cycleboard thread_cards.json` + inpact (only droplist is writable).

**Corrections to the sketch:**
- "delta-kernel → droplist intake" is **reversed** in reality (droplist emits TO delta-kernel). `rg "api/drop"` → no external caller; only droplist's own server + test.
- "droplist → cycleboard" has **no direct wire** — they only meet at the atlas-map `/items` read layer.

**The one real gap (❌) — now CLOSED:** loops/auto-triage → droplist. `auto_triage.py:36` targeted only `OPTOGON_URL :3010`; nothing reached droplist. **Built 2026-06-22:** `services/cognitive-sensor/droplist_bridge.py` (`drop()` — env-gated by `DROPLIST_DROP_URL`, fail-soft, composes existing intake, no new classify/store logic) + wired as **Rung 4** in `auto_triage.py` (parallel to the Cortex Rung 3). Speaks the intake contract `{rawInput}`. Tests `test_droplist_bridge.py` 6/6 green; auto_triage imports clean. Dormant until `DROPLIST_DROP_URL=http://localhost:3073/api/drop` is set. Uncommitted.

**Two MORE latent gaps found+fixed 2026-06-22 (commit `7640010`), via live e2e:** (1) intake/DAG were disjoint — `/api/drop`→`engine.process_drop` built a packet and stopped; the lattice signal only fired from `graph_engine.run_graph` (CLI-only). Split into `run_graph_from_packet`; `intake.chain_intake` now settles the secured packet (env-gated `DROPLIST_ATLAS_SIGNALS_URL`, fail-soft). (2) `atlas_signal.emit_signal` sent NO auth but delta-kernel ingest requires the aegis tenant key → silent 401. Added `_delta_auth_header()` (`.aegis-tenant-key` / `DROPLIST_ATLAS_TOKEN`). **CHAIN NOW LIVE-PROVEN**: POST /api/drop → DAG settle → authed ingest (202) → lattice `viewmodel.items` (provenance=droplist). NOTE: viewmodel response is `{ok, viewmodel:{items,...}}` — items are NESTED. NOTE: e2e left a few test packets ("Verify the spine end to end: spinetest-*") in `services/droplist/data` — harmless, dedup'd, removable.

**GO-LIVE 2026-06-22 (weapon mission):** spine now proven LIVE on the real :3073 (not just lab). Killed stale 06/20 droplist (PID 41556, was serving pre-/api/drop code), restarted on current code; `DROPLIST_ATLAS_SIGNALS_URL` arming persisted in `.claude/launch.json` droplist entry (commit `96e4a56`). Live proof: drop_3f23448bd6b8 → lattice viewmodel item (provenance=droplist). NOTE: the live :3073 was started ad-hoc this session; for durability let the normal launch.json autostart own it (now armed). PR experiment→main HELD (85-commit branch, user deferred). Triage-side `DROPLIST_DROP_URL` still unset (set it in the run_daily/triage env to light the triage→droplist half).

**To make the loop live:** set both env vars — `DROPLIST_DROP_URL` (triage→droplist) and `DROPLIST_ATLAS_SIGNALS_URL` (droplist→lattice). ## project_st3gg_png_substrate_pairing

<a id="project-st3gg-png-substrate-pairing"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_st3gg_png_substrate_pairing.md`*
*Name:* st3gg-png-substrate-pairing
*Description:* Intent + verified topology for pairing ST3GG (covert steg) with PNG-Substrate (overt PNG-as-program VM), binre, and a new JIT/AoT compiler.

Bruke wants to pair the ST3GG steganography toolkit with his code tooling — a **JIT/AoT compiler** he's building, the [[project_binre]] RE pipeline, and [[project_png_substrate_seed]] — to "make really cool things." Verified 2026-06-25.

**Key correction — these are NOT compression and they are NOT the same thing:**
- **ST3GG** (`~/tools/ST3GG`, see [[project_st3gg_install]]): hides data **covertly** in carrier LSBs; output is ~carrier-sized (expansion, not compression — demoed: 53-byte payload → 561 KB stego). CLI path air-gapped (no network in steg_core/cli/crypto/analysis_tools/injector/app); AI-agent/OpenRouter lives ONLY in index.html + webui.py. Broader than PNG: 5 modalities (image/text-unicode/audio/network-pcap/docs), 54 detectors, 95 example files. The `st3gg` skill only documents the PNG path.
- **PNG-Substrate**: a **shipped spec family** (NOT just a "seed") for PNG-as-computational-substrate. 5 patterns: `lut.v1`, **`vm.v1`** (programs in `.prog.png`, fixtures fib/collatz), `trace.v1`, `fsm.v1`, `integrity.v1`. Data lives in **visible R/G/B pixel values** (A=validity); overt + documented ("anything that reads PNGs can read it"). Explicitly NOT a language/runtime/AGI-substrate, and **"compression beyond PNG" + crypto are out of scope**.

**Topology (all in `Pre Atlas\.claude\worktrees\`, likely UNMERGED — verify before relying):**
- spec: `trusting-germain-a57cb0\specs\png-substrate\` (README + per-pattern .md + conformance vectors)
- impls: `lucid-bohr-24156a\apps\` — `c110-png-vm`, `c121-png-calc`, `c140-png-fsm` (NOT yet read)
- scale tests: `heuristic-buck-35f73f\tests\png-substrate-scale\` (embed_swap, channel-survival)

**The fusion (grounded):** ST3GG and PNG-Substrate are opposites on the same medium — overt (substrate) vs covert (steg). They STACK (hide a `.prog.png` inside an innocent carrier → extract → run on png-vm), but ST3GG only earns inclusion where **concealment** is the requirement; if you just want "runnable code in a PNG," vm.v1 already does it and steg is not needed (no false symmetry). Compiler could target vm.v1 as an AoT backend; binre reverses the visible grid. Compression (zstd) is a separate layer BEFORE either substrate.

**SECURITY:** decode→compile/execute = arbitrary-code-injection channel (the exact "code smuggling via image" surface ST3GG demonstrates). Gate decoded payloads as untrusted data; never auto-run.

**vm.v1 ISA (VERIFIED from `lucid-bohr-24156a\apps\c110-png-vm\gen_program.py` + `.weapon/spec.md` M4) — this is the compiler's AoT target:** 1 pixel = 1 instruction, row-major in an NxN grid. `R=opcode, G=arg1, B=arg2, A=255 (recommended, not required post-M4)`. 4 registers R0-R3, 8-bit wrap (&0xFF). Opcodes: HALT=0, SETI=1, MOV=2, ADD=3, SUB=4, PRINT=5, DEC=6, JNZ=7, JZ=8, MOD=9, DIV=10, MUL=11. **R==255 = padding sentinel** (skip; pad cell RGBA=(255,0,0,0)) — M4 change so programs survive alpha-strip/JPEG (program lives in pixel VALUES, robust to recompression). JNZ offset = signed(arg2-128). Reference VM = `index.html` (browser). **CONSTRAINT:** vm.v1 survives JPEG (overt pixel values); ST3GG LSB steg is DESTROYED by JPEG (PNG-only) — so concealing a `.prog.png` via ST3GG LSB loses vm.v1's recompression robustness unless you use ST3GG F5/DCT mode. Pick robustness(substrate) vs concealment(steg), or F5 for both. Doc gap: spec README links `vm.v1.md` but that file does NOT exist.

**GROUNDWORK FINDING (2026-06-25, verified 3-angle):** The JIT/AoT compiler does **NOT exist on this machine** — confirmed by (a) worktrees-by-mtime, (b) active-festivals scan, (c) machine-wide compiler/codegen/lexer/jit/aot filename + content search (only deps/system/screenshot-to-code noise). Today's 3 newest worktrees (recursing-newton/crazy-benz/silly-ardinghelli) are **identical Atlas-repo clones** spun by background agents (same byte-sizes), NOT a compiler. So the compiler is either a Claude web/remote chat (not on local disk) or not-yet-started. PNG-Substrate is a **self-disclaimed DEMO** ("not a programming language, not a runtime") with toy VMs (4x4 fib / 5x4 collatz); **all 5 per-pattern spec .md files are missing** (only README + impls + conformance exist). NET: the compile→substrate→RE→steg pipeline is 3 real-but-DISCONNECTED pieces (ST3GG, binre, png-substrate demo) + a missing keystone (compiler); nothing is wired; the pairing is aspirational, not in-progress-on-disk.

**RESOLVED 2026-06-25 — "the compiler" = STRUDEL, and it's a DIFFERENT DOMAIN.** Bruke's "compiler in another chat" is `C:\Users\bruke\STRUDEL` (github.com/lionestenzol/strudel) — his fork of **Strudel, the TidalCycles-in-JS music live-coding language** (JS/TS pnpm monorepo, ~55 packages, 122k LOC JS). It is NOT a machine-code compiler. It ALREADY has a full compiler stack: front-ends `mini`/`mondo`(DSL→js)/`transpiler`(live-eval JIT)/`node-engine`(graph→code)/`hs2js`(Haskell→js) → IR `symbolic-ir` → `deterministic-compiler` (structured IR→validated Strudel code; files compileToStrudel.ts/executionEngine.ts/normalizeIR.ts/preflight.ts/ir.schema.json) → `deterministic-runtime` (browser/Node/REPL/CLI targets) + audio engines (superdough/webaudio/csound). So **JIT already = transpiler+eval; AOT already = deterministic-compiler + node-engine + WAV export.** ⚠️ **llvmlite/Cranelift/LLVM advice was WRONG for this** — those are native-codegen backends; STRUDEL compiles music IR→JS/audio. TWO "compiler" threads got conflated in conversation: (T1) general code-perception + machine-code compile + binre RE + ST3GG/PNG-substrate [binary domain]; (T2) STRUDEL music-DSL compiler [already mature]. llvmlite belongs only to T1 IF T1 is about native code. NEXT: ask Bruke which thread he's pushing; if STRUDEL, find the ACTUAL gap (the compiler exists).

**Open gaps / next recon:** (1) RESOLVED above — compiler = STRUDEL (music DSL, mature). (2) Read `c110-png-vm` impl to know the real vm.v1 byte encoding before planning a compiler→substrate→steg seam. (3) Confirm whether PNG-Substrate worktrees are committed anywhere. Then groundwork Stage 2 (fest) is plannable. TODO: update [[project_png_substrate_seed]] (under-describes it as a "seed") and [[project_st3gg_install]] (add air-gap + breadth facts).

<a id="project-stack-integration-seam"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_stack_integration_seam.md`*
*Name:* stack-integration-seam

# Stack integration seam — connect-first audit verdict (2026-06-26)

Connect-first, claim-verified audit (verified-audit shape via custom Workflow; 83 agents, 74 load-bearing claims: **56 verified / 17 partial / 1 busted**, well under the 40% halt gate, 0 security busted). Report committed at repo root: `Pre Atlas\STACK_INTEGRATION_AUDIT_2026-06-26.md` (commit on `feat/atlas-setup-ui`). Goal: wire [[project_binre]] + [[project_sigil_substrate_codec]] + delta-scp + code-recon/groundwork + groundwork-cli/skill-pytools + ST3GG into ONE pipeline (perceive -> compile -> carry). STRUDEL excluded (separate music domain); PNG-Substrate = promote-or-kill (stranded demo).

The "2 failed" in the run = 2 of 83 subagents hit HTTP 429 in the parallel **verify** burst (claim-verifiers on secondary keystone claims), NOT recon agents — coverage intact, report trustworthy (fail-safe pipeline drops a failed verify to null, never injects an unverified claim).

## KEYSTONE = CAN (load-bearing, verified file:line)
atlas-map `/call` (+ MCP `atlas_call`) already shells out to a LOCAL CLI: `gateway.call_capability` -> `_invoke_cli` -> `subprocess.run(argv, cwd=<surface dir>, shell=False, timeout=20s)`, returning `{stdout,stderr}` in the normalized envelope. Hardened: opt-in behind `DESCRIBE_GATEWAY_CLI=1` (fail-closed 501 when unset), argv-only with `--` sentinel + leading-dash rejection (no flag injection), `shutil.which` abs-path. Registration = per-surface `atlas.surface.json` overlay declaring `kind:cli` + an invoke string. Evidence: `services/atlas-map-api/.../gateway.py:247-277 / 138-160 / 311-315`, `mcp_server.py:318-342`; live overlay on disk. **=> wiring the stack is ASSEMBLY (register overlays), NOT building a shell-out adapter.** Relates [[project_atlas_gps_substrate]] [[project_self_describing_surfaces]].

## Join key = sigil SGL1 sha256
Computed over ORIGINAL bytes, enforced on unpack (ValueError on mismatch); `container_info()` is the clean read-only minting fn. The ONLY sound content-address in the fleet. binre also content-addresses (`out/<sha256-of-binary>/report.json`). delta-scp (UUID id), code-recon (timestamp+git-diff), groundwork-cli, ST3GG (CRC32) all LACK content-addressing and must CONSUME sigil's sha as the join key rather than mint their own.

## SEAM #1 FOUNDATION — SHIPPED 2026-06-26 (commit `a79c620` on `feat/atlas-setup-ui`)
The connective tissue is built + proven live, NOT just designed:
- **`services/atlas-map-api/src/atlas_map_api/seam.py`** — `Receipt` (pydantic v2, frozen, `seam.v1`) `{seam_version, tool, sha256, produced_at, status, data, error}` + `Receipt.from_envelope(env, *, produced_at, sha256)` that maps the gateway envelope/refusal into ONE shape and lifts sigil's sha256 as the join key. Only justified hand-roll.
- **gateway change** — `build_argv` now does `{param}` POSITIONAL substitution (mirrors the http path-param convention) so positional-arg tools are reachable; `declared_params` scans the whole invoke. **This corrects an audit over-optimism:** the cli path was NOT assembly-ready — it only did trailing `--key value`, so `sigil info -- --input X` fails argparse (verified empirically). All arg-safety invariants preserved + added an exe-must-be-literal guard. **security-reviewer: 0 critical/high** (3 LOW, 1 closed).
- **`POST /seam/call`** (server.py) — always returns one Receipt (refusal/fail/ok folded into `status`).
- **`tools/sigil/atlas.surface.json`** — first real `kind:cli` surface (info=read, pack/unpack=write-gated).
- Proven LIVE against the real `sigil.EXE`: join key `9f17f0f2…` lifted through the gateway (not an HTTP-200). Full atlas-map-api suite **157 passing**. Gate is opt-in: set `DESCRIBE_GATEWAY_CLI=1` to use the cli path.

### Carry direction PROVEN + record correction (2026-06-26, commits `4cd83fc` sigil / `1b646d5` atlas-map-api)
**CORRECTION (verify-discipline):** the prior note "only `sigil info` prints JSON to stdout" was WRONG (read the source: `sigil.py` `cmd_pack`/`cmd_unpack`/`cmd_info` all `print(json.dumps(...))` a receipt carrying `sha256`). So sigil's WHOLE lifecycle (pack=perceive-side write, info=read, unpack=carry-side write) is already seam-ready — the join key is on stdout in BOTH directions. The write capabilities write a FILE *and* print the receipt; the seam reads the receipt.
- **Shipped:** symmetrized sigil's `unpack` receipt (`4cd83fc`) so the carry side reports the same content-address metadata as pack (`container_bytes`/`ratio`/`dict_id`/`uses_dict`), not just `{sha256,bytes,verified}`. New CLI test in `test_sigil.py` (6/6). Proven LIVE: `pack`→`unpack` both print `sha256:31ae3c42…`, round-trip byte-identical.
- **Anchored at the seam:** `test_seam.py::test_lifecycle_receipts_chain_on_one_join_key` (`1b646d5`) — pack/info/unpack receipts all lift the SAME join key through `Receipt.from_envelope`. atlas-map-api **158 passing**.
- Net: sigil is DONE for the seam (3 capabilities, both directions). The real gap is the OTHER tools, which genuinely lack stdout JSON.

### FAN-OUT SHIPPED — 4 of 5 tools wired + proven LIVE (2026-06-26, autopilot+ultracode)
Read-only recon Workflow (5 scouts, file:line scopes) → solo build (ST3GG is an injection channel → my eyes) → live gateway proof per tool → adversarial review → review-fix. Commits: Pre Atlas `d5f86f0`+`56ec8fd`; groundwork-cli `22705c0`+`28bbdcc`; ST3GG `9b3b4fc`. **atlas-map-api 158 + seam suite 20 passing.**

Overlay `kind:cli`, invoke `python tools/binre/report.py {target}` (path RELATIVE to repo_root = the gateway cwd; mirrors `tools/codex-partner`). **KEY CORRECTION:** recon claimed cwd=overlay-dir — WRONG; `_invoke_cli` does `cwd = repo_root/sub.path else repo_root`, and binre has no `sub` (not in launch.json) → cwd=repo_root. Proven live: carrier `d9f03a50…`. Thematic: a sigil-rendered PNG fed straight into ST3GG analyze.

Receipt `{verdict,action,map,tokens,sha256}`. Proven live: STRUDEL map → `ce99987f…`. (The separate `~/.claude` hygiene — commit untracked orient.mjs + SKILL.md, optionally add a native sha256 field — is still open but NOT needed for the seam; the wrapper sidesteps it.)

### Perceive-stage recon engines ALSO wired (2026-06-26, `b424c54`) — the user named /code-recon /groundwork /repo-inventory as perceive
Same binre wrapper pattern (Pre Atlas adapter over a `~/.claude` skill engine, NO skill edits):
- **code-recon** → `orient_seam.py` (above).
- **repo-inventory** → `tools/repo-inventory/inv.py` wraps `~/.claude/skills/repo-inventory/scripts/inventory.py` `analyze()`; content-addresses the canonical inventory (absolute `root` excluded → path-stable). Receipt `{sha256,system_count,total_files,total_code_lines,systems}`. Proven live: `cba0d776…`.
- **groundwork** → its deterministic engine IS `gw` (groundwork-cli), ALREADY wired (`22705c0`). The `/groundwork` SKILL is the agentic wrapper (delta-scp→code-recon→fest), not a single headless surface — nothing to wire. Resolves backlog #4's two-things-named-groundwork: `gw`=seam surface, skill=orchestrator.
KEY LESSON: these "perceive tools" are SKILLS, not CLIs; the seam wires their deterministic ENGINE (orient.mjs / inventory.py / gw), reached via a thin in-repo wrapper so the global `~/.claude` repo is never touched. Seam now spans **7 surfaces**: sigil + binre + gw + st3gg + delta-scp + code-recon + repo-inventory.

### `seam` RUNNER — standalone, model-agnostic entry point (2026-06-26, `58e7384`)
`tools/seam/run.py` + on PATH via `~/bin/seam` (bash) + `~/bin/seam.cmd` (PowerShell/cmd). Self-contained: injects atlas-map-api `src` on sys.path and drives the SAME `gateway.call_capability` the HTTP endpoint uses — **no Claude, no MCP, no server needed**. Any shell/script/cron/LLM can run it. Subcommands: `seam list` (42 surfaces), `seam call <surface> <capability> k=v…` (→ one Receipt), `seam perceive <repo> [--writes]` (inventory+orient+index → manifest). `--json` = machine manifest `{pipeline,target,produced_at,receipts[],summary}`; exit 0 iff all receipts ok. Writes gated behind `--writes` (refusals shown with the right tool name, not silently skipped). THREE model-agnostic access paths now: `seam` CLI (in-process) · HTTP `POST /seam/call` :3072 · MCP `atlas_call`. ## project_strudel_compiler

<a id="project-strudel-compiler"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_strudel_compiler.md`*
*Name:* strudel-compiler
*Description:* Bruke's STRUDEL = fork of Strudel (TidalCycles-in-JS music live-coding language); already a full music-DSL compiler stack, NOT a machine-code compiler.

`C:\Users\bruke\STRUDEL` = Bruke's fork of **Strudel** (github.com/lionestenzol/strudel), the TidalCycles-in-JavaScript **music live-coding language**. JS/TS pnpm monorepo, ~55 packages, ~122k LOC JS (only 5 tiny Rust files, 11 Python). Fork adds vs upstream (codeberg.org/uzu/strudel): a saveable/exportable **DAW** (`loop-pad` :8770), two CLIs (`strudel-compose`, `strudel-agent`), a `/fest` bridge, and a bespoke deterministic compiler stack. Verified via /groundwork 2026-06-25.

**It is ALREADY a full compiler** (textbook frontend → IR → compiler → runtime) — does NOT need an external backend bolted on:
- Front-ends: `mini` (mini-notation), `mondo` ("a language that translates to js"), `transpiler` (rewrites user JS → evaluatable Strudel = the live-eval JIT), `node-engine` (visual graph → Strudel code), `hs2js` (experimental Haskell→JS).
- IR: `symbolic-ir` (symbolic IR for music composition); `symbolic` (IR → pattern renderer).
- Compiler: `deterministic-compiler` — "structured IR → validated Strudel code" (src: compileToStrudel.ts, executionEngine.ts, normalizeIR.ts, preflight.ts, ir.schema.json, telemetryStore.ts).
- Runtimes: `deterministic-runtime` (browser/Node/REPL/CLI targets); audio via superdough/webaudio/csound.
- Engines: `loop-engine` :3456 (HTTP+SSE pattern compiler + state store), `node-engine` :3457 (graph→code compiler).

**JIT/AOT already exist here:** JIT = `transpiler` + live `eval` (compile live-coded patterns on the fly). AOT = `deterministic-compiler` (IR→validated code ahead-of-time) + `node-engine` (graph→code) + WAV export.

⚠️ **Do NOT recommend llvmlite/Cranelift/LLVM for STRUDEL** — those are native-machine-code backends; STRUDEL compiles music IR→JS/audio (zero native-codegen surface). Two "compiler" threads got conflated in conversation 2026-06-25: **(T1)** general code-perception + *machine-code* compile + [[project_binre]] reverse-engineering + ST3GG/PNG-substrate = a binary/native domain (llvmlite/Cranelift apply ONLY here, and only if T1 is truly about native code); **(T2)** STRUDEL = music-DSL compiler, already mature. They share only the word "compiler." See [[st3gg-png-substrate-pairing]]. If Bruke is pushing STRUDEL, the compiler EXISTS — find the actual gap (recon `deterministic-compiler` + `symbolic-ir` fresh), don't add a backend.

## project_strudel_forensic_map

<a id="project-strudel-forensic-map"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_strudel_forensic_map.md`*
*Description:* Strudel compiler forensic x-ray — durable mod-ready map + delta-scp artifact so future modding is instant, no re-recon.

Forensic audit of the **Strudel music compiler** (`C:\Users\bruke\STRUDEL`, 55-package pnpm monorepo) done 2026-06-26 via /groundwork (delta-scp + verified code-recon Workflow: 7 stages, 14 agents, 252 file:line citations adversarially re-opened, 8 cosmetic line-drifts corrected, all stages HIGH confidence). Purpose: make future changes "butter under a hot knife." See [[project_strudel_compiler]] and the strudel-edit skill (themes/UI).

**Durable deliverables (live at repo root + .audit, refreshable, NOT auto-committed):**
- `STRUDEL\STRUDEL-FORENSIC-MAP.md` (1257 lines) — the x-ray: 4 macro-layers, both compiler pipelines as ASCII flow w/ entry function:file:line per hop, 55-pkg inventory, internal DAG, 7 per-stage deep maps (every key-fn table + mod-points + gotchas), determinism notes, toolchain plug-ins.
- `STRUDEL\STRUDEL-IO-TRACE.md` (2026-06-27) — the **execution-trace** companion (input→output, no gaps). Adds the layer the main map omits: the `website/src` React front-end (every UI affordance → backend entry, file:line) + the **return/feedback loop** (code-highlight, scope/spectrum analyser tap, flash, error banner, beat-pulse). Key facts verified at HEAD: React→engine handoff = `codemirror.mjs:200` `this.repl=repl(...)`; output-sink choice = `useReplContext.jsx:72` (`webaudioOutput` vs `superdirtOutput`); default scheduler = **`Cyclist`** (`repl.mjs:75`) w/ `zyklus` clock (NeoCyclist is sync-only SharedWorker fallback); analyser tap = `superdough.mjs:986` `effectSend(post,analyserNode,1)`; highlight de-highlights by per-frame omission (`codemirror.mjs:193`), NOT a remove event. TWO decoupled clocks: audio `setInterval` (zyklus) vs visual `requestAnimationFrame` (Drawer), sharing only `scheduler.now()`. Built via /groundwork: existing skeleton reused + 3 parallel recon agents + adversarial spine re-verify (10/12 hops line-exact, 2 cosmetic param footnotes) + 3 load-bearing joints hand-verified.
- `STRUDEL\STRUDEL-LANGUAGE.md` (2026-06-27) — the **verbiage + conjugation** reference (what words you type, how they combine). 4 word-classes verified from source: **~540 controls** (`registerControl` [controls.mjs:63](packages/core/controls.mjs:63), nouns that set a Hap `.value` field, alias = shared prototype method) · **136 pattern-fns** (`register`/`stepRegister` [pattern.mjs:1743](packages/core/pattern.mjs:1743), verbs that reshape time) · ~10 combinators (`stack`/`cat`/`seq`/`polymeter`) · ~15 signals (`signal.mjs`). THE conjugation engine = `register()` gives every verb 5 forms: curried top-level fn + chainable `Pattern.prototype[name]` (prefix `f(x,pat)` ≡ postfix `pat.f(x)`) + patternified args (so `fast("<1 2>")` works, per-cycle via applicative) + `_`-prefixed fast path + multi-name alias. `reify` [pattern.mjs:1409](packages/core/pattern.mjs:1409) coerces string→mini-parse / value→`pure` / pattern→itself. Layer-2 conjugation = COMPOSERS×ALIGNMENTS matrix [pattern.mjs:1183](packages/core/pattern.mjs:1183) (`add/mul/set/keepif`×`In/Out/Mix/Squeeze/...`); KEY finding **`struct`=`keepif.out`, `mask`=`keepif.in`** (not primitives, [pattern.mjs:1257](packages/core/pattern.mjs:1257)). Mini-notation = sugar that desugars into the same core combinators (grammar `packages/mini/krill.pegjs`: `* / [] <> {} , ~ @ _ ! ? : .. | (p,s,r)`); reify routes strings to it. Pattern = pure fn `(TimeSpan)->Hap[]` ([pattern.mjs:45](packages/core/pattern.mjs:45)); rational time (Fraction). § cross-refs FORENSIC-MAP/IO-TRACE/MOD-PLAYBOOK.
- **Agent-coding-Strudel stack ALREADY EXISTS in the fork (assemble-first win, don't rebuild) + eval-gate PROVEN LIVE 2026-06-27.** "How do I get an agent to write Strudel reliably with no errors" → these packages: `eval-server` (`strudel-eval` CLI + MCP; headless validator `eval.mjs` catches errors at 3 stages transpile/pattern_check/query, exit 0/1) · `mcp-server` ("intent in, valid code out", 656L `server.mjs`) · `cli-agent` (`sa`/`strudel-agent`: suggest/eval/apply/`chat` = deterministic keyword router NO LLM; drives loop-engine :3456) · `reference-index` (machine-readable ground truth: 620 methods + 24 sounds, `all-methods.json`, 16k-line `strudel-index.json`, pre-made `claude-tool.json`) · `schema` (14k-line capability schema) · `fuzz-harness` (combinatorial headless eval → SQLite `fuzz-results.db`, has been run). RELIABILITY MECHANISM = grounding (agent picks from reference-index, stops hallucinating) + proof-gate (every pattern through headless eval-server BEFORE sound; invalid → repair, never plays). PROVEN: `node packages/eval-server/cli.mjs --validate <code>` rejects hallucinated method `fastquux` ("s(...).fastquux is not a function") + unbalanced syntax ("Unterminated string constant"), passes valid (`fast(2)`: 4 notes→8 events), fully headless (warns "cannot use window" then "@strudel/core loaded"). CORRECTED ARCHITECTURE (verified `loop-engine/boot.mjs:28,151` + `compiler.mjs:196`): **loop-engine :3456 makes NO sound** — it's the deterministic headless brain (intent/state → normalize → validate-via-eval-server → guard → compile → Strudel code → **broadcast over WebSocket**; desc "state→…→compile→Strudel code", deps have zero audio backend, only `broadcast`×20/`ws`×17). **The BROWSER is the speaker** (WS client subscribes → StrudelMirror evaluates → webaudioOutput→superdough→WebAudio, per IO-TRACE). So full loop = intent → loop-engine compiles+validates+broadcasts code → browser tab on the WS plays it. To HEAR agent output you MUST point a browser client at :3456 (missing wire). NOT YET verified this session: mcp-server runtime, loop-engine boot (native dep better-sqlite3), the WS→browser→sound leg (needs user's ears). Two usage paths: (A) wire mcp-server+reference-index(`claude-tool.json`)+eval-server as MCP tools into Claude → talk in English → eval-checked code → broadcast → browser plays; (B) `sa suggest <genre> --apply` deterministic no-LLM.
- `STRUDEL\STRUDEL-MOD-PLAYBOOK.md` (253 lines) — 25 intent recipes ("I want to X" → verified file:line + how + blast radius). Add control/combinator/signal/notation-op/synth/effect/IR-node/backend/MCP-tool/CLI-cmd/theme/etc.
- `STRUDEL\MASTERY_CAPSULE_strudel_2026-06-27.md` (2026-06-28) — 20-section **Target Mastery Capsule** produced by the new `/groundwork brownfield` mode (source-branch counterpart to FL356's binary capsule). Reused the `.audit`/forensic docs but **re-verified 8/8 load-bearing spine claims at HEAD** (register@1743, Pattern@45, transpiler@32, superdough@456, 55 pkgs, node22/pnpm10, sampler path-traversal, git state). Caught a real live bug: **sampler path-traversal** `sampler/sample-server.mjs:118` `decodeURIComponent(req.url)` (confirmed at HEAD, not inherited from the stale March audit). Step-11 binre rung correctly skipped (source, not binary).
- `STRUDEL\STRUDEL-FEATURES.md` (2026-06-28, 561 lines) — the **deterministic feature catalog**: 422 registered features (146 pattern verbs + 276 controls) extracted straight from `register()/registerControl()` calls by `scratchpad/extract_strudel_features.py`, each row carrying a real `file:line` (3 spot-checked exact). Grouped into 17 areas + Appendix A (synth oscillators `superdough/synth.mjs:23` waveforms) + Appendix B (15 mini-notation operators cited to `krill.pegjs` line-by-line). **Count reconciliation (not a contradiction):** this catalog counts distinct *registrations* (422); the STRUDEL-LANGUAGE.md note's "~540 controls / 136 fns" counts total *exposed names* incl. aliases + `registerMultiControl` numbered expansions (fm1/fm2…). Both correct at different granularity. The extractor is the right pattern for any "map ALL the features" ask — the list can't confabulate because it IS the code.
- `STRUDEL\.audit\delta-scp-map.json` — the COMPRESSED whole-system skeleton (2122 nodes · ~129k tokens · saves 5.94M vs re-reading). Refresh: `cd C:/Users/bruke/STRUDEL && node C:/Users/bruke/.claude/skills/code-recon/scripts/orient.mjs --regen` (24h TTL gate; only source-code changes trigger regen). Companions in `.audit/graphs/`: export-registry.json (file→exports), package-dependencies.json (DAG), module-graph.json, reachable/unreachable sets; `.audit/lattice/system-map.html` = interactive Cytoscape graph (auto-regen by the pre-commit "wall" hook every commit).

**System shape (the load-bearing facts):**
- 4 macro-layers: (1) notation front-ends `mini`(krill PEG)/`mondo`+`mondough`/`tidal`+`hs2js`(tree-sitter) → (2) `core` FRP pattern engine + `transpiler`(acorn→escodegen) → (3) TWO backends: JIT-live audio `webaudio`(Cyclist scheduler)→`superdough`/`supradough`→WebAudio, and deterministic `symbolic-ir`(Piece/Layer/Event)→`deterministic-compiler`(normalizeIR→compileToStrudel)→`deterministic-runtime` → (4) agent surfaces `mcp-server`/`cli-agent`/`compose`/`eval-server` + knowledge `schema`/`reference-index`.
- **#1 extension point = `core/packages...pattern.mjs register()` / `stepRegister()`** (every pattern fn note/s/gain/fast/jux attaches here); controls via `controls.mjs registerControl`.
- Time is RATIONAL (Fraction, not float). Parsers are GENERATED (edit `krill.pegjs` not the generated parser; tree-sitter is WASM/async). `node-engine` = visual node-graph→code (NOT a WAV renderer — a priori guess was wrong, verifier caught it). `supradough` = platform-agnostic reimpl of `superdough`; `Dough` class at `dough.mjs:1021`.

**Plugs into:** /delta-scp (refresh skeleton) · /seam (content-address the repo through the atlas gateway → Receipt) · /autopilot + /fest. **Stage-2 fest already built:** festival `strudel-mod-ready-SM0001` at `C:\Users\bruke\festival-project\festivals\planning\strudel-mod-ready-SM0001` — 5 phases / 25 tasks (one per Playbook recipe), validates 100/100, each task DoD provable via `code-recon verify`/grep/vitest/strudelEval. <a id="project-strudel-tauri-v2-release"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_strudel_tauri_v2_release.md`*
*Name:* project-strudel-tauri-v2-release
*Description:* STRUDEL desktop app migrated Tauri v1.4.0 -> v2 + matrix release pipeline; done + cargo-check-clean on branch release/tauri-v2, HELD at human gate (not pushed)

Drove festival `product-level-SS0002 / 006_DISTRIBUTION / 02_tauri_v2_release / matrix_release`
to completion on 2026-06-26 (via `/autopilot` escalation `no_match` → executed the plan).
This is the **desktop packaging** of STRUDEL — distinct from [[project_strudel_compiler]]
(the music DSL compiler).

**Repo:** `C:\Users\bruke\STRUDEL` · remote `github` = github.com/lionestenzol/strudel.git ·
`origin` = codeberg.org/uzu/strudel.git (upstream). pnpm monorepo (pnpm-workspace.yaml);
website is `@strudel/website` (astro). src-tauri at repo root.

**Starting state was a BROKEN half-migration:** JS tooling already v2
(`@tauri-apps/cli ^2.2.7`, `@tauri-apps/api ^2.2.0`, clipboard plugin `2`) but Rust crates +
config still **v1.4.0** (allowlist, devPath/distDir). The v2 CLI can't read a v1 conf, so there
was NO known-good v1 baseline — only path was forward to v2.

**What shipped (branch `release/tauri-v2`, commit `a15ffc39`, NOT pushed):**
- `npx tauri migrate` codemod did Cargo→2 + conf→v2 shape + capabilities skeleton. Gotcha:
  it parses EVERY `.js` in the repo and choked on Strudel composition files (`compositions/*.js`
  are pattern notation, not valid JS). Fix: temporarily relocated `compositions/` to scratchpad,
  ran migrate, restored. (Frontend API scan was a no-op anyway since api was already v2.)
- Residuals by hand: added `tauri-plugin-{dialog,fs,updater} = "2"`; dropped dead
  `custom-protocol` feature (removed in v2); `capabilities/default.json` (dialog + clipboard
  write-text + scoped fs `$HOME`/`$AUDIO` + updater) replacing the v1 allowlist; `main.rs`
  `get_window`→`get_webview_window` + `.plugin(...)` registration; `loggerbridge.rs`
  `Window`→`WebviewWindow` + `use tauri::Emitter` (emit moved to a trait in v2); version 0.1.0→0.2.0.
- Updater: generated signing keypair, wired `plugins.updater` (GitHub releases `latest.json`
  endpoint + pubkey) + `bundle.createUpdaterArtifacts`. **rc1 key is a THROWAWAY empty-password
  key in scratchpad** (`...scratchpad/strudel-signing/strudel-rc.key`) — regen with a password
  for the real release.
- `release.yml` already had the correct matrix (ubuntu-22.04 / macos-13 / macos-14 / windows) +
  tauri-action@v0 + signing secrets; only removed a broken `--filter strudel` prebuild step
  (frontend is built by tauri-action via `beforeBuildCommand: npm run build` = jsdoc-json+astro).
- gitignored generated `gen/schemas/`.

**Two real bugs caught BY building (not assumed) — see [[feedback_built_at_me_verified_mechanism_not_outcome]]:**
1. Local Rust was 1.83; a tauri-v2 transitive dep (`moxcms`) needs edition2024 → `rustup update`
   to **1.96** (matches CI's rust-toolchain@stable).
2. Stale `time 0.3.22` in Cargo.lock fails to compile on Rust 1.96 (E0282) →
   `cargo update -p time` → 0.3.51.
Result: `cargo check` **clean, 0 errors** on 1.96 (2 pre-existing dead-code warnings only).
NOTE: `cargo check ... | tail` masks cargo's exit code (you get tail's 0) — capture
`; echo CARGO_EXIT=$?` BEFORE any pipe.

**Hygiene:** the repo had unrelated WIP (`website/package.json` workspace-dep adds, docs,
my-patterns) already dirty at session start — staged ONLY the migration files by explicit path,
kept WIP out of the commit. (`.audit/graphs/module-graph.json` rode along: a repo pre-commit
`map` hook regenerated it — harmless.)

**DoD: 3/4 done.** Items 1-3 (conf v2, updater pubkey, release.yml matrix) ✅ checked off in
`01_matrix_release.md`. Item 4 (push `v0.2.0-rc1` → installers) is **HUMAN-GATED** — not pushed.
Full plan: `...02_tauri_v2_release/PLAN_matrix_release.md`.

**OPEN before tag push (spawned as a task chip, id task_58046364):** frontend still calls v1
globals that compile but silently break at RUNTIME — `website/src/tauri.mjs` uses
`window.__TAURI_IPC__` (v2 = `__TAURI_INTERNALS__`), and `website/src/repl/files.mjs`
destructures `window.__TAURI__.fs` incl. `readBinaryFile` (v2 renamed to `readFile`). Needs a
`npx tauri dev` run to fix+verify. Deliberately NOT slipped into the release commit.
