## project_strudel_ui_restyle

<a id="project-strudel-ui-restyle"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_strudel_ui_restyle.md`*
*Name:* strudel-ui-restyle
*Description:* Executable spec — give the Strudel desktop editor modern '2026' vibes (keep the terminal/editor, don't remove it). Levers + exact files + recommended theme.

**INVOKE SKILL (2026-06-26):** `/strudel-edit` (global, `~/.claude/skills/strudel-edit/SKILL.md`, built on Bruke's "make it an invoke skill called strudl edit"). It is the ORIENT for any future Strudel restyle from ANY session/cwd: the honest "there is no special tool — edits are plain Read/Edit/Write on `C:\Users\bruke\STRUDEL` absolute paths, works from any cwd" framing + repo/branch + the 4-lever file map + the data-theme effect recipe + ✦/x- markers + the live verify loop (astro dev :4321 + Playwright) + the pre-commit map-hook gotcha + the graphics-stack decisions. Grounded against live code at build time (themes.mjs imports/registration, settings.mjs:52 fontFamily, SettingsTab.jsx:107 OURS map, Repl.css data-theme blocks, activateTheme:223). Use it instead of re-deriving all this; this memory file = the deeper spec/history behind the skill.

**STATUS — SHIPPED 2026-06-26 (first slice):** landed on branch `feat/ui-restyle-midnight-synth` (commit `910efe2f`) in `C:\Users\bruke\STRUDEL`, branched off `release/tauri-v2`. Delivered: new default theme `midnightSynth` (adapted from the ported Tokyo Night Storm palette) + default font `monospace`→`JetBrains` (`settings.mjs:51-52`) + editor-scoped chrome polish in `Repl.css` (neon caret glow, rounded active line/selection, bracket-match glow). Registered in `packages/codemirror/themes.mjs`. **Verified LIVE** via astro dev `localhost:4321` (Playwright): computed `--background #10131f`, scroller font `JetBrains`, caret `box-shadow rgb(240,171,252) 0 0 7px`, **0 console errors**. **NOT pushed.** Two carry-overs: (1) the installed desktop `.exe` bundles the OLD web build — needs a fresh `pnpm exec tauri build` to reship; (2) persisted-settings users (anyone who already opened the app) keep their saved theme/font in localStorage — the new *default* only applies to fresh/reset profiles, else pick `midnightSynth`+`JetBrains` in Settings. Below = the original spec (now history). Next levers untouched: beat-pulse highlight (lever 4), header frosted-glass depth.

**OURS MARKER CONVENTION (Bruke, 2026-06-26) — applies to ALL future Strudel work:** everything WE add gets marked so it's distinguishable from upstream at a glance. (1) UI-facing display names get a `✦ ` prefix (e.g. `✦ Midnight Synth`). (2) New source files get an `x-` filename prefix. For themes the theme *key* stays clean camelCase (flows into localStorage `strudel-settings*` + share-URLs — never put `✦`/spaces there); the `✦` lives only in the dropdown LABEL via the `OURS` map in `website/src/repl/components/panel/SettingsTab.jsx`. Themes so far (8, all ours, ✦): muted/cool family — `midnightSynth` (default), `catppuccinMocha`, `rosePine`, `poimandres` (`00491485`); variety pack (distinct vibes) — `synthwaveNeon` (neon near-black), `sunsetTape` (warm analog), `daylight` (real LIGHT theme, `light:true`), `phosphor` (green CRT) in `x-*.mjs` files (`24de637e`). Picker marker committed (`a38060db`). LEARNING: the first 4 looked samey because all share dark cool blue-purple bgs + 3 are muted-pastel by design; variety needs different hue families + light/neon, not more dark cousins. NOTE: the 4 existing theme *files* were created before the `x-` rule and were NOT retro-renamed (offered). Commits on `feat/ui-restyle-midnight-synth`: `910efe2f` (midnightSynth+JetBrains), `00491485` (3 themes), picker-marker.

**EFFECTS ERA — the `data-theme` unlock (2026-06-26, commit `a5ccdf7b`):** the per-theme `customStyle` path is capped at ONE rule (`injectStyle`→`insertRule`, [themes.mjs](packages/codemirror/themes.mjs)), so it can't carry orbs+glass+keyframes. THE UNLOCK: `activateTheme` now stamps `document.documentElement.dataset.theme = name` → any theme can carry *unlimited* scoped CSS via `[data-theme="<key>"] ...` blocks in `website/src/repl/Repl.css`. KEY FACT: `.cm-editor` is `background-color: transparent !important; z-index: 11` (Repl.css) — Strudel renders the editor transparent over a back layer, so effects go on `.code-container` (the wrapper, `position:relative; overflow:hidden`) and glow *through* the code. Per-token neon glow is free via `textShadow` in the theme's `styles[]` (HighlightStyle passes arbitrary CSS). **✦ Outrun** = first effect-bearing theme (`x-outrun.mjs` palette + Repl.css `[data-theme=outrun]`: drifting aurora orbs via `::before/::after` radial-gradients + `mix-blend:screen` + @keyframes, indigo→magenta gradient, frosted-glass active line via `backdrop-filter`, `prefers-reduced-motion` guarded). Verified live :4323 (0 errors, all computed styles pass). Pure CSS, no new deps.

**✦ LIQUID shipped (2026-06-26, commit `5237d92d` on `feat/ui-restyle-midnight-synth`):** second effect-bearing theme, rode the `data-theme` unlock identically to Outrun — pure CSS, zero new deps. `x-liquid.mjs` = cool slate palette (azure/aqua/mint/gold/lavender, warm gold numerals as the one "lit" accent) + per-token specular `textShadow` (lighter than Outrun's neon bloom). Repl.css `[data-theme=liquid]`: frosted slate gradient canvas; a slow diagonal **specular sheen** sweep (`::before`, `liquidSheen` keyframe holds dark between passes so it reads occasional, not strobing); a drifting **refractive light pool** (`::after`, `liquidDrift`); **glass-edge active line** (`backdrop-filter` blur+saturate+brightness + inset top-edge 1px gleam = the Apple Liquid-Glass tell). Verified live :4323 (data-theme=liquid, both layers animating screen-blend, editor transparent, 0 errors). 5 pieces, same recipe as Outrun. **REPO GOTCHA (don't re-fight):** STRUDEL has a `core.hooksPath=scripts/hooks` **pre-commit hook** that regenerates `.audit/graphs/module-graph.json` + `.audit/lattice/system-map-data.js` ("the wall") from live source and `git add`s them on EVERY commit — so a clean 4-file theme commit lands as 6 files; the 2 `.audit/` files are the map auto-syncing to the new module, INTENDED, not stray WIP. Don't amend them out (the hook re-adds). Skip with `STRUDEL_MAP_HOOK=0` or `--no-verify` only if you must.

**✦ BEAT-PULSE shipped (2026-06-27, commit `471765cb` on `feat/ui-restyle-midnight-synth`):** the 5th effect theme and the FIRST audio-reactive one — the editor glow throbs with the music. Architecture (the repeatable "reactive theme" recipe): a dependency-free hook `website/src/repl/x-beatpulse.mjs` taps the **superdough master bus** (`getSuperdoughAudioController().output.destinationGain` → `AnalyserNode`), runs an **asymmetric attack/release envelope follower** on the time-domain RMS (fast snap up on the kick, slow decay), and writes `--beat` (0..1) onto `<html>` each rAF. The `[data-theme='beatPulse']` block in Repl.css reads `--beat` purely via `calc()` (sub-bass bloom `::before`, peak-only pink core `::after` via `--beat`², whole-panel cyan halo `box-shadow`, active-line throb). Gate = a **MutationObserver on `<html data-theme>`** (editor-agnostic — works for both StrudelMirror and the Dough mirror; `activateTheme` stamps data-theme for both); the rAF loop only runs while beatPulse is active, reduced-motion holds a steady glow. Hook is installed once from `useReplContext.jsx` (`installBeatPulse()` after `window.strudelMirror = editor`). Verified live :4322 + Playwright with `s("bd*4")`: `--beat` peaks ~0.99 per kick with a clean decay envelope, `box-shadow` scales to `calc(0.99*40px)`=39.6px, **0 console errors**. **TWO LOAD-BEARING GOTCHAS (general, not just this theme):** (1) **module-instance mismatch** — a bundled `import { getSuperdoughAudioController } from '@strudel/webaudio'` resolved to a DIFFERENT, *silent* superdough copy than the one the REPL actually plays through; the imported tap read pure silence while `window.getSuperdoughAudioController` (the global Strudel exposes via prebake/evalScope) read maxRms ~1.5. FIX: the hook prefers the `window.*` globals, falls back to the import. **Any future runtime audio / live-REPL-state tap MUST use the `window.*` globals, not a package import.** (2) **corrected graphics-stack decision** — the older plan said "anime.js for Beat-Pulse"; recon showed the signal is a single scalar and the right smoothing is a 3-line asymmetric envelope follower (anime.js's symmetric `damp` fits worse AND would add a dep the other themes don't have). Hand-rolled, zero new deps. All 4 effect themes (Outrun/Liquid/CRT/Beat-Pulse) are now zero-dep. The commit landed 8 files (6 mine + the 2 auto-added `.audit` map files = the documented hook).

**GRAPHICS-STACK DECISION (don't re-litigate):** (1) ambient editor chrome (orbs/glass/scanlines/bloom) → **pure CSS** `@keyframes` — library would be *worse*, not just later. (2) audio-reactive glow on active line (Beat-Pulse, the moat) → SHIPPED `471765cb` with a **hand-rolled asymmetric envelope follower** (NOT anime.js — corrected after recon; symmetric `damp` fits a beat worse + adds a dep). Master-bus RMS via `window.getSuperdoughAudioController().output.destinationGain` → AnalyserNode → `--beat` CSS var. anime.js stays parked (no use case has earned it yet). (3) generative audio-reactive VISUALS → **Hydra is ALREADY in Strudel** (`packages/hydra/hydra.mjs`, wired via `packages/repl/prebake.mjs`) — assemble, don't add three.js. (4) three.js → **PARKED**, only for a future bespoke true-3D set-piece (not editor chrome; net-new, no WebGL-3D in repo today). Effect themes SHIPPED (all on `feat/ui-restyle-midnight-synth`, pure CSS, no deps): ✦ Outrun `a5ccdf7b` (synthwave aurora orbs), ✦ Liquid `5237d92d` (Apple glass: sheen+refraction), ✦ CRT `0133803c` (amber-phosphor tube: scan-beam `crtSweep` behind code + scanlines/flicker `crtFlicker` OVER code at z-20 + vignette; AMBER not green to differ from flat `phosphor`; KEY TRICK = scanline pseudo at z-20 beats the editor's z-11 so the texture crosses glyphs, pointer-events:none keeps clicks). ✦ Beat-Pulse `471765cb` (audio-reactive — the FIRST runtime-signal theme; full record in the ✦ BEAT-PULSE paragraph above). All FOUR effect themes now prove the data-theme unlock is a repeatable ~5-file recipe; Beat-Pulse extends it with a +2-file audio-tap (the rAF hook + the window-globals master-bus tap). NEXT: no theme queued — pick a new vibe, or move to chrome/header depth (lever 3, untouched).

**GOAL (Bruke, 2026-06-26):** make the Strudel desktop app's editor look modern/aesthetic — "2026 vibes, not old cold terminal." **KEEP the terminal/code-editor** (it IS the product); just make it *better*, not gone. This is an **assemble + restyle** job, not a rebuild — Strudel already has a full theme framework. See [[strudel-compiler]].

**CURRENT STATE (verified this session):** the desktop app is **built + installed + working** on this machine. Repo `C:\Users\bruke\STRUDEL`, branch `release/tauri-v2`, commit `a15ffc39` (Tauri v1→v2 migration, `cargo check` green). Installed at `C:\Users\bruke\AppData\Local\Strudel\Strudel.exe`; desktop + Start-menu shortcuts exist; user pinned to taskbar/Start by hand (Win11 blocks programmatic pin). The desktop window wraps **`website/dist`** (the Strudel REPL), so restyling the REPL restyles the app. Local installers were at `src-tauri/target/release/bundle/{msi,nsis}/Strudel_0.2.0_*`.

**THE 4 LEVERS (grounded — file:line verified 2026-06-26):**
1. **Font (1 line, biggest bang):** `website/src/settings.mjs:52` is `fontFamily: 'monospace'` — that bare `monospace` IS the cold-terminal tell. Change default to `'JetBrains'` (already bundled — declared in `website/src/styles/index.css`, no new dep). Other bundled faces: Monocraft, x3270, BigBlueTerminal, PressStart, galactico, CutiePi.
2. **Default theme (assemble-first — DON'T hand-roll a palette):** framework at `packages/codemirror/themes.mjs` + `packages/codemirror/themes/` (~25 themes already: strudelTheme, dracula, aura, androidstudio, atomone, darcula, duotoneDark, …). The *face* currently leans cold (`blackscreen`/`teletext`/`redText`/`greenText`). Port ONE modern palette into `packages/codemirror/themes/<name>.mjs` following the existing module shape (each exports `default` theme + `settings`), register it in `themes.mjs`, set it default. Off-the-shelf palettes to lift: **Tokyo Night, Catppuccin Mocha, Rosé Pine, Poimandres.**
3. **Chrome + depth:** `website/src/repl/Repl.css` + `website/src/styles/index.css`. Swap flat `#000` for a near-black with a faint tint; round the editor panel; frosted translucent header (`backdrop-filter: blur`); soft glow on the active/playing line.
4. **Make it breathe (the "alive" move):** Strudel already highlights events as they play — lean into that so the highlight **pulses with the beat**. This is the thing a dead terminal can't fake.

**RECOMMENDED FIRST SLICE (~3-4 files, reversible, no new deps):** font→JetBrains + port **Midnight Synth** (cool neon — deep indigo-charcoal `#10131f` bg, cyan `#7dd3fc` + magenta `#f0abfc` + lavender `#a5b4fc` syntax, amber numerals; map closest to **Tokyo Night / Poimandres**) as default + the chrome/depth polish. Alternatives shown to Bruke: **Sunset Tape** (warm amber/peach, cozy analog) and keeping the cold one. Bruke leaned (a)=bank-the-spec; he did NOT hard-lock a mood, so Midnight Synth is the *recommended default to build*, easy to swap if he redirects.

**VERIFY LOOP:** quick web preview = `cd website && npm run build` (or `npm run dev`). Full desktop proof = `pnpm exec tauri build` (NOTE: run from repo ROOT; the crate is in `src-tauri/`; ~10-20 min release build), then re-run the NSIS installer / relaunch `Strudel.exe`. Known-good baseline to diff against = `a15ffc39`.

**CONSTRAINTS:** keep the editor (don't remove it); assemble-first (port a known theme, don't invent a palette — solved category); contain to ~3-4 files; work on a feat branch off `release/tauri-v2`; rebuild + relaunch to see it. AGPL-3.0 fork (origin=upstream uzu/strudel, github remote=lionestenzol fork) — additive restyle is fine; matters only if shipped closed/commercial.

## project_supagetti_v0

<a id="project-supagetti-v0"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_supagetti_v0.md`*
*Name:* project-supagetti-v0
*Description:* SupaGetti v0 — local Python CLI report factory for messy software projects (C:\Users\bruke\supagetti\)

# SupaGetti v0

**Location:** `C:\Users\bruke\supagetti\` (separate repo, NOT inside Pre Atlas)
**Stack:** Python + click (CLI) + Jinja2-style templates + JSON schemas
**Status:** All 7 core modules written. Phase-by-phase CLI testing pending.

## What it is

A local CLI report factory: drop a messy software project, get a structured 13-section diagnostic report. No cloud, no LLM calls — pure filesystem scan + deterministic analysis.

**Pipeline:** DROP -> TRIAGE -> MAP -> INTERPRET -> REPORT -> COMMAND -> RECORD -> REFINE

**CLI commands:** `new-case`, `intake`, `load`, `scan`, `analyze`, `govern`, `report`, `ledger`, `cast` (full pipeline), `list`

## Architecture

```
supagetti.py          — click CLI entry point (11 subcommands)
core/
  case_manager.py     — case folder CRUD (SG-{slug}-{timestamp}), meta.json, artifacts
  intake.py           — interactive intake questionnaire (7 questions)
  scanner.py          — filesystem walker, framework detection, structural facts -> scan.json
  analyzer.py         — findings generator (9 categories) -> findings.json
  governor.py         — truth/safety layer, banned-language checker -> governor_report.json
  reporter.py         — 13-section markdown report generator -> report.md
  ledger.py           — case learning record, mess classification -> ledger_entry.json + ledger.jsonl
schemas/
  intake_schema.json, scan_schema.json, finding_schema.json, ledger_schema.json
templates/
  report_template.md  — 13-section Jinja-style template
cases/                — per-case folders (SG-*)
ledger.jsonl          — global append-only learning ledger
```

## Key concepts

- **Governor:** truth/safety layer — blocks overconfident language ("definitely", "always", "never"); enforces "Detected"/"Not detected"/"Likely"/"Requires confirmation"/"Cannot determine from provided files"
- **Ledger:** every case teaches the system — mess_type classification, automation candidates, pricing signal
- **cast command:** runs full pipeline: scan -> analyze -> govern -> report -> ledger
- **Definition of done:** `supagetti cast <case-id>` produces scan.json + findings.json + governor_report.json + report.md + ledger_entry.json

## Testing phases (pending)

| Phase | Test | Status |
|-------|------|--------|
| 0 | `python supagetti.py --help` | PENDING (cp1252 bug FIXED) |
| 1 | `new-case --name test-project` | pending |
| 2 | `intake <case-id>` | pending |
| 3 | `load <case-id> --folder <path>` | pending |
| 4 | `scan <case-id>` | pending |
| 5 | `analyze <case-id>` | pending |
| 6 | `govern <case-id>` | pending |
| 7 | `report <case-id>` | pending |
| 8 | `ledger <case-id>` | pending |
| 9 | `cast <case-id>` (full pipeline) | pending |
| 10 | Real messy repo end-to-end | pending |

## Origin

Designed in a ChatGPT conversation with the "wizard/grimoire" metaphor (user = wizard/operator, AI = grimoire/active command system). Spec was complete — this is pure assembly from spec, using click + stdlib.

**Why:** [[project-close-it-out-strong]] — "we did everything but build the actual thing." Assemble-first posture: click for CLI, no hand-rolling.

**How to apply:** Next session picks up at Phase 0 test and runs through Phase 10. Build phase by phase, test after each, don't advance until current phase passes.

## project_system_autonomy

<a id="project-system-autonomy"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_system_autonomy.md`*
*Name:* System Autonomy Audit (2026-04-06)
*Description:* What runs autonomously vs manually in Pre Atlas — daily/weekly governance is solid, execution workflows are API-only

## What's Autonomous (no human needed)

- **Daily pipeline** (7:00 AM via Windows Task Scheduler): `run_daily.py` → loops, closure stats, mode routing, cycleboard sync, daily brief + governance state
- **Weekly pipeline** (8:00 AM Sunday): full daily + behavioral audit + idea pipeline refresh + weekly governance packet
- **Service daemons** (at login): delta-kernel :3001, openclaw :3004, orchestrator :3005

**Why:** Registered as Windows Scheduled Tasks (`PreAtlas-Governor-Daily`, `PreAtlas-Governor-Weekly`). CycleBoard brain files confirmed fresh as of 2026-04-06 07:00.

## What's API-Only (needs POST request)

- `POST /api/v1/workflows/daily` — orchestrator daily loop (duplicate of governor daily, different entry point)
- `POST /api/v1/workflows/stall-check` — stall detector
- `POST /api/v1/workflows/idea-simulation` — idea-to-simulation pipeline
- `POST /api/v1/tasks/execute` — ad-hoc Claude task execution

## What's Manual-Only (not wired in)

- `ghost_executor.py` — generates execution directives from Genesis tree. Governor reads its output IF it exists, but nothing runs it automatically.

**How to apply:** Before adding new infrastructure (queues, event sourcing), wire existing manual workflows into the scheduled pipeline. Ghost executor → daily pipeline. Stall check → daily pipeline. This gives more autonomy with zero new code.

**Key design facts for docs view:** .md files already indexed as nodes + previewed raw (80 lines, Prism "markdown"); viewer theme = CSS vars --bg/--panel/--text/--text-2/--text-3/--border/--accent with prefers-color-scheme dark; data contract = window.SERVICES/SERVICE_EDGES/FILE_GRAPHS/FILE_CHURN/INSIGHTS/PREVIEWS/GEN_AT/REPO_NAME; fuse index weights label .45/symbols .30/purpose .10/content .10/lang .05.

**Codex handoff FAILED (2026-07-08) — do not retry `-s workspace-write` on this machine.** Codex CLI's Windows sandbox cannot apply file patches at all here: `windows unelevated restricted-token sandbox cannot enforce split writable root sets directly; refusing to run unsandboxed`. Verified via git log (zero commits) — not a policy block, a real environment bug. Bruke chose "Claude executes directly" over `-s danger-full-access`. Codex may still work fine in read-only mode (review/second-opinion) — only write-mode is confirmed broken.

**Execution resumed by Claude directly, 2026-07-08.** `system-map` is now git-initialized (baseline `e19dac2`). Task `001_IMPLEMENT/01_correctness/01_main_guard_refresh_index.md` DONE — committed `4e734ae`. Verified 3-way (import test, byte-identical output vs pre-change run on groundwork-cli, grep for leftover module-scope statements) before marking complete.

**fest gate gotcha discovered (new, not yet in [[project_fest_windows_native]]):** the `Recon Verify` quality gate reads `.audit/recon-ledger.jsonl` from the **festival root** (cwd when `fest task completed` runs), NOT the linked `project_path`. Its `file_line` citations must resolve as real paths **relative to the festival root** (e.g. `../../../../system-map/refresh_index.py:296`, not an absolute path, not bare `refresh_index.py`) or the gate reports "cited file no longer exists" even though it's real. Absolute forward-slash paths that `node fs.existsSync()` confirms exist still fail the gate — the Go binary's path resolution doesn't match Node's. Always test the relative-path form with `node -e "console.log(require('fs').existsSync('<path>'))"` from the festival root before writing ledger entries.

**Next up:** `001_IMPLEMENT/01_correctness/02_fix_default_root_paths.md` (the `parents[2]` → `C:\Users` bug in all 3 scripts), then `03_update_stale_prog_names.md`. 11 of 13 real tasks remain in Phase 001 alone. Session parked at ~78% context — resume by reading this file + the task files directly, no need to re-derive the gate-path workaround.

<a id="project-three-js-path2-ecosystem-rig"></a>

# Path 2: three.js ecosystem head-to-head comparison rig

**Status:** SHIPPED 2026-05-10, v2 repair pass 2026-05-11.
**Location:** `C:/Users/bruke/three-js/ecosystem/path2/` (outside Pre Atlas worktree).
**Scope rule:** path2/ only; no edits to `~/three-stack/`, `~/.claude/skills/three-js/`, or `audit/cards/`.

## What it is

The empirical-verification layer of the 3-path three.js ecosystem map. Path 1 was source-grounded recommendations (50 audit cards at `~/three-js/ecosystem/audit/cards/`). Path 3 was the live scaffold (`~/three-stack/`). Path 2 builds the SAME minimal demo in each contested category's candidates, measures bundle/LOC/ergonomics, and writes one comparison card per category.

## What landed

```
path2/
├── physics/        4 demos (r3f-rapier · r3f-cannon · vanilla-rapier · lo-th-phy)
├── postprocessing/ 2 demos (pmndrs-postprocessing · three-addons-unrealbloom)
├── ao/             3 demos (n8ao · pmndrs-ssao · three-addons-gtao)
├── text-3d/        3 demos (troika · bmfont · three-addons-textgeom)
├── csg/            2 demos (three-bvh-csg · three-csg-mesh)         [manifold cut]
├── webgl-vs-webgpu-pp/  2 demos (webgl-pmndrs-bloom · webgpu-tsl-bloom)
├── shader-libs/    3 demos (lygia · shader-park-core · raw-glsl)
└── cards/          7 markdown comparison cards
```

19 demos. All compile + bundle. 7 cards. Empirical bundle + LOC measurements across the board.

## Cross-category pattern

Vanilla `three/addons` paths consistently win bundle by 1.5-3×. The win is dominated by React + R3F overhead (~170-200 KB gz), NOT by the postprocessing/AO/physics libs themselves. R3F-with-drei wins ergonomics (29-41 LOC less per app than vanilla, verified 2026-05-11 after retrofitting demos to use drei).

```
already using R3F?  → keep Path 1 picks (pmndrs, n8ao, rapier, troika)
vanilla only?       → use three/addons paths. saves ~180 KB gz per category.
```

## Honest gap (structural)

FPS / TTI / visual quality / screenshots NOT measured. Chrome MCP-driven tabs are background-tabs (`document.hidden === true`), Chrome suspends rAF — verified 0 frames in 32s. To close: open each demo in foreground Chrome, observe stats.js. Documented per-card.

## Bug-fix pass (furniture rule)

- ✅ **csg-mesh THREE.Face3 dead path:** FIXED — replaced legacy block with explicit throw at `src/vendor/three-csg.js:111-114`.
- ⚠️ **shader-park-core duplicate three.js:** INVESTIGATED + DEFERRED with concrete criteria. shader-park@0.2.8 inlines three v0.155 as class definitions (not via `import`), so npm overrides / Vite dedupe can't reach it. Fix requires patch-package, fork, or upstream PR. Deferral owner = future-Bruke; trigger = 2+ shader-park apps or @0.3.x release.
- ❌ **R3F canvas 300×150:** FALSE POSITIVE — index.html CSS was correct; the 300×150 reading was symptom of same hidden-tab quirk.

## v2 repair pass (2026-05-11)

Bruke pushback ("did you look at the code or cherry pick"). Triggered v2 mission:

1. Read drei + R3F-rapier + use-cannon + R3F Canvas source (paths in addendum #2)
2. Retrofitted 5 R3F demos to use idiomatic drei (`<Stats />` + `<OrbitControls autoRotate />`). LOC down 11-20 per demo. Bundle delta ~0 KB gz (drei tree-shakes).
3. Updated SUMMARY.md, PROPOSAL_vanilla_helper.md, and 3 priority cards with file:line citations to lib source.

Real LOC delta (verified): **+29 to +41 LOC** vanilla pays vs R3F-with-drei. Not the +11/+30 (without drei) or +38/+57 (estimated) I'd previously reported.

## Vanilla helper proposal

[PROPOSAL_vanilla_helper.md](C:/Users/bruke/three-js/ecosystem/path2/PROPOSAL_vanilla_helper.md). **Not built.** ~150 LOC sketch of a zero-React convenience layer that closes ~70-80% of R3F's ergonomic win for vanilla apps. Build trigger: 2+ vanilla three.js apps in flight or planned. Otherwise YAGNI.

## Discrepancies vs Path 1 audit (documented in SUMMARY.md, not fixed inline)

1. R3F-defaultism over-recommends pmndrs/n8ao/troika for non-R3F apps (~180 KB gz premium per category)
2. Bundle winner ≠ recommendation in 3/7 categories (physics, csg, shader-libs)
3. shader-park-core duplicate-three footgun verified in production dist

## What this enables next

- Update `~/.claude/skills/three-js/references/ecosystem-map.md` (future session, per HANDOFF rules)
- Manual FPS pass (foreground browser) — closes the only remaining gap
- Build the vanilla helper IF 2+ vanilla three.js apps land

## Furniture rule origin

Bruke established `~/.claude/rules/common/code-as-furniture.md` mid-mission: "code we keep is furniture; no broken furniture; no throwing out either; fix it or set explicit deferral with criteria." Applied retroactively to Path 2's bugs.

## Files

- `path2/HANDOFF.md` — handoff doc from session 1

## project_tool_lattice_architecture

<a id="project-tool-lattice-architecture"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_tool_lattice_architecture.md`*
*Description:* The combinatorial architecture — repomix + deepwiki-open + the existing code-understanding stack composed into a Receipt-joined tool lattice with a router on top; the build that turns it real

Bruke's ask (2026-06-27): not "which tool" but ALL combinations/permutations of excellence+efficiency+capability across the new tools ([[project_external_code_tools_vs_stack]]) and the existing stack. He loved this framing and wants to BUILD it. This is the design + the grounded build plan.

## The reframe: tools are an instruction set, compose by type signature
```
Δ  delta-scp    : repo            → skeleton     (cheap, lossy)
R  repomix      : repo × globs    → full content (lossless, costly)
G  gw/lattice   : index           → topology graph
CR code-recon   : question×scope  → evidence     (file:line, PROOF)
Do deepwiki-open: repo            → wiki+answer  (narrative, CONFABULATES; local/private Ollama :8001/:3000)
Dh deepwiki-mcp : public-repo     → answer       (OUTWARD only)
B  bearings     : git×sessions    → temporal     (churn, staleness)
RI repo-inv     : repo            → census
S  seam         : surface×cap×args→ Receipt       (content-addressed, JOINABLE)
AM atlas-map    : query           → location + /call + MCP
F  fest         : evidence        → plan          (proof-gated)
```
Once every tool emits/consumes through S (the Receipt fabric), any output joins any input by content-address. That join is what makes "this AND that AND that" real. Don't chase the enumeration (infinite) — traverse the AXES.

## The 5 axes (the permutation space)
1. **Fidelity** `Δ→G→R→CR`: heterogeneous fidelity in ONE pass = a zoom dial over a whole repo (Δ everywhere, R on hot regions, CR on the risky line), all in one Receipt manifest.
2. **Trust** `narrator→prover`: every confabulator (Do/Dh/subagent) paired with CR. Deeper: TRIANGULATE Do vs Δ vs G on one question — agreement=confidence, **disagreement=detector** (stale doc / dead edge / bug). ([[feedback_agent_report_distrust]])
3. **Time** `B × anything`: B×G=churn heat-map; B×Do=regenerate wiki only for changed subsystems (kills Ollama-slowness); B×R=temporal carry; B×CR=cheap drift.
4. **Direction** `inward⇄outward`: closes [[feedback_assemble_first_posture]] into a runnable circuit — `Dh+R-remote` evaluate a lib WITHOUT cloning → adopt → `R-remote→S` makes it a first-class subsystem → Do narrates use → CR proves integration.
5. **Reflexivity**: register R+Do as AM surfaces → seam carries/narrates/proves via `/call` → MCP exposes to any session → `repomix generate_skill` reads AM's own registry → emits a skill that drives the whole stack (the apex). Closes [[project_self_describing_surfaces]].

**Order encodes intent (permutations):** `Δ→R` cheap zoom vs `R→Δ` waste; `Do→CR` hypothesize-then-prove (investigation) vs `CR→Do` prove-then-narrate (HONEST doc generation).

## Phase change: toolbox → autonomic organism (MAPE-K, his own vocab [[system_framing_pre_atlas_vocabulary]])
Monitor=post-commit hook+gw-map-auto-refresh+bearings · Analyze=delta-scp+code-recon+**deepwiki-open**+**repomix** · Plan=groundwork+fest · Execute=weapon+autopilot+agents · Knowledge=atlas-map registry+**seam Receipts**+.audit cache+**the wiki**. The two new tools complete Analyze+Knowledge → the loop can close on itself (code change→hook→map+incremental-wiki refresh→bearings churn→recon→drift→fest re-plan), self-maintaining with no human in loop. THAT is "better than the sum."

## The meta-move that subsumes the rest
Build the two things that make the infinity usable: (a) put EVERY tool behind the Receipt fabric so any output joins any input; (b) a **router over the lattice** that picks the COMBINATION per query shape (fidelity + narrator/prover pair + order) and learns from each Receipt. Seed already exists: [[project_tool_outcome_ledger]] (transcript→ledger + Thompson router) — extend it to score COMBINATIONS (tool tuples), not single tools.

## VERIFIED SEAMS (file:line, grounded 2026-06-27 — not confabulated)
- seam is perceive→compile→carry but only PERCEIVE is implemented as a tool list: `tools/seam/run.py:42` `PERCEIVE=[(repo-inventory,inventory,root),(code-recon,orient,root),(groundwork-cli,index,root)]`. `_call`→`gateway.call_capability`→content-addressed Receipt at `run.py:53`. Surfaces discovered via `services|apps|tools/*/atlas.surface.json` at `run.py:84` (`_list_surfaces`).
- **seam add-a-tool contract** (`tools/seam/docs/extending.md`): tool is seam-ready if CLI prints stdout JSON containing a `sha256` hex field, OR HTTP returns a JSON body. PREFER WRAPPING external tools in `tools/<name>/` with a thin adapter (e.g. existing `tools/binre/report.py`, `tools/repo-inventory/inv.py`, `tools/code-recon/orient_seam.py`). Strip wall-clock timestamps + absolute paths before hashing → stable content-address.
- **atlas.surface.json schema** (template `apps/ai-exec-pipeline/atlas.surface.json`): `{surface, headline, kind, lifecycle, capabilities:[{id,...}]}`.
- code-recon orient gate caches FULL skeleton at `.audit/delta-scp-map.json` (`~/.claude/skills/code-recon/scripts/orient.mjs:31`) — the region list R consumes for scoped zoom.
- repomix MCP tools live: pack_codebase, pack_remote_repository, grep_repomix_output, read_repomix_output, generate_skill, attach_packed_output. deepwiki MCP: ask_question, read_wiki_structure, read_wiki_contents.

## BUILT (2026-06-27) — the lattice is real, all proven live + tested
Shipped this session (uncommitted at write-time; on `feat/atlas-setup-ui`). assemble-first throughout: thin wrappers over repomix/deepwiki/the existing ledger, no reimplementation.
1. **`tools/repomix/pack.py` + `atlas.surface.json`** — CARRY stage. Wraps `repomix` CLI (npm 1.15.x); packs a scoped dir, parks the bundle in content-addressed `.seam/carry/<sha>.<ext>` (gitignored), prints a receipt whose `sha256` = bundle bytes (VERIFIED deterministic: 2 runs → identical sha `d660aeba…`; no wall-clock header; relative paths). Receipt is a pointer+metrics, NOT inlined bytes (binre shape). Gateway passes only `{root}` — charset rejects `*`, so glob `--include` is STANDALONE-only; scope by directory through the gateway. repomix ~5s node startup → wrapper self-caps at 19s under the 20s gateway limit.
2. **`tools/deepwiki/ask.py` + `atlas.surface.json`** — NARRATE stage. Wraps deepwiki-open `GET /api/wiki_cache` (api/api.py:463) — reads the CACHED wiki (fast, deterministic), content-address = sha256 of the wiki JSON with volatile keys scrubbed. Mirrors code-recon orient: read-don't-regen. found:false (exit 0) when backend down / no cache (legit absence). Live RAG `--ask` (POST /chat/completions/stream) is STANDALONE-only, NEVER gateway-exposed (Ollama-slow blows 20s; stochastic = no stable address). `narrate` is the only exposed cap; live `ask` stays off the seam (st3gg-decode pattern).
3. **`tools/seam/run.py`** — `CARRY`/`NARRATE` stage lists + `PIPELINES` dict + `seam carry`/`seam narrate` subcommands (generalized the perceive tail). Stages are lists so a 2nd carrier/narrator can join without touching dispatch. `seam list` now 44 surfaces.
4. **`~/.claude/scripts/ledger/combo.py`** — scores tool TUPLES on top of the existing `tool-outcomes.jsonl` (no transcript re-parse; imports router.py for loader/reward/Beta — proven Brick-3 stays SoT). Two combo kinds: `cofire` (same turn, order-free, `a+b`) + `seq` (later turn, ORDER matters, `a>b`); reward = turn reward (cofire) / chain-end reward (seq). Thompson-ranks combos; `suggest`; `--evaluate` (combo vs single-skill vs random, same holdout, honest). Cold-start safe.
**DoD MET (proven live):** `seam carry tools/repo-inventory` → ok Receipt, sha `d660aeba…`; `seam narrate <repo>` → ok Receipt (absence live; found:true content-address proven hermetically in test); combo.py surfaces REAL combos (`code-recon+delta-scp` n=5 = the groundwork stack co-fire; `repo-inventory>delta-scp` seq). **26 seam tests (6 new) + 166 total atlas-map-api tests green.** Honest eval: on the tiny 93-row/5-holdout ledger the combo router does NOT beat random (same regime as the single-skill router, margin −0.06) BUT best combo +0.30 > best single skill 0.00 → pairing adds signal; N is the limit, not the mechanism.

## DONE 2026-06-27 (#2) — objective combo feed wired (`27da2e9`, option a)
- **Feed seam Receipts → ledger — SHIPPED.** Chose option (a): `tools/seam/run.py` `_append_ledger()` appends ONE objective row per tool after each perceive/carry/narrate manifest. **Per-run** synthetic session (`seam:<pipeline>:<target>`, NOT one global session — so combo.py's `hash(session)%10` holdout split can actually spread recurring cofire pairs across train/holdout; a constant session dumps them all in one bucket) + shared turn key = same `request` → combo.py's existing cofire grouping picks the combo up UNCHANGED (zero combo.py change). Reward is OBJECTIVE: all-receipts-ok → +1, any error → −1 (replaces the sentiment proxy for combos that actually ran = closes [[project_tool_outcome_ledger]] Brick-2 with a non-proxy signal). **Opt-in via `SEAM_LEDGER=1`** (`SEAM_LEDGER_PATH` overrides the file for hermetic tests) so unit/exploratory runs never pollute the real ledger; the appender is fail-safe (telemetry write error never crashes the seam run / flips its exit code). Furniture-rule guard added to `~/.claude/scripts/ledger/backfill.py` (`main()` preserves `source=="seam"` rows; a full re-mine writes `"w"` from transcripts only and would else wipe them — NOT committed, lives in the ~/.claude tree).
  - **PROOF (live, honest):** fed 12 `perceive --writes` runs over distinct small tool dirs → +36 objective rows (ledger 93→129, all all-ok +1). `combo.py --evaluate` now **BEATS random across 3 fixed splits** (`PYTHONHASHSEED=0/1/2` → margin **+0.57/+0.39/+0.19**); the pre-build floor was **−0.06 (did NOT beat random)**. Winning combo = `groundwork-cli+repo-inventory` / `code-recon+groundwork-cli` mean **+1.000 n=12** (the perceive stack's own cofire pairs). 3 new hermetic tests (gate-off / objective cofire shape / failed-combo penalty); **29 seam + 169 atlas-map-api green.** Real ledger backed up to scratchpad `tool-outcomes.backup.jsonl` (93 rows) before populating.
  - **HONEST GAP (the real next enrichment):** `margin_vs_single = +0.000` — the best COMBO only TIES the best singleton (`/groundwork-cli` +1.0), because an all-ok run credits +1 to each member too, so the singleton looks as good as the pair. Knowing *which pair* now beats random; it does NOT yet beat knowing *the single best tool*. To make combos beat singletons the reward must capture **combination-specific value** — a combo that succeeds where its members alone don't, or order-dependent payoff (Δ→R cheap vs R→Δ waste). That, not more rows, is what closes the loop. (Also: combo/router `--evaluate` use builtin `hash()` for the split → per-process-randomized; pin `PYTHONHASHSEED` for reproducible numbers. Pre-existing in the Brick-3 SoT, not fixed here.)

## DONE 2026-06-27 (#3) — combination-specific reward (synergy/lift) in combo.py
- **SHIPPED** (uncommitted; `combo.py` + `test_combo.py` live in `~/.claude/scripts/ledger/`, the untracked sibling tree). New **SYNERGY** metric: `lift(combo) = combo_mean_reward − best_member_solo_mean` — *does firing this combination beat just firing its best member alone?* `combo_synergy()` + `pick_synergy()` + a `combo.py synergy` ranking + synergy block in `--evaluate` (selection on TRAIN means, verdict measured on HOLDOUT, no leakage). This answers the precise "does pairing add value" question raw per-turn reward couldn't.
  - **PROOF:** 3 hermetic tests (`test_combo.py`): homogeneous all-ok → lift ~0 (honest zero, nothing to find); a rescue fixture (A fails alone, B fails alone, A+B succeed) → lift >0.5 and `pick_synergy` puts A+B on top; `evaluate` carries the synergy fields. Live on the real ledger the metric **correctly reads lift +0.000 on the seam perceive pairs** (homogeneous +1 data) AND **surfaced real organic synergy the old `margin_vs_single=0` was blind to**: `repo-inventory>code-recon` n=14 lift **+0.09** (orient→recon beats either alone), holdout synergy verdict **+0.37 "combo BEATS its members."** Caveat: the eval's top pick can be an n=1 exploration combo (`repo-inventory>jargonize`); the robust, well-sampled signal is `repo-inventory>code-recon` (n=14). Trust n, not just lift.
  - So the gap is mechanically closed: the router CAN now prefer combos with combination-specific value. It stays ~0 on the seam rows because those runs are genuinely homogeneous — to move THAT number, the seam feed needs heterogeneous outcomes (a stage that fails alone but the pipeline rescues), which is the CARRY heterogeneous-fidelity pass below.

## DONE 2026-06-27 (#5) — CARRY heterogeneous-fidelity pass SHIPPED (`17d2559`)
- **`seam zoom <repo> [--top K]`** — the 5-axes "zoom dial" in ONE content-addressed manifest: cheap read skeleton (repo-inventory + code-recon) over the WHOLE repo PLUS lossless repomix carries on only the top-K hot immediate subdirs. Hot-region selection = pure deterministic `_hot_regions` (rank by repo-inventory receipt `code_lines`, fallback `os.walk` file count). Reuses `_call`/`_emit`/`_append_ledger`/`_summary`; own dynamic branch (not a PIPELINES entry). A `SEAM_LEDGER=1` zoom records a richer skeleton+N-carries combo automatically (zero combo.py change).
- Built + adversarially verified via a **6-agent Workflow** (ground→build→verify). Verify caught a REAL bug: the #1 region `anatomy-research` (most LOC) made an EMPTY carry — it's git-ignored (`.gitignore:190`) and repomix honors .gitignore, so on-disk-LOC ranking and git-tracked carry disagreed. **Fixed inline** ([[code-as-furniture]]): `_carry_eligible()` drops wholly-git-ignored regions via `git check-ignore -- <regions>` (args form; `--stdin` returns nothing under subprocess on Windows git — gotcha). Fail-soft.
- **PROOF (live):** `seam zoom tools --top 2` → skeleton + 2 NON-empty content-addressed carries (anatomy-extension 27, fest-reconcile 49); git-ignored anatomy-research dropped. **+8 hermetic tests; seam 37 / full 177 green;** ledger unpolluted.

- **NARRATE found:true — PROVEN LIVE** (parallel /groundwork session, RECON #4): `seam narrate https://github.com/lionestenzol/groundwork-cli` → ok receipt, join key `acb74b45…`, deterministic. Boot = `deepwiki-open/.venv` python `-m api.main` with `NODE_ENV=production` (NOT `uv run` from root — that empty-env mistake misled my workflow's boot agent too).

## DONE 2026-06-27 (#4 — /groundwork+/autopilot resume; NARRATE live + per-receipt reward SHIPPED `3abdf2d`)
Verified at HEAD, two findings that REWRITE the "prove NARRATE found:true" + "combos beat singletons" plan:
- **NARRATE found:true — PROVEN LIVE (Seq 01 DONE).** Earlier this session I wrongly diagnosed deepwiki-open as a "wiped/broken env" — that was OPERATOR ERROR: I started it with `uv run` from the repo root, which (no `pyproject.toml` at root) spun up an empty ephemeral env. The real fully-populated venv is `deepwiki-open/.venv` (186 pkgs, built 2026-06-26, adalflow/ollama/google/faiss all present). **Correct boot:** `cd deepwiki-open && NODE_ENV=production PORT=8001 ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m api.main` (NOT `uv run`; NODE_ENV=production skips the dev `watchfiles` import). Backend → HTTP 200, "Application startup complete." Then cache a wiki via POST `/api/wiki_cache` (`WikiCacheRequest`: repo{owner,repo,type}, language, wiki_structure{id,title,description,pages[{id,title,content,filePaths,importance,relatedPages}]}, generated_pages{}, provider, model — schema `api/api.py:40-110`; fixture at scratchpad `seed_wiki.json`; cache file lands at `~/.adalflow/wikicache/deepwiki_cache_<type>_<owner>_<repo>_<lang>.json`). **PROOF:** `seam narrate https://github.com/lionestenzol/groundwork-cli` → receipt status **ok**, join key `acb74b45985b200f…`, deterministic (same sha twice). The found:true content-address lane works end-to-end through the gateway. (Real Ollama wiki GENERATION is still slow/out-of-band; this proves the seam READ+content-address lane, which is what NARRATE exposes.)
- **CRITICAL — the plan's premise was false: NARRATE can't create reward variance.** `gateway.py:308` sets a CLI receipt `ok = proc.returncode == 0`, and `ask.py` `read_wiki` ALWAYS exits 0 (even found:false). So a NARRATE cache-MISS records as a **successful (ok) receipt**, identical to a hit → the objective combo feed gets +1 either way → NARRATE failure was never going to give the discriminating signal `margin_vs_single=0` needs. The live backend was the wrong lever. **The real small fix:** make a data-level absence a non-ok outcome for the LEDGER reward — either (a) `read_wiki` returns `_exit:1` on found:false (but the surface deliberately treats absence as legit ok for READ semantics — would change that contract), or cleaner (b) `run.py` reward inspects each receipt's tool-data `found`/`ok` (not just manifest all-ok) so a pipeline with a NARRATE-miss scores below one with a hit. That + the CARRY heterogeneous-fidelity rescue pass (a stage that fails alone but the pipeline rescues) are the only things that move `margin_vs_single` off 0. ~10-line change in `tools/seam/run.py` `_ledger_rows`, NOT a backend resurrection.
- **SHIPPED the fix from finding-2 (`3abdf2d`, option b + the combo-side half).** `tools/seam/run.py`: new `_receipt_ok(r)` = status ok AND has sha256; `_ledger_rows` now scores reward PER-RECEIPT (a tool that ran but content-addressed nothing — NARRATE found:false — scores -1, not the old manifest-shared +1). `~/.claude/scripts/ledger/combo.py`: cofire reward = `min(member_a, member_b)` over the turn's per-row rewards (was `router._row_reward(t[0])` shared) — a pair drags to its weakest member. Homogeneous all-ok data unchanged (min of equals). **PROOF (scratchpad `prove_combo.py`, same runs both ways):** a deepwiki that delivers half the time → OLD singleton +1.00 / reliable-pair vs narrator-pair TIED at +1.00 (scorer blind); NEW singleton +0.00 / reliable-pair +1.00 vs narrator-pair +0.00 → separation +1.00 DISTINGUISHABLE. Tests: +1 seam (`...penalizes_ok_receipt_with_no_join_key`), rewrote the old all-or-nothing test to per-receipt (`...scores_each_receipt_for_its_own_outcome`), +1 combo (`...weakest_member...`); **seam 38 / full atlas-map-api 178 / combo 4 green.** combo.py/test_combo.py live in the untracked `~/.claude/scripts/ledger` tree (NOT in `3abdf2d`, which is run.py + test_seam.py only).
- **SHIPPED the `full` combined pipeline (`299adf0`).** `run.py` `FULL=[repo-inventory inventory, code-recon orient, repomix pack, deepwiki narrate]` (writes-gated gw index excluded) + `PIPELINES["full"]` + `seam full <localpath>` subcommand. This is the run shape that finally CO-FIRES deepwiki with the structural tools in ONE manifest (perceive/carry/narrate alone never co-fire it). +1 test (`...full_pipeline_cofires...`), updated the PIPELINES registry assertion; seam 39 green. **GOTCHA:** the gateway charset guard rejects SPACES in the target path — `seam full "C:/Users/bruke/Pre Atlas/tools/seam"` errors ALL tools; use a relative path (`seam full tools/seam`) or a space-free root. (Same family as the `*`-charset rejection.)
- **LIVE PROOF the scorer now discriminates (`prove_combo.py` + real `seam full` over 6 tool dirs, temp ledger):** seeded fixture wikis for 3 dirs (seam/binre/repo-inventory → narrate found:true) and left 3 uncached (deepwiki/repomix/code-recon → found:false). Real `seam full` rows: repo-inventory **+1** (always a join key), repomix **+1**, deepwiki **+1 on cached / -1 on uncached** (mean +0.00), code-recon **-1** (orient returns ok-but-NULL-sha on these tiny dirs — the SAME ok-but-empty absence as deepwiki found:false, correctly penalized → the rule generalizes beyond deepwiki). Best combo `repo-inventory+repomix` **+1.00** vs deepwiki singleton **+0.00** → real separation. Under the OLD manifest-shared rule every receipt was status-ok → all +1, everything TIED. The combo scorer can now rank combinations.
- **The genuinely-remaining piece:** this proves discrimination on a TEMP ledger with seeded fixtures. To move `margin_vs_single` on the REAL ledger you'd (a) populate real (Ollama-generated, not fixture) wikis for the repos you actually run, and (b) make `seam full` part of the normal feed (e.g. post-commit, or the router calling it). The mechanism is done + proven; what's left is operating it on real data over time.
- Fest shell `combo-reward-narrate-CR0003` created (planning/, 3 seqs: narrate_live_proof / discriminating_combo_data / prove_margin_vs_single_positive); `fest validate` fails on unfilled template markers only. Watchfiles installed into deepwiki root .venv (harmless no-op).

## project_tool_outcome_ledger

<a id="project-tool-outcome-ledger"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_tool_outcome_ledger.md`*
*Name:* project-tool-outcome-ledger
*Description:* Retroactive reward ledger for Claude skills/tools — backfill DONE; router built but FAILS beats-random eval; live hook unwired; Brick 2 enrichment is the real gap

The "compounding leverage / OP ninja" build (Bruke, 2026-06-25). Every tool reports "I ran," never "I did well," so spamming /weapon can't compound. Fix = a shared outcome ledger fed by a reward signal, then a router that biases what fires next.

**Shipped (v1 backfill):** `~/.claude/scripts/ledger/backfill.py` (stdlib only) → writes `~/.claude/logs/tool-outcomes.jsonl`. Mines past transcripts under `~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl` (skips `subagents/`). Unit = one invocation (a `Skill` tool_use OR a user message starting with `/`). **Reward = sentiment of the NEXT human message** after that turn (blunt keyword classifier: NEG/POS/APPROVE; "go"/"yes"/"continue" = approve = +1). Proxy, noisy, fuzzy credit-assignment — but noisy labels beat zero.

**Two bugs the sanity-check caught (DO NOT trust first numbers):**
1. Harness-injected skill bodies pose as user messages → carry **`isMeta: True`**. Filter them or a skill's own SKILL.md prose ("NOT/fail/stop") scores as negative feedback (code-recon falsely netted -42).
2. `<task-notification>`, pasted errors, "Execute Wave 1…" are machine text not feedback → `is_noise()` prefix filter.

**v1 result (75 rows, 211 transcripts):** code-recon net +3 (most-fired, earns its spam), jargonize +2, weapon +1, show -1 (a real /show failure). Most rows neutral (next msg is a task-continuation, not a verdict) → signal is thin per-skill; N is small.

**Honest limits:** proxy reward only; neutral-heavy; retrospectives (`~/.claude/logs/session-retrospectives/`, 173 files) are mostly EMPTY stubs, useful only as a session→transcript index, NOT pre-digested gold (earlier overclaim corrected).

**UPDATE 2026-06-25 PM (verified vs code, not memory):** `router.py` (`pick_tool()` Thompson bandit, cold-start safe, `--evaluate`, `suggest`) AND `append_outcome.py` (live Stop-hook appender) NOW EXIST (written 08:53–08:58). Plus `DOD.md` (4-brick proof spec) + `HOOK_SETUP.md`. So Bricks 3+4 are CODE-COMPLETE but **unverified/unwired**:
- **Brick 4 FAILS its own proof:** `python router.py --evaluate` → router +0.3000 vs random +0.3875, **margin −0.0875 → does NOT beat random** on the 74-row ledger. `suggest` currently returns `/schedule` off a single n=1 arm (exploration noise, not earned reward).
- **Brick 3 WIRED 2026-06-25:** `append_outcome.py` appended to the `settings.json` `Stop` array (`async`, `timeout:10`, fail-safe; settings re-validated JSON). Verified idempotent — ran twice on an already-mined transcript → `added 0` both times (B3.3 dedup holds). B3.2 (auto-append on a fresh session) self-verifies at next session end.
- **Brick 2 still unbuilt** = the real gap. `router.py` already reads `reward_score` first (plug-in ready); enrichment (shipped/retried/fused) is what makes signal sturdier than sentiment so eval can pass.

**Real NEXT:** wire Stop hook → ledger grows live → build Brick 2 → re-run `--evaluate` until margin>0 → surface `suggest` gated on n. Full plan in `Pre Atlas\BEARINGS_2026-06-25.md` §5. Reward should ultimately be proof-gate pass (droplist done_conditions, fest verify), not just sentiment. Related: [[project-droplist-lifecycle-bricks]] (autofire substrate), continuous-learning-v2 skill (instinct confidence = another orphaned reward signal).

**UPDATE 2026-06-27 — combo layer added (`combo.py`):** new `~/.claude/scripts/ledger/combo.py` scores tool TUPLES (not single arms) on top of this same ledger — `cofire` (same-turn `a+b`) + `seq` (cross-turn ordered `a>b`); reuses router.py's loader/reward/Beta (no fork). `combo.py` / `suggest` / `--evaluate` (combo vs single-skill vs random). Surfaces real combos (`code-recon+delta-scp` n=5). Honest eval: doesn't beat random on the tiny ledger (same N-limit as the single-skill router) but best combo +0.30 > best single 0.00. This is the router-over-combinations the [[project_tool_lattice_architecture]] lattice needs. The Brick-2 enrichment gap is now ALSO the combo-enrichment gap: feed `seam` Receipt manifests (objective all-ok signal) → combo rows, so combos learn from Receipts not sentiment.

**UPDATE 2026-06-27 (#2) — OBJECTIVE FEED LANDED (`27da2e9`, Pre Atlas):** Brick-2's non-proxy signal is in for combos. `tools/seam/run.py` now appends one objective row per tool per perceive/carry/narrate run (`source:"seam"`, reward = all-receipts-ok → +1 / any error → −1, opt-in `SEAM_LEDGER=1`). `backfill.py` `main()` got a furniture-guard so a full re-mine preserves `source=="seam"` rows (else the `"w"` rewrite wipes them). Fed 12 perceive runs → +36 rows (ledger 93→129); **`combo.py --evaluate` now BEATS random** (margin +0.19..+0.57 across `PYTHONHASHSEED=0/1/2`, was −0.06). REMAINING gap: `margin_vs_single = 0` — combos only TIE the best singleton because all-ok credits +1 to each member; next = **combination-specific reward** (value a combo has beyond its members) in `combo.py`. Full record + the per-process `hash()` split caveat in [[project_tool_lattice_architecture]].

**UPDATE 2026-06-27 (#3) — SYNERGY/LIFT metric shipped (`combo.py`):** closes the `margin_vs_single=0` gap mechanically. `lift = combo_mean − best_member_solo_mean` (does the combo beat its best member alone?) via `combo_synergy()`/`pick_synergy()` + `combo.py synergy` ranking + a synergy block in `--evaluate` (train-selected, holdout-measured). 3 hermetic tests (`test_combo.py`): ~0 on homogeneous, >0.5 on a rescue fixture. Live it correctly reads lift +0.000 on the seam perceive pairs (homogeneous +1) AND surfaced real organic synergy `repo-inventory>code-recon` n=14 lift +0.09 (holdout +0.37) the old metric was blind to. Trust n over raw lift (top eval pick can be n=1 noise). To move the SEAM rows' synergy off 0, the feed needs heterogeneous outcomes (a stage that fails alone but the pipeline rescues) = the CARRY heterogeneous-fidelity pass.

## project_trading_probe

<a id="project-trading-probe"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_trading_probe.md`*
*Name:* project-trading-probe
*Description:* Automated trading bot grounded on existing Atlas engine; fest TP0001 scaffolded, tasks UNFILLED — fill next.

**Idea (2026-06-28):** Bruke's gestalt answer to "what shape is Atlas×ATM×UASC" → an **automated trading bot service, 2 kinds.** It's the engine he already built, given a *metabolism* (money). See [[project_atlas_atm_uasc_synthesis.md]].
- **Kind A — the probe (prop-firm path):** bot passes a funded-account eval (trade paper 3-5 days, hit targets inside strict rules → firm funds you → keep 80-90%).
- **Kind B — the SaaS:** sell signals, not capital. Users add an API key, the service sends trade-signal notifications. (Internal autotroph vs external wrapper = the keep-it-internal/sell-the-wrapper split.)

**Grounding (`/groundwork` run, file-cited, verified first-hand 2026-06-28):**
- **HAVE (assemble onto):** order-placement = uasc-executor `http` step (`executor.py:116`, urllib `:187-221`); position/PnL ledger = delta-kernel append-only deltas + `/api/state`; signal object+ingest = `/api/signals` + `handleSignalIngest` (`server.ts:2015`); signal push = delta-sync "No blobs" 220-byte (`delta-sync.ts:11,48`); job queue = `/api/work/*`; rules-engine shape = `/api/law/*` (`server.ts:899-1436`).
- **GAPS (greenfield — prior-art `es` sweep: ZERO trading code on machine):** market-data feed (cognitive-sensor reads CONVERSATIONS/OSINT, not prices), broker connector, strategy logic, backtester, trading risk-math (drawdown%/daily-loss — law engine is governance not risk), SaaS layer (billing/keys/delivery).
- **ATM relationship:** ATM is a DOC (`GEMINI_ATM_MAP.md`), not software. Only delta-kernel's sync engine is built. A trading bot is pure software = on the *buildable* side of ATM's hardware wall.

**THE REALITY CHECK (don't collapse these):** "probe" = TWO gates. Gate A = paper-trading probe (Alpaca paper, free, fully automatable, shippable today). Gate B = prop-firm funded eval = a **COMPLIANCE question, not code** — most futures firms (Apex/Topstep/FTMO) run NinjaTrader/Tradovate/Rithmic/MT5 and many **restrict/ban full automation**; read the specific firm's rulebook before assuming a Python bot can legally run the eval.

**Assemble-first (named):** data+paper broker = **Alpaca** (`alpaca-py`, free paper, equities+crypto, no gatekeeper); strategy/backtest = **nautilus-trader** (unifies backtest→paper→live) or **backtrader** (fastest today); NOT vectorbt (analysis, not a live loop). Smallest real loop: Alpaca price → SMA-cross → paper order → log fill as delta to `/api/signals` → max-daily-loss guard halts.

**DONE 2026-06-28:** fest **`trading-probe-TP0001`** FILLED + **validates 100/100**. Structure: phase `001_IMPLEMENT` → seq `01_paper_probe` → **7 file-cited tasks** (01 scaffold+alpaca-py · 02 alpaca paper smoke · 03 SMA-cross pure+pytest · 04 risk guard pure+pytest · 05 paper loop · 06 log fill→`/api/signals` · 07 e2e Gate-A proof) + 4 quality gates. Citations re-verified at HEAD (`server.ts:2015`/`:2000`/`:357`, `types-core.ts:85-86`, `executor.py:116`/`:176`). Gotchas recorded: `fest create sequence` with `--festival` drops the seq at festival ROOT — must `cd` into the phase dir; auto-link disabled in `fest.yaml` (`auto_link.enabled:false`) because target `services/trading-probe` lives in the Pre Atlas repo, not under the fest campaign root.

**NEXT:** still in `planning` status → `fest promote` to active, then execute. Tasks **01/03/04 need NO external creds** (pure Python + pytest — buildable anytime). Tasks **02/05/06/07 need free Alpaca paper keys (alpaca.markets) + delta-kernel running on :3001**. User parked at the fill checkpoint 2026-06-28 (dismissed the build-now/keys-first/bank fork) — wait for instruction. Broader synthesis artifact: `ATLAS_ATM_UASC_ANALYSIS.md`.

**2026-06-30 REFRAME (DO NOT FORGET):** This fest = built but **PARKED, not the move.** The trading-bot was Bruke's *gestalt recognition of Atlas's engine shape*, mistakenly literalized by me into a paper-trading product. Bruke called the drift ("are we losing the plot?", "we just had better-sounding naming, not a plan"). The real next move is NOT to ship a trading probe — it's **[[project_atlas_physical_leaves]]**: make Atlas's own action verbs physically bite the world (start with `@SEND_DRAFT` → ntfy.sh push to phone). Trading-probe stays parked unless explicitly revived; do not auto-resume it. The "what shape is Atlas" answer was about the *architecture*, not the *product*.

## project_transcript_index

<a id="project-transcript-index"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_transcript_index.md`*
*Name:* project-transcript-index
*Description:* SQLite index of every Claude Code tool call across all transcripts on disk, built to find repeatable duties worth scripting/agent-izing

Built 2026-07-15: `/transcript-index` skill (`~/.claude/skills/transcript-index/SKILL.md`) + engine `~/.claude/scripts/transcript_index/index_transcripts.py`. Incrementally parses every `.jsonl` transcript under `~/.claude/projects/**` (main sessions, subagents, workflow agents, workflow journals) into `~/.claude/logs/transcript_index/transcripts.db` — one row per `tool_use` event: tool name, file target, Bash command, timestamp, owning session/project.

**Why:** Bruke's stated goal — "look at what claude does and the codes and edits... so i can create scripts and python agents to fulfill the duties moving forward and reduce tokens and increase speed and efficiency." Sibling question surfaced twice same day (sessions `0d49c454` "does claude use less tokens if it uses scripts and py agents" and this one). This is the evidence layer for that: before hand-rolling automation, see what actually repeats.

**Distinct from `audit_sessions.py`** (also in `.claude/scripts/`) — that tool audits conversation *sentiment/topics* (frustration, success, abandonment, TF-IDF) and only counts tool calls per session. This indexes the tool calls themselves (what was actually run/edited), which `audit_sessions.py` never captured. Not a duplicate; checked before building ([assemble-first](../../../rules/common/assemble-first.md) / code-as-furniture — verified via Read before writing net-new code).

**First-run numbers (2026-07-15):** 1,100 transcript files, 31,048 tool calls, ~100s to index, DB well under 100MB. Top tools: Bash 11,215 · Read 7,203 · Edit 3,327 · Grep 1,974 · Write 1,496.

**Pattern-clustering pass (2026-07-15):** normalized Bash commands into shapes, tracked cross-session file repetition, checked tool-sequence bigrams/trigrams. Ruled out `python -c` one-liners (32 calls, 14 sessions — looked repetitive by count, but content is all distinct library probes, no shared shape). Confirmed 3 real candidates:
1. **SHIPPED** → [memory-write tool](project_memory_write_tool.md) — `MEMORY.md` touched in 94 distinct sessions (528 hits), a fixed 2-step save template. Mechanized.
2. **SHIPPED** → [register-preview-server tool](project_register_preview_server.md) — `.claude/launch.json`, 33 sessions/95 hits, verified every edit is the same Read-then-insert-JSON-block shape. First run found 2 real live port collisions (`3011`, `8897`) predating the tool — **RESOLVED 2026-07-15** (ws-gateway→3013, c110-trace→8901; propagated to atlas-manifest.yaml + 3 other living docs).
3. **SHIPPED** → [file-inspection-nudge hook](project_file_inspection_nudge_hook.md) — `PreToolUse` advisory for the cat/tail/grep-via-Bash anti-pattern (~140 calls); scope evidence-checked (kept cat/head/tail/grep, dropped sed/awk/echo for lack of real occurrences). Registered in `settings.json`, takes effect next session.

`cd` was 62% of all Bash calls (6,901/11,215) — mechanical shell hygiene, correctly excluded from clustering rather than treated as a "duty."

**All 3 proposals from this pass now SHIPPED (2026-07-15/16).** Thread closed unless a new pattern-clustering pass surfaces more candidates — re-run `python index_transcripts.py --report` (incremental) periodically and re-cluster if the corpus has grown meaningfully.

Related: [DoD on every message](feedback_every_message_has_dod.md), [tools beat paper](feedback_tools_must_beat_paper.md), [memory-write tool](project_memory_write_tool.md), [register-preview-server tool](project_register_preview_server.md), [file-inspection-nudge hook](project_file_inspection_nudge_hook.md).

## project_triage_loop_trace

<a id="project-triage-loop-trace"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_triage_loop_trace.md`*
*Name:* project-triage-loop-trace
*Description:* The Atlas triage/close loop is built end-to-end in code but dormant — two triage engines (TS daemon + Python arm), gates default-off, Python arm unscheduled

Static trace at HEAD (2026-06-20, branch experiment/droplist-remediation-2026-06-15) of the "intake → sort → route → close" loop Bruke feels "runs but doesn't do." The code chain is INTACT — no broken rungs in code. What stops it from acting is scheduling + default-off gates, not missing code.

**The chain (cognitive-sensor Python arm):**
- producer: `cycleboard/brain/machine_scan.json` (es_scan/loops)
- Rung 1: `auto_triage.py` → POST Optogon `:3010` → `proposed_verdict/action/confidence`
- Rung 2 (Arm A, close): `auto_triage.py:168 _apply_verdict` → `thread_decisions.json`. Gate: `AUTO_TRIAGE_APPLY=1` + conf ≥ 0.85 (`AUTO_TRIAGE_MIN_CONFIDENCE`)
- Rung 3 (Arm B, execute): `auto_triage.py:189 from cortex_bridge import emit` → `cortex_bridge.py` builds Directive.v1 → POST Cortex `:3009`. Fires ONLY when `proposed_verdict=="ARCHIVE"` + in-repo evidence (`_within_repo`). Gates: `CORTEX_BRIDGE_APPLY=1` → proposals.json, then `CORTEX_BRIDGE_RUN_PROPOSAL=1` → spawn proposal_runner
- Rung 4 (loop close): `decisions_to_atlas.py` reads `thread_decisions.json` → prunes `governance_state.open_loops` + `loops_latest.json` so CycleBoard stops re-surfacing closed cards

**Four reasons it doesn't "do" anything (ranked):**
1. **The Python arm is UNSCHEDULED.** Verified 2 angles: nothing in repo (.ps1/.cmd/.bat/.json/.py/.sh) invokes `auto_triage`/`decisions_to_atlas`/`atlas_triage_cli` except the files themselves. Runs only when Bruke hand-types `at` (at.cmd on PATH, outside repo). So the dormant loop is hand-cranked.
2. **Three stacked default-off env gates** between a card and a real file action: `AUTO_TRIAGE_APPLY` → `CORTEX_BRIDGE_APPLY` → `CORTEX_BRIDGE_RUN_PROPOSAL`, all "0" by default.
3. **Execute arm is ARCHIVE-only** — CLOSE/DROP/KEEP update state but never produce an action (correct: they don't need a file op).
4. **decisions_to_atlas also hand-cranked** — so closed verdicts don't prune → cards reappear → the "reminds me it's not done" feeling.

**The twist (proves Bruke's "it's all droplist / all recursive" thesis):** TWO triage engines, same primitive, two languages —
- `delta-kernel/src/governance/governance_daemon.ts` preparation job (TS, runs every 5 min via `PREPARATION_CRON */5`) → taskTriage → `complete_task` auto-tier. THIS is the every-5-min popup cadence (heartbeat+preparation both `*/5`), NOT the Python arm.
- `cognitive-sensor/auto_triage.py` (Python) → Optogon → thread_decisions + Cortex. Dormant.

**Rails exist:** optogon `:3010` and cortex `:3009` ARE in `.claude/launch.json` autostart (29 entries total, incl. triage-server, thread-cards).

**The reversible ladder to turn "runs" into "does"** (when there's an afternoon): (1) schedule Python arm in DRY-RUN, watch proposals accumulate; (2) review a week; (3) flip `AUTO_TRIAGE_APPLY` (state-only, reversible) + run decisions_to_atlas after so cards leave & stop reappearing; (4) last & deliberate: flip Cortex execute gates for ARCHIVE-only, in-repo, watched.

Related: [[project-droplist-packet-engine]] (the most-evolved instance of this same primitive), [[project-atlas-lattice-seam]], [[project-optogon-stack]]. Part of the "islands → country" arc — the country is one sort-core fed by many mouths, shown through inPACT (the chosen single face).

## project_uasc_bridge

<a id="project-uasc-bridge"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_uasc_bridge.md`*
*Name:* UASC-M2M → Delta-Kernel Bridge
*Description:* Executor bridge connecting delta-kernel governance to UASC-M2M command execution (port 3008)

UASC-M2M research project promoted to `services/uasc-executor/` (port 3008) and wired into delta-kernel via executor bridge.

**Why:** Delta-kernel decides what actions to take (mode routing, prepared actions, drafts) but had no execution layer. UASC-M2M could execute commands but had no governance brain. Connecting them closes the "last mile" gap.

**How to apply:**
- `services/uasc-executor/` — Python HTTP server, 7 commands: @WORK, @BUILD, @DEPLOY, @CLEAN, @WRAP, @CLOSE_LOOP, @SEND_DRAFT
- `services/delta-kernel/src/core/executor-bridge.ts` — Maps ActionType → UASC token, signs requests, fires them
- `services/delta-kernel/src/api/server.ts` — New endpoints: GET/POST /api/actions/pending, POST /api/actions/confirm/:id, POST /api/actions/cancel/:id, GET /api/executor/health
- Auth: HMAC-SHA256, client_id=delta-kernel, shared secret for local IPC
- Flow: PendingAction PENDING → confirm → bridge fires UASC token → result logged to timeline
- Profiles are JSON in `services/uasc-executor/profiles/`
- run_all.ps1 updated to launch both services

## project_wall_panel_sync

<a id="project-wall-panel-sync"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_wall_panel_sync.md`*
*Name:* project-wall-panel-sync
*Description:* NEXT MISSION — make the monitor wall a synced PANEL in the system-map (not a full-screen takeover), syncing selection both ways with the graph

**Scoped 2026-06-20. The wall ([[project-item-backbone]] sibling) is comprehensive + sortable (`9972120`) but is a full-screen TAKEOVER mode. Bruke wants it to be a PANEL that coexists with graph + file-preview and SYNCS selection with the graph like the other panels.**

## SHIPPED 2026-06-20 — bricks 1+2 (`ae9e00d` generated, `54d43f3` template)
Wall is now a **dockable 5th panel** (full-width row in `.panels`, closed by default, full affordance pack min/max/drawer/close + restore chip, lazy-loaded iframe). **Two-way sync** via the existing `setSelection()` hub + `postMessage`: graph→wall focuses a subsystem's tiles (verified: delta-kernel → its 6 surfaces), wall→graph selects+highlights the node (verified: tile click → ai-exec-pipeline node). Join = path segment (`services|apps|tools/<name>`) via new `subsysOf()` in wall.html — NOT `/map/locate` (in-page derive is simpler, no fetch). Critical: ported to the `_build_map.py` TEMPLATE (rebuild produces byte-identical output) so it survives regen — DONE criterion #4 met. Did NOT use SNAP_PRESETS/"ops" layout from the original plan — the wall is an independent row orthogonal to the 2×2 quad, cleaner than cramming 5 into 2×2.
**Brick 3 (thumbnails on graph nodes) NOT done** — deferred by Bruke as its own pass. Plan: add `'background-image':'data(thumb)'` to the `node[w]` cy style (system-map ~line 2112) + `thumb` field in `buildServiceElements()`; needs a headless screenshot pipeline for 33 subsystems + a regen story (code-as-furniture, don't let thumbs rot). Note: the preview_screenshot MCP tool times out on the system-map page (cytoscape canvas + iframes), so thumbnail capture needs playwright/headless, not that tool.
Verification gotcha: wall.html `view`/`ALL` are `let`-scoped (NOT on `window`); read tiles via DOM, not `iframe.contentWindow.view`. `subsysOf`/`tileClick` ARE on window (function declarations).

## Current state (where the code is)
- `apps/lattice/wall.html` — comprehensive wall: fetches `GET /map/surfaces` (50 surfaces, every served HTML), sort (group/name/recent) + filter, start-all (`POST /map/launch/{name}`), `#focus=<name>` jump. Cross-origin from the map (wall on `localhost`, map often `127.0.0.1`) — display + hash + postMessage all work cross-origin.
- system-map (`audit/imports/_build_map.py` TEMPLATE → generates `apps/lattice/system-map.html` + audit/ + atlas/pages/): panel grid of 4 panels — `graph`, `detail` (file preview), `tree`, `list` — arranged via `SNAP_PRESETS` (2×2 slots TL/TR/BL/BR) with min/max/drawer/close per panel. Wall is currently a full-screen overlay: `#wall-view` (fixed inset:0), `toggleWall()`, `openMonitor(id)` sets the wall iframe to `wall.html#focus=<id>`.

## DONE criteria
1. Wall is a PANEL (not full-screen) that can sit in the grid alongside graph + file-preview (its own slot, min/max/drawer/close).
2. Select a service node in the graph → its tile highlights in the wall panel (if open).
3. Click a tile in the wall → the corresponding graph node selects + centers.
4. Survives rebuild (in the `_build_map.py` template).

## Steps
1. **Wall as a panel:** add a `data-panel="wall"` element (panel head + the wall iframe) to the template. The 2×2 `SNAP_PRESETS` are built for exactly 4 panels — DON'T cram 5 into 2×2. Add a dedicated layout preset (e.g. "ops": wall takes one big slot + graph the other), and let the wall panel participate in the panel-action system (min/max/drawer/close). Retire/keep `toggleWall` as the preset switch.
2. **postMessage bridge** (cross-origin, validate `event.origin`):
   - parent→wall: on graph node select (`setSelection`), `wallFrame.contentWindow.postMessage({type:'focus', name}, '*')`; wall's message handler focuses the tile (reuse `focusFromHash` logic).
   - wall→parent: on tile click, `parent.postMessage({type:'select', name, file}, '*')`; parent maps surface→subsystem via `GET /map/locate?file=<tile.file>` → selects + centers that graph node.
3. **Surface→node mapping:** a wall tile is a FILE/surface; the graph node is a SUBSYSTEM. Use `/map/locate` (already exists) on the tile's `file` to get the owning subsystem, then select that node. (Many surfaces map to a subsystem; some top-level ones won't — no-op gracefully.)
4. Rebuild, verify both sync directions in browser, review, commit.

## Risks / notes
- The panel grid CSS + `SNAP_PRESETS` assume 4 panels — the 5th-panel integration is the hard part. A separate "ops" layout (wall + graph only) is lower-risk than a true 5-panel grid.
- Don't start at the tail of a long session — Bruke + Claude agreed this needs fresh context (it's a real panel-architecture change). This session already shipped: live state, drive, item-backbone bricks 1-3, lattice workflow render, cross-nav, inPACT feed, droplist-ui serve, monitor wall + map mode + node→tile jump + comprehensive surfaces. Commits `e0dea1c`→`9972120`.

## project_weapon_skill

<a id="project-weapon-skill"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke-Pre-Atlas/memory/project_weapon_skill.md`*
*Name:* Weapon Skill
*Description:* Autonomous project execution skill (/weapon). Takes a target, defines DONE, executes step-by-step, validates, closes. Integrates with Atlas (auto-targeting) and Festival (task tracking).

The Weapon skill (`~/.claude/skills/weapon/SKILL.md`) is an autonomous execution engine invoked via `/weapon`.

**Why:** User's documented weakness is `start → jump → switch → stall → stop`. Weapon forces `start → step → step → step → finish`. Unlike Project Finisher (which only plans), Weapon EXECUTES.

**How to apply:**
- `/weapon` — resume or auto-target from Atlas governance_state.json
- `/weapon <path>` — target a specific project
- `/weapon status` — check progress
- `/weapon abort` — pause and save state

4 phases: SPEC EXTRACTION → EXECUTION PLAN → EXECUTION LOOP → CLOSURE
State files: `.weapon/` directory (mission.json, spec.md, plan.md, progress.jsonl, closed.md)
Integrates with Festival (fest next/complete) and Atlas (mode-aware targeting)

# zoekt → native Windows binary (no WSL/Docker) — 2026-07-22

Bruke wanted to "install sourcegraph 'z' tool" (= **zoekt**, Sourcegraph's trigram
code-search engine) per the DECIDED SEQUENCE in [[project_atlas_rag_context_engine]]
(Sourcegraph FIRST, Docker-free). Native `go install` FAILS on Windows: the `index`
package is Unix-only. Verified two ways: 3/3 binaries fail to build, and
`index/indexfile.go` is gated `//go:build linux||darwin||freebsd||netbsd`.

Ran it through /groundwork + /code-recon (audit mode). `go list -deps` on the 3 CLI
binaries → 22-package closure; the ENTIRE Unix surface inside it was **one gated file
+ two `unix.Umask` lines** (`indexfile.go`, `builder.go`) — nothing hiding in
gitindex/search/query/ctags/grpc/tenant. = fest-style clean port, not a rabbit hole.
(The indexserver/webserver Unix baggage is OUT of the 3-CLI closure.)

## The port (3 files, +88/-7, builds windows/amd64 clean)
- `index/indexfile_windows.go` (NEW, `//go:build windows`): memory-backed
  `NewIndexFile` — reads the shard into a `[]byte` instead of `unix.Mmap`. The
  `IndexFile` iface only needs Read/Size/Close/Name and Read is a pure slice, so it's
  behavior-equivalent (trades address space for heap; fine for local indexes). Future
  upgrade = Windows `CreateFileMapping`/`MapViewOfFile` for true mmap.
- `index/umask_unix.go` (NEW, unix build tag): moved builder.go's umask `init()` here;
  the `umask` var defaults to 0 on Windows (no umask concept).
- `index/builder.go` (EDIT): dropped the now-unused `golang.org/x/sys/unix` import.

## Locations
- **Binaries (on PATH):** `C:\Users\bruke\go\bin\{zoekt,zoekt-index,zoekt-git-index}.exe`
  (copied into go/bin which is already on PATH — callable bare, fest pattern).
- **Also at:** `C:\Users\bruke\zoekt-win\bin\`
- **Source clone:** `C:\Users\bruke\zoekt-win\src` (upstream `sourcegraph/zoekt` @ `3c8b39b1`),
  port committed on local branch **`windows-port`** (NOT pushed — not our repo; fork to
  lionestenzol + push if we want to preserve it, like fest-win did).
- **Rebuild:** `cd C:\Users\bruke\zoekt-win\src; CGO_ENABLED=0 go build -o C:\Users\bruke\zoekt-win\bin\zoekt.exe ./cmd/zoekt` (+ zoekt-index, zoekt-git-index).

## Proven (not just compiled)
`zoekt-index -index <dir> <corpus>` produced a `corpus_v16.00000.zoekt` shard;
`zoekt -index_dir <dir> <query>` returned correct hits for a literal token AND an RE2
regex (`quick.*fox`). Write path (builder) + read path (memIndexFile) both live.

## Free win: symbol ranking
**universal-ctags 6.1.0 is on PATH** → zoekt auto-uses it for symbol-aware ranking
(function/class defs outrank text hits). This is the capability google/codesearch and
ripgrep CANNOT do — the reason to prefer zoekt for big code repos.

## MCP wrapper — DONE 2026-07-22 (registered ✔ Connected)
- `C:\Users\bruke\zoekt-win\zoekt_mcp.py` — FastMCP stdio server (official `mcp` SDK,
  `C:\Python313\python.exe`). Tools: `zoekt_search(query,max_results)` (pass-through
  zoekt query language: regex, `sym:`, `file:`, `lang:`, `case:`) + `zoekt_status()`.
  Shells `C:\Users\bruke\go\bin\zoekt.exe -index_dir <INDEX> <query>`.
- Registered USER scope: `claude mcp add zoekt -s user -- C:\Python313\python.exe C:\Users\bruke\zoekt-win\zoekt_mcp.py`
  (in `~/.claude.json`; `claude mcp list` = ✔ Connected). Tools `mcp__zoekt__*` appear
  NEXT session (MCP loads at session start), like atlas-rag did.
- `C:\Users\bruke\zoekt-win\reindex.py` — repeatable index builder. Uses **zoekt-index
  (filesystem)**, NOT zoekt-git-index: go-git rejects the `worktreeconfig` extension
  (Pre Atlas + STRUDEL use git worktrees). IGNORE_DIRS excludes node_modules/build/
  `.atlas-logs` etc. (running Atlas services hold `.atlas-logs\*.log` open → a locked
  file aborts the whole index).
- **Indexed (`C:\Users\bruke\zoekt-win\index`):** STRUDEL (17k files, 113MB) +
  pre-atlas/delta-scp (1459 files, 46MB). Search PROVEN: plain + regex + `file:` scoping
  return real `file:line` hits.
