## SESSION 2 (2026-07-22 pt2) — both follow-ups CLOSED, run through /groundwork
Ran the parked NEXT through /groundwork (Stage-1 recon first-hand, file:line cited; plan
locked with Bruke before any build). Two open items fixed + code-recon wired.

**#1 Pre Atlas clean index — DONE.** Root cause of the earlier 880MB/3-shard balloon +
timeout was NOT tree size per se — it was gitignored DATA dirs riding along (crucix
submodule's own 27MB node_modules, `data/`, backups, corpora). Fix = beef up
`-ignore_dirs`. zoekt-index matches ignore names by **basename at any depth**
(`cmd/zoekt-index/main.go:51`), so one name covers every nested copy. Built the ignore set
as **git's ignored-dir basenames MINUS basenames that also hold tracked source** (compare
`git status --ignored` vs `git ls-files` path segments). Critically EXCLUDED from the
ignore list: `data, fixtures, skills, tools` — Pre Atlas tracks real source under those
(e.g. `tools/lattice/run_chain.py`), so a basename ignore would drop it. Result: **48s,
one 193MB shard** (was 880MB/3 tmp shards that never finalized). Verified: MCP returns real
`C:\Users\bruke\Pre Atlas\...` file:line hits. Baked into `reindex.py` (+ per-repo filter
arg, + stale-`.tmp` cleanup).

**#2 `sym:` symbol search — FIXED (was TWO bugs).** (a) zoekt does
`exec.LookPath("universal-ctags")` (`index/builder.go:292`) but Windows has only
`ctags.exe` → 0 symbols. zoekt honors a `CTAGS_COMMAND` env override (`builder.go:288`) —
point it at `C:\mingw64\bin\ctags.exe`, no rebuild. (b) THEN it still hung: upstream
go-ctags drives ctags as one **persistent interactive pipe** (`--_interactive=default`)
whose stdout never flushes back on Windows → 60s timeout, 0 symbols/file. ctags itself is
fine (proved batch AND interactive directly). Fix = **batch mode**: new
`src/internal/ctags/batch.go` (implements the `goctags.Parser` iface) drives ctags one
process per file on the real on-disk path; `internal/ctags/parser.go` routes to it when
`runtime.GOOS=="windows"`. Rebuilt zoekt-index. Proven: **2588 symbols/2s** (was 0/60s);
`sym:WorkController` → `work-controller.ts:243:export class WorkController`. `reindex.py`
now defaults **ctags ON** (`ZOEKT_CTAGS=0` to skip).

**code-recon wired.** Added an "Indexed-search fast path — zoekt" section to
`~/.claude/skills/code-recon/SKILL.md` (after the atlas-map rung, before Step 0): zoekt =
persistent twin of `rg`+`ctags` for the 3 indexed repos; `sym:`=ctags, bare/regex=rg;
**fall back to rg for un-indexed repos OR uncommitted/just-edited code** (index is a
snapshot). This is the persistent-SEARCH complement to Step 0's persistent-MAP skeleton.

**Caveat (verified 2026-07-22, corrected — in code-recon too):** `sym:` is best-effort, NOT
a complete symbol oracle; absence ≠ code absence. universal-ctags' TS parser can DESYNC on
some construct mid-file and then silently drop EVERY remaining symbol in that file. Measured
on delta-kernel `cockpit.ts`: only **3 of 15 exported functions tagged** — `createPendingAction`
(line 499) and all functions after it missing, though each has a normal `export async function`
sig that tags FINE in isolation. NOTE: I first (wrongly) blamed "multiline signatures" — proved
false: multiline/async/default-param/object-return all tag fine standalone; the trigger is a
stateful-parser desync earlier in the file (exact construct not chased — rabbit hole, doesn't
change the caveat). Our batch port is faithful — it returns every tag ctags emits; the gap is
upstream ctags. Practical rule: an empty `sym:` can hide whole file-tails of real code — always
confirm with bare/rg before any absence claim. (Fix path if ever needed: scip-ctags via
SCIP_CTAGS_COMMAND has a stronger TS parser, but that's a separate Rust binary to build.)

  risk = MSVC C toolchain for tree-sitter grammars.
- **One-shot mode EXISTS** (my "interactive-only" fear was false): `scip-syntax` bin has a clap
  CLI `index files <files>` / `index workspace <dir>` / `index tar` — "Index source files using
  Tree Sitter parser", writes a SCIP index (`-o ./index.scip`). Plus an `evaluate` subcommand.
- **TWO binaries:** `scip-ctags` (ctags-protocol, interactive — what zoekt wants via
  SCIP_CTAGS_COMMAND, but interactive→Windows pipe deadlock like universal-ctags had) vs
  `scip-syntax index` (one-shot, tree-sitter, but outputs SCIP protobuf not ctags JSON).
- **zoekt wiring is opt-in:** LanguageMap must route `typescript`→ScipCTags + SCIP_CTAGS_COMMAND
  (builder.go:111,317; ctags.go:58; default LanguageMap empty→all universal). parser.go
  newParserProcess drives BOTH types via go-ctags interactive pipe → scip-ctags-the-bin would
  deadlock on Windows unless given the same batch treatment as batch.go.

## scip-ctags upgrade — TS/TSX symbols via tree-sitter (2026-07-22, DONE+LIVE-VERIFIED)

Universal-ctags batch mode tags .ts/.tsx poorly (regex-based, misses most exported decls:
**3/15** exported funcs on `delta-kernel/src/core/cockpit.ts`). Fix = route TS/TSX to
Sourcegraph's **scip-ctags** (tree-sitter based). Built native-Windows from
`sourcegraph-public-snapshot` — sparse+shallow clone at `C:\Users\bruke\scip-ctags-src`,
build dir `docker-images/syntax-highlighter/`, `cargo build --release --bin scip-ctags`
(pinned Rust 1.78.0 via rust-toolchain.toml + MSVC). Binary staged at
**`C:\Users\bruke\zoekt-win\scip-ctags.exe`** (34MB). GOTCHA: first build died out-of-disk
(needs ~3-4GB target/ headroom; had ~1GB) — freed space, 27GB, done. `scip-syntax` is a
SEPARATE workspace crate (`crates/scip-syntax`, needs `-p` not `--bin`); scip-ctags builds
from the ROOT package.

Wiring (all in `C:\Users\bruke\zoekt-win\src`): the zoekt framework already had a `ScipCTags`
parser type + bin map + `SCIP_CTAGS_COMMAND` env, just UNUSED (no CLI language-map flag → map
empty → everything fell to universal). 3 changes:
1. **`internal/ctags/scip_batch.go`** (NEW): `scipBatchParser` — spawns scip-ctags per file,
   speaks its interactive protocol (`{"command":"generate-tags","filename":..,"size":N}\n`
   + N content bytes on stdin; newline-JSON `_type:tag` replies on stdout), closes stdin →
   clean exit. Mirrors batch.go's proven per-file-spawn (NOT the persistent pipe that
   deadlocks on Windows). Safe because scip-ctags flushes stdout per-request (ctags.rs:268).
2. **`internal/ctags/parser.go`**: on Windows route `ScipCTags` → `newScipBatchParser` (was
   only handling `UniversalCTags`).
3. **`index/builder.go` SetDefaults**: when a scip binary is present + no explicit LanguageMap,
   default `{"typescript":ScipCTags, "tsx":ScipCTags}`. **JavaScript deliberately NOT routed**
   — go-enry lumps .js/.jsx/.mjs/.cjs as "JavaScript" but scip only knows the .js extension
   (verified against `ParserId::from_file_extension`), so routing the language would drop
   .jsx/.mjs/.cjs symbols. Routing is by enry LANGUAGE name (normalized lowercase), not
   extension — verified enry names empirically: .ts→TypeScript, .tsx→TSX, .js*→JavaScript.
4. **`reindex.py`**: `SCIP_CTAGS_COMMAND` default → staged binary, passed only if it exists.

VERIFIED: DoD test (`scip_batch_dod_test.go`) → **scip 15/15 vs universal 3/15** exported funcs.
Full live pipeline (rebuilt `zoekt-index.exe` → SCIP env → shard → `sym:` query) proven on
cockpit.ts: 132 symbols, `sym:createPendingAction/confirmPendingAction/getEffectiveRiskTier/
shouldRerender` all resolve (each was a universal-ctags miss). Full Pre Atlas reindex kicked
off after wiring. KNOWN FOLLOW-UP: per-file spawn of the 34MB tree-sitter binary is slow
(~1940 TS/TSX files in Pre Atlas); if painful, a persistent-pipe driver is the upgrade — but
scip's per-request flush means the pipe MIGHT not deadlock like universal did (unverified;
per-file spawn chosen as the proven-safe transport, furniture-first). COMMITTED+PUSHED to the

## 3-layer reindex failsafe (2026-07-22..23) — ALL 3 LAYERS DONE

Goal: keep the zoekt index fresh via 3 complementary layers, each catching a DIFFERENT
miss, all gated by a shared staleness check so they never reindex redundantly (the lesson
from today's atlas-consolidation autostart audit, transcript `b16d9fb0`, where
`PreAtlas-DailyPipeline` was redundant with the governance-daemon cron — both ran the same
pipeline unconditionally).

**Shared gate (DONE, committed fork `8494410`):** `reindex.py --if-stale` reindexes only if
newest `*.zoekt` shard > `ZOEKT_STALE_HOURS` (default 24). Verified: skips on fresh index.

- **Layer 1 — scheduled task (DONE):** `PreAtlas-ZoektReindex`, daily 05:30, FULL reindex
  (heartbeat, no --if-stale), `StartWhenAvailable`. Runs `C:\Python313\python.exe
  C:\Users\bruke\zoekt-win\reindex.py`. State=Ready.
- **Layer 3 — boot/logon catch-up (DONE+VERIFIED):** `PreAtlas-ZoektReindex-Boot`, AtLogon
  +3min, `reindex.py --if-stale`, scoped `-User MSI\bruke` (any-user logon trigger needs
  admin → access denied; per-user scope fixes it). Ran once → LastTaskResult=0.
- **Layer 2 — governance-daemon cron (DONE 2026-07-23, fest ZR0001):** added
  `ZOEKT_REINDEX_CRON = '35 5 * * *'` + `runZoektReindex()` runner to
  `services/delta-kernel/src/governance/governance_daemon.ts`, cloning `runSensorDaily`
  exactly (10 insertion points: JobName union, DaemonState field + init, process field,
  cron constant + timeout, schedule block, stop() cleanup, startup log, runJob switch,
  updateLastRun switch, runner). Also extended `TimelineEventType` with
  `ZOEKT_REINDEX_COMPLETE` and added `'zoekt_reindex'` to `server.ts` `/api/daemon/run`
  allow-list so it can be triggered manually. `tsc --noEmit` clean. VERIFIED live: after
  restart with `GOVERNANCE_DAEMON=1`, startup log emits `- Zoekt Reindex: 35 5 * * *`;
  `POST /api/daemon/run {"job":"zoekt_reindex"}` returned status=success in 114ms;
  `daemon.last_zoekt_reindex = 2026-07-23T06:59:38.808Z`. Underlying `reindex.py --if-stale`
  is a ~1s no-op on fresh index (`index fresh (6.7h < 24.0h) - skipping reindex`, exit 0) —
  layers stay complementary, never redundant. Pushed 2026-07-23 as commit `ce1d31e`
  to `lionestenzol/pre-atlas` `feat/atlas-setup-ui` (still unmerged from main by design).
  Fest ZR0001 marked completed → `festival-project/festivals/dungeon/completed/2026-07-23/`.
  **All 3 failsafe layers now LIVE:** L1 (Task Scheduler daily 05:30) + L2 (daemon cron
  05:35, requires `GOVERNANCE_DAEMON=1`) + L3 (logon catch-up), all gated on `--if-stale`.

## Agent Cookbook + Autopilot — COMPLETE

**Location:** `/root/festival-project/festivals/active/agent-cookbook-AC0001/` (WSL)

**Two skills:**
- `/cookbook` — run agents directly (scan, logs, validate, config, status, codegen, refactor, tests)
- `/autopilot` — gemma reads fest state, picks agents, executes lightweight free, escalates heavyweight to Claude

**Orchestrator design (minimal errors):**
- Python parses all fest state deterministically (TODO.md checklists, phase/sequence/task files)
- Gemma makes ONE decision: "which agent fits this task?" (simple prompt, constrained answer)
- If gemma fails → keyword fallback (deterministic matching)
- If both fail → escalate to Claude
- Heavyweight agents always escalate to Claude (never auto-run expensive work)
- Tasks never auto-marked as done (Claude verifies first)

**Key files:**
- `orchestrator.py` — gemma-powered fest orchestrator
- `mcp_server.py` — MCP server (built but using skills instead)
- `run.sh` — standalone CLI launcher
- `agents/` — 8 agent scripts
- `dispatcher/gemma_waiter_v2.py` — gemma dispatcher
- `Modelfile` — gemma-executor model definition
- `.venv/` — Python venv with mcp package

- `~/.claude/skills/cookbook/SKILL.md`
- `~/.claude/skills/autopilot/SKILL.md`

## project_full_commitment

<a id="project-full-commitment"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke/memory/project_full_commitment.md`*
*Name:* Full commitment — day job quit, canvas product is the real play
*Description:* On 2026-04-22 user fully committed to building the 2026 Wix/Figma canvas product. Day job quit. 90-day decision window starts. This is the load-bearing fact for all future sessions.

On **2026-04-22** user made the commitment: day job is quit, going all-in on building the canvas-first AI-native website builder. sitepull is now step 1 / infrastructure, not the product.

**The real product (new direction):**
- 2026 Wix/Figma — AI-native canvas for designing entire websites
- "Start from" drawer offers multiple entry points: URL (sitepull), Figma, screenshot, prompt, template, blank
- Chat + canvas + direct manipulation, no one-UI-rules-all
- Real code output, deployable
- Designer-developer audience, not designer-or-developer
- Category: "Figma meets Claude for the whole website"

**Why the pivot (keep this context):**
- "URL cloner" framing is too on-the-nose, legally exposed, single-use
- Canvas framing positions against an unclaimed hole (no AI-native canvas exists as of 2026-04-22)
- Lovable/Bolt/v0 all $40-50M ARR in ~6 months proves appetite; they all skipped the canvas
- User's sitepull tech becomes a silent engine room, not the storefront

**The 90-day decision window (starting 2026-04-22, ends 2026-07-21):**
- Week 1: runway math, brand+domain pick, record sitepull demo, landing page with waitlist
- Week 2: scrappy canvas prototype (Next.js + iframe), one "start from" works (URL), one edit via Claude
- Week 3: second "start from" works (prompt), waitlist live, 10+ user interviews
- Week 4: first paying beta user (or firm "yes I'd pay"), decide green/yellow/red

**Day-90 decision criteria:**
- GREEN: 50+ waitlist, 3+ "I'd pay" → fundraise or continue solo
- YELLOW: some signal, unclear shape → pivot framing, extend runway
- RED: crickets → sitepull returns to tool, rebuild finances

**Atlas goal:** `g-moake7hk-canvas-product-90-day-commitment` (project: `reframe`, deadline 2026-07-21, 7 criteria). Use `node ~/.claude/scripts/claude-goal.js show g-moake7hk-canvas-product-90-day-commitment` to check status. Mark criteria done via `claude-goal.js done <goal-id> <c1|c2|...>` as they ship. Tenant key lives at `C:\Users\bruke\Pre Atlas\.aegis-tenant-key` — export it as `ATLAS_API_KEY` before calling the CLI from other cwds.

**How to apply:**
- Treat every session as time-on-the-clock. Do not waste it on polish, refactors, or speculative features.
- When user suggests any work, sanity-check: does this move toward Week-N deliverables above, or is it scope creep?
- Forbidden time-sinks (call these out if user drifts): rewriting sitepull in TypeScript, redesigning anatomy map again, polishing brand before users, building Discord/blog/conf talks, hiring, fundraising before signal.
- The sitepull demo video is now BOTH validation AND fundraising artifact AND recruiting signal — its urgency multiplied.
- User is the only one who can: talk to users, name the brand, decide runway depth. Do not guess at these.
- At the start of each session, briefly acknowledge which week of the 90 the user is in, and what the week's goal is.
- Brand name is TBD as of 2026-04-22. Canvas repo location is TBD. Ask, don't assume.

## project_pre_atlas_services_fleet

<a id="project-pre-atlas-services-fleet"></a>
*Source: `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-bruke/memory/project_pre_atlas_services_fleet.md`*
*Name:* project-pre-atlas-services-fleet
*Description:* Pre Atlas has a 20-service fleet in services/; Bruke loses track of built ones (forgot openclaw existed)

`C:\Users\bruke\Pre Atlas\services\` holds **20 services** as of 2026-07-05: aegis-fabric, atlas-map-api, canvas-engine, cognitive-sensor, cortex, crucix, delta-kernel, delta-scp, droplist, memory-hub, mirofish, mosaic-dashboard, mosaic-orchestrator, openclaw, optogon, perception, search-stack, triangulation, uasc-executor, ws-gateway.

They form an interconnected fleet, not islands — e.g. **openclaw** (FastAPI multi-channel messaging gateway, port 3004, Atlas-native skills: status/brief/fest/simulate/approve) calls out to **mosaic-orchestrator** (port 3005), cognitive-sensor (8000), and mirofish (3003). Most of the fleet is powered down at any given time.

**The pattern:** Bruke ships fast and loses track of finished systems the moment they work. On 2026-07-05 he installed npm `openclaw` and was surprised to discover he'd already hand-built his own service of the same name — "i forgot i built this." openclaw runs fine; it just went quiet.

**Why:** velocity tax — completed-and-working = out of attention. This is precisely what his memory system + Atlas orchestration vision ([[user_atlas_vision]] if present) exist to solve: he needs an index of what he's built more than more built.

**How to apply:** When Bruke touches anything in Pre Atlas, don't assume he remembers the surrounding fleet. Offer a service inventory (what each is / does it run / what it depends on) before building anything new — the reuse case is strong and the "did I already build this" risk is real. Naming collisions with public packages are a live footgun (openclaw npm vs his). Relates to his assemble-first doctrine.
